# Simplification survey, round 3: 16 subsystem audits

Status: implemented
Archived: 2026-09-06

**Date**: 2026-08-26 · **Method**: whole-subsystem architectural audits, adversarially verified · **Actionable net**: -4090 LoC, -141 elements

## 0. Why a third round asks a different question

Rounds [1](./2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md) (49 shipped, #11416-#11422) and [2](./2026-08-26-simplification-survey-round2.md) (40 shipped, #11433-#11441) both hunted **redundant code** — the first partitioned by directory, the second by cross-cutting pattern. Between them they have already swept dead exports, unread fields, single-caller wrappers, expired compatibility, and duplicated helpers. A third pass of the same kind would mostly return noise.

Round 3 asks instead whether each subsystem needs the **shape** it has. Sixteen agents each took one subsystem, traced it end to end, and answered: how many mechanisms own the same decision here, could a whole layer stop existing, what is the smallest concept set that expresses what this does today.

That framing makes a third kind of result possible and valuable:

| Kind       | Meaning                                                                                                                                        |
| ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `delete`   | A concrete removal, same evidence bar as rounds 1-2.                                                                                           |
| `collapse` | A design change: two mechanisms become one, a layer is absorbed, a concept stops existing. Cost estimates include what the migration **adds**. |
| `keep`     | An apparent redundancy was examined and the current shape is right. Recorded so the next round stops re-asking.                                |

|                          | Count  |
| ------------------------ | ------ |
| Findings produced        | 80     |
| **Actionable, verified** | **26** |
| **Keep rulings**         | **32** |
| Refuted                  | 21     |

Every actionable finding went to an independent adversarial verifier at high reasoning effort, with the round-3-specific bar: a design change must **beat the recorded rationale by citing and defeating the dated document**, and the honest migration cost must survive recomputation. Checklist §13 is the standing warning here — 22 previous "reduction" PRs netted +5,046 LoC.

### Two findings the reconciliation removed

Two agents independently proposed retiring the `@shared/schemas` deep-import ratchet. One verifier accepted it; the other **refuted** it, citing `.claude/skills/find-simplification/SKILL.md:21` ("proposing to _shrink_ one is a good candidate; proposing to delete the ratchet mechanism is not") and two dated re-verify passes from 2026-08-21 and 2026-08-22 that made the identical observation and concluded "sealed — no action". The refutation wins; the item is in §4, not §2.

The two name-tombstone findings (from `repo-shape` and `guard-redundancy`) were the same item at two scopes; they are merged into the superset.

### The kill condition rounds 1-2 taught us

Round 2 shipped two items that had to be reverted on review. Both times a surveyor grepped the repo, found no reader, and concluded a value was dead — but the consumer was **outside the repo**: `resumeOutcome` rides the frozen CLI NDJSON wire, and `texra.agent.rounds` is honored from a user config file because `JsonConfigProvider` reads user stores before the catalog. Round 3 carried that as an explicit kill condition from the start.

### Suggested sequencing

These are architectural changes, not mechanical deletions, and two of them are larger than any single item in rounds 1-2 combined. Ship them in three tiers rather than one batch:

- **Tier A — batch normally.** The ten `low`-risk items plus the mechanical `medium` ones. Same lane-PR shape as rounds 1-2.
- **Tier B — one PR each.** `Retire the in-place workspace-storage transition` (-1550) and `Delete the nested Core config tree` (-220). Large, self-contained, and each deserves its own review thread.
- **Tier C — hold for an explicit decision.** `Delete the CLI external-inquiry surface` (-1050, high) and `One policy gate for all seven request kinds` (-50, high). The first removes a user-visible capability from one host; the second reshapes approval authority, which has its own ratchet.

## 1b. Outcome of implementation (2026-08-26)

Nine of the ten lanes shipped as #11452-#11460. Central validation — typecheck,
lint, dead-code ratchet and the full 10k-test suite, run on the merged set and
then on each branch in isolation — overturned three verified findings. All three
were caught by tests, not by review, and each is recorded here so they are not
re-proposed.

**Reverted: `RunFlowLifecycleOptions.onRunEnd` (run-lifecycle).** Deleting the
injection seam and importing `stopLeanServersForEndedRun` directly pulls
`src/tools/lean/leanLanguageServices.ts` into bash's module closure, which
`src/test-kernel/architecture/toolRegistryCycle.vitest.ts` forbids. The seam's
own doc comment stated this ("Kept injected so this module does not statically
reach tool-domain services such as the Lean language adapter"); the verifier
judged the module-level boundary illusory because the ratchet baseline already
carries an `agent -> tools` edge. The ratchet was the wrong guard to check.

**Lane deferred: `P7-events`.** Its lead finding — delete the `streamStates`
option on `onStreamMetadataChanged` because "it round-trips a map the renderer
already has" — is false. `SessionFactApplier.buildStreamStatesForRefresh`
splices in the stream's _about-to-be-applied_ status, which the status machine
has not been written yet at that point, and says so in its own docstring.
Without it the first metadata send reports `ready` where it must report
`running`, which two independent suites pin. The lane's other two findings
(fold the payload-free port methods into `invalidate`, one owner for
stream-removal barrier settlement, -36 LoC together) are unaffected and can be
re-proposed on their own.

**Partly reverted: the approval-policy read (settings-pipeline).**
`readPlatformSetting` resolves through the ambient platform provider, but the
workspace transition re-seeds from the provider instance whose workspace store
was just swapped, so reading through the platform returns the pre-swap value.
The transition's post-commit and post-rollback re-seeds regressed. That one call
site and its host-neutral reader are restored; the finding's other three rewires
stand.

**Excluded by maintainer decision.** `Delete the CLI's entire external-inquiry
surface` (-1050) was not implemented. It remains a verified finding, not a
refuted one.

The lesson generalizes the round-2 one. Round 2 learned that an in-repo grep
does not prove deadness when the consumer is outside the repo. Round 3 adds:
at the architectural level, a claim that a seam or parameter is redundant must
be checked against **what the code says it is for**, not only against who calls
it. All three overturned findings had a docstring or a guard stating the reason,
and in each case the verifier reasoned past it.

## 2. Findings in detail

### run-lifecycle — The run lifecycle, end to end

#### Delete `RunFlowLifecycleOptions.onRunEnd`: an injection seam with one implementation, guarding an import edge its own caller already has

- **Kind**: delete · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -10 LoC, -1 elements (proposer claimed -16; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/agent/runtime/AgentRunLifecycle.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/executeAgent.ts`

**Evidence**

`RunFlowLifecycleOptions.onRunEnd` (AgentRunLifecycle.ts:79-90) is documented as "Kept injected so this module does not statically reach tool-domain services such as the Lean language adapter." It is consumed by the `runOnRunEnd` guard (:659-668) and fired at :730 (parked-handle teardown) and :801 (the run-end finally).

It has exactly one implementation. `runFlowWithLifecycle` has two production call sites, both in executeAgent.ts (:450 and :596), and both build their options through `buildLifecycleOptions` (:218-231), whose only `onRunEnd` value is `(runId) => stopLeanServersForEndedRun(runId)` (:228). A repo-wide grep for `onRunEnd` outside these files returns nothing in src/ or packages/.

The boundary it protects does not exist. `executeAgent.ts:41` already does `import { stopLeanServersForEndedRun } from '@tools/lean/leanLanguageServices'`, and :39 imports `@tools/delegation/nativeSubagentStrategy` — the same directory, one hop from the module the seam is protecting. The ratchet that governs this is `config/ratchets/architecture-edges-baseline.json`, which is stated to hold "Directed top-level src subsystem edges" and already lists `{ "from": "agent", "to": "tools", "kind": "value" }`, so moving the import one file sideways widens no baseline. Neither `RunFlowLifecycleOptions` nor `runFlowWithLifecycle` is exported from `src/agent/runtime/index.ts` or from `packages/agent`, so there is no external supplier of a second implementation, and neither name appears in `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json`.

**Proposal**

Import `stopLeanServersForEndedRun` directly in AgentRunLifecycle.ts and call it inside the existing `runOnRunEnd` guard, which keeps its name, its try/catch, and both call sites (:730, :801) exactly as they are — only the `options?.onRunEnd` indirection and the option field disappear. Then drop `onRunEnd` from `RunFlowLifecycleOptions` and from `buildLifecycleOptions` in executeAgent.ts.

About -24 lines removed (the field plus its 12-line doc comment, the indirection, the wiring) against +8 added (the import and the direct call), so roughly -16 net LoC and 2 elements.

One check the implementer owes: `@tools/lean/leanLanguageServices` must not transitively import `@agent/runtime/AgentRunLifecycle`. executeAgent.ts proves the edge resolves at runtime one hop deeper, but run `npm run typecheck` plus a CLI smoke launch to confirm no new module cycle before landing.

**What we give up**

The stated ability to run the lifecycle with a different (or no) run-end hook. Since the option has one supplier and the module is not on any public surface, that ability is theoretical; if a second run-end side effect ever appears, it goes next to the Lean stop in the same guard, which is where the first one effectively lives anyway.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. FALSE GREP CLAIM (the finding's central evidence pillar). "A repo-wide grep for `onRunEnd` outside these files returns nothing in src/ or packages/" is wrong. `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/AgentRunLifecycle.vitest.ts` injects a second implementation at FIVE sites — lines 270, 293, 313, 337 and 1002 — each a `const stopSessionsForRun = vi.fn(async (_runId: ExecutionId) => {})` passed as `{ onRunEnd: stopSessionsForRun }`. Five tests assert against it: 'stops the Lean servers attributed to a run when the run ends', 'still stops the Lean servers when the run fails', 'does not stop the Lean servers when a tool-use run parks at WAITING', 'stops the Lean servers attributed to a subagent when it ends', and 'runs run-end cleanup when waiting transcript teardown fails' (the :730 parked-handle path). So "exactly one implementation" should read "exactly one PRODUCTION implementation"; the seam has a live test consumer the finding denies exists, and deleting the field is a compile break in that file (excess-property check on the options literal), not a no-op.

2. MIGRATION COST OMITTED. Those five tests must be rewritten onto `vi.mock('@tools/lean/leanLanguageServices', ...)`. This is cheap and idiomatic — the same file already runs two `vi.hoisted` + `vi.mock` pairs (`@agent/storage/executionLifecycle` and `@agent/trace`, lines 58-90) with a `mockClear()` in `beforeEach` — but it is roughly +8 lines of mock scaffolding against −9 lines of per-test `vi.fn` and option-literal removal, i.e. test LoC net ~0, not the zero work the claim implies.

3. THE RATCHET REBUTTAL ANSWERS A CLAIM THE RATIONALE NEVER MADE. The finding is right that `config/ratchets/architecture-edges-baseline.json` already carries `{ "from": "agent", "to": "tools", "kind": "value" }` (line 14) and that the baseline's own `semantics` string scopes it to "Directed top-level src subsystem edges", so nothing is widened. But the doc comment's rationale is module-level, not ratchet-level: "so THIS MODULE does not statically reach tool-domain services". `AgentRunLifecycle.ts` currently imports zero `@tools/*` and the change makes it the sixth file in `src/agent/runtime/` to do so (after executeAgent.ts, agentToolResolution.ts, HostInteractions.ts, agentSettingTools.ts, toolInjection.ts). The rebuttal has to be "the module-level boundary buys nothing", not "no ratchet moves".

4. THE RATIONALE IS A DELIBERATE, RECENT, AUTHOR-MADE DECISION — the finding does not cite it. The seam was not accidental: PR #10350 (`dadd8f20ca`, 2026-08-15, eleven days before this survey) landed with a third sub-commit literally titled "fix(agent): inject the Lean run-end stop at composition time", and the same PR ADDED the `@tools/lean/leanLanguageServices` import to executeAgent.ts that the finding cites as pre-existing proof the boundary is illusory. Reviewing that PR's threads: the injection was the author's own choice, not a review demand (the three review findings were about WAITING suspensions, subagent attribution, and a CHANGELOG wording nit). Any PR must beat this in its body, not silently revert it.

5. LINE-NUMBER AND SIZE DRIFT. The doc block is 8 lines (82-89) plus the field at :90, so 9 lines removed, not "the field plus its 12-line doc comment". The field span is :82-90, not ":79-90". `runOnRunEnd` is defined at :659-668 and fired at :730 and :801 — those three are correct.

6. THE "CHECK THE IMPLEMENTER OWES" IS ALREADY SETTLED, NO SMOKE LAUNCH NEEDED. `src/tools/lean/leanLanguageServices.ts` is a 105-line leaf whose only two imports are `import type { ExecutionId } from '@shared/schemas'` (:14) and `import type { ... } from './leanTypes'` (:16). Both are type-only and erased by esbuild, so the emitted module has zero runtime imports and there is no reachable path back to `@agent/runtime/AgentRunLifecycle`. Cycle risk is structurally nil; `npm run typecheck` suffices.

7. NO EXTERNAL-CONSUMER EXPOSURE — the finding's claim here holds, verified independently. `grep -rn 'RunFlowLifecycleOptions' src packages` (excluding dist) returns only AgentRunLifecycle.ts:63/:468 and executeAgent.ts:52/:220/:326. `src/agent/runtime/index.ts` does not re-export `runFlowWithLifecycle`; `packages/agent/src/**` never mentions it; the `packages/agent/dist/types/.../AgentRunLifecycle.d.ts:16` hit is a gitignored build artifact (`git check-ignore` confirms `.gitignore:2:dist/`). Neither name is in `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json`. The field crosses no CLI NDJSON wire, no settings key, no persisted file, and no texra-action result contract.

8. ELEMENT COUNT OVERSTATED. −1 element (one field on one exported interface). Nothing exported is deleted, no file is deleted, and the lambda in `buildLifecycleOptions` is not a declared construct. "−2 elements" does not hold under the R6 counting rule (files from diffstat, `^[+-]export` symbols, class/interface/enum declarations).

9. ADJACENT-BUT-NOT-BLOCKING PRIOR RULING to cite in the PR body: `docs/dev/audits/2026-07-12-agent-sdk-readiness-checkpoint.md:170-180` item 6 holds the layered run-options bags (`RunAgentOptions` / `ExecuteAgentOptions` / `AgentLaunchInput` / `RunFlowLifecycleOptions` re-declaring overlapping subsets) as "Strategic, gated — do not flatten unattended". Deleting `onRunEnd` is NOT that flattening — `onRunEnd` is synthesized inside `buildLifecycleOptions` and has no counterpart in the upstream bags — but the PR should say so explicitly so a reviewer does not read it as an unattended flatten.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The proposal itself is sound and I could not kill it on any of the seven criteria. Not a duplicate: `rounds12-index.md` (162 lines, 151 titles) has three Lean rows (maxSessions eviction, toolchain/pid fields, liveness ownership) all inside `src/tools/lean/direct/`, none touching the lifecycle seam, and neither survey doc (`.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md`, `2026-08-26-simplification-survey-round2.md`) mentions `onRunEnd`. No dated ruling decided it: no hit for `onRunEnd` or `RunFlowLifecycleOptions` anywhere in docs/proposals, docs/architecture, docs/prds, AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md or the review checklist beyond the adjacent options-bag note in the 07-12 SDK checkpoint, and `gh issue list --search` returns nothing. No settled surface is touched — the `agent -> tools` value edge is already in the architecture-edges baseline, nothing is on the frozen `@agent` SDK surface or in the knip baseline, and `leanLanguageServices.ts` has no `vscode` import so the VS Code-free zone is untouched. No external consumer: the field is a same-process function reference that never crosses the CLI NDJSON wire, a settings key, a persisted file, or the texra-action result contract. Not churn: the blast radius is two production files plus one test file, and the concept count drops by one.

I re-walked the path myself rather than trusting the write-up. `runFlowWithLifecycle` has exactly two production call sites (executeAgent.ts:450, :596), both routed through `buildLifecycleOptions` (:218-231), whose only `onRunEnd` value is the hardcoded `(runId) => stopLeanServersForEndedRun(runId)` at :228. There is no configuration path, no host variation, and no exported surface through which a second supplier could arrive. That is a single-supplier injection seam, which §13's "No trivial-identity or single-caller extractions" and AGENTS.md:645 ("grep the caller count... single-caller extractions are banned") license removing. The gold-standard PRD's own RUN-ring table (`.agents/docs/rejected/architecture/2026-06-29-prd-runtime-gold-standard.md:235`) already assigns run-scoped resource disposal — untrack, per-stream cleanup, dispose ModelCell.handler and trace — to this exact `finally`, so stopping the run's Lean servers there is in-charter for the ring rather than a foreign concern smuggled in.

What the finding got wrong is its headline evidence: the repo-wide grep claim is false, and the test kernel is a real second consumer at five injection sites. I weighed that as a possible criterion-7 kill and decided it is not one — it is a false pillar in the evidence, not a misread hop in the lifecycle trace, which I verified line by line (:659 guard, :730 parked-handle teardown, :801 terminal finally, WAITING correctly skipped via `keepLeaseWatcher`). The five tests migrate mechanically onto the `vi.mock` idiom the same file already uses twice, and they keep asserting the same four behaviors.

The real tension is that this reverses a deliberate decision made eleven days ago in a reviewed PR whose rationale still sits in the file. I judged that the rationale loses on its own terms: it protects a module-level boundary that nothing enforces, whose immediate caller in the same directory already holds the import, and whose supposed benefit — swapping the run-end hook — has no mechanism to be exercised. The honest cost against it is one new leaf import into a module that had none, and the loss is theoretical. That trade is a small win, so it survives, but the PR body owes an explicit "this reverses #10350's third commit and here is why" rather than presenting the seam as an unexplained accident.

Honest numbers, recomputed: production is −9 in AgentRunLifecycle.ts (8-line doc block + field, minus the `if (!options?.onRunEnd) return;` guard line, plus the new import) and −2 in executeAgent.ts (the `@tools/lean` import at :41, which has no other use in that file, plus the option line at :228), so −11 production LoC. Tests net roughly +1 (about +8 of hoisted-mock scaffolding against −7 of per-test `vi.fn` declarations and option-literal lines; the `{ isSubagent: true, onRunEnd }` site at :337 keeps its line). Total ≈ −10 LoC, −1 element. The claimed −16 / −2 is optimistic but the sign is right, which is the part that matters after 22 prior "reduction" PRs netted +5,046.

</details>

### identity-model — Run, session, execution and stream identity

#### Delete the `bare` arm of RunContext: production installs exactly one ambient run context and it is always `launch`

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -33 LoC, -9 elements (proposer claimed -45; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/agent/runtime/RunContext.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/AgentLaunchContext.ts`, `src/test-kernel/agent/progressTestUtils.ts`

**Evidence**

`src/agent/runtime/RunContext.ts` (226 lines) declares `RunContext` as a discriminated union: `LaunchRunContext` carries the `RunScope` struct, `BareRunContext` carries loose optional `streamId`/`executionId`/`agentName`/`workingDirectory`/`session` fields via `BareRunIdentity`. Six accessors (`getRunContextStreamId`, `…ExecutionId`, `…AgentName`, `…WorkingDirectory`, `…Session`, `…Interactions`) all route through `getRunContextField()` at `RunContext.ts:167-174`, whose only job is to dispatch on `kind`.

The ALS is installed in exactly one production place: `grep -rn "withRunContext(" src packages/*/src | grep -v vitest | grep -v test-kernel` returns two hits — the throw message at `RunContext.ts:162` and the single real call at `src/agent/runtime/AgentLaunchContext.ts:146`, inside `withExecutionRunContext`, which always passes `runScope: ctx.runScope`. `createRunContext(` likewise has exactly one non-test caller (`AgentLaunchContext.ts:147`). All 47 other call sites are under `src/test-kernel/`, and only 7 of those already pass `runScope`.

The migration target already exists: `src/test-kernel/agent/progressTestUtils.ts:444-461` exports `testRunScope(streamId, {session, signal, interactions})`, which defaults `executionId`, `agentName`, `session` and `signal`. Round 1 shipped the identical move for a different seam ("Make ToolCallContext.workPlanState required and delete the plan/todo no-session degraded branches"), so the precedent is on the record. `git log --oneline -12 -- src/agent/runtime/RunContext.ts` shows the last substantive change was #10594/#10647 (tool-policy injection), which did not revisit the union.

**Proposal**

Owner: `RunScope`. Make `CreateRunContextOptions.runScope` required, delete `BareRunIdentity`, `BareRunContext`, `CreateBareRunContextOptions`, the `kind` discriminant and the second branch of `createRunContext`. The six accessors become one-liners reading `tryUseRunContext()?.runScope[field]` — they keep returning `| undefined` because no context installed at all is still a real state, so no downstream degraded branch changes and no behavior moves.

Test migration is mechanical and adds no lines: `createRunContext({ streamId, session })` becomes `createRunContext({ runScope: testRunScope(streamId, { session }) })`. Add an `executionId` override to `testRunScope` (~3 lines) for the handful of sites that pass only an execution id.

Honest cost: about −55 lines in `RunContext.ts`, +3 in the test helper, ~40 test call sites edited in place at roughly equal length. Net about −45 LoC and one fewer run-identity shape in the ambient layer.

**What we give up**

The ability for a test to install a partial run identity — e.g. a stream id with no session — in order to exercise a "no session" branch. That state is unreachable in production, since the one installer always supplies a full `RunScope`; a test that needs "no run context" simply omits `withRunContext`. Restoring the union later is a 40-line re-add if a second, genuinely partial installer ever appears.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. THE `kind` DISCRIMINANT HAS THREE PRODUCTION READERS OUTSIDE `RunContext.ts`, and the evidence misses all of them. The claim says the six accessors "all route through `getRunContextField()` … whose only job is to dispatch on `kind`", implying the discriminant is otherwise unread. `grep -rn "kind === 'launch'\|kind !== 'launch'" src` finds: `src/tools/delegation/WorkflowScriptTool.ts:249` (`if (contexts?.runContext?.kind !== 'launch') throw new Error('delegate_multi_agents requires an active launched agent session.')`), `src/tools/delegation/subagentExecution.ts:127-130` (`parentContext.kind === 'launch' ? parentContext.runScope.delegationAgentScope : undefined`), and `src/tools/delegation/delegationAvailability.ts:149-151` (`activeDelegationScope()`). This makes the change touch production delegation code, not tests only — it also strengthens the case, since all three collapse (`delegationAgentScope` is a RunScope field with no bare counterpart, so the narrowing exists purely to reach it), but the risk rating and the file list must reflect it. The WorkflowScriptTool guard must become a "no context installed" check, not a kind check.

2. A HOST TEST MOCKS `tryUseRunContext` WITH A BARE-SHAPED LITERAL AND WILL BREAK SILENTLY. `src/test-kernel/desktop/DesktopToolEditApproval.vitest.ts:164-187` is a `doMock` of `@agent/runtime/RunContext` (it is entry `sites[29]` in `config/ratchets/host-agent-mock-baseline.json`) returning `{ session: { approvals, interactions } }` — no `kind`, no `runScope`. It is an untyped `vi.fn()`, so nothing fails at compile time; after the collapse `getRunContextSession()` reads `context.runScope.session` and gets `undefined`, and the desktop approval test changes behavior rather than erroring. Must be rewritten to `{ runScope: { session } }`.

3. "TEST MIGRATION IS MECHANICAL AND ADDS NO LINES" is false on both halves. Measured with the repo's own prettier at printWidth 80: +1.45 lines per site (11 sites, 37 → 53). And it is not mechanical, because `testRunScope` DEFAULTS a session (`options.session ?? sessionWithInteractions(interactions)`, progressTestUtils.ts:444-461) while ~12 current sites deliberately install NO session so production falls back to `defaultSession()`. Concretely, `src/test-kernel/agent/followUp/AcceptRunFilesProgressEvents.vitest.ts` registers its approval handler on `defaultSession().interactions.use({ requestToolEditApproval… })` (:50-58) and installs `createRunContext({ streamId, executionId })` with no session (:104) precisely so the tool routes there. Migrated naively it routes to the fake session and the suite breaks. Each session-less site needs a deliberate `session: defaultSession()` decision, not a sed.

4. `testRunScope` NEEDS MORE THAN THE CLAIMED +3 LINES. Its signature is `testRunScope(streamId, { session, signal, interactions })`. Sites needing `workingDirectory` (`DiagnosticsTool.vitest.ts:15-18`, `OpenPdfTool.vitest.ts:123-126`, `grep.vitest.ts:168-170`) and `executionId` (`DirectLspAdapter.vitest.ts:801`, `OpenPdfTool.vitest.ts:96-98`, `ReadToolRange.vitest.ts:24-27`, `AcceptRunFilesProgressEvents.vitest.ts:104`) both need new overrides, and ~10 session-only or workingDirectory-only sites must invent a stream id they do not currently have (`goalStore.vitest.ts:106`, `SessionIsolation.vitest.ts:33`, `DefaultSessionLifecycle.vitest.ts:176`, `InquiryContinuationSession.vitest.ts:173/206`, `ToolUseFollowUpProgressEvents.vitest.ts:126/147/164`, `DiagnosticsTool`, `grep`, `OpenPdfTool:139`). Call it +10 in the helper, +8 in new constants.

5. THE PATH LIST IS OFF BY ~22 FILES. Beyond the three listed: the three production delegation files above; `src/test-kernel/support/toolEnvironment.ts` (types `env.run` as `CreateRunContextOptions`, 5 callers pass bare `run: {…}`); `src/test-kernel/tools/DelegationTools.vitest.ts:16` and `src/test-kernel/tools/SubagentExecutionChildStreamId.vitest.ts:68`, which each hand-reimplement `getRunContextField`'s dispatch and get simpler; three files that build `kind: 'launch'` object literals directly (`DelegationAgentScope.vitest.ts:60`, `WorkflowScriptTool.vitest.ts:119`, `WorkflowScriptAgentRunner.vitest.ts:127`); `AgentLaunchContext.vitest.ts:383/423`; the desktop mock; and ~18 more vitest files holding the ~39 bare `createRunContext` calls.

6. TWO EXISTING TESTS ENCODE THE UNION AND MUST GO, WHICH THE WRITE-UP DOES NOT BUDGET. `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/RunContext.vitest.ts:45-53` ("reads a bare context model from its one-shot cell", asserts `context.kind === 'bare'`) is deleted outright, and :69-79 asserts `'streamId' in context === false` for launch contexts — an invariant that becomes vacuous. Deleting them is correct but it is coverage removal, so it belongs in the "what we give up" section.

7. "-5 elements" UNDERCOUNTS. The collapse removes `BareRunIdentity`, `BareRunContext`, `CreateBareRunContextOptions`, `CreateLaunchRunContextOptions`, the `CreateRunContextOptions` union, `RunContextCommon` (folds into the single interface), `CommonRunContextFieldNames`, `commonRunContextFields()`, `getRunContextField()`, and the `kind` field — 9-10 declarations, plus the two test-local dispatch copies.

8. "no downstream degraded branch changes and no behavior moves" is right about production but should be stated precisely: the accessors keep returning `| undefined` only for the no-context-installed case, and the WorkflowScriptTool refusal narrows from "bare or missing context" to "missing context" — unreachable in production given the single installer, but it is a semantic change to a user-facing error path, not a no-op.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The load-bearing fact — one production installer, always `launch` — is verifiable and I re-verified it end to end, including the `getCurrentToolContexts` hop that could have hidden a second construction site. Nothing in docs/proposals, docs/prds, AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, the review checklist, or the ratchets rules the other way; the one prior commit to touch the bare arm hardened it against drift rather than defending its existence, which is itself an argument that its only remaining consumer is test convenience. No wire, persisted-file, settings-key, or texra-action exposure. The honest LoC is a real if modest reduction (~-30 to -35, versus the claimed -45) because two errors offset: the call-site expansion is +64 rather than 0 (measured at printWidth 80), while the file saving is ~-90 rather than -55 (I wrote the collapsed file: 226 → 130). The durable win is the invariant, not the lines — production installs exactly one context shape, and after this the type says so. I raise risk from low to medium because the change is not test-only (three delegation files in the delegate_multi_agents / subagent path) and because one host test mocks the module with a bare-shaped literal that typechecks fine and fails silently.

</details>

#### Retire `StorageKey`, the third run-addressing vocabulary: the usage key should be the executionId the run already owns

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -25 LoC, -4 elements (proposer claimed -75; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/shared/schemas/identifiers.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/AgentLaunchContext.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/UsageMonitor.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/reflection/ReflectionServices.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/reflection/runReflectionFlow.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/reflection/output/outputState.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/reflection/output/roundSummary.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/reflection/output/outputValidation.ts`, `src/tools/agentCliShared.ts`, `src/shared/schemas/progressEvents.ts`, `src/transcript/StreamSnapshotStore.ts`, `src/controllers/progressView/backend/LitSessionRenderer.ts`, `packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/stateUtils.ts`

**Evidence**

`StorageKey` is declared at `src/shared/schemas/identifiers.ts:14` as `string & {__brand}` documented "Run-scoped storage key: a run stage ID or an execution ID" — i.e. its type IS the union of two other run vocabularies. It has exactly two mint sites and they disagree: `src/agent/runtime/AgentLaunchContext.ts:458` mints `(parentStage.id || executionId)` (a fresh nanoid per `beginRunStage`, `AgentLaunchContext.ts:270-279`; `StageHandle.id` is `string` at `src/agent/trace/AgentTrace.ts:98`, so the `|| executionId` arm is effectively dead), while `src/tools/agentCliShared.ts:79` mints `executionId as StorageKey`. From there it is threaded as a required field through 6 interfaces: `AgentLaunchContext.ts:84`, `UsageMonitor.ts:84`, `ReflectionServices.ts:22`, `runReflectionFlow.ts:70`, `outputState.ts:43`, and the wire payload `progressEvents.ts:89`.

It exists only as the key of the per-stream usage map — and **no reader keys that map**. `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/streamArtifactProjection.ts:56` does `[...store.getRunUsage(streamId).values()]`. `packages/extension/src/frontend/statusBar/StatusBarUsageTracker.ts:34` does `...getRunUsage(streamId).values()`. `packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/stateUtils.ts:94-99` (`totalRunUsage`) does `sumUsageStats(Object.values(runUsage))` behind a WeakMap memo, and `stateUtils.ts:22` already carries the comment "One run per tab — no longer keyed by runId." The two CLI `SessionRendererPort` implementors name the parameter `_storageKey` and discard it (`packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/sessionSignalsAdapter.ts:168`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/runProgressRenderer.ts:446`).

It is also circular: `UsageMonitor.ts:199` emits `stageId: logger.activeStageId() ?? storageKey` — a value derived FROM a stage id is fed back AS a stage id.

Dated context checked: `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-run-classification-consolidation.md` (Axis U) made `StreamSnapshotStore` the one usage accumulator but never questioned the key. `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-15-shared-contracts-and-retirement.md:110-121` freezes the CLI NDJSON progress vocabulary but blesses boundary re-projection as "the projection pattern done exactly right" — so the wire keeps `updateStreamUsage.storageKey` (declared `packages/cli/src/runtime/cliNdjsonProgressEvents.ts:70`, forwarded verbatim at `packages/cli/src/runtime/sessionProgressSubscription.ts:112`) and only its value changes. `gh issue view 10809` (2026-08-17 storage-layer freeze list) puts the store-side usage machinery — overlay replay, `usageUnparsed` round-trip — on a bugfix-only freeze pending the sidecar campaign, so this proposal must not touch those; it changes what is passed in, not how the store folds.

**Proposal**

Owner: `ExecutionId`. Delete the `StorageKey` brand from `src/shared/schemas/identifiers.ts` and replace every declaration with `ExecutionId`. Delete the `storageKey` field from `AgentLaunchContext` (:84), `UsageMonitorContext` (:84), `ReflectionServices` (:22), `RunReflectionFlowInput` (:70) and `OutputStateDeps` (:43) — all five already have `runScope.executionId` in scope. `UsageMonitor` emits `storageKey: executionId` on the wire payload (shape unchanged, so the frozen NDJSON contract holds; `agentCliShared.ts:79` already emits exactly this). For the one genuine stage use at `UsageMonitor.ts:199`, pass `parentStage.id` explicitly as a `runStageId` on the monitor context rather than reusing the storage key for two jobs.

No persisted migration is needed: `usageStats.json` is a record summed by every reader, so a stream whose old entries are nanoid-keyed and whose new entries are executionId-keyed still totals correctly — no double count, no loss. Record on the sidecar-campaign issue (#10809 successor) that the map itself should become one `TokenUsageStats` per stream, since after this change the key is 1:1 with the stream's execution and still nobody reads it.

Honest cost: ~90 lines deleted across 13 files, ~15 added back (the explicit `runStageId`, the wire projection). Net about −75 LoC, and one entire run-addressing vocabulary stops existing.

**What we give up**

Per-resume-attempt usage granularity, which the comment at `src/shared/schemas/progressEvents.ts:87` claims ("tool-use can resume → multiple runs per tab") and no code uses. Today each resume opens a new stage and gets its own key; keyed by executionId a resume accumulates onto the same key. Every displayed number is a sum over the map, so nothing user-visible changes. Restoring granularity later means re-introducing a per-attempt id, which is cheap because the attempt already has one (`inBandSubagentExecution.ts:167` mints `attempt`-salted ids).

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. FALSE headline claim: "no reader keys that map". Two readers key it. `src/controllers/progressView/backend/LitSessionRenderer.ts:237` does `this.state.snapshots.getRunUsage(streamId).get(storageKey) ?? usage` — a keyed read. `packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/slices/runTrackingSlice.ts:72` does `draft.runUsage[runId] = usage` — a keyed _replace_. Both survive the change (the store's per-key value at StreamSnapshotStore.ts:854-860 is cumulative, so replace-then-sum still totals), but the PR must argue "keyed identity plumbing, no per-key display" rather than assert the map is unkeyed. As written a reviewer disproves the evidence in one grep.

2. WRONG CITATION on the dead arm. "`StageHandle.id` is `string` at src/agent/trace/AgentTrace.ts:98" is wrong twice: `StageHandle` is declared at AgentTrace.ts:52 and its id is `string | undefined` (":53-54 — undefined for skipped (passthrough) stages"); line 98 is `StreamHandle.id`. The `|| executionId` arm at AgentLaunchContext.ts:458 is dead in practice — but because `beginRunStage` (AgentLaunchContext.ts:270-279) calls `openStage(label, { kind: 'run' })` with no `skip`, and both implementations always mint an id (TraceEmitter.ts:219-223, noopTrace.ts:56-57) — not because the type forbids undefined.

3. LoC OVERSTATED ~3x. "~90 lines deleted, ~15 added, net -75" does not survive a line count. Deletable physical lines in production: identifiers.ts 2-3 (comment + type), 7 single-name type-import lines (agentCliShared.ts:35, StreamSnapshotStore.ts:52, AgentLaunchContext.ts:50, runReflectionFlow.ts:21, ReflectionServices.ts:8, roundSummary.ts:13, outputState.ts:25), and 5 field/mint/return lines (AgentLaunchContext.ts:84,:458,:533; ReflectionServices.ts:22; runReflectionFlow.ts:70; outputState.ts:43 — 6, one of which is offset). progressEvents.ts, StreamSnapshotStore's map type params, LitSessionRenderer, SessionFactApplier and SessionRendererPort are 0-line type-only edits (the last two already declare the param `string`). Tests contribute ~12 import-name lines; the ~20 `as StorageKey` casts are inline and delete 0 lines. Production adds ~+4 for `runStageId`. Honest net ≈ -25 LoC.

4. ELEMENT COUNT OVERSTATED. -1 exported type (`StorageKey`) and -4 interface fields (AgentLaunchContext.ts:84, ReflectionServices.ts:22, runReflectionFlow.ts:70, outputState.ts:43). `UsageMonitorContext.storageKey` (UsageMonitor.ts:84) is _renamed_, not deleted — that interface has no `runScope` in scope, contradicting the proposal's "all five already have runScope.executionId in scope" (four do; UsageMonitorContext does not). And the proposal ADDS `runStageId` there, so UsageMonitor ends one field wider. Net -4 elements, not -6.

5. CHURN AGAINST A JUST-MERGED PR, unstated. `ReflectionServices.ts:22` and `outputState.ts:43` were _added_ by the rounds-1/2 item recorded at .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md:894-917. Round 3 deletes both. The end state is strictly better, but the PR body must cite that item or it reads as a revert of work merged days earlier.

6. MISSED STRONGER CUT. The only two production readers of the reflection-side value are debug strings: outputValidation.ts:65 (`debugInternal` template) and roundSummary.ts:77 (`logger.debug` data bag). Neither needs an id re-sourced from `runScope` — the trace already carries execution scope. Deleting the field from the roundSummary bag outright is cleaner than rewriting it to `deps.runScope.executionId`.

7. UNDER-SOLD DEFENSE. The finding buries the single fact that defeats the frozen-wire objection: `src/tools/agentCliShared.ts:79` already emits `executionId` on `updateStreamUsage.storageKey`, so the public wire has always carried both semantics. Lead with that, not with "shape unchanged".

8. TYPE CHOICE CAVEAT. `ExecutionIdSchema` (identifiers.ts:7-10) requires hex; legacy persisted keys are nanoid (`generateShortId` → `nanoid`, src/utils/core/index.ts:495, non-hex alphabet). Nothing parses the key today (`RunUsageMapSchema` = `z.record(z.string(), …)` at usage.ts:109; `UpdateRunUsageMessageSchema.runId` = `z.string()` at progressView/outbound.ts:154), so declaring the field `ExecutionId` is safe — but the PR must state that no validation may be added to this key, or a later "tighten the schema" PR rejects every persisted sidecar written before the change.

9. NON-ISSUE worth recording so a reviewer does not raise it: billing is untouched. `UsageMonitor.logToBackend` sends totals/provider only, never `storageKey`.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Round-3 architectural bar: the claim has to beat the recorded rationale, and here it does. The recorded rationale for `StorageKey` is a single comment (progressEvents.ts:87, "tool-use can resume → multiple runs per tab") that no code consumes — every one of the five consumers sums the map (streamArtifactProjection.ts:56, StatusBarUsageTracker.ts:34, stateUtils.ts:98, and the two CLI ports that name the parameter `_storageKey` and discard it). The type's own doc line is a confession that it is the union of two other vocabularies, and its two mint sites already disagree, one of them already minting exactly what this proposal makes universal. That is a genuine third addressing vocabulary with no reader-visible semantics, which is the strongest shape a collapse claim can have here.

The three dated rulings that could have killed it were all checked against their actual text rather than their titles: 2026-05-30 rules on the stageId fallback (the proposal preserves it), 2026-08-03 Axis U rules on accumulator count and never mentions the key, and #10809 freezes the store's fold/replay machinery which this change does not touch. The frozen NDJSON surface survives because the payload interface is shared verbatim and one production emitter already writes `executionId` into that field.

What the finding got wrong is presentation and arithmetic, not direction. The "no reader keys that map" sentence is disprovable in one grep and would be the first thing a reviewer attacks; the `StageHandle.id` citation points at the wrong interface; the -75/-6 numbers are inflated because most of the change is 0-line type edits and because `UsageMonitorContext` gains a field instead of losing one. Corrected, the win is about -25 LoC and -4 elements plus one exported concept gone. Under section 13's standard (22 prior "reduction" PRs netted +5,046) a corrected -25 is still real, and the concept deletion is the actual payoff.

Risk stays medium: the change alters value semantics on a frozen public wire and on a persisted key space. Both are argued safe and I verified both (mixed keys still total; no schema validates the key), but "argued safe on a persisted+public surface" is exactly the shape that forced two round-2 reverts, so it does not drop to low.

</details>

### event-planes — The event and fact planes

#### Delete the `streamStates` option on `onStreamMetadataChanged`: it round-trips a map the renderer already reads from the same owner

- **Kind**: delete · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -55 LoC, 1 elements (proposer claimed -50; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/controllers/session/SessionRendererPort.ts`, `src/controllers/session/SessionFactApplier.ts`, `src/controllers/progressView/backend/LitSessionRenderer.ts`, `src/controllers/progressView/backend/ProgressBackend.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/runProgressRenderer.ts`

**Evidence**

`SessionRendererPort.onStreamMetadataChanged` (SessionRendererPort.ts:56-62) takes `options?: { streamStates?: Map<StreamTabId, StreamPhaseState> }`. This is the last surviving member of the options bag whose sibling `activeStream` was deleted three weeks-of-commits ago as never-produced (#11393 / issue #11387, commit b2572a55a2, flagged in .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-25-cli-controller-seam-audit.md:191).

A->B->C->A round trip. Two of the three producers pass the literal expression `this.state.streamStatus.getAllStreamStates()` (SessionFactApplier.ts:678 and :700) — byte-identical to the fallback the consumer already computes when the option is absent: `streamStates ?? this.state.streamStatus.getAllStreamStates()` (LitSessionRenderer.ts:391). Five of the six `updateStreamMetadata` call sites in LitSessionRenderer (:143, :180, :207, :210, :214) already omit it and take that fallback; only :115, fed by the port option, passes it.

The third producer, `buildStreamStatesForRefresh` (SessionFactApplier.ts:915-935, called at :892), splices one entry into the same map on the documented premise that the transitioning stream's status 'hasn't been written to the machine yet when this is called during setStreamStatus'. That premise is false at HEAD. `StreamStatusMachine` writes before it publishes on BOTH publish paths: `transition` does `this.streams.set(...)` at StreamStatusService.ts:196-204 then `publishStatus` at :206; `tryAcquire` does `this.streams.set(...)` at :114-118 then `publishStatus` at :119. `getAllStreamStates()` (:311-317) projects `this.streams` through `effectiveState`, which for a reservation yields `{RUNNING, STARTING, runStartedAt}` — the same triple the fact carries. `publishStatus` (:328-354) is the only emitter of `type: 'status'` in production (grep over src+packages: the only non-test hit is StreamStatusService.ts:338), and `handleSessionFact` -> `setStreamStatus` (SessionFactApplier.ts:346-352) is the only production caller of `setStreamStatus`.

The one caller that could observe the splice is `ProgressBackend.applyStreamStatus` (ProgressBackend.ts:404-409), a pass-through whose only nine callers are all tests (ProgressBackendRetainedChildren.vitest.ts:203,285,410,456; ProgressBackendFactProjection.vitest.ts:876,923,959,1004,1053) — the same 'production surface that exists only to let a test restate a fact' class round 2 shipped a deletion for.

Consumers of the option: LitSessionRenderer.ts:115 (fallback exists) and HeadlessPort at runProgressRenderer.ts:405-414 (`options?.streamStates?.get(streamId)?.phase`). `TuiSessionRenderer.onStreamMetadataChanged` (sessionSignalsAdapter.ts:65) declares one parameter and ignores it entirely.

Dated checks run: `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search 'SessionRendererPort'` (11447 open, 11387 closed, 10669/10670/10672 closed); `git log` on SessionFactApplier/SessionRendererPort (5abad05f7f #11441, b2572a55a2 #11393, 1d17d1b29a #10875, 42098b9333 #9716 which introduced buildStreamStatesForRefresh); grep of docs/proposals for SessionRendererPort. No proposal or issue covers this member.

**Proposal**

Owner that absorbs the responsibility: `StreamStatusMachine`, which already is the single owner of phase/substate/runStartedAt and is already reachable from every renderer via `SessionState.streamStatus`.

Migration: (1) drop the `options` parameter from `SessionRendererPort.onStreamMetadataChanged`, from `SessionFactApplier.pushStreamMetadata` (:258-266), and from `LitSessionRenderer.onStreamMetadataChanged` (:108-116) and `updateStreamMetadata` (:376-394, keeping only the `getAllStreamStates()` read at :391). (2) Delete `buildStreamStatesForRefresh` (:915-935) and the `StreamPhaseState` imports it forces into SessionFactApplier and LitSessionRenderer. (3) In `HeadlessPort`, read the phase from `SessionState.streamStatus` via the renderer it already holds instead of the parameter. (4) Delete `ProgressBackend.applyStreamStatus` and re-point its nine test call sites at `session.status.transition(...)`, the real production path — without that step the splice's absence changes what those tests see, since they currently drive the applier without ever touching the machine.

Honest accounting: production about -59 (port -6, applier -37, Lit -10, headless ~0, backend -6); tests roughly neutral to +10 as the nine call sites gain a machine transition. Net about -50, two elements (`buildStreamStatesForRefresh`, `applyStreamStatus`) and one port parameter.

Behaviour note: for the two `getAllStreamStates()` sites the change is bit-identical. For the status site it is strictly fresher — `setStreamStatus` awaits `streamLogs.ensureLoaded` before building the map, so today's splice can force a stale phase if another transition lands during that await, while the direct read cannot.

**What we give up**

Nothing at runtime. What is given up is the ability for a caller to hand the renderer a phase the status machine does not hold — which today only tests do, and only by bypassing the machine. Restoring it would mean re-adding the parameter; the machine remains the authority either way.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. WRONG: "Delete ... the `StreamPhaseState` imports it forces into SessionFactApplier and LitSessionRenderer." LitSessionRenderer keeps that import — `metadataFor` (LitSessionRenderer.ts:435) still takes `streamStates: Map<StreamTabId, StreamPhaseState>` and `sendStreamMetadata` (:433) still feeds it `getAllStreamStates()`. Only SessionRendererPort.ts:1 and SessionFactApplier.ts:5 lose the import.

2. WRONG (and the load-bearing error): "without that step [deleting `ProgressBackend.applyStreamStatus` and re-pointing nine test call sites] the splice's absence changes what those tests see." It changes what the pushed message _carries_ (`streamState.status` becomes undefined for a machine-less stream), but no assertion covers it. I read all nine: ProgressBackendRetainedChildren.vitest.ts:203/285/410/456 assert `subagents[].status`, which comes from `recordChildPhase`/rosters, not from the spliced map; ProgressBackendFactProjection.vitest.ts:876 asserts only `streamInfo.name`/`agentCategory`, :923 asserts _absence_ of UPDATE_STREAM_STATUS, :959 asserts `requestEviction` not called, :1004 asserts eviction + logHead, :1053 asserts `conversationProgress`. Step (4) is therefore optional, not a prerequisite.

3. Step (4) is also partly infeasible as written. `applyStreamStatus(stream, RUNNING, RUNNING)` (FactProjection.vitest.ts:959) has no `session.status.transition(...)` equivalent: `StreamStatusMachine.transition` returns early _without publishing_ when `!fromReservation && from === to && previousState?.substate === options.substate` (StreamStatusService.ts:177-183), so a steady-RUNNING fact cannot be produced by the machine at all — that test would have to be deleted, not re-pointed. And :1004/:1053 run on `createPersistentRecordingBackend`, i.e. the one mode where `setStreamStatus` actually awaits `ensureLoaded`; the hub path is fire-and-forget (`handleSessionFact` discards the promise), so those tests would need a new settle helper. That is exactly the documented reason the method exists ("Awaitable status application for tests and hosts that cannot fire-and-forget", ProgressBackend.ts:403-404). Recommend shipping the option deletion alone and leaving `applyStreamStatus` to a separate item.

4. Element count: with step (4) dropped it is one element (`buildStreamStatesForRefresh`) plus one port parameter, not two elements.

5. Minor: the claimed per-file LoC is slightly off in both directions — port -6 (correct), applier ≈ -38 (claimed -37), Lit ≈ -7 (claimed -10), headless ≈ -5 net after the `HeadlessPort` gains a `SessionState` constructor arg (claimed ~0). No ratchet exposure: HeadlessPort would read `state.streamStatus.get(streamId)` through `@controllers/session/SessionState`, which `runProgressRenderer.ts:161` already imports, so no new `@agent/*` deep-import specifier is minted.

Everything else checks out. Verified independently: write-before-publish holds on both machine paths (`tryAcquire` StreamStatusService.ts:113-119; `transition` :195-206); `publishStatus` (:328-354) is the only production emitter of `type: 'status'`; `handleSessionFact` -> `setStreamStatus` (SessionFactApplier.ts:346) is the only production caller (`ProgressBackend.applyStreamStatus`'s nine callers are all tests); `SessionState.streamStatus = session.status` (SessionState.ts:160) is the same instance the publisher wrote, and every applier (ProgressBackend.setupEventListeners :883-891, runProgressRenderer :168, sessionSignalsAdapter :217) subscribes to that same in-process hub — no cross-process replay of status facts exists, so the "not yet written" premise in the docstring (SessionFactApplier.ts:915-921) is indeed false at HEAD. `effectiveState` (StreamStatusService.ts:56-64) yields `{RUNNING, STARTING, runStartedAt}` for a reservation, byte-matching the spliced triple. No wire shape changes (UPDATE_STREAM_METADATA is unchanged; the CLI NDJSON path forwards facts via `sessionProgressSubscription.ts:204`, never renderer options). No dated ruling covers this member: .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-25-cli-controller-seam-audit.md:166/181/191 rules only on the sibling `activeStream` option (shipped as #11393/b2572a55a2), the two survey docs never mention `streamStates`, and open issue #11447 is about `onMissingOutputsChanged`. Rounds-1/2 index line 137 ("includeActiveState knob") is a different method.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The mechanism holds under an independent re-walk. The option has exactly three producers; two pass the literal `getAllStreamStates()` the consumer already computes as its fallback (bit-identical), and the third splices one entry on a premise that is stale at HEAD — both machine publish paths write `this.streams` before emitting, the applier's machine is the same instance that published, and no status fact ever reaches an applier from another process. So the option round-trips a map the renderer reads from the same owner. The only observable difference is during `setStreamStatus`'s persistent-mode `ensureLoaded` await, where the direct read is strictly fresher than the fact-derived splice (the `transitionToTerminal` RUNNING->terminal ladder is a concrete case where today's splice can push a stale RUNNING after the terminal phase already went out). No ratchet, no wire, no persisted file, no settings key, no dated ruling is touched, and it is not a duplicate of #11393 (sibling `activeStream` option) or of the round-2 `includeActiveState` item. What does not survive is the accounting on step (4): the nine `applyStreamStatus` tests do not assert the spliced phase, so they neither block nor motivate the deletion, and one of them cannot be expressed through the machine at all. Ship the option deletion; leave `applyStreamStatus` alone.

</details>

#### Fold the last two payload-free port methods into `invalidate(streamId, slice)`, finishing the compliant stage of the 2026-08-15 port narrowing

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -18 LoC, -2 elements (proposer claimed -30; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/controllers/session/SessionRendererPort.ts`, `src/controllers/session/SessionFactApplier.ts`, `src/controllers/progressView/backend/LitSessionRenderer.ts`, `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/sessionSignalsAdapter.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/runProgressRenderer.ts`

**Evidence**

`SessionRendererPort` has two notification idioms: `invalidate(streamId, slice)` for six slices where 'the payload was never on the wire, only the field name was' (SessionRendererPort.ts:19-41, :50-54), and fourteen `on<Thing>Changed` methods. The port's own doc says slices that 'carry real delta semantics keep their own method' — but exactly two of those fourteen carry no payload at all and are therefore already `invalidate` arms wearing a different name:

- `onMissingOutputsChanged(streamId)` (SessionRendererPort.ts:90). All three impls re-read shared state: LitSessionRenderer.ts:217-227 reads `state.snapshots.getMissingOutputs`, wraps in `nonEmptyRounds`, `sendIfActive` — structurally identical to the `'files'` (:148-157) and `'compileFailures'` (:158-168) arms of `invalidate` twelve lines above it; sessionSignalsAdapter.ts:159-163 calls `markArtifactStreamHydrated`, the same body as its `'files'`/`'compileFailures'` arm at :83-93; runProgressRenderer.ts:442 is `{}`.
- `onBadgesChanged(streamId)` (SessionRendererPort.ts:86-88, whose own doc says 'hosts re-read SessionState.getStreamState(streamId).subagents'). LitSessionRenderer.ts:213-215 calls `updateStreamMetadata(streamId)`; sessionSignalsAdapter.ts:151-156 calls `invalidateChildStreams()`, byte-identical to its `'parentStreamId'`/`'queuedFollowUps'`/`'contextState'` arm at :94-101; runProgressRenderer.ts:438-440 calls `refreshFor(streamId, true)`.

Both slice names already exist as leaves in the canonical projection shape (`missing`, `subagents` in progressView/projectionShape.ts:30-34), so no new vocabulary is minted.

Dated evidence: .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-15-shared-contracts-and-retirement.md:612 tables the port as '22 x 2 impls -> 17 (payload-free ones collapse)' for the COMPLIANT plan, versus '~5' only under the hard-gated projection-zero option (:590-604, :930). Collapsing payload-free methods is therefore the ungated half. `onMissingOutputsChanged` became payload-free only when #11441 (5abad05f7f, 2026-08-26) deleted its `reset` option; issue #11447 (open, filed 2026-08-26 under umbrella #11445) already covers that one. `onBadgesChanged` is covered by nothing — `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search 'SessionRendererPort'` returns 11447, 11387, 10669, 10670, 10672 and none names it.

**Proposal**

Owner: the existing `invalidate(streamId, slice)` method and its `SessionRenderSlice` union. Extend the union with `'missing'` and `'subagents'`, delete both port methods, move each host's body into that host's `invalidate` switch (Lit: two more `sendIfActive` arms; TUI: `markArtifactStreamHydrated` / `invalidateChildStreams`, both already present as arm bodies; headless: drop the missing-outputs no-op, route badges to `refreshFor(streamId, true)`), and repoint the three applier call sites (SessionFactApplier.ts:199, :795, :811).

Ship it as one PR with #11447 rather than two, since both touch the same five files and the same switch statements. Honest accounting: port -10 (two decls plus docs, +2 union members), Lit about -6, TUI about -8, headless about -6, applier about 0 — net roughly -30, two elements. If #11447 lands alone, the residual for `onBadgesChanged` is about -18 and one element.

This executes an already-approved stage and does not touch the gated projection-zero narrowing, so it needs no supersession.

**What we give up**

The ability for these two facts to ever carry a payload again without reintroducing a method. Both lost their last payload deliberately (badges by #11109, missing-outputs by #11441), and the round-keyed data they describe lives in `StreamSnapshotStore`, which every host already reads synchronously — so a future payload would be a duplicate owner, not a restoration.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

C1 — SCOPE. Half the finding is open issue #11447. The novel, unfiled content is `onBadgesChanged` only. Do not file this as a new item covering both; either execute #11447 with badges added as an explicit scope expansion, or file the badges-only residual and cross-link. The finding's own residual number (-18) is closer to reality than its headline -30, and is itself still ~3x optimistic (see C4).

C2 — SLICE NAME COLLISION. #11447 specifies the slice literal `'missingOutputs'`; this finding specifies `'missing'`. Pick one before either lands. `'missing'` matches the canonical leaf name; `'missingOutputs'` matches the filed issue. (The finding's supporting cite is also mispathed: the leaves live in `src/shared/schemas/progressView/projectionShape.ts` — `subagents` at :31, `missing` at :33 — not `progressView/projectionShape.ts`, which reads as `src/controllers/progressView/`.)

C3 — HEADLESS ACCOUNTING IS BACKWARDS. The finding claims "headless about -6". `runProgressRenderer.ts:403` is `invalidate(_streamId, _slice): void {}` — a one-line empty no-op. Routing badges through it means growing that no-op into a body (`if (slice === 'subagents') this.renderer.refreshFor(streamId, true);`), i.e. +2 lines against -6 deleted. Honest headless net is about -4, not -6, and it is the one host where the collapse makes a method _appear_ rather than disappear.

C4 — TEST MIGRATION IS UNPRICED, AND ONE SITE IS NOT MECHANICAL. Three spies in `src/test-kernel/progressView/ProgressBackendStreamLifecycle.vitest.ts` target `onBadgesChanged` (:264, :381, :1305). Two are assertion swaps (`toHaveBeenLastCalledWith(parent)` → `toHaveBeenCalledWith(parent, 'subagents')`; note `LastCalledWith` becomes fragile once the spy catches every slice, so it must change form, not just arguments). The third (:1304-1309) is a `mockImplementationOnce` that captures the roster and then _throws_ to simulate a renderer failure during roster scrub — once folded, `mockImplementationOnce` would fire on whichever `invalidate` call happens to come first, regardless of slice, so it must become a slice-filtered `mockImplementation` with its own once-guard. Budget +5..+8 test lines and one careful rewrite; the finding budgets zero.

C5 — DOC AMENDMENTS THE FINDING OMITS. (a) The `SessionRenderSlice` doc block (SessionRendererPort.ts:18-27) enumerates every slice and its owning field; adding two members means editing that prose, not just the union. (b) `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/childExecutions.ts:47` names `onBadgesChanged` in a comment listing the tombstone triggers and must be updated in the same PR.

C6 — THE "ALREADY-APPROVED STAGE" FRAMING IS ONE METHOD TOO GENEROUS. The compliant row in `2026-08-15-shared-contracts-and-retirement.md:930` tables "22 × 2 impls → 17 (payload-free ones collapse)", and the landing note in `2026-08-15-single-substrate-hosts-as-renderers.md:404-405` records #10932 arriving at 18. HEAD is 18 methods. So #11447 alone consumes the compliant plan's remaining budget (18 → 17); folding badges takes it to 16, which is arithmetic the compliant table never wrote down — it predates badges losing its payload in #11109 four days later. This does not gate the work (the _idiom_ "payload-free → invalidate" is the ungated one, and §4b's gated projection-zero is about methods that still carry payloads, plus two named supersessions that have not been granted), but the PR body should say "the payload-free set grew after the table was written" rather than "finishing the tabled stage". Related: `§7.2` line 815 already schedules `SessionRendererPort.ts` 113 → ~35 under the gated projection-zero row, so this is small rework on code with a dated deletion plan — acceptable at this size, worth one sentence of acknowledgement.

C7 — MINOR. Lit's `onBadgesChanged` calls `updateStreamMetadata` with no availability check, while the existing `'parentStreamId'` arm guards `if (!this.isAvailable()) return;` first. Move the body verbatim into its own arm; do not merge it into the `'parentStreamId'` arm, or you silently add a guard the badges path never had.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

I read the port, all three implementations, both applier call sites and the test file at HEAD rather than trusting the evidence block. The core structural claim is true and checks out line for line: two methods on `SessionRendererPort` take a `streamId` and nothing else, all three hosts respond by re-reading shared state, and in the TUI both bodies are already byte-identical to arms that exist inside `invalidate` twelve to seventy lines away. That is the definition the port's own doc gives for `invalidate`, and the 2026-08-19 narrowing commit applied exactly that rule to five siblings. Badges escaped only because it still had a payload that morning and lost it that evening in a different PR.

Why it is not killed on the usual round-3 grounds: no ruling defends either method (I read the narrowing commit message, both 2026-08-15 proposals, the define-out-of-existence doc, and the round-2 survey); the surface is internal in-process TypeScript with three implementors and no wire, persistence, settings or texra-action exposure; and no ratchet names it. The one real hit is that the missing-outputs half is already issue #11447, filed yesterday off the same reviewer observation — so the finding's novelty is the badges half, and it should ship as a scope expansion of that issue rather than as a fresh item.

Honest cost, counted by hand rather than estimated. Combined PR (badges + #11447): port about -4 (four declaration/doc lines out, two union members and a doc-prose line in); Lit about -4 (two methods out, two arms in, the missing-outputs arm carries its full ten-line `sendIfActive` body over); TUI about -12, the only place with a real win, because both methods carry four-to-five-line provenance comments that collapse onto existing case labels; headless about -4 after the no-op grows a body; applier about -1. That is roughly -25 production. Tests add +5..+8 in one file, including the non-mechanical throwing-spy rewrite. Net about -18, versus the claimed -30. Badges alone is about -11 production and +5 test, i.e. net about -6 and one method — thin enough that shipping it alone is not worth a PR, which is the finding's own conclusion and the reason its "ship it with #11447" instruction is the right call.

Elements: two interface methods removed, two string-literal union members added. I score that -2 (negative meaning removed) on the methods, with the caveat that the slice vocabulary itself grows by two — the concept count drops, the vocabulary does not. Risk is low: three implementors, one test file, exhaustive `assertNever` switches in two of the three hosts mean the compiler finds any missed arm, and `npm run typecheck` plus that single vitest file is the whole verification surface.

</details>

### settings-pipeline — The settings pipeline, end to end

#### Delete the nested Core config tree: storage is flat, so the tree exists only to be re-flattened by CORE_SETTING_PATHS + coreLeafSchema

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -220 LoC, -13 elements (proposer claimed -280; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/shared/schemas/coreSettings.ts`, `src/shared/schemas/stateSettings.ts`, `src/platform/defaults/jsonConfigProvider.ts`, `packages/extension/src/settingsView/frontend/tabs/LaTeXTab.ts`, `src/test-kernel/shared/stateSettings.vitest.ts`

**Evidence**

End-to-end trace of one setting, `texra.model.retry.maxAttempts`:

1. Value declared in `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS` (src/shared/schemas/coreSettings.ts:95-176, 82 lines) as a nested object literal.
2. Re-declared as a nested Zod leaf in `CoreSettingsShape` (coreSettings.ts:230-438, 209 lines) whose every leaf reads the value back out of #1 (49 `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS.*` references inside the shape; 21 group-level `.prefault(DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS.<group>)`).
3. Re-declared as a dotted string in `CORE_SETTING_PATHS` (coreSettings.ts:486-525, 36 entries), kept in sync with #2 by `LeafPaths`/`IsRecord`/`AssertNever`/`_AssertCorePathsExhaustive` (coreSettings.ts:446-484, 545) — ~60 lines of type machinery whose only job is to police #2 against #3.
4. Re-declared a fourth time as a key of `CORE_SETTING_ROWS` (stateSettings.ts:387-691), typed `Record<CoreSettingPath, CoreRowSpec>` so #3 polices #4.
5. `coreLeafSchema(path)` (stateSettings.ts:337-355) walks the nested tree back down a dotted path to recover the flat leaf schema; `schemaDescription` (356-365) digs the `.describe()` back out; `coreSettingRow` (692-702) assembles the flat row.

The tree models a storage shape that does not exist. `JsonStore` is `Record<string, unknown>` keyed by the full dotted string (src/platform/defaults/jsonStore.ts, `type JsonRecord = Record<string, unknown>`), and `canonicalConfigKey` (src/shared/config/configKeys.ts) produces `texra.model.retry.maxAttempts` as one flat key. Verified against real files on this machine: `.texra/config.json` is `{"texra.git.markCommits": true, "texra.skills.enabled": false}` and `~/.texra/global-storage/config.json` is `{"texra.chatgptCodex.preferSubscription": true, ...}` — flat, dotted, never nested. So the data path is A(flat on disk) -> B(nested declaration) -> A(flat rows), and nothing ever validates the nested object: `CoreSettingsSchema` (coreSettings.ts:440-442) is module-private and used only for `type CoreSettings` at 444; `grep -rn CoreSettingsSchema src packages` returns three hits, all inside coreSettings.ts itself.

The documented reason for the nesting is stale. coreSettings.ts:225-227 says the shape is "Exported as a plain object so host-specific schemas can compose with it via `z.strictObject({ ...CoreSettingsShape, ...HostExtensionShape })`" — that plan comes from .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-06-26-config-catalog-unification.md (PR1, which names `packages/extension/src/schemas/{texraSettings.ts,vscodeSettings.ts}`). Those files no longer exist (`ls packages/extension/src/schemas/` is empty; `scripts/sync-settings-configuration.mjs` is gone, only `sync-package-contributes.mjs` remains, and CLAUDE.md records that package.json must not contribute `configuration`). Nobody spreads `CoreSettingsShape` anywhere: the only two out-of-file consumers are `coreLeafSchema` (which un-nests it) and LaTeXTab.ts:735 (`CoreSettingsShape.latex.unwrap().shape[field]` — one leaf lookup that `settingSchemaWithoutPrefault(settingByKey(key))` already expresses).

The state-backed tier already proves the flat form works: every one of the ~60 `STATE_SETTINGS` rows carries `schema: z.boolean().prefault(DEFAULT_GIT_MARK_COMMITS)` inline (stateSettings.ts:989-1042). A Core row would carry `schema: boolField(true, '<description>')` in exactly the same slot.

Dated checks run: `git log --oneline -20 src/shared/schemas/coreSettings.ts` (last structural change ed7a23293d "collapse six setting catalogs onto one row shape", then d061941949, b296264368 — the row shape was unified, the tree was not touched); `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "settings catalog"` (15 results; #6605 "Unify settings access layer across both catalogs" CLOSED, #10944, #10980, #11211 all about surfacing, none about the tier split); `grep` of docs/proposals for CoreSettings — only 2026-06-26-config-catalog-unification.md, whose "two corrections" section justifies keeping state keys _out_ of `CoreSettingsShape` but never justifies keeping the config keys _in_ a tree. Neither round-1 nor round-2 (checked all 151 titles in rounds12-index.md and grepped both survey docs) touches coreSettings.ts's shape.

Not a wire format, not a persisted file format (keys and defaults are unchanged), not a settings key (every `texra.*` key keeps its exact name and default), not the texra-action contract.

**Proposal**

Owner that absorbs it: `CORE_SETTING_ROWS` in src/shared/schemas/stateSettings.ts — the row already owns slots, honoredBy, surfaces, title, category, and onWrite; it takes over schema+default+description too, which is exactly what every `STATE_SETTINGS` row already does.

Migration:

1. Move each of the 36 leaf declarations out of `CoreSettingsShape` and onto its row as `schema: boolField(<literal>, '<description text>')`. The literal comes from `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS`; the description text is the existing `.describe()` string. Both texts move verbatim — no retyping of values, so no drift window.
2. Delete `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS`, `CoreSettingsShape`, `CoreSettingsSchema`, `type CoreSettings`, `IsRecord`, `LeafPaths`, `AssertNever`, `_AssertCorePathsExhaustive`, `CORE_SETTING_PATHS`, `CoreSettingPath`, `CORE_SETTING_PATH_SET`, `getCoreSettingDefault` from coreSettings.ts; keep the `*_SETTING` frozen constants, the three exported leaf schemas, and the `boolField`/`stringField`/`numberField`/`stringRecord` builders (move the builders to stateSettings.ts or a small shared module).
3. Delete `coreLeafSchema`, `schemaDescription`, `CoreRowSpec`, `coreSettingRow` from stateSettings.ts; `CORE_SETTINGS` becomes `Object.entries(CORE_SETTING_ROWS).map(([path, row]) => ({ ...row, key: `texra.${path}`, slots: sameSlot('config') }))` plus the existing hand-written approvalPolicy row.
4. `JsonConfigProvider.get` (src/platform/defaults/jsonConfigProvider.ts:44-46) replaces `getCoreSettingDefault(storedKey)` with a catalog lookup guarded on slot: `const entry = settingByKey(storedKey); const d = entry && Object.values(entry.slots).includes('config') ? entry.schema.parse(undefined) : undefined;`. The slot guard preserves today's behavior of not handing config defaults to state-store-backed keys.
5. LaTeXTab.ts:735 uses `settingSchemaWithoutPrefault(settingByKey('texra.latex.' + field))` instead of reaching into the shape.
6. Delete the now-vacuous guardrail `it('shares no keys with the config-tree catalog')` (src/test-kernel/shared/stateSettings.vitest.ts:481-492) — there is one catalog, so there is nothing to be disjoint from.

Verification: before landing, add a throwaway assertion that for all 36 paths `settingByKey('texra.'+path).schema.parse(undefined)` deep-equals the pre-change `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS` value; run it on the old tree and the new rows and diff. Delete it before merge (AGENTS.md testing budget).

LoC accounting, honest and including what the migration adds: coreSettings.ts loses 394 lines (82+209+5+39+40+19) of which ~125 are leaf declarations that MOVE into rows rather than disappear -> -269. stateSettings.ts loses coreLeafSchema/schemaDescription/CoreRowSpec (47) and coreSettingRow (11) -> -58, and gains ~10 for the row-to-entry mapping. Tests lose ~12. Net ~-330 gross, and I am budgeting -280 to leave room for prettier rewrapping the moved builder calls and for a few rows needing an explicit `description` they previously inherited.

**What we give up**

Three things stop being possible. (a) Validating a whole nested config object in one `parse` — never done today and impossible against flat storage anyway. (b) The compile-time chain schema-tree -> path-array -> row-record that makes "add a leaf without filing its honoring hosts" a type error; after the collapse there is no separate tree to add a leaf to, so the failure mode it guards against cannot occur, but a reviewer loses a mechanical reminder. (c) The documented `z.strictObject({ ...CoreSettingsShape, ...HostExtensionShape })` extension point for a future per-host config schema — the host schema package it was written for (packages/extension/src/schemas/) was already deleted, and a future host extension would add rows to the catalog instead. Restoring any of these means re-deriving a tree from the flat rows, which is mechanical but a day of work.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. THE BIGGEST OMISSION: `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS` has SEVEN production consumers outside coreSettings.ts that the finding never mentions, and step 2 deletes it with no replacement plan. They are: src/agent/core/flows/ModelInvocationNode.ts:42,79 (`model.retry.maxAttempts`), src/agent/runtime/ModelFactory.ts:43,203 (`model.useOpenAIResponsesAPI`), src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:39,333 and modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts:56,1679 (`model.openaiParallelToolCalls`), src/replacement/engine.ts:8,183,215 (`latex.enabledReplacements`, `latex.enabledReplacementsRegex`), src/telemetry/UsageLogService.ts:11,130 (`telemetry.enabled`), plus src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/RetryState.vitest.ts:47,422,432. All are typed inline `getConfig<T>(key, DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS.<path>)` defaults. `settingByKey(k).schema.parse(undefined)` is not a drop-in: `settingByKey` returns `StateSettingEntry | undefined` and `schema` is `z.ZodType`, so the result is `unknown` and every site needs a cast. The workable plan is: retry sites -> the already-kept `MODEL_RETRY_MAX_ATTEMPTS_SETTING.defaultValue`; engine.ts -> import `DEFAULT_ENABLED_REPLACEMENTS` / `DEFAULT_ENABLED_REGEX_REPLACEMENTS` from @shared/constants/replacementCategories directly (coreSettings already imports them from there, so this is a shortening); and three NEW frozen constants for `useOpenAIResponsesAPI`, `openaiParallelToolCalls`, `telemetry.enabled`. That is what keeps FB-3's "or a shared constant" branch satisfied.

2. THE PROPOSED SLOT GUARD IS WRONG AND SILENTLY CHANGES BEHAVIOR. Step 4's `Object.values(entry.slots).includes('config')` does NOT "preserve today's behavior of not handing config defaults to state-store-backed keys." `GIT_AUTHOR_SLOTS` (stateSettings.ts:852-856) is `{ vscode: 'workspaceState', desktop: 'workspaceState', cli: 'config' }`, and four rows use it — `texra.git.markCommits`, `WorkspaceStateKey.GIT_AUTHOR_NAME` (:1000), `GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL` (:1014), `GIT_WORKTREE_SUPPORT` (:1024). All four would newly match the guard, so `JsonConfigProvider.get` would start returning their schema defaults (`DEFAULT_GIT_AUTHOR_NAME`, `DEFAULT_GIT_WORKTREE_SUPPORT`, …) where it returns the caller's `defaultValue` today. `texra.approvalPolicy` (the hand-written `sameSlot('config')` row appended to `CORE_SETTINGS`) is likewise absent from `CORE_SETTING_PATHS` today and would newly resolve to `TEXRA_APPROVAL_POLICY_DEFAULT`. The guard must be "all three host slots are 'config'" and must still exclude approvalPolicy, or the core rows need an explicit marker field. This is a settings-defaults regression class, so it belongs in the plan, not in review.

3. STEPS 1 AND 3 CONTRADICT EACH OTHER AND WOULD DROP ALL 36 DESCRIPTIONS. `StateSettingEntry.description` is optional (stateSettings.ts:228) and is filled for core rows today by `description: spec.description ?? schemaDescription(schema)` (:699). If `schemaDescription` is deleted while leaves keep `boolField(<literal>, '<text>')` (which only calls `.describe()`), every core row's `description` becomes `undefined` and the copy disappears from the settings view (`entry.description` at :1532) and the CLI. Each row must carry an explicit `description:` — at which point `boolField`'s description argument is redundant and the core leaves should be plain `z.boolean().prefault(x)` like the STATE_SETTINGS rows the finding cites as precedent. Same net LoC, but the plan as written is broken.

4. STEP 5 DOES NOT TYPECHECK AS WRITTEN. `settingSchemaWithoutPrefault` returns `unknown` (stateSettings.ts:1617-1624), and `settingByKey` returns `StateSettingEntry | undefined`. LaTeXTab.ts:735 needs an undefined guard plus `as z.ZodType` before `.safeParse`. It is still a fine replacement for `CoreSettingsShape.latex.unwrap().shape[field]` — the two `field` values are `customReplacements` and `customReplacementsRegex`, both real `texra.latex.*` rows — but it is two lines, not one.

5. TEST COST IS UNDER-BUDGETED. Beyond the disjointness test at stateSettings.vitest.ts:481-492, `it('recognizes exactly the Core paths a CLI reader honors')` at :634-643 iterates `CORE_SETTING_PATHS` twice and must be rewritten against the row record (it is a genuinely load-bearing test — it pins the whole Core half of `KNOWN_TEXRA_KEYS`, so it must survive, not be deleted). RetryState.vitest.ts also imports `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS` at :47,422,432. Net test delta is closer to -6 than -12.

6.
