ITERATION ORDER IS AN UNDECLARED TRANSFER. stateSettings.ts:384 says "`CORE_SETTING_PATHS` owns iteration order, including the Models tab's control order." `Object.entries(CORE_SETTING_ROWS)` preserves the record's own literal order (every key is a non-integer string, so no reordering), and it currently matches the path array — but the plan should say so, because a future contributor reordering the record now silently reorders the Models tab.

7. PERFORMANCE REGRESSION IN A HOT PATH, UNMENTIONED. `getCoreSettingDefault` is a `Set.has` plus a short object walk plus a `structuredClone` for object values. `entry.schema.parse(undefined)` runs full Zod validation on every default fallback — including `z.array(z.enum(...))` over the ~20 replacement categories on every `getAllReplacements()` call. Memoize the parsed default per row.

8. ELEMENT COUNT. 16 symbols deleted, ~3 new default constants added -> net -13, not -16.

9. RATCHET NOTE THE FINDING SHOULD STATE EXPLICITLY. `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` needs regeneration for the removed exports (`DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS`, `CORE_SETTING_PATHS`, `CoreSettingPath`, `CoreSettingsShape`, `getCoreSettingDefault`). `shared-schemas-deep-import-baseline.json` does NOT need it — I checked, its `surface` block pins only `@shared/schemas/log`, and the older doc text about `CLI_CORE_SETTING_PATHS` sitting at lines 120/124 describes a superseded version of that file.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The load-bearing claim is verified end to end and is not defended by any live rationale. Storage is flat (`JsonStore = Record<string, unknown>`, `canonicalConfigKey` produces the dotted key, the two real config.json files on disk are flat). The nested object is never parsed: `CoreSettingsSchema` is module-private with three in-file references and exists only to derive `type CoreSettings`, which in turn exists only to feed `LeafPaths<CoreSettings>` — a ~60-line type machine whose sole job is to police one hand-written array against a schema tree that nothing validates. The only two out-of-file consumers of `CoreSettingsShape` are `coreLeafSchema` (which un-nests it) and one LaTeXTab leaf lookup. The documented reason for the shape ("so host-specific schemas can compose with it") points at a deleted package (packages/extension/src/schemas) and a deleted script (sync-settings-configuration.mjs), and CLAUDE.md now forbids the package.json `configuration` contribution that whole chain existed to generate. The row catalog already owns key derivation for the CLI whitelist and already carries `schema` + `prefault` inline for ~60 state rows, so the target shape is proven in-repo.

What the finding got wrong is the cost side, in two ways that matter. It never noticed that `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS` is a live typed-literal namespace for seven production files, and it wrote a `JsonConfigProvider` slot guard that would newly hand schema defaults to five rows that do not get them today (the four GIT_AUTHOR_SLOTS rows have `cli: 'config'`, and approvalPolicy is a `sameSlot('config')` row outside `CORE_SETTING_PATHS`). Both are fixable inside the same PR, neither is a reason the collapse is wrong, but both are exactly the kind of silent settings-defaults drift this repo has been bitten by, so I am raising the risk rather than the verdict.

My honest numbers: net about -220 LoC and -13 elements after paying for the three new default constants, the 36 explicit row descriptions that `schemaDescription` used to supply for free, the corrected guard, the eight call-site edits, and the rewritten (not deleted) CLI-whitelist test. Still a real deletion, and the concept count drops hard — two catalogs and a compile-time path-policing chain become one row record. Risk is medium: mechanical work, but in the settings-defaults pipeline where a wrong default is silent, so the pre-landing "all 36 defaults deep-equal the old tree" assertion the finding proposes is mandatory, and it should be extended to cover the five rows in correction #2 that must NOT start returning a default.

</details>

#### Two hand-rolled readers re-implement `readSetting` for rows the catalog already owns: `readPersistedTexraApprovalPolicy` and `SettingsProfileController.readToggle`

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -70 LoC, -3 elements (proposer claimed -55; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/shared/approvalPolicy.ts`, `src/shared/settingsView/handlers/stateSettingWrite.ts`, `src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsProfileController.ts`, `packages/extension/src/extension.ts`, `packages/extension/src/frontend/vscode/texraConfig.ts`, `packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts`, `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopCredentialSettingsController.ts`

**Evidence**

`readSetting(entry, stores, host)` (src/shared/config/settingsAccess.ts:93-118) is the declared single owner of "read a catalog row": resolve slot, apply `normalizePersisted`, `safeParse`, warn once on an invalid persisted value, fall back to `settingDefault(entry)`. Two other readers do the same job by hand.

(a) `readPersistedTexraApprovalPolicy` (src/shared/approvalPolicy.ts:77-99, 23 lines) reads `texra.approvalPolicy` through a caller-supplied `get`, lowercases/trims via `parseTexraApprovalPolicy`, warns on invalid, falls back to `TEXRA_APPROVAL_POLICY_DEFAULT`. The catalog row for that exact key (stateSettings.ts:711-740) already declares `normalizePersisted: (raw) => raw.trim().toLowerCase()` with the comment "the row owns that normalization so every reader through `readSetting` accepts what `parseTexraApprovalPolicy` has always accepted", `schema: TexraApprovalPolicySchema.prefault(TEXRA_APPROVAL_POLICY_DEFAULT)`, and `slots: sameSlot('config')`. Same normalization, same schema, same default, same warn-then-default policy — written twice. Four call sites pass a `(key, fallback) => ...get(key, fallback)` lambda: packages/extension/src/extension.ts:352, packages/extension/src/frontend/vscode/texraConfig.ts:88, packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts:1289, and src/shared/settingsView/handlers/stateSettingWrite.ts:137-143. That last one is the clearest: it sits inside the shared write path, which already holds `ports.stores` and `write.entry`, and is only reached after a _reset_ — so the value it computes is by definition `settingDefault(write.entry)`.

(b) `SettingsProfileController.readToggle` (src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsProfileController.ts:145-160) re-implements `settingSlot` dispatch with its own `switch` over `SettingStore`, its own `settingDefault(entry) === true` fallback, and a `throw` for the `workspaceState` arm. That switch is `readSetting`'s body, minus the schema validation and minus `normalizePersisted`. It exists only because the controller takes `getConfig` and `globalState` as separate deps (SettingsProfileControllerDeps, lines 34-42) instead of a `SettingsStores`; both hosts already have all three stores at the construction site (packages/extension/src/settingsView/SettingsViewMessageHandler.ts:144, packages/desktop/src/main/desktopCredentialSettingsController.ts:143-148).

Dated ruling that points this way: issue #6605 "Unify settings access layer across both catalogs" (CLOSED, 2026-06-28) — "Reroute the extension and desktop host getters to read/write settings through the new shared `settingsAccess` helpers instead of calling `platform().<store>.get(...)` directly per domain." The state-store half of that was executed; the config half (`getConfig`/`readToggle`/`readPersistedTexraApprovalPolicy`) never was. `git log --oneline -25 src/shared/schemas/stateSettings.ts` shows the write path being unified repeatedly (b741de9876, b296264368, 3538f283e5) with no matching read-path pass.

One honest complication I verified rather than assumed: `readSetting` reads _only_ the global scope for a `configTarget: 'global'` row (settingsAccess.ts:99-104), while `readToggle` reads the merged value through `getConfig`. The seven Models-tab provider rows are `configTarget: 'global'` (stateSettings.ts:409, 433, 450, 469, 488, 507) and the catalog comment at 400-407 says explicitly "Models-tab and runtime reads both keep merged-config semantics ... A workspace override therefore remains visible and honored", with #11173 tracking values stranded by an earlier regression. So a naive swap would change Models-tab behavior. `texra.telemetry.enabled` (stateSettings.ts:654) is the one `configTarget: 'global'` row that is also in a derived snapshot, and its runtime reader deliberately reads both scopes (src/telemetry/UsageLogService.ts:120-131, "Either scope may opt out").

Not a wire format, not a persisted file, no key added or removed.

**Proposal**

Owner: `readSetting` / `readPlatformSetting` (src/shared/config/settingsAccess.ts, src/utils/config/platformSettings.ts).

Step 1 (low risk, do first): delete `readPersistedTexraApprovalPolicy`. At stateSettingWrite.ts:137-143 replace the lambda with `readSetting(write.entry, ports.stores, ports.host) as TexraApprovalPolicy`. At extension.ts:352 and desktop/main/index.ts:1289 replace with `readPlatformSetting<TexraApprovalPolicy>(TEXRA_APPROVAL_POLICY_CONFIG_KEY)`. texraConfig.ts:88 runs inside a workspace transition on the config object itself and cannot go through `platform()`; call `readSetting(settingByKey(TEXRA_APPROVAL_POLICY_CONFIG_KEY)!, { config: this, workspaceState, globalState }, 'vscode')` or keep one small local read there and say why in a comment. `parseTexraApprovalPolicy` stays — the CLI flag parser (cliContext.ts:390) and `/approval` (approvalCommand.ts:30) parse user _input_, which is a different job from reading persisted state.

Step 2 (medium risk, separable PR): give `readSetting` an explicit scope answer for `configTarget: 'global'` rows instead of two mechanisms disagreeing. Either (i) make it merged and let the settings view show what the runtime honors — which is what the catalog comment at stateSettings.ts:400-407 already claims — with telemetry keeping its own two-scope reader, or (ii) keep global-only and change the Models-tab comment. Whichever is chosen, then replace `SettingsProfileControllerDeps.{globalState,getConfig}` with a single `stores: SettingsStores` and delete `readToggle` in favor of `readSetting(entry, this.deps.stores, this.deps.host) === true`. Do not do step 2 without resolving #11173's scope question first — the two readers currently paper over it.

LoC: step 1 removes 23 lines plus four lambda call sites (~~-30); step 2 removes the 17-line switch and two dep fields plus their wiring in two hosts (~~-25), and adds nothing. Net ~-55.

**What we give up**

`readPersistedTexraApprovalPolicy`'s host-neutral `(key, fallback) => T` signature — usable by a caller that has a bare config getter and no `SettingsStores`. Exactly one caller is in that position (texraConfig.ts, mid-transition), so the generality serves one site. Step 2 also gives up the ability for the Models tab to read a different scope than the settings-view snapshot, which is currently an undocumented divergence rather than a feature.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. WRONG, and contradicted by a dated ruling the finding did not find. "it is only reached after a _reset_ — so the value it computes is by definition `settingDefault(write.entry)`." False. `resetSetting` (settingsAccess.ts:186-193) delegates to `writeSlot(entry, undefined, …)` with `target ?? entry.configTarget ?? 'workspace'`, so a reset clears only the _workspace_ layer; a surviving global `texra.approvalPolicy` is exactly what the merged read returns. That is the whole point of CLOSED issue #9749, which required this site to stop seeding `TEXRA_APPROVAL_POLICY_DEFAULT` precisely because "if the global layer has \"never\", the session runs \"ask\" while the settings UI shows \"never\" — a silent policy mismatch." The proposed replacement (`readSetting(write.entry, ports.stores, ports.host)`) is still correct because it preserves the merged read — but the stated rationale would, if a reviewer believed it, license replacing the call with `settingDefault(...)` and re-open #9749.

2. WRONG, in the finding's favor. "texraConfig.ts:88 runs inside a workspace transition on the config object itself and cannot go through `platform()`." `platform().config` **is** this object: `createExtensionTexraConfig` (extension.ts:271) produces the `ExtensionTexraConfig` instance that `initPlatform(createNodePlatform({ config, … }))` installs at :279-282. And the argument is never evaluated before `initPlatform` runs, because `tryDefaultSession()?.setApprovalPolicy(arg)` short-circuits argument evaluation when the session is absent — which is the documented activation case at texraConfig.ts:82-85. So `readPlatformSetting<TexraApprovalPolicy>(TEXRA_APPROVAL_POLICY_CONFIG_KEY)` works at all four sites uniformly; the hand-built `{ config: this, workspaceState, globalState }` and the "keep one small local read with a comment" escape hatch are both unnecessary, and with them goes the entire "what we give up" paragraph about the host-neutral `(key, fallback) => T` signature serving one site. Zero sites need it.

3. WRONG. "Step 2 also gives up the ability for the Models tab to read a different scope than the settings-view snapshot, which is currently an undocumented divergence rather than a feature." There is no live divergence, and it is documented that there isn't. `readSetting` has exactly two non-`readPlatformSetting` callers repo-wide: `settingsSnapshot.ts:40` and `packages/cli/src/commands/config.ts:65`. The snapshot builder iterates `settingsViewSnapshotEntries(snapshot)` for a `DerivedSettingsSnapshot`, and `SETTINGS_SNAPSHOT_COMMANDS` (settingsViewMessages.ts:194-202) deliberately omits `profile` and `models` with the comment "A snapshot absent from this map is one whose payload is not a plain list of catalog rows (`profile`, `models`); those arms still declare their own shape below or in their own module." So `readSetting` never reads the seven Models-tab provider rows; `readToggle` is their sole reader, and the runtime reader is also merged (`modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts:1739`, `getConfig<boolean>('texra.model.gpt5ReasoningSummary', false)`). The catalog comment at stateSettings.ts:400-407 is therefore currently TRUE, not aspirational. Step 2 would _create_ the divergence it claims to remove.

4. INCOMPLETE ruling search on step 2. `readSetting`'s global-only branch is not an unexamined default: `git log -S` pins it to d061941949 ("refactor(settings): derive outbound settings snapshots from the catalog"), which landed it with the explicit rationale at settingsAccess.ts:99-101 — "`writeSetting` targets `entry.configTarget`, so a global-target row read through the merged `get()` could report a workspace value the settings view can never write." Step 2 option (i) reverses that ruling and reintroduces the bug it fixed for every `configTarget: 'global'` row in a derived snapshot (`telemetry.enabled`, stateSettings.ts:649-657, is one). Step 2's gate must be "beat d061941949", not only "resolve #11173."

5. UNDERCOUNTED. Deleting `readPersistedTexraApprovalPolicy` also deletes `src/test-kernel/shared/ApprovalPolicy.vitest.ts:61-88` — two `it` blocks (~28 lines) plus three now-unused imports — since they test only this function's warn-and-fallback contract, which `readSetting` already covers (stateSettings.vitest.ts:811 asserts the `settingsAccess` warn channel). Step 1 is ~-60 LoC, not the claimed ~-30.

6. MISSED FOLLOW-ON EDIT. The catalog row declares `honoredBy: { vscode: { reader: 'src/shared/approvalPolicy.ts' }, desktop: { reader: 'src/shared/approvalPolicy.ts' } }` (stateSettings.ts:725-727). After step 1 that file no longer reads the key. `stateSettings.vitest.ts:164-186` only asserts the reader path _exists_, so nothing fails — a silent documentation lie. Re-point both to `src/utils/config/platformSettings.ts` in the same PR.

7. WRONG CITE (conclusion still holds). "both hosts already have all three stores at the construction site (… desktopCredentialSettingsController.ts:143-148)" — those lines are the `SettingsProfileController` construction and show only `globalState` and `config`. The actual proof is `DesktopCredentialSettingsControllerOptions extends SettingsStatePorts` (:42) with `readonly config: ConfigProvider` (:43), and `SettingsStatePorts` (src/shared/settingsView/types.ts:14-17) supplying `workspaceState` and `globalState`. Extension side is fine as cited.

8. UNSTATED BEHAVIOR CHANGE. The warn moves from `warn('approval-policy', 'Ignoring invalid texra.approvalPolicy "auto"; using "ask".')` to `log.warn('Ignoring invalid persisted value for setting "texra.approvalPolicy": …')` on the `settingsAccess` channel. This still satisfies #9750, but #9750 is a closed follow-up with a named §15 rationale, so the PR body must say so explicitly or a reviewer will read the deletion as regressing it.

9. UNDER-ARGUED. `readToggle`'s `throw` on the `workspaceState` arm carries a 6-line comment (SettingsProfileController.ts:139-144) defending it as deliberate loudness. Handing the controller real `SettingsStores` does not violate that intent, it dissolves it (`readSetting` then reads the correct store instead of falling through to the config tree). Say that, or the comment reads as a defense the proposal ignored.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The core claim is verified end to end. The approvalPolicy catalog row's `normalizePersisted` comment (stateSettings.ts:714-716) is a smoking gun: it was written to make `readSetting` accept exactly what `parseTexraApprovalPolicy` accepts, i.e. the row was _prepared_ for this migration and the migration never happened. All four lambda sites are merged-config reads and the row has no `configTarget`, so `readSetting` takes the identical merged path — step 1 is behavior-preserving apart from the warn text. No ratchet, no wire, no settings key, no SDK export is touched. #6605 is real, closed, and its config half genuinely unexecuted. Step 1 alone is a clean, low-risk win at roughly -60 LoC once its dead tests are counted.

Step 2 is weaker than presented but not fatal, because the finding gates it. Its central factual support — "two readers currently paper over" the scope question — is wrong: `readSetting` never reads the Models-tab rows, so the divergence is prospective, not existing, and step 2's option (i) would reverse a deliberate 2026-08 ruling (d061941949) rather than merely settle an open question. The dep-shape half of step 2 (`{globalState, getConfig}` → `stores: SettingsStores`, delete `readToggle`) is independently sound and could ship _without_ touching `readSetting`'s scope rule at all, if the entry passed is one whose slot is `config` and `configTarget` is undefined — but that is not true of the seven provider rows, which are `configTarget: 'global'`, so it genuinely cannot be separated. Step 2 therefore stays blocked on the scope decision, exactly as the finding says, just for a bigger reason than it gives.

Honest accounting including migration: step 1 = -24 (function + doc) -8 (four call sites) -28 (two test blocks) -2 (unused test imports) +2 (import churn across three host files) ≈ -60. Step 2 = -29 (`readToggle` + its doc) -1 (deps fields) +3 (two host construction sites) +1 (call site + import) ≈ -26, and -4 more only if option (i) also deletes the global branch, which I would not do. Total ≈ -70 (step 1 alone ≈ -60). Elements: -1 exported function, -1 private method, -1 dep field (two fields collapse into one) = -3, matching the claim.

Recommendation: ship step 1 as its own PR with corrections 1, 2, 5, 6, and 8 folded into the body; file step 2 as a blocked issue that must argue against d061941949 and #11173 together, and record correction 3 there so the next reader does not repeat the "existing divergence" framing.

</details>

#### The `defaultValue` second argument is dead at ~28 config reads — JsonConfigProvider already resolves the schema default first, and one copy has silently drifted

- **Kind**: delete · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -40 LoC, 0 elements (proposer claimed -60; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/latex/TikzPictureManager.ts`, `src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerOpenAI.ts`, `src/agent/modelHandlers/openai/modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts`, `src/agent/modelHandlers/google/modelHandlerGoogleInteractions.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/ModelFactory.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/selectAutoOpenFinalOutput.ts`, `src/replacement/engine.ts`, `src/latex/texTools.ts`, `src/tools/approval/toolEditApproval.ts`, `src/tools/approval/bashApproval.ts`, `src/utils/media/img.ts`, `packages/extension/src/commands/git/gitCommands.ts`, `src/test-kernel/support/FakePlatform.ts`

**Evidence**

`JsonConfigProvider.get` (src/platform/defaults/jsonConfigProvider.ts:39-47) resolves in this order: workspace store, global store, `getCoreSettingDefault(storedKey)`, and only then the caller's `defaultValue`. So for any key in `CORE_SETTING_PATHS` the caller's argument is unreachable.

All three hosts go through that class: `createNodePlatform` -> `toConfigProvider` -> `new JsonConfigProvider` (src/platform/defaults/nodeHost.ts:81-84), called from packages/cli/src/runtime/initPlatform.ts:271, packages/desktop/src/main/platform/index.ts:93, and packages/extension/src/extension.ts:280 (the extension passes `ExtensionTexraConfig`, which `extends JsonConfigProvider` and overrides only `update`, never `get` — packages/extension/src/frontend/vscode/texraConfig.ts:32-63). Verified by reading each file.

I enumerated every `getConfig`/`getValidatedConfig` call in production (`grep -rn "getConfig<\|getConfig(\|getValidatedConfig(" src packages/*/src` minus tests and the definitions): 37 total, 30 of which name a key that is in `CORE_SETTING_PATHS`. I checked each argument against `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS` by hand. One has drifted: `src/latex/TikzPictureManager.ts:23-38` inlines a 16-line TikZ template whose every line is indented two spaces, while the catalog default (`DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS.latex.tikzTemplate`, coreSettings.ts:139-149) has no leading indentation. Two copies of a LaTeX preamble, already different, one of them unreachable — the exact drift the second argument was supposed to prevent.

The other 29 agree today: `texra.maxImageDimension` 2000 (src/utils/media/img.ts:68) vs 2000; `texra.git.numberOfCommitsToShow` 20 (packages/extension/src/commands/git/gitCommands.ts:57) vs 20; `texra.bib.zoteroPort` 23119 (src/tools/zotero/bbtClient.ts:35) vs 23119; `texra.model.useGoogleBackgroundResponses` false (modelHandlerGoogleInteractions.ts:500) vs false (which had to be re-synced by 8a6081a084 "fix: default Google background responses off (#11364)" — a change that had to be made in two places); and six sites that import `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS` purely to pass it back to the provider that already applied it (ModelInvocationNode.ts:79, ModelFactory.ts:203, modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:333, modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts:1679, engine.ts:183, engine.ts:215).

The one thing that makes the argument _look_ load-bearing is a test-double divergence: `FakeConfigProvider.get` (src/test-kernel/support/FakePlatform.ts:76-82) returns `defaultValue` with no catalog lookup, so a fake diverges from the only production implementation on default resolution. Seven test files touch these keys (`grep -rln` over the key names in src/test-kernel).

Dated checks: `gh issue list --state all --search "getConfig default duplicate"` returns nothing; the only related issue is #8031 "Sync packages/extension/package.json retry-setting defaults with DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS" (CLOSED 2026-07-12) — a drift incident of exactly this kind. Neither round-1 nor round-2 filed it (checked all 151 titles and grepped both survey docs for `getConfig`, `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS`, `getCoreSettingDefault`).

No wire format, no persisted file, no settings key removed, no CI contract: this deletes only an unreachable function argument.

**Proposal**

Owner: `JsonConfigProvider` (after finding 1, the catalog row) stays the single resolver of "the default when a key is absent".

Migration:

1. Teach `FakeConfigProvider.get` the same last step the one production provider has — fall back to the catalog default before the caller's `defaultValue` (~4 lines). This is the correct fix independently: a fake that answers a resolution question differently from the only real implementation is a silent-degradation trap (checklist §15).
2. Drop the second argument at the ~28 `getConfig` sites whose key is in `CORE_SETTING_PATHS`. Multi-line calls collapse to one line; `src/latex/TikzPictureManager.ts:23-38` loses 16.
3. Drop the six now-unused `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS` imports.
4. EXCLUDE from the sweep: `src/housekeeping/utils.ts:126` and `src/housekeeping/pack.ts:253` read `texra.agent.rounds`, which is in no catalog — per the round-2 REVERTED-ON-REVIEW note, an uncataloged key is still honored from a user's `.texra/config.json`, so `DEFAULT_MAX_ROUNDS` there is genuinely load-bearing and must stay. Also leave the four `getValidatedConfig` sites alone: their third argument is the fallback for an _invalid stored_ value, and removing it changes the helper's signature for a redundancy worth ~4 lines.

LoC: ~28 sites averaging 1.5 lines each (-42), the tikz duplicate (-16), six imports (-8), FakeConfigProvider (+4). Net ~-62; budgeting -60.

**What we give up**

Callers can no longer state a per-call-site default that differs from the catalog. That is the point — a differing default is the drift this finds — but it means a future caller wanting a deliberately different fallback for a cataloged key must say so explicitly rather than by passing an argument that silently loses to the provider. Restoring the argument at one site is one line.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. MISSED THE DATED RULING. The finding says "gh issue list ... returns nothing" and cites only #8031. It missed .agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-07-09-state-of-the-architecture.md:837 (FB-3 "config double-defaults"), which ruled the opposite remedy on the same 28 sites. The PR must quote FB-3, state that 958460a0d5 (2026-08-02) superseded its premise, and amend that table row — otherwise a reviewer will refuse it on sight.

2. LoC OVERSTATED BY ~1.5x. "~28 sites averaging 1.5 lines each (-42)" is wrong: 16 of the 28 sites carry the default on the SAME line as the key (bbtClient:35, bashApproval:97, audio:36, img:68, selectAutoOpenFinalOutput:26, ExtractBibliographyTool:64, gInteractions:500, openAIResponse:414 and :1736, userVars:158 and :238, debugMessageSaver:36, engine:165, agentReviewCommitWatcher:63 and :103, gitCommands:57) and save ZERO lines. Only 11 sites are multi-line 4→1 collapses (-33). Real call-site total is -33, not -42.

3. IMPORT COUNT WRONG. "six now-unused DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS imports (-8)" → only FOUR files can drop the import (ModelFactory.ts:43, modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:39, modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts:56, engine.ts:8), each one line = -4. engine.ts has two use sites behind one import. ModelInvocationNode.ts:42 KEEPS its import because :79 is a `getValidatedConfig` site the proposal itself excludes — listing it among the six droppable imports contradicts the proposal's own exclusion rule.

4. LINE NUMBER OFF. modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts's `DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS.model.openaiParallelToolCalls` is at :1676, not :1679.

5. SECOND FAKE OMITTED. `FakeScopedConfigProvider.get` (FakePlatform.ts:163-170) has the SAME divergence and its own doc comment claims it "mirror[s] the platform config providers' resolution order" — a false claim today. Teaching only `FakeConfigProvider` leaves two fakes diverging in two different ways. I verified teaching both is free (60/60 pass), so fix both.

6. PORT CONTRACT UNDOCUMENTED — the real architectural cost the finding does not price. `ConfigProvider` (src/platform/interfaces.ts:35-40) declares `get<T>(key, defaultValue?): T` with no mention of catalog resolution. Deleting the argument makes an implementation detail of one class load-bearing for every caller, and nodeHost.ts:84 lets a caller (the SDK's own embedding path) inject an arbitrary `ConfigProvider`. Add a JSDoc line on `ConfigProvider.get` stating that an implementation must resolve `CORE_SETTING_PATHS` defaults before the caller's fallback (+5 lines). Without it this is an undocumented coupling, which is exactly what round 3 is supposed to catch.

7. FENCE A THIRD SITE THE PROPOSAL DOES NOT NAME. `getConfigBeforePlatformInit` (configUtils.ts:36, sole caller logger.debugMode at logUtils.ts:125) ends in `?? defaultValue` and runs when `tryPlatform()` is undefined — its default is genuinely load-bearing and must not be swept. The proposal's exclusion list names housekeeping and getValidatedConfig but not this.

8. OPTIONAL EXTRA, NOT COUNTED: after the collapse `getTikzTemplate()` (TikzPictureManager.ts:22-38) is a one-line single-caller wrapper, which the CLAUDE.md abstraction-discipline rule ("single-caller extractions are banned") says to inline into `createStandalone` — another -4 LoC and -1 element if taken.

9. IN-FLIGHT CONFLICT. ModelFactory.ts and modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts are being edited right now in an uncommitted OpenRouter-routing change in this working tree; sequence the sweep after that lands or those two sites will conflict.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The load-bearing claim is inertness, and it is true: JsonConfigProvider is the single production `get`, its catalog step precedes the caller's argument, no cataloged default is undefined, and all four host/SDK composition roots reach it. I re-derived the 28-site list myself and it matches exactly, including the key constants and the two bare `agentReview.runOnCommit` reads that rely on canonicalConfigKey. The tikz drift is real and is the strongest single argument: an unreachable 16-line LaTeX preamble that already differs from the live one is precisely the failure the redundant argument was meant to prevent.

The genuine round-3 hazard here was the dated ruling, and the finding walked past it. FB-3 in the 2026-07-09 state-of-the-architecture doc ruled on these exact 28 sites and prescribed keeping the inline default while sourcing it from DEFAULT_CORE_SETTINGS. That ruling is not beaten by argument, it is beaten by a commit: 958460a0d5 on 2026-08-02 added `getCoreSettingDefault` to `JsonConfigProvider.get` and made the argument unreachable. That is a clean supersession, and the proposal avoids both traps FB-3 named. But the finding claiming "no dated ruling exists" is the kind of error that gets an architectural PR reverted, so it must be corrected and the doc row amended in the same PR.

Cost: I measured rather than estimated. The single scariest cost line — "seven test files touch these keys" — is empirically zero: teaching both fakes and running all 61 FakePlatform-consuming suites produced no new failures. The LoC claim, though, does not survive: 16 of 28 sites are single-line and save nothing, and the import count is 4 not 6. Removals: -33 (eleven 4→1 collapses) -15 (tikz) -4 (imports) = -52. Additions: +6 FakeConfigProvider, +2 FakeScopedConfigProvider, +5 ConfigProvider port JSDoc, +1 FB-3 doc amendment = +14. Honest net ≈ -38, so -40 budgeted, not -60. Element count is genuinely 0: no export appears or disappears (unless getTikzTemplate is inlined, which would make it -1).

Risk is low. Behavior is identical at all 28 sites for every user, including one with a hand-edited .texra/config.json, because a stored value still wins at the first two hops; the only value that stops being reachable is one that was already unreachable. No wire, no persisted file, no settings key, no ratchet, no frozen SDK edge. The residual is the undocumented port contract, which a five-line JSDoc closes.

</details>

### approval-system — Approval, permission and policy

#### One bypass-ancestry graph, not three: the per-kind split belongs to the bypass _values_, and was mirrored onto the parent graph where nothing reads it apart

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -82 LoC, -7 elements (proposer claimed -60; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/agent/runtime/streamApprovalQueue.ts`, `src/tools/approval/index.ts`, `packages/cli/src/chat/chatSessionController.ts`, `src/test-kernel/agent/followUp/ChildStreamYoloInheritance.vitest.ts`

**Evidence**

`createSessionApprovals` builds THREE independent ancestry graphs — `parentOf: Record<BypassAncestryKind, Map<StreamTabId, StreamTabId>>` at src/agent/runtime/streamApprovalQueue.ts:266-270 — plus a curried `resolveParentFor(kind)` (:271-274) and `resolveDescendantsFor(kind)` (:275-291), a `bypassByKind` record (:312-316), a per-kind `detachKindFromParent` (:318-329), and three `for (const kind of ALL_BYPASS_ANCESTRY_KINDS)` loops (:331, :360, :373). The `kinds` parameter on `registerStreamParent` (:238, defaulted at :352) is what makes the three graphs able to diverge.

Production writers of the graph, grepped repo-wide (`rg registerStreamParent`): exactly two. `src/tools/approval/index.ts:57` (`configureDelegatedChildApprovals`, itself called from src/tools/delegation/subagentExecution.ts:140, workflowScriptAgentRunner.ts:329, WorkflowScriptTool.ts:528) passes no `kinds`; packages/cli/src/chat/chatSessionController.ts:458 passes no `kinds`. Every reader/eraser — `detachStreamFromParent` (called only from src/agent/runtime/executionRegistry.ts:724,729), `forgetStreamAncestry` (src/tools/approval/index.ts:79), `clearAll` — operates on all three kinds unconditionally. So in production the three Maps have identical key sets at every instant. The only callers that pass a subset are tests: src/test-kernel/agent/followUp/ChildStreamYoloInheritance.vitest.ts:230,233,283 and src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/ExecutionRegistry.vitest.ts:1630.

The justifying comments conflate two different per-kind facts. streamApprovalQueue.ts:263-265 says the graphs are "deliberately NOT shared … so that e.g. linking `bash` ancestry for a delegated subagent can never let it also inherit `toolEdit`"; src/tools/goal/goalAutoApproval.ts:15-18 says "per-kind ancestry means a goal parent with bash bypassed but edits gated propagates exactly that split." The second claim is false as a justification: the split it describes comes from the per-kind _value_ maps (each `createStreamApprovalBypass` owns its own `byStream`, :62), and survives unchanged with one shared parent graph. Only the first claim depends on the graph split, and it guards a subset-link that no production caller performs.

Dated checks: `git log --oneline -20` on src/agent/runtime/streamApprovalQueue.ts shows no change to the ancestry machinery since #10741 (2026-08). `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search approval` returns no issue on the ancestry graphs. .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-prd-approval-policy-unification.md:194-197 lists "No change to the per-stream scoped-bypass machinery (`streamApprovalQueue.ts`), its ancestry inheritance" under **§6 Non-goals** — a scope fence for that migration, not a design rationale defending three graphs; §7.4 in the same PRD separately rules only that the `'proposal'`-vs-`'superYolo'` key/label split is intentional, which this proposal does not touch. Kill-condition check: the graphs are in-memory session state only — no NDJSON payload, no persisted file, no settings key, no texra-action field.

**Proposal**

`createSessionApprovals` keeps one `parentOf: Map<StreamTabId, StreamTabId>`. `resolveParent` becomes `(id) => parentOf.get(id)` and `resolveDescendants` a single non-curried walk, both passed to all three `createStreamApprovalBypass` instances. `registerStreamParent(child, parent)` loses its `kinds` parameter and its 19-line jsdoc paragraph about per-kind linking. `detachStreamFromParent` iterates the three bypass objects (kept as a 3-element array, since each kind's _effective value_ must still be preserved separately on promotion) over the one graph, so `detachKindFromParent` and `bypassByKind` collapse into it; `forgetStreamAncestry` and `clearAll` lose their kind loops. `BypassAncestryKind` and `ALL_BYPASS_ANCESTRY_KINDS` cease to exist.

Owner absorbing the responsibility: the three `StreamApprovalBypass` instances already own per-kind semantics through their own `byStream` maps; the graph only ever answered "who is this stream's parent", which is kind-independent in every production path.

Migration: one file changes production-side. Delete the two ChildStreamYoloInheritance tests that assert subset-linking (:225-245 and :280-290) — per AGENTS.md "Testing discipline", tests pinning retired behavior get deleted with the behavior. The rest of that suite (inheritance, super-YOLO override, round-to-round carry-forward) is unchanged and is the real regression net.

**What we give up**

The ability for a future caller to link a child to a parent for only some bypass kinds. Nothing does this today; re-adding it is re-introducing the `kinds` parameter and re-splitting one Map into three — roughly the same diff in reverse. Until then it is an unexercised safety property whose comments have already caused two other comments to claim it does work the value maps actually do.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. PROVENANCE IS MISSING AND IT IS THE STRONGEST ARGUMENT. The finding never says why the split exists. `git log -S"kinds"` gives two commits. b7752f7bdd (#8504, 2026-07-16) added it mid-PR, and its own message records the reason verbatim: "Cursor Bugbot (high severity, #3e336d8a): registerStreamParent used one ancestry graph shared across toolEdit/bash/proposal, so linking a delegated child for bash inheritance also silently let it inherit tool-edit YOLO and super-YOLO ... registerStreamParent takes an optional `kinds` list (default: all three ...) so inheritBashBypassOnChildStream can link 'bash' only." Then 8d85c06ecb (#8620, 2026-07-16) deleted that bash-only caller ("Delegation now registers ancestry for both kinds"), and f5348d88b0 (#9093) added proposal linking. So the guard's only production client was removed by two later fixes. That is the real case for vestigiality and it belongs in the proposal; without it a reviewer will read the ChildStreamYoloInheritance:281-284 comment ("Ancestry used to be one shared graph ... silently let it inherit tool-edit YOLO too") as a live regression and reject.

2. DATE CITATION IS WRONG. "no change to the ancestry machinery since #10741 (2026-08)" is not what git says. The file's most recent commit is 29925ac85d "refactor: consolidate shared host helpers" (2026-08-24); #10741's commits are 4befc30036/093daa816f/39ea3c7272 and are not it. The ancestry machinery itself is unchanged since 8d85c06ecb (#8620, 2026-07-16) — that is the correct citation.

3. UNSTATED IMPLEMENTATION TRAP (ordering). Today `detachKindFromParent` (:318-329) reads the effective value, deletes THAT kind's edge, then writes the value back. With one shared Map, all three effective values must be captured BEFORE the single `parentOf.delete(streamId)`; delete-first silently promotes every kind to `false` and quietly revokes an inherited bypass. Same ordering constraint in `forgetStreamAncestry`. The proposal says "iterates the three bypass objects over the one graph" without naming this, and it is the one way this collapse can regress.

4. PRD COLLISION IS NARROWER BUT REAL. §7.4 (.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-prd-approval-policy-unification.md:352-353) pins "the `'proposal'`-vs-`'superYolo'` key/label split in `streamApprovalQueue.ts` is intentional". `BypassAncestryKind` is one of the two carriers of that `'proposal'` key. The PR must keep the `SessionApprovals.proposal` field (:208) and the `createStreamApprovalBypass('superYolo', …)` label (:305) to stay inside the ruling. Saying the PRD "does not touch" this is slightly too clean.

5. MIGRATION FOOTPRINT UNDERCOUNTED. "one file changes production-side" is right for logic, but three production files change: streamApprovalQueue.ts, plus stale doc comments at src/tools/approval/index.ts:42-45 ("Ancestry is tracked per bypass kind, so the CLI's distinct AUTO-BASH / AUTO-APPROVE grants are respected") and src/tools/goal/goalAutoApproval.ts:15-18 — both assert the graph split does work the value maps do, so leaving them is exactly the comment-rot the finding complains about. And a SECOND test file needs editing, not just deleting: src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/ExecutionRegistry.vitest.ts:1628-1630 passes `['toolEdit']` and must lose the argument.

6. TEST LINE RANGES. The two tests to delete are :224-242 ("preserves independent ancestry when one kind loses its parent", 19 lines) and :280-291 ("keeps ancestry graphs independent per bypass kind", 12 lines), not :225-245 and :280-290.

7. MINOR LINE-NUMBER SLIP. `resolveDescendantsFor` spans :275-292, not :275-291. `parentOf` is :263-270 including its 3-line rationale comment (the finding cites :266-270, excluding the comment it proposes to delete).

8. CLAIMED NUMBERS ARE TOO CONSERVATIVE. -60/-4 undersells it; see netLoc/netElements below. `resolveParentFor`/`resolveDescendantsFor` are renamed-in-place (uncurried), not removed, so they should not be counted as element deletions — the real removals are elsewhere and there are more of them.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The claim's load-bearing assertion — that the three Maps are provably identical in production — is verifiable from the code alone and I verified it: two writers, both defaulting `kinds` to all three; three erasers, all looping all three kinds unconditionally; the per-kind early-return in `detachKindFromParent` keyed on a `has()` that is uniform by construction. The subset capability that `kinds` exists for is exercised only by three test call sites.

The history seals it. The split was a Bugbot-driven fix in #8504 for a caller (`inheritBashBypassOnChildStream`, bash-only) that #8620 deleted six hours later when delegation moved to linking both kinds, and #9093 later added proposal linking too. The trend across three PRs has been monotonically toward "link everything", and no caller has passed a subset since 2026-07-16. That is a guard whose client is gone, not defense in depth.

The two comments the finding calls out are genuinely wrong, and that is a live cost, not a stylistic one: src/tools/approval/index.ts:42-45 and src/tools/goal/goalAutoApproval.ts:15-18 both credit the graph split with the AUTO-BASH/AUTO-APPROVE separation, which is actually delivered by each `StreamApprovalBypass`'s own `byStream` map. A structure whose documentation misattributes its own value to a different mechanism is the definition of a concept that should be one, not three.

Honest cost recount, file by file:

- streamApprovalQueue.ts: -8 (type + const + their comment, :20-27), -1 (`kinds` param), -6 (per-kind jsdoc paragraph on registerStreamParent, :219-225), -7 (parentOf record + its 3-line rationale comment), -2 (uncurry resolveParent), -3 (uncurry resolveDescendants), -3 (bypassByKind record → 3-element array), -6 (detachKindFromParent + detachStreamFromParent merged, 17 lines → ~11), -5 (registerStreamParent body 8 → 3), -3 (forgetStreamAncestry 10 → 7), -1 (clearAll loop). ≈ -45.
- src/tools/approval/index.ts + src/tools/goal/goalAutoApproval.ts: ≈ -4 (stale comment sentences).
- ChildStreamYoloInheritance.vitest.ts: -31 (two tests + blanks).
- ExecutionRegistry.vitest.ts: -2.
  Net ≈ -82 LoC. No new file, no shim, no relocated logic — this is the rare case where the honest number beats the claim rather than inverting it, because the only "migration" is deleting an argument two callers never pass.

Elements removed: `BypassAncestryKind`, `ALL_BYPASS_ANCESTRY_KINDS`, `bypassByKind`, `detachKindFromParent`, the `kinds` parameter (5 named), plus 2 tests = -7. `resolveParentFor`/`resolveDescendantsFor` survive as `resolveParent`/`resolveDescendants` and must not be counted.

Risk stays medium and should not be argued down. This is approval-bypass code, the removed structure was once a high-severity review-bot finding, and the ordering trap in correction 3 is a genuine way to silently revoke a user's inherited auto-approval on child detach. What keeps it at medium rather than high: the default path is bit-for-bit unchanged (both production callers already get all three kinds), so no new footgun is created — only an option nothing uses disappears. The regression net that matters (round-to-round carry-forward, nested-orchestrator proposal propagation, super-YOLO pinning, torn-down-parent preservation, ExecutionRegistry's detach-preserves-approvals test) survives untouched and is what CI should be judged on.

</details>

#### One policy gate for all seven request kinds at the request boundary — the plan/proposal/retry/user-question/inquiry gate lives only in the two CLI adapters, so extension and desktop silently ignore `never` and `yolo` for four of the PRD's six rows

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: high
- **Net**: -50 LoC, -6 elements (proposer claimed -100; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `packages/cli/src/runtime/approval/settleApprovals.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/approvalAdapter.ts`, `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/subscribeApprovals.ts`, `src/tools/plan/PlanTool.ts`, `src/tools/delegation/proposalFlow.ts`, `src/agent/core/flows/ModelInvocationNode.ts`, `src/tools/userQuestion/UserQuestionTool.ts`, `src/tools/inquiry/ExternalInquiryTool.ts`, `src/test-kernel/architecture/approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet.vitest.ts`

**Evidence**

Traced every one of the seven `HostInteractions` request kinds from origin to settlement.

Bash and tool edit are gated in host-agnostic core, at the tool boundary: src/tools/approval/bashApproval.ts:105-118 and src/tools/approval/toolEditApproval.ts:216-228 both call `decideTexraApproval` before ever touching `session.interactions`. Every host inherits that.

The other five originate in core with NO gate and go straight to the session: src/tools/plan/PlanTool.ts:241, src/tools/delegation/proposalFlow.ts:197, src/agent/core/flows/ModelInvocationNode.ts:564, src/tools/userQuestion/UserQuestionTool.ts:72, src/tools/inquiry/ExternalInquiryTool.ts:337. Their policy gate exists only inside the two CLI adapters — `decideWithPolicy`/`settleExecutable` at packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/subscribeApprovals.ts:293-305, `settleRetry` at :387, `settleHumanInputDenial` at :508, `denyExternalInquiryIfNoHumanInput` at :856, and the mirror set in packages/cli/src/runtime/approvalAdapter.ts:107-111,195 — all backed by packages/cli/src/runtime/approval/settleApprovals.ts.

The shared webview host adapter that serves BOTH extension and desktop, src/controllers/progressView/backend/progressHostInteractions.ts:379-436, calls `handlers().planApproval.request(...)`, `.proposal.request(...)`, `.retry.request(...)`, `.userQuestion.request(...)` with no policy consultation at all. `rg 'approvalPolicy' packages/extension packages/desktop` returns only seeding sites (packages/extension/src/extension.ts:351, texraConfig.ts:87, SettingsViewMessageHandler.ts:547; packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts:1288, desktopSettingsIpc.ts:258) — zero readers. So on the two webview hosts, `texra.approvalPolicy: yolo` still opens a plan-approval modal and an agent-proposal modal, and `never` still prompts instead of denying, for four kinds.

That contradicts a dated, ratified spec: .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-prd-approval-policy-unification.md:76-83 ("Decision matrix (completed)") gives explicit `never`/`ask`/`yolo` behavior for "Plan approval / agent proposal", "Retry request", "Retry after credential exhaustion", and "User question / external inquiry", and §7.2 (:245-247) says the shared evaluators exist "so that a future desktop/extension retry or question surface calls them instead of re-implementing". gh issue 9597 (CLOSED, tracking, risk:high) states the completion condition as "all three hosts use the same live evaluator and authority" — it was closed with bash/edit done and these four rows CLI-only.

Kill-condition check: no NDJSON field, persisted file, settings key, or texra-action result field changes. The denial _messages_ are already the shared `texraApprovalDenialMessage` / `texraRetryDenialMessage` / `texraHumanInputDenialMessage` constants, so agent-visible text is unchanged where the CLI already denies.

**Proposal**

Move the gate to where bash and tool edit already have it: the five core origin sites. Each gains the same six-line shape those two use — read `session.approvalPolicy`, read `canPresent` from `tryUseRunContext()?.approvalPromptsUnavailable !== true`, call the matching shared evaluator (`decideTexraApproval` for plan/proposal, `decideRetryApproval` for retry, `decideHumanInputRequest` for user-question/inquiry), and settle with the shared denial message instead of calling `session.interactions.*`. `isCredentialRetryFailure` (packages/cli/src/runtime/approval/settleApprovals.ts:88-94) moves next to `decideRetryApproval` in src/shared/approvalPolicy.ts; it already reads only shared-schema fields.

Owner that absorbs it: the request boundary in core, which is what #9597's completed-work note calls "the shared request boundaries" for #9692. Every host then inherits identical semantics, which is exactly what that issue's completion condition asked for.

What then ceases to exist: `settleExecutable`, `settleRetry`, `settleHumanInputDenial`, `denyExternalInquiryIfNoHumanInput`, `executableDecision`, `livePolicy`, `canPresent`, `isCredentialRetryFailure` — i.e. all of packages/cli/src/runtime/approval/settleApprovals.ts except `cliApprovalPromptsUnavailable`, which moves to cliContext.ts. `decideWithPolicy` and the `immediate` branches in both CLI adapters go with them.

Honest cost: ~-178 LoC removed from the CLI, ~+66 added across the five core sites plus ~+8 for the moved credential classifier, net ≈ -104. The `EVALUATOR_CALL_ALLOWLIST` in src/test-kernel/architecture/approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet.vitest.ts goes from 6 entries to 8 (drop settleApprovals.ts, add the five origin sites) — a real cost in allowlist size, but each entry is a call to the one shared evaluator, which is the invariant the ratchet actually guards. Do NOT instead put the gate inside `SessionHostInteractions.enqueue`: gh issue 9597's required authority model says "Host interactions remain transport and presentation only", and the same PRD §6 forbids a policy resolver/facade.

The CLI's one-line `[warn] [cli-approval]` operator notice must reach the CLI from core for these kinds; that is the channel the next finding builds, so land these two together.

**What we give up**

Extension and desktop users who set `yolo` or `never` today get the current behavior for these four kinds (always prompt). After the change `yolo` auto-approves plans/proposals and auto-denies retries and questions, and `never` denies all four. That is the specified behavior, but it IS a user-visible behavior change on two hosts and needs a changelog line, not a silent refactor. Also given up: the CLI's ability to diverge from the other hosts on these kinds without touching core.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. "zero readers" is false. `rg 'approvalPolicy' packages/extension packages/desktop` does NOT return only seeding sites: `packages/extension/src/extension.ts:673` reads `statusBarSession.approvalPolicy` and `:677` formats it into the `texra.taskStatus` tooltip, and `settingsState.ts:239` / `ToolsTab.ts:164,198` render it. The correct claim is "zero ENFORCEMENT readers" — there are three display readers.

2. The warn channel already exists; the finding's stated dependency on "the next finding" is wrong and should be deleted. `RunContext.onApprovalPolicyDenial` (`src/agent/runtime/RunContext.ts:17,61,86,99`) already propagates through fresh, resumed, delegated, and workflow-script execution (`executeAgent.ts:429,585`, `resumeRun.ts:373`, `subagentExecution.ts:167,212`, `workflowScriptAgentRunner.ts:316`, `nativeSubagentStrategy.ts:300,386`), and both CLI entry points already wire it to `warnApprovalDenied` (`runExecution.ts:472`, `chatSessionController.ts:350,442`). `bashApproval.ts:114` and `toolEditApproval.ts:224` call it today. The only work is widening the callback to `(gate?: string) => void` so the four new gate labels survive; ~8 lines. Do NOT block this on another finding.

3. Ratchet arithmetic is wrong. `EVALUATOR_CALL_ALLOWLIST` has 6 entries today and goes to 10, not 8 (drop `settleApprovals.ts`, add five origin files) — and to 11 if `executableDecision` moves to `cliContext.ts` as proposed, since the ratchet is a text regex over `decideTexraApproval\s*\(`. That is a near-doubling of the allowlist, a materially worse cost than stated.

4. `settleApprovals.ts` is 160 lines, not 178, and it cannot be reduced to "everything except `cliApprovalPromptsUnavailable`". That survivor transitively needs `executableDecision`, `livePolicy`, `canPresent` and the `decideTexraApproval` import — roughly 30 lines relocate rather than die, because `runExecution.ts:468` and `chatSessionController.ts:404` still call it to compute `approvalPromptsUnavailable`.

5. `isCredentialRetryFailure` cannot move to `src/shared/approvalPolicy.ts` as proposed. It calls `isCredentialExhausted`, which lives in `src/shared/schemas/errors.ts`; `src/shared/schemas/index.ts:14,27` re-exports both `errors` and `stateSettings`, and `stateSettings.ts:9` imports `@shared/approvalPolicy` — so the barrel import is a cycle, and the deep import `@shared/schemas/errors` creates a NEW entry in `config/ratchets/shared-schemas-deep-import-baseline.json`, whose semantics line says "Any new entry in any class fails" against CLAUDE.md's "never widen a baseline". Correct move: inline the two-line classification at the `ModelInvocationNode` gate, which already sits outside `src/shared/`.

6. The proposal site is mistraced, and PR #9975 (2026-08-11) rules against the proposed shape. `proposalFlow.ts:185-196` already carries a documented gate — when `tryUseRunContext()?.approvalPromptsUnavailable === true` it returns APPROVE, the opposite of the PRD's "deny if unpresentable" row. #9975's body: "the proposal is the interactive review surface, not the security gate", landed to fix a main-only CI outage where `--approval-policy never` denied the `delegate_multi_agents` proposal and the workflow-script child never launched (`packages/cli/scripts/validate-run.mjs`). Because `never` makes `approvalPromptsUnavailable` true (`decideTexraApproval` returns `deny-policy` before consulting `canPresent`), this branch fires first: today `never` does NOT deny a proposal on any host, and the CLI's `decideWithPolicy` proposal arm is only live for `yolo`. Installing "the same six-line shape" here re-breaks #9975 and the run-validation suite. The proposal row must either be excluded, or implemented as "yolo -> allow, else existing #9975 behavior" with an explicit note that it deliberately does not implement the PRD's never/unpresentable rows. Either way it is not one of five identical sites.

7. The behavior-change section understates the retry row. Under `yolo` on the extension/desktop today, a transient model failure surfaces `RetryRequestPanel` where the user can switch credentials. After the change, `decideRetryApproval` returns `{deny:'yolo-retry'}` and the run fails with no panel. The PRD's rationale for that row ("must not invent human intent") is a headless argument; a GUI host has a human present. Same for `ask_user_question` under `yolo`. This is a product decision that needs an owner ruling, not a changelog line — the PRD §10.5/§10.6 do specify it, but the rows were authored in a CLI-only world (§7.2 anticipated "a future desktop/extension retry or question surface", not a core gate). Risk is high, not medium.

8. Migration cost the finding omits entirely: the tests. `src/test-kernel/cli/ApprovalAdapter.vitest.ts` (934 lines, 20 `approvalPolicy` refs), `TuiApprovalRetry.vitest.ts` (1676 lines), `ApprovalDeniedWarn.vitest.ts` (61 lines) pin the CLI-adapter policy behavior directly and must be rewritten or relocated to the tools/agent kernels. Roughly LoC-neutral but a real fraction of the work, and the repo's testing budget will want a couple of new gate tests at the core boundary.

9. Not an error, but worth stating in the PR: `src/agent/runtime/SessionHandle.ts:271` documents the value as "Live host-neutral approval policy for **executable requests**", and the shipped settings copy in `src/shared/approvalPolicy.ts` reads "Deny Bash commands and tool edits" / "Allow Bash commands and tool edits without approval". Extending enforcement to four more kinds makes that copy inaccurate, so `TEXRA_APPROVAL_POLICY_COPY` and `ToolsTab.ts` need updating in the same PR.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The finding's central factual claim holds under independent re-walking: bash and tool edit are gated in host-agnostic core at `bashApproval.ts:105-118` / `toolEditApproval.ts:216-228`, while plan, proposal, retry, user-question and external-inquiry reach `session.interactions` ungated, and the shared webview adapter serving both extension and desktop consults no policy at all. It is a genuine spec gap against a ratified PRD (§3 matrix, §10.5, §10.6, §10.12) and the closed tracking issue's completion condition, not a re-litigation of one.

It is not a duplicate, does not cross a frozen wire, does not touch a persisted file or settings key, and is not churn. The two settled-surface collisions it hits are both survivable: the evaluator ratchet is explicitly designed to be extended by reviewed allowlist edit, and the shared-schemas deep-import problem disappears if the credential classifier stays out of `src/shared/`.

What it gets wrong is the shape of the work. It invents a missing warn channel that already exists and already propagates through every execution path, which subtracts a claimed dependency. It misses PR #9975, a dated ruling nine days after the PRD that inverted the proposal row for exactly the unpresentable case the finding wants to deny — implementing "the same six-line shape" there re-breaks the headless `delegate_multi_agents` scenario that #9975 was written to fix. Its allowlist arithmetic is off by two to three entries, its source file is 160 lines rather than 178, and it counts no test migration for three CLI suites totalling ~2,700 lines that pin the behavior being moved.

On cost: CLI deletions are real — roughly 130 net lines out of `settleApprovals.ts` after the `cliApprovalPromptsUnavailable` survivor relocates, ~45 from `approvalAdapter.ts` (the `immediate` branch, `toPromptedApprovalSettlement`, `toRetryResult`'s deny arm, the headless question denial), ~30 from `subscribeApprovals.ts`. Core additions are larger than "six lines each": each of the five sites needs imports plus a denial settled into that site's own result union (`ToolResult`, `DelegationProposalDecision`, `RetryResult` alongside `logRetryLifecycle`, the inquiry thread drop plus its yolo message), realistically ~20 lines apiece, plus the callback-label widening. That lands near -50, not -104. Still negative, which is rare enough in this repo to be worth having, but the finding should not be shipped on its stated number.

Risk is high rather than medium: it changes user-visible behavior on two hosts for four request kinds, one of those changes removes the GUI retry panel under `yolo` on a rationale that was written for headless runs, it edits `ModelInvocationNode`'s manual-retry loop, and it collides with a CI-outage fix.

</details>

### tool-layer — The tool layer as an architecture

#### One owner for the model-facing tool list: delete the reflection family's second, weaker tool filter

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -157 LoC, -3 elements
- **Files**: `src/agent/implementations/flows/reflection/ResponseCycleFlow.ts`, `src/agent/core/flows/ModelInvocationNode.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/agentToolResolution.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/agentSettingTools.ts`, `src/agent/modelHandlers/toolConversion.ts`, `src/test-kernel/agent/ResponseCycleTools.vitest.ts`

**Evidence**

End-to-end trace of "which tool definitions reach the provider". There are two mechanisms and they belong to the two flow families. (1) Tool-use: `runToolUseFlow` (src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/runToolUseFlow.ts:181-186) calls `resolveAgentTools` (src/agent/runtime/agentToolResolution.ts:102-163), which applies six rules — runtimeUnavailable, approval-prompts-unavailable, user-disabled, external-dependency-missing, conditional injections, delegation annotation — and then stores the result as `setting: {...setting, tools: resolvedTools}` (runToolUseFlow.ts:241). `ModelInvocationNode` picks it up through its default `services.setting.tools` (src/agent/core/flows/ModelInvocationNode.ts:705-709); `createToolUseRoundFlow` configures no `getTools` (ToolUseRoundFlow.ts:48-71). (2) Reflection: `createResponseCycleFlow` sets `getTools: responseCycleToolsForModel` (ResponseCycleFlow.ts:626), which applies two of those six rules plus an all-or-nothing `supportsFunctionCalling` gate (ResponseCycleFlow.ts:207-224). It is the only `getTools` user in the repo (grep `getTools`: ModelInvocationNode.ts:141,707-708 and ResponseCycleFlow.ts:626 only).

The flow it serves cannot dispatch a tool call: `grep -rn 'toolCall|tool_use|dispatch' src/agent/implementations/flows/reflection/` returns zero hits, and `createResponseCycleFlow` configures no `getFinalTool`. The loader already says so out loud — src/agent/runtime/agentSettingTools.ts:29-40 warns at load time that "Workflow runs never dispatch tool calls, so these are inert". So the reflection filter guards a list nothing consumes locally.

Its capability gate is also strictly worse than the owner that already exists. `toOpenAIResponseTools` (src/agent/modelHandlers/toolConversion.ts:494-513) drops function tools per-tool when `!supportsFunctionCalling` while KEEPING native `web_search`; `responseCycleToolsForModel` returns `undefined` for the whole list, so a workflow agent on a deep-research model loses the native web search the adapter would have preserved. `toAnthropicTools` (toolConversion.ts:527-560) owns the same decision for Anthropic natives.

Dated provenance: the function was added by #5061 (c3cc28e0b4, 2026-06-01) closing issue #5060, back when ResponseCycleFlow lived in `src/agent/core/flows/` and was the cycle flow for BOTH families — `git show c3cc28e0b4 --stat` shows the file at its old core path. The reflection/tool-use family split later moved the file to `implementations/flows/reflection/` and gave tool-use its own round flow, and the filter came along as residue. #5060's own text concedes the blast radius: "Built-in workflow agents currently do not declare tools" — verified still true, `grep -rl 'agentCategory: workflow' packages/extension/resources/agents/` returns 7 files, none of which declares `tools:`. Also checked: not in the round-1/round-2 surveys except as part of the shipped `<C>`-generic erasure (.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-26-simplification-survey-round2.md:811,825); `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "responseCycleToolsForModel workflow tools"` returns only the unrelated #6945.

**Proposal**

Owner that absorbs the responsibility: `ModelInvocationNode`'s uniform default (`services.setting.tools`) for what the model is shown, `resolveAgentTools` for filtering on the only family that can dispatch, and the per-provider converters in `toolConversion.ts` for capability gating. Migration: (1) delete `getTools: responseCycleToolsForModel` from `createResponseCycleFlow` (ResponseCycleFlow.ts:626) and the exported function (ResponseCycleFlow.ts:207-224), narrowing that services Pick's `toolRegistry`/`toolPolicy` away; (2) `getTools` then has zero users, so delete the optional field and its "`?? services.setting.tools` would be wrong here" branch from `ModelInvocationConfig`/`invoke` (ModelInvocationNode.ts:141, 705-709), leaving one unconditional `tools: services.setting.tools`; (3) delete src/test-kernel/agent/ResponseCycleTools.vitest.ts (131 lines), whose entire subject is the deleted function. Net: -18 production LoC in ResponseCycleFlow, -6 in ModelInvocationNode, -131 test, +~2 for the simplified call. Verify with the reflection suites (ResponseCycleCancellation/ResponseCycleContinuation) plus one manual workflow run.

**What we give up**

#5060's parity guarantee: under `--approval-policy never`, a CUSTOM workflow agent that declares an approval-gated tool would again have it advertised to the model. The honest counter is that the exposure is cosmetic — there is no dispatcher on that path, so an emitted tool call is dropped rather than executed or auto-denied; the concrete cost is wasted prompt tokens, not an unapproved side effect. If the team still wants the guarantee, keep it as a two-line filter at the point where the reflection services' `setting` is built, which still removes the duplicate `getTools` hook and the coarse capability gate (net then ~-140 instead of -157).

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

FIVE factual errors, none fatal.

1. PROVENANCE IS WRONG (the most substantive). The finding says ResponseCycleFlow "was the cycle flow for BOTH families" at #5061 and that the filter "came along as residue" of the reflection/tool-use split. `git grep -n createResponseCycleFlow c3cc28e0b4 -- src` returns only `src/agent/implementations/flows/reflection/nodes/ResponseCycleNode.ts:6,91` plus the definition itself. It lived at `src/agent/core/flows/` but was ALREADY reflection-only on 2026-06-01. So #5061 deliberately added a reflection-only filter with full knowledge of the path; this is a considered fix being reverted, not residue. `git show c3cc28e0b4` also shows the `supportsFunctionCalling` gate PREDATES #5061 (it was an inline `getTools` ternary that #5061 merely extracted), so the capability gate has separate, older provenance the finding never traces. Rewrite the "residue" framing as "revert a considered 2026-06-01 fix whose premise a 2026-08-15 ruling has since undercut" — the finding is still arguable on those terms, but not on the ones it states.

2. THE "DEEP RESEARCH LOSES NATIVE WEB SEARCH" HARM IS UNREACHABLE TODAY. llm-zoo 1.30.0 has exactly two `supportsFunctionCalling: false` sites: `OPENAI_DEEP_RESEARCH_DEFAULT_CAPABILITIES` (dist/index.js:2502) and `deepseek-reasoner` speciale (:1278). Every model carrying either is `deprecated: true, retired: true`, and `computeModelOptions.ts:295-296,439` makes a retired model unselectable ("retired and cannot be enabled"). The gate is therefore DEAD for every selectable model, not "strictly worse" — which strengthens the deletion but means the finding's stated evidence is wrong in kind.

3. A FOURTH REFERENCE SITE IS MISSED. The finding's grep was for `getTools`, so it missed `src/agent/runtime/helperModelPreference.ts:31-41`, which names the function in a load-bearing comment: "The tool-use flow drops every tool unless the model positively declares function calling (`responseCycleToolsForModel` returns nothing when `!capabilities.supportsFunctionCalling`), so mirror that check". That comment is ALREADY STALE — the tool-use flow does not call `responseCycleToolsForModel` and `resolveAgentTools` has no capability gate — and deleting the function leaves it dangling. `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/helperModelPreference.vitest.ts:16-18,69` carries the same mirrored rationale. The guard's code stays correct (a helper model that cannot call functions cannot run latexFixer), but both comments must be amended in the same PR.

4. `toolRegistry` CANNOT BE NARROWED AWAY, ONLY THE PICK. It is declared on `CycleRunServices` (`src/agent/core/flows/CycleServices.ts:23`), which `ToolUseRoundServices` also extends and `ToolUseDispatchNode.ts:228,346` genuinely reads. So no interface field or element is removed; worse, `reflection/nodes/ResponseCycleNode.ts:99` keeps constructing `toolRegistry: getDefaultToolRegistry()` for a reflection flow that no longer reads it — the PR trades one duplicate filter for one new latent dead field unless it also pushes `toolRegistry` down to `ToolUseRoundServices` (out of the stated scope, and it would touch the ToolUseCycleNode bridge).

5. ONE OMITTED SAFETY ARGUMENT THAT ACTUALLY HELPS THE CASE. The finding never checks the `undefined` → `[]` behavior change for the capability-gated path. It is benign: every handler guards on length (`modelHandlerAnthropic.ts:697 if (tools?.length)`, `modelHandlerVscodeLm.ts:243 options.tools?.length`, `modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:340`), and the common path already sends `[]` today, since `responseCycleToolsForModel` returns `setting.tools.filter(...)` = `[]` for the seven built-in workflow agents. State this; a reviewer will ask.

Also worth stating explicitly, since the finding leaves it implicit: the strongest argument for the collapse is not "the tools are inert" but "the family that CAN dispatch has no whole-list capability gate at all and is fine" — `resolveAgentTools` (agentToolResolution.ts:102-163) never consults `supportsFunctionCalling`, leaving it to the per-provider converters. The reflection copy is the inconsistent one.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Recounted the migration cost line by line rather than trusting the claim. ResponseCycleFlow.ts: the exported function is lines 207-224 (18 lines), plus `getTools: responseCycleToolsForModel` at :626 (1), plus `type ToolDefinition,` at :31 which becomes unused since :212 is its only other use in the file (1) = -20. ModelInvocationNode.ts: the `getTools?:` field at :141 (1), plus the two-line comment at :705-706 and the three-line ternary at :707-709 collapsing to one `tools: services.setting.tools,` = -5 to -6. Test: `wc -l src/test-kernel/agent/ResponseCycleTools.vitest.ts` = 131, and its whole subject is the deleted function plus the deleted `getTools` branch ("honors an explicit getTools result even when it is undefined" at :87-95), so it goes entirely. helperModelPreference.ts + its vitest need comment rewrites, roughly net zero. Total ≈ -157, so the claimed number is honest and does NOT fall into the section-13 "+5,046 net add" trap — there are no call sites to migrate (one production caller), no wire contract, no docs to amend beyond two code comments. Elements: `responseCycleToolsForModel` export, the `getTools` config field, and one test file = -3, as claimed. Risk stays medium rather than low: this is a genuine behavior change under `--approval-policy never` / `--no-input` for a CUSTOM workflow agent that declares an approval-gated tool, and it reverts a filed-and-fixed CLI bug (#5060/#5061). The mitigation the finding already offers — keeping a two-line approval filter where the reflection services' `setting` is built, at ~-140 instead of -157 — is the version I would ship, because it removes the duplicate `getTools` hook and the dead capability gate without reopening #5060. Verification: the two reflection suites named plus `helperModelPreference.vitest.ts` (its comments change) and `ToolConversion.vitest.ts` (unchanged, but it is the surviving owner of the capability gate at :520-593).

</details>

#### A tool's contract is materialized three times before the model sees it; the registry should own it once, at run time

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -95 LoC, -6 elements (proposer claimed -120; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/tools/core/define.ts`, `src/tools/registry.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/agentSettingTools.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/agentLoad.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/agentToolResolution.ts`, `src/agent/core/definition/AgentDataclass.ts`, `src/agent/modelHandlers/toolConversion.ts`, `src/test-kernel/tools/ToolRegistryAliases.vitest.ts`

**Evidence**

Trace of a tool's schema/description from declaration to wire. (A) `defineTool` eagerly converts every tool's Zod schema to JSON Schema at construction and stores BOTH representations on the definition: `parameters: toToolParameters(def.schema)` and `zodSchema: def.schema` (src/tools/core/define.ts:14-20, 92-94), for all 53 registry entries (src/tools/registry.ts:95-149). (B) At agent load, `resolveAgentSettingTools` -> `resolveToolDefinitions` replaces YAML tool NAMES with those same definition objects (src/agent/runtime/agentSettingTools.ts:16-47, src/tools/registry.ts:244-275), called from src/agent/runtime/agentLoad.ts:57,118,184 and the remote loader. (C) At run time `resolveAgentTools` filters that list and re-consults the registry anyway for `has()` and `requiresApproval` (src/agent/runtime/agentToolResolution.ts:118-142). (D) The provider adapters then IGNORE the materialized `parameters` whenever `zodSchema` is present and re-derive JSON Schema from Zod per request, with `parameters` only as the fallback for a definition that has no Zod schema (src/agent/modelHandlers/toolConversion.ts:186-195, 231-246).

So `parameters` on a registered tool has zero production readers: repo-wide, the only non-test reads of `ToolDefinition.parameters` are those two fallback lines and `overridesContract`'s warning comparison (src/tools/registry.ts:228). Nothing serializes a resolved definition — `AgentSetting` is re-derived from YAML on every load and on resume (`buildAgentLaunchContext({config: resume.agentConfig})`, src/agent/runtime/executeAgent.ts:555), and `kv.writeRunRecord` persists `AgentConfig`, not `AgentSetting`, so `zodSchema` is never lost across a boundary.

Step (B) also generates its own machinery: because materialization happens BEFORE the YAML inheritance merge (src/agent/runtime/agentLoad.ts:160-185 merges a parent whose tools are already resolved with a child whose tools are still raw strings), `resolveToolDefinitions` needs `overridesContract` + `differsFrom` + a `fast-json-stable-stringify` deep compare (registry.ts:211-230) purely to tell "inherited copy of the registry's own contract" from "author trying to override the contract" — a distinction that only drives a warning. Precedent that names work fine as the stored form: src/agent/index/agentYamlScanner.ts:243 and src/agent/index/inlineAgents.ts:148 already run `extractToolNames(rawSettings.tools)` off raw settings, and src/agent/prompt/userVars.ts:577 and ToolUsePrepareNode.ts:35 only ever read `.name`.

Checked for prior rulings: `parameters`, `toToolParameters`, `resolveToolDefinitions` and `defineTool` do not appear as candidates in either survey (.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md, .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-26-simplification-survey-round2.md — the only define.ts hit is the shipped `hosts` -> `unavailableHosts` collapse at round2:1666). `git log` on src/agent/runtime/agentSettingTools.ts shows it was extracted for loader sharing (#10475, #9477), never argued as the right materialization point.

**Proposal**

Owner: `getDefaultToolRegistry()`, consulted once, at run time. Migration, sequenced AFTER finding 1 (while the reflection flow still hands `setting.tools` straight to the provider, the settings copy must stay materialized): (1) keep `AgentSettingBaseSchema.tools` as the `string | ToolDefinition` union (`AgentToolInputSchema` already exists, src/agent/core/definition/AgentDataclass.ts:35) instead of collapsing it to `ToolDefinitionSchema` (:28); (2) in `resolveAgentTools`, replace `const def = typeof config === 'string' ? {name: config} : config` (agentToolResolution.ts:129) with `registry.get(name)?.definition ?? config`, which is the same object the load-time pass was inserting, and keeps the existing "declared tool not found in registry" warning as the single typo report; (3) delete `resolveAgentSettingTools` (47 lines) and its three call sites in agentLoad.ts plus the remote loader's; (4) delete `resolveToolDefinitions`, `overridesContract`, `differsFrom`, and `VALID_TOOL_NAME` from registry.ts (~60 lines) — the contract-override protection they warned about becomes structural, since the run-time resolver always takes the registry's own definition and a YAML description can never win; (5) delete `toToolParameters` and the `parameters:` write in define.ts (~8 lines), leaving Zod as the single schema representation on registered tools and `parameters` on the schema purely for definitions that arrive without one. Test churn: src/test-kernel/tools/ToolRegistryAliases.vitest.ts (107 lines) loses its subject; three suites that read `definition.parameters.properties` (ClaudeAgentSchema.vitest.ts:9-12, DelegationHeadless.vitest.ts:1259-1263, agentLoad.vitest.ts:433-462) switch to converting `zodSchema`. Honest net: ~-115 production LoC, roughly -215 with the tests, -6 exported symbols. Keep the workflow-category "tools are inert" warning by moving it into the loader beside the category check.

**What we give up**

Two things. (a) YAML tool-name typos move from one load-time warning on the loader's channel to one warning per run resolution — same message, different channel and cadence. (b) The eager `toJSONSchema` call currently doubles as a startup smoke test that every registered tool's schema is convertible; after this, an unconvertible schema surfaces on the first request that includes that tool. Both are recoverable: (a) is a one-line log-channel choice, (b) can be pinned by a single registry-wide conversion assertion in the existing tool test if anyone wants it.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. WRONG: "src/test-kernel/tools/ToolRegistryAliases.vitest.ts (107 lines) loses its subject." Its FIRST test (lines 9-26) covers `getDefaultUnavailableToolNames`, a different export that survives. Five of six tests go; the file shrinks to ~26 lines. Test deletion there is ~-82, not -107.

2. MISSED TEST SUITE. The churn list omits src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/ToolConversion.vitest.ts entirely, which is hit three ways: `resolveToolDefinitions` at :785 and :804 (must become `registry.get(n).definition`), and — worse — `expectDescribedParameters(definition.name, definition.parameters, paths)` at :408, where `definition` is a live `new WebSearchTool().definition`. Removing the `parameters:` write makes that argument `undefined` and breaks a six-tool description-coverage assertion; it has to be rewritten to convert `zodSchema` first. Also src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/agentLoad.vitest.ts:462 asserts `settings.tools[0]?.parameters` — that block pins the deleted load-time behavior and must be deleted, not converted. Five suites, not four.

3. STEP (1) IS THE WRONG CHOICE and its cost is uncounted. Widening `AgentSettingBaseSchema.tools` (AgentDataclass.ts:28) to the `string | ToolDefinition` union erodes a type invariant three sites consume for free: ToolUsePrepareNode.ts:35 (`.map(t => t.name)`), ToolUseCycleNode.ts:80-81 (`tools[0]?.name`), and ModelInvocationNode.ts:707-709, which passes `services.setting.tools` straight into `createResponse({tools: ToolDefinition[]})`. At runtime those three read the ALREADY-resolved list (runToolUseFlow.ts:241 sets `setting: {...setting, tools: resolvedTools}`), so the union is a type lie that forces new narrowed service types — added elements, exactly what §14 R5 penalizes. Cheaper equivalent: keep the base schema as `z.array(ToolDefinitionSchema)` and have the load step normalize names with one line, `tools: settings.tools.map(t => typeof t === 'string' ? {name: t} : t)`. Same deletion of `resolveToolDefinitions`/`overridesContract`/`toToolParameters`, zero downstream branching, zero new types. `resolveAgentTools` already tolerates either form.
