4. UNDERCOUNTED RELOCATION. The workflow-category "tools are inert" warning currently fires from `resolveAgentSettingTools`, which BOTH loaders call. "Move it into the loader beside the category check" covers agentLoad.ts only; the inline path (agentLoad.ts:118) and `loadRemoteAgent` (RemoteAgentLoader.ts:66) lose it unless duplicated. Budget +15-25 LoC, or accept the loss and say so.

5. MINOR TRACE ERRORS. `agentLoad.ts:57,118,184` — the third site is :180. `resolveAgentTools` has exactly ONE production caller (runToolUseFlow.ts:180), not a general run-time pass; the reflection flow reaches the provider through `responseCycleToolsForModel` (ResponseCycleFlow.ts:207-225, wired at :626 as the only `getTools`), which duplicates the same gates and never annotates delegation availability. The finding is right that this blocks the change, and right to sequence behind finding 1 — but it should state plainly that if finding 1 is refuted, this finding must absorb the reflection-path fix itself or not ship at all.

6. ADD A DEDUPE GUARD. `resolveToolDefinitions` dedupes by name (`seenNames`); `resolveAgentTools` keeps `resolvedNames` but only consults it for injections, not for declared configs. A YAML listing a tool twice would emit it twice to the provider after the move. +2 LoC.

7. UNSTATED WIN worth claiming: after the change `RemoteAgentLoader.ts` no longer imports `@tools/registry` at all, so its six-line module-closure warning comment (lines 1-7) can go too, and the `@agent/remote` -> `@tools` architecture edge disappears.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The load-bearing claim is verifiable and true: every one of the 53 registered tools is built by `defineTool`, so every registered definition carries `zodSchema`; both provider converters prefer `zodSchema` and fall back to `parameters` only for definitions that have none; and a repo-wide grep confirms the only non-test reads of `ToolDefinition.parameters` are those two fallback lines plus `overridesContract`'s comparison. The eager `toJSONSchema` at construction is therefore duplicated work whose only surviving function is a startup smoke test, which the finding honestly discloses.

The load-time materialization pass is likewise redundant for the one flow that actually resolves tools at run time: `resolveToolDefinitions` returns `tool.definition` and `getDefaultTools()` builds singletons once, so the object the load-time pass inserts is object-identical to what `registry.get(name).definition` returns. Step (2) is behavior-preserving for the tool-use path. And because `mergeInheritedAgentObject` replaces arrays rather than concatenating them, the entire `overridesContract`/`differsFrom`/`fast-json-stable-stringify` apparatus exists only to keep quiet about a child that omitted `tools:` and inherited its parent's already-resolved array — a problem that does not exist if names stay names.

Nothing crosses a frozen boundary: no host wire carries `ToolDefinition`, `AgentConfig` (the persisted `RunRecord` arm) has no `tools` field, resume rebuilds settings from YAML, and no shipped agent YAML uses object form.

What keeps this at medium rather than low risk is two things the finding treats too lightly. First, it is gated on a sibling finding: while `responseCycleToolsForModel` reads `setting.tools` directly, de-materializing the settings copy would send bare names to the provider for reflection agents and would let an untrusted remote YAML's fabricated description reach the model. Second, the proposed step (1) widens a type that three flow sites depend on, and step (4) silently drops a warning that a dated owner-approved ruling deliberately made loud. Both are fixable within the same PR — normalize to `{name}` instead of widening, and keep the override compare at the new run-time site — but they cost roughly a fifth of the claimed win.

Honest accounting. Production: define.ts -11, registry.ts -75 (VALID_TOOL_NAME, RawToolConfig, differsFrom, overridesContract, resolveToolDefinitions and their doc blocks, the stableStringify import), agentSettingTools.ts 47 -> ~20 for -27, RemoteAgentLoader/agentLoad -8, minus +16 to retain the override warning, +15 to relocate the workflow warning to two sites, +4 in agentToolResolution for name extraction and dedupe. Net production about -95, versus the claimed -115. Tests: -82 (ToolRegistryAliases), -25 (agentLoad.vitest parameters block), +10 (ToolConversion rewrite), +8 (ClaudeAgentSchema, DelegationHeadless), net about -89. Total about -184 against the claimed -215. Elements removed: resolveToolDefinitions, resolveAgentSettingTools (whole module), toToolParameters, overridesContract, differsFrom, RawToolConfig, VALID_TOOL_NAME = 7, minus at most one small normalizer helper if extracted, so -6 net. The claim's element count is right; its LoC is about 20 percent optimistic in both columns.

</details>

### flow-families — The two flow families

#### Delete the tool-use round-state trio: roundIndex mirrors run.totalRounds, and the two accumulators are single-assignment locals

- **Kind**: delete · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -55 LoC, -4 elements (proposer claimed -36; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/toolUseRound/roundShared.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/toolUseRound/ToolUseProcessNode.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/toolUseRound/ToolUseRoundPrepNode.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/nodes/ToolUseCycleNode.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/ToolUseRoundFlow.ts`

**Evidence**

ToolUseRoundShared declares roundIndex, roundResponseTimeMs, roundNormalizedUsage (roundShared.ts:41,46,51) with a docstring saying it 'uses flat structure (like ResponseCycleFlow) for consistency' — i.e. copied shape. All three are provably redundant.
(a) roundIndex is seeded from run.totalRounds (ToolUseCycleNode.ts:90) and incremented in advanceRound (ToolUseProcessNode.ts:55); run.totalRounds is incremented unconditionally in the same post() at ToolUseProcessNode.ts:207, before the branch that may or may not call advanceRound. On CONTINUE/DEFAULT both increment; on COMPLETE the round ends and roundIndex is never read again, and the next cycle re-seeds it from run.totalRounds. Its only two readers are debug-file naming (ToolUseRoundFlow.ts:68, ToolUseRoundPrepNode.ts:92), both evaluated before ToolUseProcessNode.post runs, so run.totalRounds is equal at every read. It is a mirror, not a second fact.
(b) roundResponseTimeMs has exactly one writer (ToolUseProcessNode.ts:195, `+=` onto a value reset to 0) and one reader three lines later (:203). It is reset twice per round — advanceRound (:56) and again in ToolUseRoundPrepNode.ts:89, which always runs next because every advanceRound is followed by an edge back to prepNode (ToolUseRoundFlow.ts:78-79). So its value at the read is always `shared.responseTimeMs ?? 0`, which ModelInvocationNode.ts:796 just wrote.
(c) roundNormalizedUsage is written at :198 from execRes.normalizedUsage and read at :204, with the same double reset (:57 and ToolUseRoundPrepNode.ts:87). Always equal to execRes.normalizedUsage.
None of this is persisted: ToolUseRoundShared belongs to the inner Flow created in ToolUseCycleNode.exec and discarded; ToolUseRoundFieldsSchema is never `.parse`d anywhere (grep across src and packages returns only its own z.infer). Contrast reflection, where the same three facts live in ConversationRoundStateSnapshot (core/state/AgentState.ts:11-16) and genuinely accumulate across continuations before one recordRound at ResponseCycleFlow.ts:442 — that is the real difference between the families. No wire, no settings key, no CI contract: these names appear nowhere in packages/cli, packages/desktop, packages/extension, or any NDJSON payload.

**Proposal**

Owner: the model call's own result plus AgentRunStateSnapshot.totalRounds. Delete all three fields from ToolUseRoundFieldsSchema; replace ToolUseProcessNode.ts:194-205 with one call `recordCycleMetrics(run, shared.responseTimeMs ?? 0, execRes.normalizedUsage ?? null)`; delete advanceRound (ToolUseProcessNode.ts:53-58) and its three call sites (:231, :268, :280); drop `roundResponseTimeMs = 0` and the 'roundNormalizedUsage' reset key from ToolUseRoundPrepNode.ts:87-89; drop the two initializers in ToolUseCycleNode.ts:90-91; point the two debug-file sites (ToolUseRoundFlow.ts:68, ToolUseRoundPrepNode.ts:92) at `services.run.totalRounds`, which ToolUseRoundServices already carries via CycleRunServices. Debug filenames are byte-identical before and after. Five test files (BashTool, ToolUseRoundFollowUpMedia, ToolUseDispatchInterruption, FinalToolSynthesis, ToolUseProcessNodeResponseFinalized) drop 13 lines of literal `roundIndex: 0` setup — extend those suites, do not add a new one.

**What we give up**

Nothing. The only capability that could be lost is per-round debug-file uniqueness, and that is preserved by reading the counter the trio was mirroring. If a future change makes a round call ToolUseProcessNode.post twice, the accumulator semantics would have to come back — but no such edge exists in the graph today.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. "run.totalRounds is incremented unconditionally in the same post()" is overstated. The `execRes.kind === 'skipped'` early return at ToolUseProcessNode.ts:178-180 bypasses :207 entirely. Harmless — roundIndex is not advanced on that path either and the returned COMPLETE ends the flow — but the implementer should not read ":207 always runs" as a license to reorder it.

2. LoC is understated by roughly a third. Honest count is ~-55, not -36: roundShared.ts -18, ToolUseProcessNode.ts -16, ToolUseRoundPrepNode.ts -6, ToolUseCycleNode.ts -2, tests -13.

3. The claim misses that deleting roundNormalizedUsage also orphans the `NormalizedUsageSchema` import at roundShared.ts:10 — it has no other use in that file, so the import line must go too or lint/knip will flag it.

4. The claim misses that removing `'roundNormalizedUsage'` from the resetCycleState array is compile-forced, not optional: `resetCycleState(state: T, additionalFields: (keyof T)[])` (CommonCycleTypes.ts:38-40) types the array as `keyof ToolUseRoundShared`, so the stale key becomes a type error. That is a good thing — it means no silent miss — but it also means the array collapses from 6 formatted lines to 1, which is where the extra LoC the claim didn't count comes from.

5. The debug-site rewrite needs a two-parameter callback, not a services-only closure. `getDebugFileOptions` is declared `(shared: TShared, services: TServices) => CycleDebugFileOptions` (ModelInvocationNode.ts:153-156) and invoked with both (`:800-803`), so ToolUseRoundFlow.ts:67 becomes `(_shared, services) => ({ continuationCount: services.run.totalRounds, baseName: 'tooluse_response' })` — the underscore is required or no-unused-vars fires. In ToolUseRoundPrepNode.ts:92 it is `this.services.run.totalRounds`. The claim is right that `run` is already on the services object (`CycleServices.ts:21 readonly run: AgentRunStateSnapshot` on `CycleRunServices`, which `ToolUseRoundServices` extends at :38).

6. "Five test files drop 13 lines" is right on the count but not on the compile pressure. Four of them (BashTool.vitest.ts:203-205, ToolUseRoundFollowUpMedia.vitest.ts:50-52, ToolUseDispatchInterruption.vitest.ts:117-119, FinalToolSynthesis.vitest.ts:53-54) are typed object literals, so excess-property checking forces the edit. The fifth, ToolUseProcessNodeResponseFinalized.vitest.ts:44-45, sits inside an `as unknown as ToolUseRoundShared` cast (:46) and will compile untouched — those 2 lines are cleanup the implementer must remember, not a compiler-caught one. A sixth file, ToolUseRoundPrepNodeFollowUpTranscriptLog.vitest.ts:33, uses the same cast and needs no edit at all.

7. Unmentioned doc cost (0 net LoC but do it): the ToolUseRoundShared docstring at roundShared.ts:63-64 says "Uses flat structure (like ResponseCycleFlow) for consistency". That copied-shape rationale is precisely what the change repudiates, so the comment should be amended rather than left standing as a claim the code no longer supports.

8. Sharpen the residual risk the claim already gestures at. After the change `run.totalRounds` is the single counter feeding both the round count and the debug filename. Today there is exactly one write site for it. The invariant a future edit must not break is not "no round calls post() twice" (the claim's framing) but the narrower and more checkable "run.totalRounds has exactly one increment site, and it stays after both debug-file reads." Worth a one-line comment at ToolUseProcessNode.ts:207.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

This is the rare round-3 architectural claim whose central factual assertions I could verify end to end rather than take on trust, and every one of them held.

The load-bearing proof is object identity plus graph reachability, and both are checkable in a few minutes. `ToolUseCycleNode.ts:90` seeds `roundIndex` from `prepRes.runState.totalRounds` and `:105` hands that same `prepRes.runState` object to the flow as `services.run` — not a copy, the same reference. `run.totalRounds` then has exactly one write in the entire tool-use path (`ToolUseProcessNode.ts:207`), which I confirmed by grepping every `totalRounds` occurrence in `src` and `packages` and finding that the only other writers live in the reflection family. Meanwhile the round flow's edge set makes `processNode` reachable only via `callNode`, and `callNode` only via `prepNode`, so both reads of `roundIndex` are guaranteed to precede the one place the two counters could diverge. That is a mirror by construction, not a coincidence that happens to hold today.

The two accumulators fall to the same reachability fact. `ModelInvocationNode.ts:796` assigns rather than accumulates `responseTimeMs` (I checked this specifically, because if the p-retry loop had been summing across attempts the `+=` at :195 would have had real semantics), and `prepNode.post` zeroes the accumulator before every model call. So the `+=` is an `=` and the read three lines later is the value just written.

The kill checks that usually land at round 3 all came up empty for good reasons rather than by luck. The frozen-wire check (5) is decisively closed: `ToolUseRoundFieldsSchema` is a module-private const that is never `.parse`d, existing purely to feed `z.infer`, and the persisted tool-use state is a different schema (`ToolUseRunShared`) that carries `totalRounds` but not `roundIndex`. The dated-ruling check (2) came up empty against a targeted grep of docs/, AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md and .claude/ — the five files that mention these symbols discuss a deleted re-export shim and praise the type for being Zod-schematized; neither defends the fields. And `advanceRound` traces to #10475, where it was a mechanical dedupe of three repeated inline resets, which is the opposite of a design decision worth preserving.

The claim's own contrast with the reflection family is what convinced me the deletion is principled rather than opportunistic, so I verified it independently: `ResponseCycleFlow.ts:356` really does accumulate onto a services-level `round` across multiple ProcessNode runs, drained once at a single finalization node whose docstring states that every exit path routes through it. Tool-use has no such fan-in. So the two families are not gratuitously divergent — reflection needs the accumulator and tool-use copied its shape without inheriting its reason. Deleting the copy makes the genuine difference between the families legible instead of hiding it behind superficially matching field names.

On cost I recounted every hunk myself rather than accept the claim's number, and it is understated by about a third — the claim missed an orphaned import and did not notice that the `resetCycleState` array collapses from six formatted lines to one. Understating a reduction is a harmless error, and the migration has no tail: five production files, five test files, one subsystem, no call sites outside the flow directory.

I graded this low risk. The behavior-identity argument is mechanical, the compiler catches most of the edit (the `keyof T` typing of `resetCycleState`'s additionalFields turns the stale key into a type error), and debug filenames come out byte-identical. My corrections are mostly implementation detail — the two-parameter callback signature, the one test file whose `as unknown as` cast means the compiler will not flag it, the orphaned import — plus one framing fix I think matters: the invariant a future change must not break is "one increment site for run.totalRounds, placed after both debug reads", which is narrower and far easier to check than the claim's "no round calls post() twice."

</details>

#### The reflection checkpoint stores the message array twice: `conversation` is an alias of `context.messages`

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -8 LoC, -1 elements (proposer claimed -4; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/agent/implementations/flows/reflection/ReflectionFlowState.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/reflection/nodes/PrepareContextNode.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/reflection/nodes/ResponseCycleNode.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/reflection/runReflectionFlow.ts`

**Evidence**

ReflectionFlowStateSchema persists both `context: {messages, stateRoundSnapshot}` (ReflectionFlowState.ts:18-30) and `conversation: ProviderMessageArraySchema` (:33). They hold the same array object, not merely equal data. PrepareContextNode.exec builds `messages` from shared.conversation (PrepareContextNode.ts:26, :57-61) and stores it at shared.context.messages (:71-82). ResponseCycleNode.exec passes context.messages into modelCell.handler.initializeOutputAndPrefill (ResponseCycleNode.ts:71-77), whose single implementation (modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts:1520, no overrides — grep returns one definition) mutates and returns the same array; the inner cycle then appends to that same array via updateMessageContent/addContinueMessage and compaction's replaceMessagesInPlace (CommonCycleTypes.ts:79). ResponseCycleNode.post:185 then assigns `shared.conversation = prepRes.context.messages` — a self-assignment of the reference. PersistedFlow structuredClones the shared on every step write, so each checkpoint serializes the full provider message list twice. For a reflection run over a large LaTeX document that is the dominant term in the checkpoint, paid on every node boundary. The only reader of `conversation` is PrepareContextNode.prep at the start of the next round, and `shared.context.messages` holds exactly that value at that moment (nothing resets `context` between rounds; RoundPersistedFlow's resetForNextRound only clears workspaceSnapshot, runReflectionFlow.ts:250-252). The cancel path (ResponseCycleNode.ts:170-177) returns before :185, but it also ends the run and resumes on the same cursor, so the divergence is unreachable.

**Proposal**

Owner: RoundContext.messages. Change PrepareContextNode.prep to read `shared.context?.messages ?? []`, delete the assignment at ResponseCycleNode.ts:185, and drop `conversation` from ReflectionFlowStateSchema and from the fresh-state literal at runReflectionFlow.ts:213. Persisted-file discipline applies: an older build's ReflectionFlowStateSchema requires `conversation`, so a record written by the new build cannot be resumed by an older one. Ship it as `.optional()` with a deprecation comment for one minor release and remove the field one release later, the same deprecate-then-delete clock the repo uses for the frozen NDJSON vocabulary — do not delete outright in one PR. Existing records parse unchanged either way, since z.object strips the extra key.

**What we give up**

The ability to resume a new-build reflection checkpoint on an older build during the one-release deprecation window, and a redundant copy that would let a corrupted `context` be reconstructed — a recovery path no code implements today.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. WRONG MECHANISM FOR THE DOUBLING. "PersistedFlow structuredClones the shared on every step write, so each checkpoint serializes the full provider message list twice" misattributes the cost. `structuredClone` (persistedFlow.ts:330) preserves intra-graph reference identity, so the in-memory clone holds ONE array reachable from two keys. The doubling happens one layer down, at `KVStore.write` → JSON.stringify (StorageFSKVStore, src/agent/storage/ExecutionKVStore.ts:183-193, compactJson), which has no reference dedupe. The on-disk conclusion is correct and the per-node-boundary framing is correct; the causal chain in the evidence is not.

2. THE ALIAS IS NOT UNIVERSAL. "They hold the same array object" holds continuously only for handlers whose `createRoundMessages` mutates in place — Anthropic (modelHandlerAnthropic.ts:1085, `messages.push`), OpenAI Responses (:1186), OpenAI/OpenRouter via `createChatRoundMessages`, Google Interactions (:970). `ModelHandlerVscodeLm.createRoundMessages` (modelHandlerVscodeLm.ts:320-333) returns `[...messages, {...}]`, so from PrepareContextNode.post until ResponseCycleNode.post the two keys hold DIFFERENT arrays there. Additionally, after any resume the Zod parse in `readShared`/`safeParse` de-aliases them unconditionally. Neither breaks the proposal — what the proposal actually needs is content-equality at the single read point (PrepareContextNode.prep), which I verified holds on every reachable path — but the reference-identity framing should not be the PR's load-bearing argument.

3. THE DEPRECATION WINDOW IS BOTH INEFFECTIVE AND RULED AGAINST. Shipping `conversation` as `.optional()` for one minor release buys none of the compatibility it claims: an older build's `ReflectionFlowStateSchema` requires the key to be PRESENT in the record, and the proposal simultaneously deletes ResponseCycleNode.ts:185 and the fresh-state literal at runReflectionFlow.ts:213, so the new build stops WRITING it immediately. Old-build resume breaks on day one regardless of what the new schema permits. A genuine deprecate-then-delete would have to keep the writer for a release — and AGENTS.md "Compatibility and format retirement" rules against adding one: "TeXRA has a short compatibility window. Do not preserve an old internal format indefinitely merely because a parser or migration already exists… Breaking an old internal format is acceptable when no current public contract or demonstrated user need justifies the additional system." The flow record is an internal format with no public contract. Delete outright in one PR; state the downgrade-resume consequence in the PR body instead of building a writer for it.

4. STALE DOCSTRING NOT ACCOUNTED FOR. ReflectionFlowState.ts:44 reads "Provider-message format used by the persisted conversation" and documents `modelHandlerCompatibilityKey`. Removing `conversation` leaves it a dangling reference; it must be reworded to name `context.messages`, which becomes the sole carrier the key describes.

5. TEST IMPACT UNDERSTATED. Four test sites hold the literal, not zero: src/test-kernel/agent/progressTestUtils.ts:137, ReflectionFlowStateRecovery.vitest.ts:132 and :209, and SessionResumeRetrieval.vitest.ts:536. The last one is inside a test titled "retrieves a workflow record with the current conversation field" (:528) whose entire subject disappears; it must be renamed, not just edited. I checked the sibling test it contrasts with ("rejects a workflow record that only has the retired messages field", :544): that record still fails on `workspaceSnapshot`, `context`, `outputLocation`, `runStateSnapshot`, `roundOutputs`, `continueRounds` and `endTurn`, so dropping one required key does not weaken it.

6. THE "WHAT WE GIVE UP" OVERSTATES THE LOST BACKUP — in the finding's own favor. For every push-based handler, PrepareContextNode calls `createRoundMessages(shared.conversation, …)`, which mutates the LIVE conversation array in place and hands it back as `context.messages`. So `conversation` is not an independent copy that could reconstruct a corrupted `context`; it is the very array the cycle mutates. The only thing actually given up is downgrade-resume.

7. LINE-NUMBER NITS. `RoundContextSchema` is defined at ReflectionFlowState.ts:18-21 and the `context:` field is at :30 (the cited "18-30" spans both); PrepareContextNode.exec returns the context at :71-74 and post assigns `shared.context` at :82 (the cited ":71-82" merges the two); `initializeOutputAndPrefill` begins at ModelHandler.ts:1519, not :1520. All immaterial.

8. UNCITED SUPPORTING EVIDENCE the PR should carry, since a reviewer will ask: the failure path is provably not a re-entry hazard because `FINALIZE` is in `TERMINAL_ACTIONS` (src/agent/node/index.ts:18) → `nextNodeId: null` → `src/agent/storage/resumability.ts:28` rejects the record as unresumable; and no resumable record can carry `context: null` with a populated `conversation`, because the replay cursor only exists since bb8eba865e (2026-07-06) and `resetForNextRound` has cleared only `workspaceSnapshot` throughout that window.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The core claim is true and the trace survives an independent end-to-end walk. `conversation` has exactly one reader in the entire repo (PrepareContextNode.ts:26) and exactly two writers (runReflectionFlow.ts:213 initializing it empty, ResponseCycleNode.ts:185 pointing it back at the array the cycle just finished mutating). At the single moment it is read — the top of round N — `shared.context.messages` provably holds the same content, because the only route back to PrepareContextNode is `transitionToNextRound`/`rewindToStart`, which runs strictly after ResponseCycleNode.post has executed line 185; the cancel path suspends on the same node and re-reads `context`; and the failure path terminates the cursor, which `resumability.ts` refuses to resume. So the field is a pure mirror with a measurable cost: the checkpoint JSON carries the full provider message list twice, rewritten at every node boundary, which for a reflection run over a large LaTeX document is the dominant term.

The external-consumer check is the one that usually kills a claim like this at round 3, and I pushed on it hard because two round-2 items had to be reverted for exactly that. It does not kill this one. Every surface that renders a "conversation" for a completed run — the executions tool, `texra history`, chat export, the trace viewer — reads the transcript sidecar through `readCompletedRunConversation`, whose declared source type is `'streamLog' | 'none'`. The old flow-record importer was deliberately retired (#9590 Stage 7). Compatibility-key inference carries an in-code note that it does not parse the persisted messages. The write-through projection that the PersistedFlow docstring mentions ("derived views (todos, conversation)") belongs to the tool-use flow alone. Nothing crosses the NDJSON wire, a settings key, or the texra-action contract.

What is genuinely wrong with the finding is its ship plan, not its analysis. The `.optional()` deprecation window is a no-op for the compatibility it names — an old build needs the key written, not merely permitted — and AGENTS.md's "Compatibility and format retirement" section explicitly declines to add a compatibility writer for an internal format with no public contract. So the honest shape is one PR that deletes the field, the assignment, and the initializer, repoints PrepareContextNode at `shared.context?.messages ?? []`, fixes the now-dangling docstring at ReflectionFlowState.ts:44, and updates four test literals plus one test title. Downgrade-resume across the change is the single real loss and belongs in the PR body.

Cost accounting: production is -3 lines (schema field, ResponseCycleNode.ts:185, runReflectionFlow.ts:213; PrepareContextNode.ts:26 is a same-line edit), tests are about -5 (three bare literals plus the SessionResumeRetrieval case's field line and retitle). Call it -8 LoC and -1 element (one persisted schema field, and with it one of the two owners of the round message array). The claimed -4 is in the right direction but under-counts tests and would have been a net add under its own deprecation plan. Risk stays medium: the change is small and provable, but it edits authored-tier persisted state on the workflow resume path, so it wants a real resume dogfood (interrupt a multi-round reflection run mid-cycle, resume, confirm round N+1 still sees prior turns) rather than a unit test alone.

</details>

### progress-rendering — Progress rendering across four hosts

#### One owner for stream-removal barrier settlement: the fact path and the command path answer the same question twice, and disagree on `undefined`

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -18 LoC, 0 elements (proposer claimed -41; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/controllers/session/SessionFactApplier.ts`, `src/controllers/progressView/backend/ProgressBackend.ts`

**Evidence**

`SessionFactApplier` owns provisional removal barriers: on removal it captures an incarnation, buffers facts that race the host delete, and on settlement either retires the barrier (replaying the buffer) or commits the tombstone (discarding it). That single concept has TWO implementations that grew apart in two different PRs.

Fact path — `case 'removeStream'` (SessionFactApplier.ts:355-441). Begin: `beginStreamRemoval` → `registeredWithRenderer.delete` → `beginPendingDeletion` → `notifyRosterParents` (:392-398). Settle: the inline `settleDeletion` closure (:382-421) computes `retained = outcome === 'active' || outcome === 'failed'`, retires when `retained || outcome === 'superseded'`, commits when `!retained && outcome !== 'superseded'`, notifies roster parents, replays when `retained && retirement.retired`. Rejection arm (:437-443): drop the buffer, keep the barrier.

Command path — `beginCommandRemoval` (:559-576), `completeCommandRemoval` (:578-611), `abortCommandRemoval` (:613-627). Begin: the same four steps in the same order. Settle: `retained = outcome === 'active' || outcome === 'failed' || outcome === undefined`, retires when `outcome !== 'deleted'`, commits when `outcome === 'deleted'`, notifies, replays when `retirement.retired && retained && pending`. `abortCommandRemoval`'s own doc comment concedes the duplication verbatim: 'exactly as the fact path does on a rejected host delete' (:615-618).

The two settle bodies agree on `'deleted'`, `'active'`, `'failed'` and `'superseded'` and DISAGREE on `undefined`: the fact path COMMITS the tombstone (SessionRendererPort's headless host relies on this — packages/cli/src/runtime/runProgressRenderer.ts:159-163 `deleteStream: () => undefined` with the comment 'reporting nothing keeps the removal barrier'), while the command path RETIRES it (:582-586 'anything the command path reports as `undefined` … retires the barrier because nothing was deleted'). Both policies are deliberate and documented — but the same value means opposite things in two functions 200 lines apart, which is precisely the drift a single owner prevents.

Provenance: `git log -S beginCommandRemoval -- src/controllers/session/SessionFactApplier.ts` → 4315719865 'fix(session): unify stream removal and metadata reads (#10805)'. `git log -S settleDeletion` → 02010a5b62 'fix(session): harden stream removal ownership (#10839)'. Two PRs, two hardenings, one concept.

Callers of the command path: exactly one, `ProgressBackend.deleteStream` (src/controllers/progressView/backend/ProgressBackend.ts:456-459, :528-534, :508-514).

Dedupe: `grep beginCommandRemoval|completeCommandRemoval` over both survey docs = 0. Not in rounds12-index.md. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "removal barrier tombstone"` returns nothing. .agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-08-15-single-substrate-hosts-as-renderers.md §4 promoted the tombstone into `SessionState` (LANDED #10805) but says nothing about the applier's two settlement paths — this code is downstream of that promotion, not covered by it.

Settled surfaces: none engaged. No ratchet, no wire format, no persisted file, no settings key, no `@agent/*` edge. `SessionState._removedStreams` and `clearStream` are untouched; this is purely the applier's barrier bookkeeping above them.

**Proposal**

Owner that absorbs it: one private `settleRemoval(streamId, pending, incarnation, outcome, policy)` on `SessionFactApplier`, where `policy` names the one real difference — `unreportedOutcome: 'retain' | 'commit'` (the fact path passes `'commit'`, the command path `'retain'`).

Migration: (1) extract the settle body once, taking `{ streamId, incarnation, pending, outcome, unreportedOutcome }` and doing finish-buffer → retire/commit → `notifyRosterParents` → conditional replay. (2) The fact path's `settleDeletion` closure keeps only its `created`/`settled` re-entry latch and calls it. (3) `completeCommandRemoval` becomes a `created` guard plus the same call. (4) `abortCommandRemoval` and the fact path's rejection arm both become one `discardRemovalBuffer(streamId, incarnation)`. (5) `beginCommandRemoval` and the fact path's begin block both call one `beginRemoval(streamId)` returning `{ incarnation, created, pending }`.

Honest accounting: deletes ≈ 104 LoC of duplicated settle/abort/begin logic (:382-421 = 40, :578-611 = 34, :613-627 = 15, begin duplication ≈ 15), adds ≈ 63 (one settle ≈ 35, two thin call sites ≈ 20, one discard ≈ 8). Net ≈ −41 LoC; elements net −1 (`abortCommandRemoval` folds, `settleRemoval` and `discardRemovalBuffer` appear, `completeCommandRemoval` stays as the public command-path entry). The real payoff is not LoC: it is that the `undefined` divergence becomes one named parameter at two call sites instead of two comment blocks in two functions.

Why the risk is medium and not low: this code arbitrates tombstone commitment and stale-fact replay, and #10839 exists because the first version got it wrong. The PR must keep the existing `src/test-kernel/progressView/ProgressBackendCleanup.vitest.ts` and `ProgressBackendStreamLifecycle.vitest.ts` suites green without editing their assertions — they are the parity harness, and any assertion change is a signal the collapse changed behavior.

**What we give up**

Nothing behavioural, if the `unreportedOutcome` parameter is threaded correctly. What is given up is the ability for the two paths to drift further independently — a real cost only if a future removal source needs a third settlement policy, which the parameter already accommodates. The risk is execution risk, not lost capability: a mis-threaded policy silently resurrects a deleted stream or freezes a live one, and neither failure is loud.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

C1 — LINE NUMBERS DRIFTED (rebase before citing). ProgressBackend call sites are `beginCommandRemoval` at :464 (not :456-459), `abortCommandRemoval` at :506-510 (not :508-514), `completeCommandRemoval` at :523-528 (not :528-534). The CLI headless option is runProgressRenderer.ts:166, not :159-163. `let settled` is :381 and the closure is :382-421 (finding said the closure is :382-421 — correct); the rejection arm's guard is :437-439 inside the `.then` rejection handler ending :441.

C2 — THE BEGIN BLOCKS ARE **NOT** "the same four steps in the same order", and step (5) of the migration is unsafe as written. The two paths compute `created` from DIFFERENT sources. The fact path destructures only `{ incarnation, changedRosterParents }` from `state.beginStreamRemoval` and takes its `created` from `beginPendingDeletion` (:374-377), i.e. "did I create the pending BUFFER". The command path takes `created` straight from `state.beginStreamRemoval` (:565-568), i.e. "did I create the TOMBSTONE", and only then conditionally calls `beginPendingDeletion`. These diverge in a reachable state: after a rejected host delete the barrier is kept but the buffer is dropped (:437-439), so a subsequent removeStream fact gets state.created=false + buffer.created=true (fact path proceeds as owner), while a subsequent command gets state.created=false and ProgressBackend returns `'superseded'` without deleting (:465). A single `beginRemoval` returning one `created` would silently change one of those two behaviors. The PR must keep two begin shapes (or return BOTH booleans), which erases most of the claimed ~15 LoC of "begin duplication" savings.

C3 — SECOND UNSTATED DIFFERENCE: buffer matching. The fact path captures the `pending` OBJECT and relies on `finishPendingDeletion`'s identity check (`this.pendingDeletions.get(streamId) === pending`, :505). The command path re-looks-up by `streamId` and matches on `pending.incarnation === incarnation` (:580-583, :619-622). Incarnation is NOT bumped by `beginStreamRemoval` (SessionState.ts:453 reuses `incarnationOf`; only `claimStreamIdentity` bumps at :476), so two distinct PendingDeletion objects for the same stream can carry the SAME incarnation after a rejected delete. An extracted helper keyed on incarnation is therefore not equivalent to the fact path's identity capture and could drop a successor barrier's buffer. The shared helper must take the `pending` object (fact path) or look it up and compare identity, not incarnation — this is the concrete execution hazard behind the medium risk, and the finding does not name it.

C4 — ELEMENT ARITHMETIC AS WRITTEN IS +1, NOT -1. The finding's own list ("abortCommandRemoval folds, settleRemoval and discardRemovalBuffer appear, completeCommandRemoval stays") is -1+2 = +1. It only reaches 0 if `discardRemovalBuffer` absorbs `finishPendingDeletion` (2 private methods → 1). Honest figure is 0.

C5 — CHEAPER VARIANT WORTH PROPOSING INSTEAD. Since the only real difference is one value, the fact path's closure can call the EXISTING `completeCommandRemoval`/`abortCommandRemoval` (renamed to `settleRemoval`/`discardRemovalBuffer`) with `unreportedOutcome: 'commit'` plus its captured `pending`, adding ZERO new elements and deleting the 40-line closure body outright. That is strictly better on both axes than extracting a third private method and leaving `completeCommandRemoval` as a forwarder.

C6 — ONE FRAMING NIT. "SessionRendererPort's headless host relies on this" is imprecise: `deleteStream` is a `SessionFactApplierOptions` field (SessionFactApplier.ts:88-96), not a `SessionRendererPort` method. Two hosts rely on the commit-on-unreported semantics, not one: runProgressRenderer.ts:166 (`() => undefined`) and sessionSignalsAdapter.ts:211 (returns void).

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The duplication is real, is in the most hazard-prone bookkeeping in the file, and the file itself documents it as duplication. The two settle bodies agree on 'deleted'/'active'/'failed'/'superseded' and diverge on exactly one value, and I confirmed that divergence is reachable from the SAME host through the same `ProgressBackend.deleteStream` (storage-generation change returns `undefined`), which is precisely the drift a single owner with a named policy parameter prevents. No ruling, no ratchet, no wire, no issue protects it, and the parity suites drive it only through public entry points so nothing has to be rewritten. What does not survive is the LoC figure: `SessionFactApplier` is a heavily-commented file, the ~25 lines of rationale on the fact path plus the ~15 on the command path must both be preserved and merged into the shared helper's doc comment, and C2 shows the begin-block unification (~15 of the claimed 104 deleted lines) is unsafe and must be dropped. Deleting ~90 lines of body and re-adding ~72 (a 45-55 line shared settle including merged rationale, a ~12-line fact call site with the re-entry latch, a ~10-line command call site, a ~10-line discard) gives about -18, and the C5 variant would push it toward -30 while keeping elements flat. Elements net 0, not -1, by the finding's own list. Risk stays medium and I would raise it slightly: C2 and C3 are two silent-behavior-change traps the finding did not identify, and both failure modes (a resurrected deleted stream, a frozen live one) are quiet, exactly as the finding concedes.

</details>

### webview-architecture — The three webview trees as an architecture

#### One owner for the webview theme: the DOM observer already derives it, so delete the extension-side THEME_SET pipeline and stop clobbering VS Code's body classes

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -215 LoC, -6 elements
- **Files**: `src/shared/BaseWebviewApp.ts`, `src/shared/wa/waColorScheme.ts`, `src/shared/wa/hostTheme.ts`, `packages/extension/src/progressView/ProgressViewProvider.ts`, `packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/slices/uiSlice.ts`, `packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/components/UserMessage.ts`, `src/controllers/progressView/backend/LitSessionRenderer.ts`, `src/controllers/progressView/backend/ProgressBackend.ts`, `src/test-kernel/progressView/ProgressThemeSync.vitest.ts`, `packages/extension/src/webview/MainViewMessageHandler.ts`, `packages/desktop/src/renderer/main.ts`, `packages/desktop/src/renderer/messageRoutes.ts`

**Evidence**

Two independent mechanisms own "is this webview dark". (A) The autonomous one: `src/shared/wa/index.ts:17-20` calls `applyInitialWaColorScheme()` at module load, and every one of the three frontend entry points imports `@shared/wa` on its first line (`packages/extension/src/webview/frontend/index.ts:2`, `progressView/frontend/index.ts:2`, `settingsView/frontend/index.ts:2`). That installs a MutationObserver (`src/shared/wa/waColorScheme.ts:107-127`) on `document.body` filtered to `['class','data-vscode-theme-kind']` which classifies through `isDarkTheme()` (`:62-83`) and keeps `<html class="wa-light|wa-dark">` in sync, including live host flips and a prefers-color-scheme fallback. VS Code sets both of those body signals itself, so this path needs no message at all. (B) The message one: `MainViewMessageHandler.ts:126-140` answers GET_THEME with THEME_SET; `ProgressViewProvider.ts:217-226` listens to `onDidChangeActiveColorTheme` and calls `renderer.setTheme(...)`; `ProgressViewProvider.ts:293-301` recomputes the theme on every `syncRenderedStreams` and threads it through `ProgressBackend.syncRenderedStreams({theme})` (`src/controllers/progressView/backend/ProgressBackend.ts:178-201`) into `LitSessionRenderer.sendStreamMetadata(projected, active, theme?)` (`LitSessionRenderer.ts:403-410`) and `setTheme` (`:522-528`). It lands at `BaseWebviewApp.onThemeChange` (`src/shared/BaseWebviewApp.ts:145-153`), which sets `this.theme`, writes `document.body.className = theme`, and calls `setWaColorScheme(themeIsDark(theme))` — i.e. re-does what (A) already did. Consumers of the value it produces: `themeContext` has exactly ONE production consumer repo-wide, `packages/desktop/src/renderer/TexraDiffView.ts:87-89` (monaco); grep for `this.theme`/`themeContext` across all three extension frontends returns zero hits (the only `theme` reader there is `TerminalOutput.ts:126` `resolveXtermTheme(this)`, which reads computed CSS custom properties, not this value). That answers open issue #11423 ("Verify whether themeContext is dead", 2026-08-25, area:webview): it is not dead, but its only consumer is a desktop component. Worse, (B) actively damages (A)'s substrate: `document.body.className = theme` assigns the bare kind ('dark'), deleting the `vscode-dark`/`vscode-high-contrast` class VS Code put there. The only `:host-context(.vscode-*)` rule in the repo, `packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/components/UserMessage.ts:169-170`, therefore cannot match in the progress webview after the first sync; and `ProgressViewProvider.ts:223-224` collapses ColorThemeKind to `'dark' | 'light'`, so high-contrast is reported as light. On desktop, one THEME_SET reaches FOUR body-class writers (three mounted BaseWebviewApp instances via `main.ts:55-56` importing all three frontends, plus `messageRoutes.ts:135` → `main.ts:1392` → `applyHostBodyTheme`), whose vocabularies conflict — `packages/desktop/src/renderer/themeTokens.css:542-553` keys off `body.vscode-light/.vscode-dark`, which the BaseWebviewApp write erases; correctness there depends on listener registration order. Dated checks: round 1 SHIPPED "Carry the parsed Theme through themeContext and delete the dead body-class re-sniff" — its verifier block (.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md:1629) explicitly relies on `document.body.className = theme` preceding the reads, i.e. it deleted the reader and left the writer unexamined; the writer is still at HEAD. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "theme webview"` → #11423 (open, the narrow themeContext question), #3528 (closed, desktop token bridge). No dated ruling defends the message path. No settled surface is touched: this is webview IPC, not the frozen CLI NDJSON vocabulary, not persisted, not a settings key, not the texra-action result JSON.

**Proposal**

Owner: `@shared/wa/waColorScheme`'s observer owns the wa-* color scheme in every host (it already reacts to both VS Code's own attributes and the class `applyHostBodyTheme` writes on desktop); `themeContext` keeps owning the theme VALUE for its one consumer, the desktop diff view. Migration: (1) in `BaseWebviewApp.onThemeChange`, replace `document.body.className = theme` with `applyHostBodyTheme(theme)` (`src/shared/wa/hostTheme.ts:26-33`) so the writer speaks the same `vscode-<kind>` vocabulary as the host instead of erasing it — this alone restores the UserMessage high-contrast rule and removes the desktop ordering hazard. (2) Delete the extension-side progress theme producers: the `onDidChangeActiveColorTheme` block (`ProgressViewProvider.ts:217-226`), the theme recomputation at `:293-301`, the `theme?` option on `ProgressBackend.syncRenderedStreams`, the `theme?` parameter and `if (theme) this.setTheme(theme)` on `LitSessionRenderer.sendStreamMetadata`, `LitSessionRenderer.setTheme`, the corresponding member of the ProgressView outbound union, and the no-op handler plus its 8-line apology in `progressView/frontend/slices/uiSlice.ts:12-19`. (3) Delete `src/test-kernel/progressView/ProgressThemeSync.vitest.ts` (~170 lines) — its entire subject is that a theme flip sends THEME_SET without a metadata rebuild; with no theme flip message there is nothing to pin, and #9959's real invariant (no metadata rebuild on theme change) is enforced by construction. Keep the main-view GET_THEME/THEME_SET pair and the desktop `nativeTheme` producer (`packages/desktop/src/main/desktopViewStateIpc.ts:28-56`): desktop has no host-set body attributes of its own until `applyHostBodyTheme` runs, and the diff view needs the value. Verify in a light VS Code theme that all three webviews still render light after step 2 (the observer is the only remaining path).

**What we give up**

The extension loses the ability to push a theme the DOM does not already carry — i.e. a theme the webview's own body attributes disagree with. No such case exists in VS Code, which is the only host affected. Restoring the push later is one listener plus one message and would be cheap; the deleted test is the real cost to re-derive.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. THE #11423 ANSWER IS BACKWARDS, and it reverses a correction already on record. The finding says themeContext "is not dead, but its only consumer is a desktop component." Round 1's own verifier block established the opposite at .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md:1625, and I re-confirmed it: `<texra-diff-view>` is created detached at reviewPane.ts:37 and mounted as a workbench SURFACE (main.ts:385 -> :558), a sibling of `<main-app>` (main.ts:287) and `<settings-app>` (main.ts:336), never a descendant, so the @lit/context request never reaches a @provide. Theme reaches it imperatively: main.ts:1394 `reviewPane.setTheme(theme)` -> reviewPane.ts:213 `diffView.hostTheme = nextTheme` (initial at :199). themeContext, the @provide, `this.theme` and the @consume decorator are all inert, so "themeContext keeps owning the theme VALUE for its one consumer" justifies nothing; that channel is #11423's deletion, not this PR's keeper.

2. DESKTOP MOUNTS TWO BaseWebviewApp INSTANCES, NOT THREE. main.ts creates `<main-app>` (:287) and `<settings-app>` (:336) only; main.ts:1431 states outright that progress-view messages "dispatch directly into the shared messageDispatcher, with no <progress-app> mounted for plumbing." The ordering hazard is real but it is two body-class writers plus applyHostBodyTheme, not four.

3. STEP (1) AS WRITTEN IS A HIGH-CONTRAST REGRESSION. applyHostBodyTheme (hostTheme.ts:26-33) writes `body.dataset.vscodeThemeKind = theme` in addition to the class. Both surviving producers collapse ColorThemeKind to two values (MainViewMessageHandler.ts:127-132 and ProgressViewProvider.ts:223-224 both report high contrast as 'light'). Today `className = theme` leaves the data attribute alone, so under an HC theme the observer falls through to `data-vscode-theme-kind: vscode-high-contrast` and correctly picks wa-dark (waColorScheme.ts:70-78). Swapping in applyHostBodyTheme would overwrite that attribute with 'light' and flip the whole webview to wa-light under a dark HC theme — strictly worse than the bug being fixed. Two honest options: (a) map HighContrast -> 'high-contrast' and HighContrastLight -> 'light' at the producers first (ThemeSchema already carries 'high-contrast'); or (b) better, delete the body write AND the `setWaColorScheme(themeIsDark(theme))` call from onThemeChange entirely — VS Code owns those attributes and the desktop already calls applyHostBodyTheme at main.ts:1393, so onThemeChange has no DOM job left in either host. Option (b) makes the finding's own one-owner thesis actually true and removes the cross-host ordering hazard; option (a) leaves two writers.

4. MISSING MIGRATION SITES (all further reductions, but the path list is incomplete). src/shared/schemas/progressView/outbound.ts:15 (the SetThemeMessageSchema import) and :385 (the union member) must go with the uiSlice entry or handler-registry exhaustiveness breaks. src/test-kernel/progressView/ProgressViewMessageDispatcher.vitest.ts:288-296 ("does not throw for THEME_SET") must be deleted with the union member. src/test-kernel/progressView/ProgressBackendStreamLifecycle.vitest.ts:837 asserts `sendStreamMetadata` was called with `(stream, '', undefined)` and must drop the third argument.

5. UNCITED DATED RATIONALE. .agents/docs/archived/feature/2026-05-02-prd-electron-app.md:118 records the opposite design ("frontends don't read prefers-color-scheme or data-vscode-theme-kind"). It is superseded by the WA observer, but the PR must say so and amend that line, or the next survey re-litigates this.

6. #9959 FRAMED SLIGHTLY WRONG. Its item (2) reads "theme change sends THEME_SET alone instead of a full syncFullView" — a bounded memory/perf fix, not a decision that the push must exist. The "enforced by construction" argument for deleting the test is right, but the test's other half pins that a theme flip does not call syncRenderedStreams; that also disappears only because the listener disappears. Say it explicitly.

7. Minor: LitSessionRenderer.setTheme is at :522-528 (the earlier survey's :544 is stale drift). And the strongest single fact deserves to be stated outright: no CSS anywhere reads `body.dark` / `body.light` / `body.high-contrast` — a grep over all of packages/*/src and src returns zero, so the class that writer sets has never had a reader.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Verified the mechanism at HEAD rather than from the claim's quotes. The autonomous path is complete: src/shared/wa/index.ts:19 calls applyInitialWaColorScheme() at module load, all three frontend entry points import '@shared/wa' first, and the MutationObserver (waColorScheme.ts:107-127) watches body class + data-vscode-theme-kind with a prefers-color-scheme fallback. The message path duplicates it and, in the extension, damages it: `document.body.className = theme` erases the vscode-* class VS Code sets, and UserMessage.ts:169-170 is the repo's only `:host-context(.vscode-*)` rule, so it is dead in the progress webview after the first sync. Nothing in the extension progress frontend reads the theme value (grep over the whole frontend returns only comments plus TerminalOutput's computed-CSS read), so deleting the producers is observationally inert once the observer is the only path.

The two-owner diagnosis is real and the direction is right. What the finding gets wrong is which owner it keeps: it preserves themeContext for a consumer that provably never receives from it, and its step (1) swap would break high contrast because applyHostBodyTheme stamps the data attribute the observer falls back on while both producers still collapse HC to 'light'. Both are fixable inside the same PR, and the fix makes the change smaller and cleaner.

Cost recomputed by hand: ProgressViewProvider listener block plus theme recomputation and threaded argument ~16; ProgressBackend option + argument ~3; LitSessionRenderer param, guard line and setTheme method ~10; outbound.ts import + union member 2; uiSlice entry plus its 8-line apology 9; ProgressThemeSync.vitest.ts 173; dispatcher THEME_SET test ~12. Gross ~225, less comment rewordings and (under option (a)) ~4 lines of HC mapping. -215 net is honest and slightly conservative. Elements retired: the onDidChangeActiveColorTheme listener, LitSessionRenderer.setTheme, the sendStreamMetadata theme parameter, the ProgressBackend theme option, the progress-view THEME_SET union member, and its handler entry — six, not five.

Risk stays medium: the payoff is visual behavior in a host that cannot be unit-tested, and the deleted test is the only automated pin. Verification must cover all three VS Code theme kinds (light, dark, and a high-contrast theme), not just light as the proposal says, because high contrast is exactly where both the current defect and the proposed step (1) misfire.

</details>

#### Give the launcher webview one holder and one guarded post path: five copies of the reference, four posting mechanisms, two of them blind to the sidebar's current mode

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -35 LoC, -4 elements (proposer claimed -55; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `packages/extension/src/webview/managers/BaseWebviewManager.ts`, `packages/extension/src/webview/managers/InstructionManager.ts`, `packages/extension/src/webview/managers/FileManager.ts`, `packages/extension/src/webview/managers/DiffManager.ts`, `packages/extension/src/webview/MainViewMessageHandler.ts`, `packages/extension/src/webview/MainViewProvider.ts`, `packages/extension/src/common/webview/BaseViewMessageHandler.ts`, `packages/extension/src/common/webview/BaseWebviewProvider.ts`

**Evidence**

"The launcher webview" is stored in five places for one view: `BaseWebviewProvider._view` (`packages/extension/src/common/webview/BaseWebviewProvider.ts:14`, assigned at `MainViewProvider.ts:333`), `BaseViewMessageHandler._activeView` (`BaseViewMessageHandler.ts:42`, assigned on every dispatch at `:68`), and one `protected webview` per manager (`BaseWebviewManager.ts:12`) for FileManager, DiffManager and InstructionManager. The three manager copies are re-synced on every inbound message by an extension point that exists only for that purpose: `BaseViewMessageHandler.onDispatch` (`:97-104`, the fourth knob of the family round 2 shipped as "three parity-only knobs in the shared webview base classes") implemented at `MainViewMessageHandler.ts:86-90` as three `attachWebview` calls. On top of the five holders sit four accessors — `getActiveView` (`:53`), `postToActiveView` (`:78`), `withActiveWebview` (`:84`), and a fifth hand-rolled one, `MainViewMessageHandler.runWithActiveView` (`:144-149`) — plus two external handles: `texra.getWebviewView` (registered at `packages/extension/src/commands.ts:64` as `mainViewProvider.getMainModeView()`) reached through `getMainWebview()` by four production callers (`frontend/events/agentEventListeners.ts:122`, `commands/system/mainViewCommands.ts:56`, `commands/extensionCommandSurface.ts:78`, `settingsView/SettingsViewMessageHandler.ts:650`), and `ProgressViewProvider.getActiveWebview` (`ProgressViewProvider.ts:485-490`) reaching in through `_mainViewProvider.getWebviewView()`. Four of these post outbound messages, and they disagree on both guarantees: `MainViewProvider.postToWebview` (`:118-128`) asserts against the outbound schemas and calls itself "Sole outbound path to the launcher webview" — which is false; `BaseWebviewManager.postMessage` asserts (that is what closed issue #8123, "Route outbound webview/desktop IPC through existing Zod schemas", 2026-07-12, delivered — it covered BaseWebviewManager and desktop `postToRenderer` and nothing else); `BaseViewMessageHandler.postToActiveView` does not assert; `getMainWebview()` callers post raw (e.g. `SettingsViewMessageHandler.ts:653-657` posts a MainView SET_BANNER into another tree's webview). Two of the four are also blind to the sidebar's mode: `MainViewProvider.switchMode` (`:413-443`) disposes the message listener and swaps the HTML but never calls `messageHandler.clearActiveView()`, so after a switch to progress content the handler and all three managers still hold the sidebar view. `InstructionManager.handlePolishInstructionText` (`:40-68`) awaits an LLM call and then posts INSTRUCTION_TEXT_POLISHED through that stale reference — if the user pressed Execute meanwhile (which auto-switches the sidebar to progress), the polished text is posted into the progress document and lost. `MainViewProvider`'s own posts avoid this only because they route through the mode-aware `getMainModeView()` (`:159-163`), a check the other paths do not make. Dedupe: none of `BaseWebviewManager`, `attachWebview`, `onDispatch`, `postToActiveView` or `getMainWebview` appears in the rounds 1-2 index or in either survey document; `gh issue list --state all --search "BaseWebviewManager"` → 0 rows.

**Proposal**

Owner: `BaseViewMessageHandler` keeps `_activeView` (it is already updated on every dispatch, which is exactly when the managers are refreshed today) and gains one guarded `protected post(message)` that runs `assertKnownOutboundMessage` before `postMessage`. Migration: (1) delete `packages/extension/src/webview/managers/BaseWebviewManager.ts` and give FileManager/DiffManager/InstructionManager a `post` callback at construction (`new FileManager({ post: (m) => this.post(m) })`, three constructor lines in `MainViewMessageHandler`); their two `if (!this.webview) return` guards (`InstructionManager.ts:43,88`) go away because `post` already no-ops without an active view. (2) Delete the `onDispatch` extension point from the base class and its implementation. (3) Move the outbound assertion from `BaseWebviewManager.postMessage` into `post`, which makes `MainViewProvider.postToWebview` and the manager path one mechanism rather than two with different guarantees. (4) Add the one line `switchMode` is missing — `this.messageHandler.clearActiveView()` when leaving main mode — which is the whole point of the collapse: today that fix has to be applied at five holders, afterwards at one. (5) Optional follow-on, not counted above: replace `texra.getWebviewView` (a command that hands out a raw webview) with a command that posts, so the four external callers stop bypassing the guard. Honest accounting: -27 (BaseWebviewManager) -16 (onDispatch + three attach calls + doc) -6 (two guards) +3 (constructor wiring) +8 (post method) +1 (clearActiveView), tests unchanged; the win is one owner and one guarantee, not size.

**What we give up**

Managers lose the ability to post to a webview other than the currently dispatching one — nothing does that today, and the one case that looks like it (SettingsViewMessageHandler posting into the main view) goes through `getMainWebview`, which this finding deliberately leaves alone. The `onDispatch` hook disappears as an extension point for future per-message setup; that is the same call round 2 already made for `readyCommand`.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. "-55 LoC" contradicts the finding's own arithmetic: -27 -16 -6 +3 +8 +1 = -37, not -55. My independent count is ~-35.
2. "tests unchanged" is FALSE. `src/test-kernel/webview/MainViewMessageHandler.vitest.ts:103-105` defines `class ManagerStub { attachWebview = vi.fn(); }` and mocks all three managers with it; deleting `attachWebview` requires editing that stub. Two more references need comment edits: `src/shared/utils/dispatcher.ts:224` names `BaseWebviewManager.postMessage` as the canonical single-domain example for `assertOutboundMessage`, and `src/test-kernel/progressView/ProgressViewMessageDispatcher.vitest.ts:359,381` names it in a comment and a test title.
3. "gh issue list --state all --search 'BaseWebviewManager' → 0 rows" is FALSE: it returns 2 rows — #8123 (closed, the very issue the finding cites) and #7879 (closed, extension/desktop file-listing dedup, unrelated).
4. The assertion move is under-specified and, as written, WEAKENS the #8123 guarantee. `BaseViewMessageHandler` is shared by MainView, ProgressView and SettingsView handlers, so a `post()` on it cannot use today's strict `assertOutboundMessage(MainViewMessageSchema, …)` (throws on an unrecognized command); it would have to use `assertKnownOutboundMessage`, which `dispatcher.ts:220-240` documents as passing unknown commands through unchecked. Either add a per-subclass outbound-schema member (+~8 LoC, +3 elements, unaccounted) or — cheaper and stronger — do not add `post` to the base at all: have `MainViewMessageHandler` build one guarded lambda (`(m) => { assertOutboundMessage(MainViewMessageSchema, m); this.postToActiveView(m); }`) and hand it to the three managers. My numbers below assume that variant.
5. "one guarded post path" overstates the result. The MainView tree keeps at least five other post paths after this change: `MainViewMessageHandler.ts:174`, `:182`, `:203` (raw `webviewView.webview.postMessage`), `bannerSlice.ts:40` (raw, via `host.getActiveView()`), and `RecordingManager` (`packages/extension/src/frontend/media/RecordingManager.ts:38-91`, seven raw posts on a view passed in from `chatSlice.ts:19-21` via `runWithActiveView`). The base also retains `postToActiveView`, `withActiveWebview` and `getActiveView` untouched. Honest framing: 9 paths → 7.
6. "Five copies of the reference" undercounts by one and mis-frames one. `RecordingManager` holds the view as a parameter across an await (transcription), so it is a sixth holder with the identical staleness bug — and the proposal does not fix it, which undercuts "today that fix has to be applied at five holders, afterwards at one". Also the three manager copies are one field on one base class instantiated three times, not three independent designs.
7. The `switchMode` fix is smaller than sold. Because the `WebviewView` object is unchanged by a mode swap, `clearActiveView()` converts "posted into the progress document, silently ignored" into "not posted" — the polished text is lost either way. The win is correctness hygiene (no cross-document message), not user-visible recovery; a real fix would queue the result.
8. `onDispatch` is not "the fourth knob of the family round 2 shipped". It predates round 2 by months (`git log -S onDispatch` → e8f83de5df, #6883), and the round-2 knobs lived in `BaseWebviewApp`/`BaseViewContentProvider`, a different pair of base classes.
9. Line-number drift (minor): `_view` is assigned at `MainViewProvider.ts:330` (not 333); `postToWebview` is `:121-131` (not 118-128); `postToActiveView` is `BaseViewMessageHandler.ts:81` (not 78); `withActiveWebview` is `:86` (not 84); `ProgressViewProvider.getActiveWebview` is `:483-491` (not 485-490).
10. Bonus in the finding's favour, unclaimed: the two `if (!this.webview) return` guards are dead code today — both handlers are only ever invoked from `dispatchInbound`, which calls `onDispatch` (attaching the view) immediately before the dispatcher runs, so `this.webview` is never undefined at those lines. Deleting them is behavior-preserving, not a behavior change.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Verified against HEAD (91b9b49444), not memory. Read `BaseWebviewManager.ts` (27 lines, one field, two methods, three subclasses), `BaseViewMessageHandler.ts` (`onDispatch` declared `:99`, called `:114`, exactly one implementor at `MainViewMessageHandler.ts:86-90`), `BaseWebviewProvider.ts` (`clearActiveView?.()` only in `cleanupView`), and `MainViewProvider.switchMode`. Confirmed the mode-switch staleness end to end, including the real trigger (`ProgressViewProvider.ts:391`). Checked all four dedupe surfaces: rounds12-index, both survey docs, `gh issue --state all`, and `git log -S`. Checked the three PRDs/proposals that mention these symbols — all descriptive, none a ruling that this collapse must beat. Confirmed no ratchet, no wire contract, no settings key, no external consumer is crossed. The residual objections are all accounting and framing, not viability: the honest number is still a reduction, the migration is contained to eight files plus one test stub, and the change removes a single-implementor extension point and a 27-line base class — squarely inside AGENTS.md's abstraction-discipline rule against single-caller seams. It survives, at a smaller win than claimed and with the assertion strategy corrected so #8123's strict single-domain guarantee is preserved rather than downgraded.

</details>

### cli-architecture — The CLI as an architecture

#### Two citty command-tree walkers that disagree about lazily-resolved subcommands — one accessor, one owner

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -28 LoC, -4 elements (proposer claimed -30; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `packages/cli/src/commands/_helpers/dispatch.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/completionCommandTree.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/completion.ts`, `packages/cli/src/commands/help.ts`

**Evidence**

Citty lets `meta`, `args`, and `subCommands` each be a value, a promise, or a thunk, so anything that walks the tree needs three tiny resolvers. The CLI has two independent sets:

- `packages/cli/src/commands/_helpers/dispatch.ts:126` `export type AnyCommand = CommandDef<any>`, `resolveCommandMeta` (:136-140), `commandSubCommands` (:142-150), `commandArgs` (:152-158). Consumers: `resolveDeepestSubCommand` (:171-219), `detectUnknownCliCommand` (:381), `showUsage` (:487, :647-648) — i.e. `--help` scoping and unknown-command suggestions, reached from `packages/cli/src/commands/help.ts:12-31`.
- `packages/cli/src/runtime/completionCommandTree.ts:8` a SECOND `export type AnyCommand = CommandDef<any>`, plus `resolveValue` (:44-49), `commandMeta` (:125-127), `commandArgs` (:129-131), `commandSubcommands` (:133-147). Consumer: `collectCommands` (:149-171) → the three shell generators.

They are not identical, and the difference is a latent bug, not a style choice: `completionCommandTree.commandSubcommands` additionally resolves each CHILD through `resolveValue` (:141-144), so a lazily-declared subcommand completes correctly; `dispatch.commandSubCommands` does not, so the same lazy child would be invisible to `--help` scoping and to unknown-command detection. Round 1 already landed "One owner for CLI usage rendering: route the launcher's Help through dispatch's showUsage" (#11416, `git log` on this path) — that consolidated the RENDERER but left the two walkers underneath it. `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-07-prod-structural-leads-triage.md:150-151` separately flagged `completion.ts` as a re-export shim over `completionCommandTree`, so this file is already a recorded seam.

**Proposal**

Owner: `packages/cli/src/runtime/completionCommandTree.ts` (the established direction — `dispatch.ts` already imports `@cli/runtime/logSinks` and `@cli/runtime/cliContext`, so commands → runtime is the existing edge; the reverse does not exist).

Migration: export `AnyCommand`, `commandMeta`, `commandArgs`, `commandSubcommands` from `completionCommandTree.ts` (three already-written private functions get the `export` keyword, each with a consumer in the same PR, so the dead-export ratchet is satisfied). Delete `dispatch.ts:126,136-158` and import them. Preserve dispatch's `undefined`-means-leaf distinction by having its call sites read `Object.keys(subs).length === 0` or by keeping a one-line local adapter; do NOT change the leaf semantics in the same PR. The child-resolution difference is then decided once, in one place — take the completion behavior, which is the citty-correct one.

**What we give up**

Nothing. The only cost is that `commands/_helpers/` gains one more import from `runtime/`, an edge that already exists twice in the same file.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. CONSUMER MISLABELED. dispatch.ts:487 (`commandArgs`) sits inside `commandFlagSpecs` (:483-503), whose caller is the unknown-FLAG detector at :543-548 — not `showUsage`. Only the :647-648 `resolveCommandMeta` pair (inside `usageParentWithFullPath`) is on the showUsage path.

2. REACH UNDERSTATED. The walkers are not "reached from help.ts:12-31". `runCli` calls `detectUnknownCliCommand(rootCommand, rawArgs)` on EVERY invocation (root.ts ~:160), root.ts:174-175 and :193 call resolveDeepestSubCommand + showUsage for `--help`, and orchestrate.ts:379 calls showUsage. This is the CLI startup hot path, which raises both the value and the care required; the regression net is CliRootArgs.vitest.ts (imports detectUnknownCliCommand/detectUnknownCliFlag) and it needs no edits.

3. LATENT-BUG MECHANISM IS WRONG. A lazily declared child would not be "invisible" to --help scoping or unknown-command detection. `subCommands[token]` returns the thunk, which is truthy, so `resolveDeepestSubCommandPath` (:208-217) descends INTO the function and `detectUnknownCliCommand` (:384) accepts it as a real command. The failure is a degenerate empty-meta/empty-args usage block and a mis-scoped help, not a missing subcommand. The direction of the fix is still right.

4. BOTH WALKERS SHARE A SECOND, UNMENTIONED DIVERGENCE. citty's `_findSubCommand` falls back to `meta.alias` matching (index.mjs:260-263); neither walker does, so `--help` scoping and typo detection already disagree with real dispatch for any aliased subcommand. Consolidation gives one place to fix that later — do not claim the merge fixes it.

5. THE LEAF DISTINCTION IS REDUNDANT, NOT MERELY PRESERVABLE. No adapter and no `Object.keys(...).length === 0` rewrite is needed. With `{}`: the guard at :200-202 is behaviorally dead (the loop breaks at the first non-matching positional and returns the identical object), and `if (!subCommands) return undefined` at :382 is equivalent to falling through, because `isPureSubCommandContainer` already requires `Object.keys(subCommands).length > 0` (:337) so an empty map reaches the `return undefined` at :404 anyway. Delete both guards outright: 5 more lines than the proposal budgeted.

6. IMPORT ACCOUNTING MISSING. After the deletion, dispatch.ts's citty import drops `ArgsDef`, `CommandDef`, `CommandMeta` (:8-10), keeping only `ArgDef` + `renderUsage` (-3 lines); the new 4-symbol import from '@cli/runtime/completionCommandTree' costs 6 lines after prettier (+6). Net +3 on imports, which the claimed -30 did not price.

7. "WHAT WE GIVE UP: NOTHING" IS TOO STRONG. `commandSubcommands` resolves every child of each visited level eagerly via Promise.all. Zero cost today (all 25 root children and every nested map are plain object literals — grepped, no thunks), but it forecloses lazy command modules for startup latency: a future `() => import('./foo')` child would be force-resolved by `detectUnknownCliCommand` on every invocation. Say so rather than claiming no cost.

8. ELEMENT ACCOUNTING HAS A COUNTERVAILING SIGN. -3 functions and -1 type alias, but knip's export count goes UP by 3 as commandMeta/commandArgs/commandSubcommands become exported; that passes the dead-export ratchet (consumers in the same PR) and needs no knip-baseline edit. Bonus not claimed: `dispatch.ts:126 export type AnyCommand` currently has zero importers anywhere (the only `AnyCommand` import in the repo is completion.ts sourcing it from completionCommandTree), so this also clears residue #11416 left behind.

9. OWNER CHOICE IS DEFENSIBLE BUT MISNAMED. completionCommandTree.ts is not a neutral module — it also owns the completion flag model and agent-category sources (:213, :253) and is named for the shell-completion feature. dispatch.ts importing citty walkers from "completionCommandTree" is a naming smell. Correct move is to keep the merge and rename the file (e.g. cittyCommandTree.ts) in the same PR; do NOT create a third module for the walkers.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

I read both walkers in full at HEAD, checked citty's real type (`SubCommandsDef = Record<string, Resolvable<CommandDef<any>>>`) and its runtime (`_findSubCommand` and `renderUsage` both resolveValue each child), so the divergence and its direction are real: dispatch's non-resolving version is the type-unsound one. I re-walked every consumer of the four dispatch helpers and found two mislabeled call sites and a much wider reach than claimed (every CLI invocation, not just `texra help`). I re-derived the leaf semantics and found the `undefined`-vs-`{}` distinction is redundant at both call sites, so the migration is smaller than proposed, not larger. Dedupe: rounds12-index.md, both survey docs, `git log` on all three paths, and gh issue search turn up nothing covering this; #11416 touched dispatch.ts only to un-export types. Ratchets/eslint/architecture-edges do not reach a packages/cli-internal import, and `grep` confirms zero runtime→commands imports so the stated direction is the only existing one and no cycle is created. No external surface: the CLI ships a bin only, and none of these symbols reaches NDJSON, settings, persisted files, or the texra-action contract. Cost: removed = 5 comment lines + 1 type alias + resolveCommandMeta (5) + commandSubCommands (9) + commandArgs (7) + 4 blanks + the two now-dead guards (5) ≈ 36; added = +6 import, -3 citty type imports, three `export` keywords (0 lines). Net ≈ -33, or ≈ -28 if the CommandDef-invariance comment is moved to the new home (it should be). Zero test and zero doc edits. I report -28 as the conservative honest figure. Elements: -3 functions, -1 duplicate type alias = -4, with the caveat that three exports become public. Risk is low: mechanical, fully type-checked, and pinned by an existing suite that needs no changes; the one behavioral delta (children now resolved) is a no-op on the current tree and strictly closer to citty.

</details>

#### cliPresentationHost keeps two five-entry handler maps where exactly one event's payload differs

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -26 LoC, -2 elements (proposer claimed -30; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `packages/cli/src/runtime/cliPresentationHost.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/logSinks.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/cliContext.ts`

**Evidence**

`createRuntimePresentationNdjsonHandlers` (packages/cli/src/runtime/cliPresentationHost.ts:52-73) and `textModeHandlers` (:106-135) implement the same five `RuntimePresentationEventPayloads` keys. Diffed entry by entry, the real differences are:

- `requestShowError` / `showAgentConfigBanner`: identical bodies plus a `runProgress?.preserve()` call. `runProgress` is `createRunProgressRenderer(context)`, which returns `undefined` unless `context.renderRunProgress === true` (:84), and the ONLY writer of that field is `buildHeadlessRunContext` → `shouldRenderRunProgress` = `!quietLogs && outputFormat !== 'ndjson'` (packages/cli/src/runtime/runModel.ts:66-72, runProgressRenderer.ts:74-77). So in ndjson mode `preserve()` is a no-op by construction — the two bodies are already the same behavior.
- `requestOpenFile` / `requestEnsureProgressView`: text logs a debug line, ndjson does not. This one must NOT be unified upward: `createCliLogSink('ndjson')` is the `NdjsonStdoutSink` (logSinks.ts:294), so a debug call in ndjson mode would emit a `kind:'log'` record onto the frozen public stdout wire.
- `requestShowInstruction`: the only genuine difference — text appends `formatInstructionActionHint(payload.actions,'cli')` to the message; ndjson passes `{key, actions, showSuppress}` as fields. `StderrTextSink.write` (logSinks.ts:171-177) DISCARDS `record.fields` entirely, so a single call passing both the ternary'd hint and the fields is byte-identical in both modes.

A trap the next round must not fall into (this is the round-2 revert pattern): the ndjson arm is NOT dead in the interactive TUI. `HEADLESS_ONLY_GLOBAL_ARG_NAMES` (packages/cli/src/commands/_helpers/globalArgs.ts:127-131) blocks only the `--output-format` FLAG for `chat`/`orchestrate`/`setup`; `buildCliContext` still resolves `outputFormat` from `TEXRA_OUTPUT_FORMAT` and from `.texra/config.json`'s `outputFormat` key (cliContext.ts:399-410, schemas/cliSettings.ts:25). A user with that key set runs the Ink TUI with `outputFormat === 'ndjson'`, so `emitApprovalBypassState` (:141-152) and the ndjson handler map are both reachable from `createTuiHostInteractions` (subscribeApprovals.ts:165,214). Do not delete either as unreachable.

**Proposal**

Owner: one `PresentationEventHandlers` map built once in `createCliRuntimeHost`, with `const ndjson = context.outputFormat === 'ndjson'` captured at construction. `requestShowError` and `showAgentConfigBanner` become single bodies (`runProgress?.preserve()` is already inert in ndjson). `requestOpenFile`/`requestEnsureProgressView` become `if (!ndjson) logDebugEvent(...); return false;` — preserving the frozen-wire exclusion explicitly instead of by map selection. `requestShowInstruction` becomes one call: `ensureLogger().info(\`${payload.message}${ndjson ? '' : hint}\`, {key, actions, showSuppress})`. Delete `createRuntimePresentationNdjsonHandlers`and the`ndjsonHandlers`lazy field (:80-82, :158-161);`emit` then dispatches against the single map.

Second, unrelated-but-adjacent observation worth recording rather than fixing here: in the interactive TUI (`quietLogs: true`, runChatTui.tsx:249-253) `requestShowError` still routes to `StderrTextSink`, which writes a raw `ISO-ts LEVEL message` line to stderr underneath Ink's live region. The CLI already owns better surfaces for that (`appendLocalErrorTranscript`, `setTransientNotice`). Fixing it ADDS ~20 LoC, so it is a defect to file, not a simplification to fold into this collapse.

**What we give up**

The compile-time exhaustiveness that two separate literal maps give you (the file's own comment says the per-key reproduction makes a future payload addition a compile error) is preserved — one typed `PresentationEventHandlers<RuntimePresentationEventPayloads>` literal still requires every key. What is lost is the ability to read the ndjson wire behavior without reading the ternaries, so the frozen-wire exclusion on the two debug arms must carry an explicit comment.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. "`preserve()` is a no-op in ndjson mode BY CONSTRUCTION" is true only in production.
