The test fixtures do build ndjson contexts with `renderRunProgress: true` — the local `context()` in src/test-kernel/cli/RunProgressRenderer.vitest.ts:43-52 sets `renderRunProgress: true, stderrColorEnabled: true` and is used at :1146 and :1192 with `outputFormat:'ndjson'`, and src/test-kernel/cli/fixtures/cliContext.ts:37 (`createRunCommandCliContext`) does the same. The unified handler is still safe there, but the reason is the guard `if (this.ansi && this.liveLine)` in `DefaultRunProgressRenderer.preserve()` (runProgressRenderer.ts:198-203) plus the fact that no test attaches the renderer before emitting — not that the renderer is undefined. Cite the guard, not "by construction".

2. Relatedly, "the ONLY writer of that field is `buildHeadlessRunContext`" is a production-only statement; four test files write `renderRunProgress` directly (RunProgressRenderer, RunExecution, AgentsRun/MultiAgent/Workflow command suites, fixtures/cliContext.ts).

3. The finding UNDERSELLS its own safety net. It never mentions `RUNTIME_PRESENTATION_NDJSON_CASES` (RunProgressRenderer.vitest.ts:70-116) or the "applies an explicit ndjson policy to every runtime presentation request" test (:1189-1216), which already pin the exact stdout record set for all five events with `quietLogs:false`. That test is what makes the `if (!ndjson) logDebugEvent(...)` rewrite verifiable rather than comment-enforced, and it is why zero test edits are needed. The PR should reference it in the code comment the proposal asks for.

4. Stale-comment provenance the finding misses: the ndjson map's JSDoc as written in #9508 said "None of these events have an NDJSON wire representation of their own today, so `requestOpenFile`, `showAgentConfigBanner`, and `requestEnsureProgressView` are deliberate no-ops". #10354 (00ad7e1f9d) then gave `showAgentConfigBanner` a real ndjson error record and only partially rewrote that paragraph. So one of the two maps is already carrying half-rotted rationale — an extra argument for the collapse, and evidence that the duplicated comment block is itself a maintenance cost.

5. Net LoC is optimistic. Deleting the ndjson function plus its 9-line JSDoc (-30), the lazy `ndjsonHandlers` field (-3), and the branch in `emit` (-5) is offset by the fields object the unified `requestShowInstruction` must pass (+4 formatted), the `const ndjson = ...` binding (+1), and the frozen-wire comment the finding itself says is mandatory (+5-7). Honest net is about -26, not -30.

6. Nit: the two "elements" are one module-private function and one mutable local. `-2 elements` is defensible but neither is an export, so this does not move `knip-baseline.json` or any ratchet — do not claim ratchet-surface reduction in the PR body.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

This is one of the rare round-3 architectural claims where every load-bearing fact checks out on re-walk and the blast radius is a single 171-line file with no exported symbol removed.

The three things that usually kill a claim like this all fail to fire. There is no dated ruling: the two-map shape is a mechanical residue of #9508's switch-to-handler-literal conversion, and the commit message says so in as many words. There is no external consumer: `createRuntimePresentationNdjsonHandlers` is module-private with exactly one call site, and the frozen NDJSON stdout wire — the correct thing to worry about here, since `createCliLogSink('ndjson')` routes `logger.debug` straight onto stdout as `kind:'log'` — is already guarded by an exhaustive per-event policy table and a stdout-capturing test with `quietLogs:false`. And the cost is genuinely a delete: no call sites to update, no tests to rewrite, no docs to amend.

The behavioral analysis is correct on all three axes. `StderrTextSink.write` interpolates only ts/level/message, so passing the ndjson fields unconditionally is invisible in text mode. `preserve()` is inert in ndjson, though for a slightly different reason than claimed (the `ansi && liveLine` guard, not an undefined renderer, once you account for test fixtures that do set `renderRunProgress:true` alongside ndjson). And the reachability trap the finding raises is real and correctly diagnosed — `HEADLESS_ONLY_GLOBAL_ARG_NAMES` gates only the `--output-format` flag while `buildCliContext` still honors `TEXRA_OUTPUT_FORMAT` and the config key, so neither arm may be deleted as unreachable. That the author found and defused the exact round-2 revert pattern before proposing raises confidence in the rest.

What the finding gets wrong is small and all in the same direction: it is slightly too confident about "by construction" invariants that are really guard-enforced, and its -30 LoC ignores the lines the collapse must add back (the fields object, the ndjson binding, and the frozen-wire comment it itself mandates). Honest net is about -26 on a 171-line file, roughly 15%, with two concepts retired.

Risk is low rather than trivial. The one durable cost is that the wire exclusion moves from "structurally impossible, different map" to "a ternary you must read" — but that regression is precisely what the existing ndjson policy test catches, so the guard is executable rather than a comment. Worth shipping, with the comment pointing at that test.

</details>

### platform-ports — The platform ports layer

#### Retire the in-place workspace-storage transition: four commit/rollback ledgers exist for one VS Code event that two other hosts already answer with a restart

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -1550 LoC, -22 elements (proposer claimed -1400; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/platform/interfaces.ts`, `src/platform/defaults/workspaceStorage.ts`, `src/platform/defaults/jsonConfigProvider.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/SessionHandle.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/index.ts`, `src/controllers/progressView/backend/ProgressBackend.ts`, `packages/extension/src/frontend/vscode/texraConfig.ts`, `packages/extension/src/progressView/ProgressViewProvider.ts`, `packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts`, `src/test-kernel/settings/ExtensionWorkspaceTransition.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/SessionRestartRepair.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/progressView/ProgressBackendPresentation.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/platform/WorkspaceStorage.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/settings/ExtensionTexraConfig.vitest.ts`

**Evidence**

End-to-end trace. `StorageProvider` (src/platform/interfaces.ts:150-191) is a 2-method path port with four extra OPTIONAL methods — `hasPendingWorkspaceStorageChange`, `commitWorkspaceStorageChange`, `finalizeWorkspaceStorageChange`, `rollbackWorkspaceStorageChange`. Implementations: exactly ONE in production. `grep 'implements StorageProvider'` returns `WorkspaceStorageProvider` (src/platform/defaults/workspaceStorage.ts:160) and the test `FakeStorageProvider` (src/test-kernel/support/FakePlatform.ts:476, which omits all four). `createNodeStorageProvider` (src/platform/defaults/nodeStorage.ts:37-42) is a one-line `new WorkspaceStorageProvider(...)`; the extension (packages/extension/src/extension.ts:268), desktop (packages/desktop/src/main/platform/index.ts:76), CLI (packages/cli/src/runtime/cliStateStores.ts:34), updateChecker (packages/cli/src/runtime/updateChecker.ts:299) and the SDK (packages/agent/src/node.ts:51) all end at that same class. So the four methods are always present in production and the optionality buys nothing except three-valued call sites: SessionHandle.ts:309-313 (`=== false`), :412-417 (`=== false`), :438 (`?.() !== true`), and texraConfig.ts:74-79 (`?.(...) === false`) each have to distinguish `undefined` from `false`, i.e. the optionality manufactures a silent-degradation branch (CLAUDE.md 'silent degradation is a defect').

Production triggers: ONE. `grep enqueueWorkspaceTransition|reloadAfterStorageRootChange` outside test-kernel yields packages/extension/src/progressView/ProgressViewProvider.ts:196-215 (`vscode.workspace.onDidChangeWorkspaceFolders`) -> ExtensionTexraConfig.enqueueWorkspaceTransition (texraConfig.ts:67-140) -> ProgressBackend.reloadAfterStorageRootChange (src/controllers/progressView/backend/ProgressBackend.ts:791-830) -> SessionHandle.reloadAfterStorageRootChange (src/agent/runtime/SessionHandle.ts:300-360) -> replaceStoresAfterStorageRootChange (:398-470) -> the port's commit/finalize/rollback. Nothing else calls any of it.

Four independent generation/rollback ledgers for that one event: (1) `WorkspaceStorageProvider.workspaceChangeRollback` + `activeWorkspacePath` (workspaceStorage.ts:154-238); (2) `SessionHandle.storageGeneration` + `isRepairSuperseded` + `restoreRestartRepairAfterReplacementFailure` + a full `previousStatus` snapshot/replay loop (SessionHandle.ts:183, 330-331, 364-366, 401-470); (3) `ProgressBackend.storageGeneration` + `presentationReloadPending` (ProgressBackend.ts:806-830); (4) `ExtensionTexraConfig.transitionGeneration` + `failedTransitionGeneration` + `workspaceQueue` + `previousStore` + `JsonConfigProvider.replaceWorkspaceStore` (texraConfig.ts:32-140, jsonConfigProvider.ts:50-54). They are coordinated by a three-callback hook interface (`WorkspaceStorageTransitionHooks`, SessionHandle.ts:121-126) threaded from a VS Code file into the VS Code-free core and re-exported at src/agent/runtime/index.ts:30.

Decisive precedent, same repo, already shipped: the DESKTOP host answers exactly this event by restarting. packages/desktop/src/main/index.ts:1148-1188 stores the new path under `DESKTOP_WORKSPACE_PATH_STATE_KEY` and calls `app.relaunch({args})`; platform/index.ts:66-76 reads it once at startup into a `const`. The CLI likewise pins `cliWorkspaceCwd` per process (packages/cli/src/runtime/initPlatform.ts:234). Two of the three Node hosts already chose restart-over-transition. Dated history: the machinery arrived with #8624 (`feat(extension): share the ~/.texra storage root`, bf8d152ba6) and has since needed repeated repair commits (`fix: ignore unchanged workspace transitions` b7bf8b2f67, `refactor: reuse workspace transition hooks` 44a0097d8b). Checked for a contrary ruling: no doc in docs/proposals or docs/architecture mandates seamless in-place workspace switching (`grep -rl 'workspace transition|storage root change' docs/` hits only the built VitePress dist and the approval-policy PRD); `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search 'workspace storage transition'` and `--search StorageProvider` return only #10854 (dead port members, closed by #10865, which deliberately did NOT touch these four) and unrelated tracking issues. Not in rounds 1-2: `grep -c reloadAfterStorageRootChange` is 0 in both survey docs, and the only WorktreeStateStore/platform hits there are the `sharedKeys` and `createNodeWorkspace.getRoot` default-parameter items.

Size measured at HEAD: SessionHandle.ts 1107 L (transition block :296-500 ~205 L), texraConfig.ts 151 L (~100 transition-only), ProgressBackend transition method ~50 L, provider methods + rollback state ~55 L, port declaration ~27 L, ProgressViewProvider listener ~28 L. Tests: ExtensionWorkspaceTransition.vitest.ts 433 L (entirely this feature), SessionRestartRepair.vitest.ts :520-865 ~345 L, ProgressBackendPresentation.vitest.ts ~250 of 361 L, WorkspaceStorage.vitest.ts ~60 L, ExtensionTexraConfig.vitest.ts ~35 L.

**Proposal**

Owner that absorbs the responsibility: the host's own restart path, exactly as desktop already does. On `onDidChangeWorkspaceFolders`, ProgressViewProvider first asks the provider whether the storage root actually moved (the first-folder change is the only case that does; adding a second folder is already a no-op today), and when it did, shows a modal 'TeXRA needs to reload this window to switch its workspace storage' with a Reload action running `vscode.commands.executeCommand('workbench.action.reloadWindow')` — the VS Code analogue of `app.relaunch`. Migration, in order: (1) land the reload prompt behind the existing `hasPendingWorkspaceStorageChange` check so behaviour is observable before anything is deleted; (2) delete ProgressViewProvider's transition listener body, ExtensionTexraConfig.enqueueWorkspaceTransition + its `update()` override + `transitionGeneration`/`failedTransitionGeneration`/`workspaceQueue`/`previousStore`, and `JsonConfigProvider.replaceWorkspaceStore`; (3) delete ProgressBackend.reloadAfterStorageRootChange and its `storageGeneration`/`presentationReloadPending` pair; (4) delete SessionHandle.reloadAfterStorageRootChange, replaceStoresAfterStorageRootChange, restoreRestartRepairAfterReplacementFailure, WorkspaceStorageTransitionHooks, StorageRootChangeRefusedError and the index.ts re-export, keeping `storageGeneration`/`isRepairSuperseded` only if the restart-repair path still needs them (verify: :353 uses the same counter); (5) delete the four methods from the port and `workspaceChangeRollback`/`resolveTargetWorkspacePath` from WorkspaceStorageProvider, leaving `activeWorkspacePath` a plain read of `getWorkspacePath()`, so StorageProvider becomes a two-method port with no optional members; (6) delete ExtensionWorkspaceTransition.vitest.ts whole and the transition blocks of the other four suites. Keep `hasPendingWorkspaceStorageChange` ONLY if the prompt needs it — one required boolean method is cheap; the commit/finalize/rollback trio is what goes. Because the prompt is user-visible, this needs a maintainer ruling before implementation, and it must NOT be batched with anything else.

**What we give up**

Extension users who add or remove the FIRST workspace folder mid-session get a reload prompt instead of an in-place swap. Everything else is preserved: adding a second folder is already a no-op, and desktop/CLI users see no change at all because they already restart. What is genuinely lost is the ability to keep a VS Code window alive across a storage-root move — which is also the only reason the four ledgers, the three-callback hook, and the port's optional members exist. Restoring it later means restoring roughly the same machinery, so this is a one-way door and should be taken only with an explicit ruling.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. Wrong provenance. The machinery did not arrive with #8624/bf8d152ba6. `git log -S commitWorkspaceStorageChange --first-parent main -- src packages` bottoms out at e89c5cbc05 "fix: repair crashed runs when sessions open (#9273)", whose body is a 24-item list of incremental transition fixes; the follow-ups are #9364 (storage-root deletion serialization), #9365 (restart-repair rollback) and dea0836856 "fix: make workspace settings transitions atomic (#9603)", which is where `enqueueWorkspaceTransition` first appears. The cited b7bf8b2f67/44a0097d8b are branch commits inside that same stack, not independent later repairs.

2. Central premise misstated. "The first-folder change is the only case that moves the storage root" is right; "ProgressViewProvider ... shows a modal reload prompt for it" is wrong, because VS Code never delivers that case to this listener (@types/vscode 1.125.0 index.d.ts:13879-13883). The machinery is unreachable in production, not single-trigger. The right action is straight deletion.

3. Consequently the proposal's step (1) — land a reload prompt behind `hasPendingWorkspaceStorageChange` — is unnecessary, `hasPendingWorkspaceStorageChange` need not be kept at all, no modal or new user-facing string is added, and no maintainer UX ruling is required.

4. "What we give up" is wrong. Extension users adding or removing the first workspace folder do not get an in-place swap today; VS Code restarts the extension host for them either way. Nothing observable is lost, so this is not the one-way door the finding describes (the residual risk is only that VS Code someday stops restarting, and the fallback then matches desktop/CLI: the root stays pinned until the window reloads).

5. Deleting the listener wholesale loses a real effect the finding missed: the completion callback runs `this.syncFullView()` (ProgressViewProvider.ts:201-202, method at :275). Keep the listener reduced to `void this.syncFullView()` — roughly 28 lines down to 3.

6. "Leaving `activeWorkspacePath` a plain read of `getWorkspacePath()`" is a real defect if implemented. `activeWorkspacePath` is a construction-time pin (workspaceStorage.ts:173) and reading the source live would let the storage root move mid-process under active runs. Keep the pin; that pin _is_ the desktop/CLI semantics being adopted.

7. `ProgressBackend.storageGeneration` is not a transition-only pair with `presentationReloadPending`. It is also read at ProgressBackend.ts:456, :489, :692, :696 to guard presentation leases and deletions. Once the transition goes it can never increment, so those four guards collapse too — more deletion than claimed, but the description is inaccurate.

8. The SessionHandle span is overstated. `:296-500 ~205 L` includes `enqueueRestartRepair`, `ensureRestartRepair`, `isRepairSuperseded` and `computeStartupSeedSet`, all of which stay (`ensureRestartRepair` still reads `storageGeneration`). Honest deletion there is about 160 lines: hooks interface (:121-126), `StorageRootChangeRefusedError` (:132-138), `reloadAfterStorageRootChange` (:296-347), `replaceStoresAfterStorageRootChange` (:398-470), `restoreRestartRepairAfterReplacementFailure` (:472-484), plus the `reloadTranscripts`/`transitionHooks` parameter threading in `repairStoresAfterRestart`.

9. Understated: the whole `ExtensionTexraConfig` class collapses, not just `enqueueWorkspaceTransition`. Its `update()` override and `workspaceQueue`/`transitionGeneration`/`failedTransitionGeneration` exist only to interleave with transitions, and `JsonStore.set` already serializes writes internally via `enqueueFlush` (jsonStore.ts:132-138), so ordering is preserved. texraConfig.ts goes 151 L to about 20 L (the `createExtensionTexraConfig` factory over a plain `JsonConfigProvider`) and `ProgressViewProvider.config` (:104) retypes from `ExtensionTexraConfig` to `JsonConfigProvider`.

10. Unstated rebase risk: SessionHandle.ts was reworked twice very recently by the single-owner-sessions campaign (#11311, #11318 both touch `reloadAfterStorageRootChange`), so this must be rebased fresh and not carried on a stale branch.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The finding's structural claim holds and its trace is accurate as far as it goes: four independent commit/rollback ledgers (provider `workspaceChangeRollback`, `SessionHandle.storageGeneration` + status snapshot/replay, `ProgressBackend.storageGeneration` + `presentationReloadPending`, `ExtensionTexraConfig.transitionGeneration` + queue + `previousStore`) plus a three-callback hook interface threaded from a VS Code file into the VS Code-free core, all serving one listener. One production implementor of the port; the four optional members exist only so a test fake can omit them, at the cost of three-valued `=== false` / `?.() !== true` call sites that manufacture the silent-degradation branch CLAUDE.md forbids.

The decisive fact the finding got close to but did not land is that VS Code's documented contract for `onDidChangeWorkspaceFolders` excludes the only case that can move `workspaceFolders[0]`, terminating and restarting the extension host instead. That converts the proposal from "trade a seamless swap for a reload prompt, needs a UX ruling" into "delete unreachable machinery; the host already restarts, exactly like desktop's `app.relaunch` and the CLI's per-process `cliWorkspaceCwd` pin". Same destination, no user-visible change, no new modal.

Honest accounting, all deletions, no additions beyond a short comment recording the VS Code restart contract at the surviving one-line listener:
production — interfaces.ts -27, workspaceStorage.ts -42, jsonConfigProvider.ts -6, SessionHandle.ts -160, runtime/index.ts -2, ProgressBackend.ts -62, texraConfig.ts -130, ProgressViewProvider.ts -25, about -454;
tests — ExtensionWorkspaceTransition.vitest.ts -433 (whole file), SessionRestartRepair.vitest.ts -345, ProgressBackendPresentation.vitest.ts about -290 (12 of 13 `it` blocks are workspace-move or move-vs-deletion cases; only "loads a presentation without reloading its live transcript" survives, taking most of the 188-line harness with the rest), WorkspaceStorage.vitest.ts -45, ExtensionTexraConfig.vitest.ts -35, about -1148.
Net about -1600; I report -1550 to leave slack for the knip/ratchet baseline edits and for test harness lines that turn out to be shared.

Elements removed: 4 port methods, 2 provider privates, `replaceWorkspaceStore`, `WorkspaceStorageTransitionHooks`, `StorageRootChangeRefusedError`, 3 SessionHandle methods, `ProgressBackend.reloadAfterStorageRootChange` + 2 fields, the `ExtensionTexraConfig` class with its 3 fields and `update()` override and `enqueueWorkspaceTransition`, 2 barrel re-exports, and 1 test file — about 22.

Risk is medium, not high. There is no user-visible change and no reversal of a recorded decision, but the correctness argument rests on documented VS Code behavior rather than an observed run, the diff spans the VS Code-free core and two hosts, and SessionHandle is churning. Worst case if the VS Code contract ever changes is that the extension keeps writing to the pinned bucket until the window reloads — the desktop/CLI behavior, not data loss. Ship it as its own PR, unbatched, with the VS Code doc quote in the PR body.

</details>

#### LanguageModelPort exposes two change events that no consumer distinguishes; fan them into one onDidChange inside the port

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -8 LoC, -1 elements (proposer claimed -13; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/platform/languageModel.ts`, `packages/extension/src/frontend/lm/createLanguageModelPort.ts`, `packages/extension/src/extension.ts`, `src/eventBus/AppSignals.ts`, `src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/ModelFactoryRouting.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandlerVscodeLm.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/model/RuntimeModelRegistry.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/frontend/createLanguageModelPort.vitest.ts`

**Evidence**

`LanguageModelPort` (src/platform/languageModel.ts:122-139) declares both `onDidChangeModels(listener)` (:126) and `onDidChangeAccess(listener)` (:138). Implementations: two. The real one (packages/extension/src/frontend/lm/createLanguageModelPort.ts:274-276 and :300-302) forwards them to two different VS Code events — `lmApi.onDidChangeChatModels` and `accessInformation.onDidChange` — and `UNAVAILABLE_LANGUAGE_MODEL_PORT` (languageModel.ts:149, :167) stubs both with the same empty disposable. Production subscribers: exactly one pair, packages/extension/src/extension.ts:316-319, which registers the SAME callback for both: `languageModel.onDidChangeModels(invalidateLanguageModels), languageModel.onDidChangeAccess(invalidateLanguageModels)`. `invalidateLanguageModels` (:311-315) does one thing for both: `invalidateRuntimeModelRegistry(); invalidateModelOptionsCache(); appSignals.emit('languageModelsChanged')`. No production code anywhere reads one event without the other — grep across src and packages returns only those two lines plus four test fakes that stub both identically (ModelFactoryRouting.vitest.ts:78,81; ModelHandlerVscodeLm.vitest.ts:99,102; RuntimeModelRegistry.vitest.ts:65,72) and one port test that asserts pure delegation (createLanguageModelPort.vitest.ts:164-168). src/eventBus/AppSignals.ts:72 already documents the pair as one fact: the `languageModelsChanged` signal is described as fed by 'lm.onDidChangeModels / onDidChangeAccess'. So two port methods, two implementations each, four test stubs, and one downstream decision. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search LanguageModelPort` returns nothing; neither survey doc mentions either method (`grep -c onDidChangeAccess` = 0 in both).

**Proposal**

Owner that absorbs the responsibility: `createLanguageModelPort`, which already holds both VS Code event sources. Collapse the two members into one `onDidChange(listener): Disposable`; inside the extension implementation return a `DisposableStore` (src/platform/disposable.ts already provides one) holding `lmApi.onDidChangeChatModels(listener)` and `accessInformation.onDidChange(listener)`. `UNAVAILABLE_LANGUAGE_MODEL_PORT` keeps one no-op. extension.ts:316-319 becomes a single `languageModel.onDidChange(invalidateLanguageModels)` push. The four test fakes drop one entry each and createLanguageModelPort.vitest.ts:164-168 asserts one subscription reaching both sources. Honest accounting: -3 lines of interface, -1 stub line, -1 subscription line, -8 lines across four fakes, +6 lines for the fan-in and its DisposableStore import, plus a small test edit — roughly -13 net, one fewer port method (12 -> 11). This is small; its value is that it removes the standing invitation to subscribe to one signal and forget the other, which is the drift the AppSignals comment is already compensating for.

**What we give up**

A future caller that wants to react to an access change (a consent prompt) without also reacting to the model list changing would have to re-split the event. Nothing wants that today, and the current no-op cost of the extra wake-up is one registry invalidation. If that caller appears, splitting back is a three-line change on one implementation.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. netLoc -13 is inflated by ~5. Honest count: languageModel.ts interface 2 lines -> 1 (-1); UNAVAILABLE_LANGUAGE_MODEL_PORT 2 lines -> 1 (-1); extension.ts 4-line push -> 1 line, `context.subscriptions.push(languageModel.onDidChange(invalidateLanguageModels));` is 78 chars and fits prettier's 80 (-3); three port fakes one line each (-3); createLanguageModelPort.vitest.ts drops one call line (-1); createLanguageModelPort.ts two 3-line methods -> one composite method (+1). Net -8.

2. "-8 lines across four fakes" is wrong on both count and arithmetic. There are THREE port fakes, one line each = -3: ModelFactoryRouting.vitest.ts:81, ModelHandlerVscodeLm.vitest.ts:102, RuntimeModelRegistry.vitest.ts:72. The claim's fourth item, createLanguageModelPort.vitest.ts:83, is a `vi.hoisted` mock of the VS CODE API (`accessInformation.onDidChange`), not of the port — it must STAY, because the fan-in test still has to assert that one `port.onDidChange(listener)` reaches both native sources. Two derived fakes spread the base and need no edit: RuntimeModelRegistry.vitest.ts:84 `failingDiscoveryPort` (`...languageModelPort([])`) and ModelHandlerVscodeLm.vitest.ts:105 `portThrowing` (calls `fakePort()`).

3. "one fewer port method (12 -> 11)" is wrong. `LanguageModelPort` has SIX members (isAvailable, selectModels, onDidChangeModels, sendRequest, countTokens, onDidChangeAccess). The change is 6 -> 5.

4. The evidence understates its own best argument by describing only the present state. `git show 1702822793` (#8400, 2026-07-14) proves `onDidChangeAccess` was born already wired to the same callback as `onDidChangeModels` in the same `context.subscriptions.push` — it never had an independent consumer. That, not the current grep, is what clears the round-3 "beat the recorded rationale" bar.

5. The proposal misses a semantic argument that makes the fan-in provably lossless: `toModelInfo` (createLanguageModelPort.ts:86) already folds `accessInformation.canSendRequest(model)` into `LanguageModelInfo.access`, so access changes and catalogue changes invalidate the same derived value and are recomputed by the same `selectModels` call.

6. src/eventBus/AppSignals.ts is listed in Paths but the proposal never says what changes there. The comment at :71-72 names both methods verbatim ("`lm.onDidChangeModels` / `onDidChangeAccess`") and must be rewritten to name the single event. Zero net lines; do not skip it, or the doc drifts the moment the method is gone.

7. Prefer a plain composite disposable over `DisposableStore`. `DisposableStore.dispose()` (src/platform/disposable.ts) collects child failures and calls `throwAggregated`, so a throwing native disposable would surface an aggregate error during VS Code's subscription teardown. `{ dispose() { models.dispose(); access.dispose(); } }` is four lines, needs no import, and matches the +1-line count above. (DisposableStore is not wrong, just heavier than the job.)

8. Not listed as a step: `packages/agent/dist/types/src/platform/languageModel.d.ts:77,80` still declares both members. It is a build artifact, so `npm run build:types` in packages/agent regenerates it — but the implementer should not hand-edit dist or be surprised to see the old pair there.

9. Line numbers in the claim were spot-checked and are all accurate at HEAD (languageModel.ts:126,138,149,167; createLanguageModelPort.ts:274,300; extension.ts:311-319; all three fake line numbers).

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Default is refuted, and at round 3 the usual kill is a dated ruling. Here the archaeology cuts the other way: `git log -S` plus `git show 1702822793` establishes that both port members were introduced already collapsed onto one callback in the same commit, so there is no design decision to overturn — only an accreted 1:1 mirroring of two VS Code APIs into a host-neutral port that exists to hide exactly that kind of host detail. The AppSignals doc comment at :71-72 is already compensating for the split by describing the pair as one fact, which is the drift signal.

The fan-in is provably lossless rather than merely "nothing needs it today": access state is a field of `LanguageModelInfo`, computed inside `toModelInfo` from `canSendRequest`, so both events invalidate the same derived catalogue and both are answered by the same `selectModels` recompute. There is no state one can invalidate that the other cannot.

No contract crossing: in-process port, not a SessionFact/AgentEvent, not NDJSON, not persisted, not a settings key, not result-JSON. The one surface worth naming is that `Platform.languageModel` reaches the exported SDK `Platform` type, but the package is unpublished by standing policy, the port is optional and defaulted in `nodeHost.ts`, and no ratchet pins member names.

The cost accounting in the claim is wrong in the usual direction — it double-counts a VS Code-API mock as a port fake and counts a 6-member interface as 12 methods. Honest result is still a clean removal: -8 LoC, one interface member. Small, but the change is bounded to eight files that are all about this one port, so it does not trip the section 14 R5 churn bar, and the recurring failure mode it closes (subscribe to one, forget the other) is the kind of drift a port is supposed to prevent rather than propagate.

</details>

### follow-up-inquiry — Follow-up, queueing and external inquiry

#### Delete the CLI's entire external-inquiry surface: the `inquiry` tool has been unreachable on the CLI host since 2026-06-27

- **Kind**: delete · **Risk**: high
- **Net**: -1050 LoC, -14 elements (proposer claimed -950; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/modals/ExternalInquiry.tsx`, `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/modals/ApprovalModal.tsx`, `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/subscribeApprovals.ts`, `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/approvalQueue.ts`, `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/appInteractionPolicy.ts`, `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/panes/SubagentListDisplay.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/approvalAdapter.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/approval/settleApprovals.ts`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/cliContext.ts`, `src/tools/inquiry/inquiryActions.ts`, `src/test-kernel/cli/ExternalInquiry.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/cli/TuiApprovalRetry.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/cli/ApprovalAdapter.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/cli/ApprovalQueue.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/cli/AppEscapeRouting.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/cli/AppInteractionPolicy.vitest.ts`

**Evidence**

End-to-end trace of the only producer. An `externalInquiry` approval can only originate from `HostInteractions.openExternalInquiry`, and that method has exactly ONE production caller repo-wide: `src/tools/inquiry/ExternalInquiryTool.ts:337` inside `executeAsk` (`rg openExternalInquiry src packages` → 3 production hits: the caller, the optional port declaration `src/agent/runtime/HostInteractions.ts:376`, and the attachment forwarder at :610-617; every other hit is a host implementation or a test). `ExternalInquiryTool` declares `unavailableHosts: ['cli']` (`src/tools/inquiry/ExternalInquiryTool.ts:230`), which `src/tools/registry.ts:193-199` projects into `getDefaultUnavailableToolNames('cli')`, and `src/agent/runtime/agentToolResolution.ts:113-127` drops any name in that set before the model ever sees the tool list. BOTH CLI launch paths pass it unconditionally: `packages/cli/src/runtime/runExecution.ts:474-476` (headless run/agents run/multi-agent run) and `packages/cli/src/chat/chatSessionController.ts:352` and `:444` (chat TUI start and every resume). Subagents inherit it via `runtimeUnavailableTools` on `RunContext`. So no CLI process can call `openExternalInquiry`, and nothing else emits that approval kind.

Dated history. The exclusion is not new: PR #6694 "Hide external inquiry tool from CLI, keep for VS Code/desktop" merged 2026-06-27 (be02f7706f) and added `'inquiry'` to `CLI_UNAVAILABLE_TOOLS`; its body states the tool "will not be dispatched during agent execution in any CLI mode (interactive chat, texra run, texra agents run, texra multi-agent run)". #10806 (b4bf6418eb, 2026-08-17) moved the fact onto the tool row, and #11436 (0c6d8f06ce) reshaped it to `unavailableHosts`. Two months of the CLI surface being dead code — and it kept accruing work: issue #10179 (CLOSED 2026-08-13, `area:cli`) added the `cause`/`reason` split to a drop path only the CLI produces.

What is still there. `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/modals/ExternalInquiry.tsx` (330 lines, whole file) plus its suite `src/test-kernel/cli/ExternalInquiry.vitest.ts` (320 lines); the TUI host method `subscribeApprovals.ts:208-211` and `handleExternalInquiry` (`:849-891`); the headless drop-with-explanation path `approvalAdapter.ts:285-297` whose message ("not available in non-TUI CLI runs … Use texra chat for the inquiry panel") is itself stale, since texra chat excludes it too; `denyExternalInquiryIfNoHumanInput` + `EXTERNAL_INQUIRY_YOLO_MESSAGE` (`settleApprovals.ts:33-34, 141-159`); the modal dispatch arm `ApprovalModal.tsx:69-75`; `approvalQueue.ts:297`; two policy branches `appInteractionPolicy.ts:206, 224`; `cliContext.ts:32`; `SubagentListDisplay.ts:54`.

Kill-condition audit. NOT a public wire format: `externalInquiry` never reaches the CLI NDJSON vocabulary — `packages/cli/src/runtime/cliNdjsonProgressEvents.ts` carries no approval kinds, and `projectCliSessionFact` (`sessionProgressSubscription.ts:62-80`) forwards only session facts. `inquiryThreadUpdated` (with its policy-protected `resumeOutcome`) stays — that fact is produced by the extension/desktop hosts and is untouched here. NOT a persisted file, NOT a settings key, NOT part of the texra-action result JSON. The `[inquiry] …` transcript rendering in `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/panes/transcriptEntries.ts:16-38` and `TranscriptEntry.tsx` STAYS — a CLI resume can legitimately replay a transcript containing an inquiry continuation authored by another host.

Dedupe: no hit for `denyExternalInquiryIfNoHumanInput`, `ExternalInquiry.tsx` (as a deletion target), or the CLI inquiry surface in either survey doc or in rounds12-index.md. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search inquiry` returns 10179/8174/5293/9142 — none proposes this.

**Proposal**

Owner that absorbs the responsibility: the tool's own `unavailableHosts: ['cli']` row already IS the single source of truth; the CLI just has to stop carrying a UI for a capability its host declares it does not have.

Migration, in order:

1. `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/approvalQueue.ts:61-66`: narrow the derivation key from `ProgressPermissionKind` to `Exclude<ProgressPermissionKind, 'externalInquiry'>`, with a comment citing #6694. This is the load-bearing step and it PRESERVES the anti-silent-gap property that block's docstring (issue #9021) exists for: every OTHER wire kind still forces a compile error when added. Once narrowed, the `externalInquiry` arms in `approvalQueue.ts:297`, `ApprovalModal.tsx:69`, and `appInteractionPolicy.ts:224` stop type-checking as reachable and are deleted; `appInteractionPolicy.ts:206`'s disjunct collapses to the `userQuestion` test.
2. Delete `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/modals/ExternalInquiry.tsx` and `src/test-kernel/cli/ExternalInquiry.vitest.ts` outright.
3. Delete `openExternalInquiry` from both CLI host-interaction implementations (`subscribeApprovals.ts:208-211`, `approvalAdapter.ts:285-297`) and `handleExternalInquiry` (`:849-891`). The port member is optional (`HostInteractions.ts:376` declares `openExternalInquiry?`), so no stub is needed; the extension and desktop implementations are untouched.
4. Delete `denyExternalInquiryIfNoHumanInput` and `EXTERNAL_INQUIRY_YOLO_MESSAGE` (`settleApprovals.ts:33-34, 141-159`); `settleHumanInputDenial` keeps its other caller.
5. Drop `'externalInquiry'` from `CliPromptRequest.kind` (`cliContext.ts:32`) and the `externalInquiry: 'inquiry'` row in `SubagentListDisplay.ts:54`.
6. Collapse `src/tools/inquiry/inquiryActions.ts:31-55`: with the CLI gone, `reason` (only producer `settleApprovals.ts:154`) and `cause` (only producers `subscribeApprovals.ts:870`, `approvalAdapter.ts:286`) have zero producers, so the hand-rolled 22-line `InquiryDropBase`/`InquiryDropAction`/`ExternalInquiryAction` three-way union becomes the canonical `InquiryActionMessage` from `@shared/schemas`, and the three-branch logging at `:97-109` becomes one `if (payload.feedback)`. This is exactly what closed issue #8174 ("Reuse the canonical external inquiry action union") asked for.
7. `handleExternalInquiryAction` (`inquiryActions.ts:153-159`) then has no production caller at all — only `persistExternalInquiryAction` + `continueExternalInquiryAction`, used by `src/controllers/progressView/ProgressViewCommandHandlers.ts:455-457`. Delete it and the `src/test-kernel/tools/ExternalInquiryAction.vitest.ts` cases that only drive it, or retarget that suite at the two-step pair the progress view actually uses.

Budget: ~-165 production LoC, ~-785 test LoC, -2 files, +4 lines for the `Exclude<>` and its comment.

**What we give up**

The ability to re-enable `inquiry` on the CLI without rebuilding a terminal answer modal. Be honest that this is a product decision with a live argument on the other side: the CLI's own stale comment (`approvalAdapter.ts:290`) frames the blocker as "non-TUI CLI runs cannot resume after finalize", which does not apply to the long-lived `texra chat` TUI, and #6694 bundled the TUI into a headless-shaped rationale without separate justification. If the maintainer intends to bring inquiry back to `texra chat`, this deletion is wrong and the right fix is instead to narrow `unavailableHosts` — which would make roughly 700 of these lines live again immediately. Ask before landing. If it lands, restoring later means rewriting the modal (the git history at packages/cli/src/chat/tui/modals/ExternalInquiry.tsx and issues #4794/#4826/#5278/#5282/#5293/#5339 record the UX work that went into it), which is maybe two days.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. "BOTH CLI launch paths pass it unconditionally" is false — there is a THIRD path. packages/cli/src/commands/resumeExecution.ts:154-159 calls `resumeRun(id, { session, executeWorkflow })` with NO `runtimeUnavailableTools` and no `approvalPromptsUnavailable`, for every non-ToolUse category (ToolUse resumes bounce to runChat at :146-148, which does pass it). Reflection runs honor the set at ResponseCycleFlow.ts:216-218, so that resume path applies no host fence at all. In practice nothing reaches openExternalInquiry there — `inquiry` is declared in exactly one agent file repo-wide, packages/extension/resources/tool_use_agents/assistant.yaml:58, a ToolUse agent; no reflection/workflow agent in packages/extension/resources/agents/ declares it. So the claim survives for shipped agents, but a user-authored reflection agent listing `inquiry` and resumed with `texra resume` would reach it. After the deletion the optional port member is gone, so that case throws "HostInteractions.openExternalInquiry is required" (ExternalInquiryTool.ts:339) instead of dropping with an explanation — louder, not silent, which is the correct direction, but the PR should say so. The missing exclusion on resumeExecution.ts is a separate real bug worth filing.

2. TWO FILES ARE MISSING FROM THE PATH LIST, both carrying live inquiry surface:
   - packages/cli/scripts/tui-harness.tsx — `SHOW_EXTERNAL_INQUIRY` env gate (:188), `EXTERNAL_INQUIRY_QUESTION` (:236-237), `EXTERNAL_INQUIRY_THREAD_ID` (:249), `appendHarnessExternalInquiryDecision` (:1134-1145), the enqueue block (:2032-2046), plus the `InquiryThreadId` and `buildContinuationText` imports (:53, :75). ~40 lines.
   - packages/cli/scripts/validate-tui.mjs — SIX recorded scenarios (`external-inquiry-long` :2047, `-long-80-cols` :2072, `-copy-question` :2098, `-submit-answer` :2115, `-submit-long-answer` :2139) plus `LONG_EXTERNAL_INQUIRY_ANSWER`/`..._FOR_TRUNCATION` (:82-84). ~135 lines. These exist because closed issues #5293 and #5339 asked for exactly this coverage.
     Consequence the finding does not state: the `-submit-answer` and `-submit-long-answer` scenarios are the ONLY drivers of the `[inquiry] … answered.` transcript-continuation rendering that the finding explicitly KEEPS (transcriptEntries.ts:16-38, transcriptEntryLayout.ts:385/:415-417, TranscriptEntry.tsx). Deleting them leaves that kept surface with zero end-to-end coverage. Either retarget those two scenarios to drive the continuation text directly, or accept and state the coverage loss.

3. A PUBLISHED USER-FACING DOC ADVERTISES THE FEATURE AND MUST BE AMENDED. docs/guide/research-tools.md:115 (on the texra.ai VitePress site, in the published allowlist) says: "In the CLI, the same flow appears as a terminal modal: paste the prepared question into your chat subscription, then paste the answer back into TeXRA." That sentence has been false since 2026-06-27. It is not in the finding's path list. It is also the strongest evidence that the CLI modal was an intended product feature, which sharpens the finding's own "ask before landing" caveat: by the host-parity audit's own "advertised ≠ real / wire-or-remove" framework (items FS3/FS5/FS6), the maintainer picks the arm. Deletion is a legitimate arm; the doc edit is mandatory either way.

4. STEP 1 IS INCOMPLETE. approvalQueue.ts:119 declares `export type PendingApprovalKind = ProgressPermissionKind;` — a SECOND derivation, independent of the `ApprovalPayload` mapped type at :61-66. `SubagentListDisplay.ts:48-56`'s `Record<PendingApprovalKind, string>` and both `appInteractionPolicy.ts` switches key off `PendingApprovalKind`, not `ApprovalPayload`. Narrowing only :61-66 leaves those three sites still requiring the `externalInquiry` arm. Both aliases need the `Exclude<>`; budget +4 more lines, not +4 total.

5. THE PRODUCTION/TEST LoC SPLIT IS INVERTED. Claimed "~-165 production, ~-785 test". ExternalInquiry.tsx alone is 330 production lines. Measured: production+scripts ≈ -670 (ExternalInquiry.tsx 330, subscribeApprovals ~50, settleApprovals ~29, approvalAdapter 13, inquiryActions ~38, ApprovalModal 9, appInteractionPolicy 6, approvalQueue/cliContext/SubagentListDisplay 3, tui-harness ~40, validate-tui ~135, knip-baseline ~20, minus +8 for the two `Exclude<>`s). Tests ≈ -500 (ExternalInquiry.vitest.ts 320 whole-file, TuiApprovalRetry ~60, ApprovalAdapter ~45, ApprovalQueue ~25, ExternalInquiryAction.vitest ~45, AppInteractionPolicy ~3).

6. AppEscapeRouting.vitest.ts IS A RETARGET, NOT A DELETE. Its five inquiry blocks (:1208-1450, ~210 lines) use `externalInquiry` as the VEHICLE for cross-stream escape-routing and foreground-swap tests, not as the subject. They must be rewritten against `userQuestion` (which shares the 'question' status arm), which is roughly LoC-neutral edit churn. The same is partly true of ApprovalQueue.vitest.ts:111/:128, whose "mixedQuestion" case exists precisely to prove externalInquiry and userQuestion both map to 'question' — that test loses its point and should be deleted, not retargeted.

7. "This is exactly what closed issue #8174 asked for" overstates it. #8174 ("Reuse the canonical external inquiry action union in progress IPC", CLOSED) scoped the PROGRESS-VIEW INBOUND schema (src/shared/schemas/progressView/inbound.ts), and that work already shipped — inbound.ts:191-204 now composes `InquirySubmitActionSchema`/`InquiryDropActionSchema` directly. The residual hand-rolled union at inquiryActions.ts:31-55 is in the same spirit but was not in #8174's scope. Say "consistent with", not "exactly what it asked for".

8. Minor: `CliPromptRequest.kind`'s `'externalInquiry'` member (cliContext.ts:32) already has ZERO producers today — approvalPrompts.ts only ever constructs `kind: 'approval'` (:258, :274). It is dead independently of this finding, so its removal is free rather than consequential.

9. Minor: the finding lists `src/tools/inquiry/inquiryActions.ts` producer analysis correctly — `reason` has one producer (settleApprovals.ts:154) and `cause` has two (subscribeApprovals.ts:870, approvalAdapter.ts:286), all CLI. The extension/desktop route (ProgressViewCommandHandlers.ts:455-457) only ever passes the canonical submit/drop shape, so the collapse to `InquiryActionMessage` type-checks. Verified.

10. Minor: four knip-baseline rows for packages/cli/src/chat/tui/modals/ExternalInquiry.tsx (config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json:305-323, incl. `boundedExternalInquiryQuestionLines`) leave the baseline with the file. Good — but the PR must re-run `npm run check:dead-code-ratchet` rather than hand-edit, per the rounds-1/2 nine-way-merge note.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The core structural claim is true and I could not break it. `ExternalInquiryTool` declares `unavailableHosts: ['cli']`; `getDefaultUnavailableToolNames('cli')` projects that; `resolveAgentTools`' `passesRuntimeGates` drops the name before the model schema is built; and both shipped CLI launch paths pass the set. `openExternalInquiry` has exactly one production caller (ExternalInquiryTool.ts:337), so nothing on the CLI can produce an `externalInquiry` approval, and the entire CLI modal + queue + policy + headless-drop surface is unreachable. The fence has been in place since be02f7706f (2026-06-27, #6694) and was independently upheld by the 2026-07-09 host-parity audit.

What I could not kill it with: it is not a duplicate of any of the 151 rounds-1/2 titles; no dated ruling protects the CLI-side UI (the one on-point ruling protects the fence, which is the finding's premise); it touches no ratcheted or frozen surface; it is not on the NDJSON wire, a settings key, persisted state, or the texra-action contract; and it reduces rather than spreads concepts.

Where it is wrong is completeness and accounting, not direction. The trace omits a third CLI launch path (`texra resume` for non-ToolUse categories, which applies no host fence at all — narrow, but the absolute wording is false). The path list omits two files carrying ~175 lines of live inquiry surface (tui-harness.tsx, validate-tui.mjs) plus a published texra.ai guide sentence that still advertises the CLI modal. The production/test LoC split is inverted. Step 1 misses the second derivation alias. And one test file listed as a deletion target is really a retarget.

Corrected, the deletion is bigger than claimed (~-1,050 net) and the element count is ~-14. I am raising risk from medium to high: the code change itself is mechanical and compiler-forced, but this removes a documented product capability, edits a published docs page, and deletes six recorded TUI validator scenarios that also happen to be the only end-to-end coverage of a rendering path the finding keeps. The finding is right to say "ask before landing" — that caveat should be a hard gate, not a footnote, and the maintainer's alternative (narrow `unavailableHosts` and make the guide true) is a live option that would make ~700 of these lines useful again.

</details>

#### Delete the per-turn `question.txt`/`context.txt`/`answer.txt` sidecars and their three manifest path fields: the manifest already stores the same text inline and is the only reader

- **Kind**: delete · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -69 LoC, -6 elements (proposer claimed -74; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/tools/inquiry/externalInquiryStorage.ts`, `src/test-kernel/tools/InquiryStorage.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/tools/InquiryContinuationSession.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/tools/ExternalInquiryResultFormatter.vitest.ts`

**Evidence**

Dual representation of the same fact, one side never read. Every inquiry turn writes its text TWICE. `recordOpenQuestion` (`src/tools/inquiry/externalInquiryStorage.ts:325-350`) does `ensureDir(t{N})` then `writeAtomic(t{N}/question.txt)` and optionally `writeAtomic(t{N}/context.txt)`, and `recordAnswerForOpenTurn` (`:394-402`) does `writeAtomic(t{N}/answer.txt)` — while the SAME strings are stored inline on the turn record (`question`, `context` at `:352-361`; `answer` at `:405-413`) and three path pointers are recorded (`questionRelativePath` `:52`, `contextRelativePath` `:53`, `answerRelativePath` `:72`).

Reader census. Zero production readers of either side of the pointer. `rg 'questionRelativePath|answerRelativePath|contextRelativePath' src packages` returns the three schema declarations, the two assignment sites, and FOUR test files (`src/test-kernel/tools/InquiryStorage.vitest.ts:410-411`, `InquiryContinuationSession.vitest.ts:74-75`, `ExternalInquiryResultFormatter.vitest.ts:23-25`) — no production consumer. `rg 'question\.txt|answer\.txt|context\.txt' src packages` returns only the writers in `externalInquiryStorage.ts` and the assertion at `InquiryStorage.vitest.ts:150-152`. Every real consumer reads the inline fields off the manifest: `buildReadOutput` (`ExternalInquiryTool.ts:151-182`), `manifestToTranscript` (`externalInquiryStorage.ts:496-511`), `manifestToSummary` (`:534-549`), `collectKnownSessionLinks` (`externalInquiryResultFormatter.ts:11-24`), `buildContinuationText` (`inquiryContinuation.ts:66-112`), and the panel replay at `src/controllers/progressView/backend/ExternalInquiryRequestHandler.ts:80-92`. The only mechanism that ever put these files in front of anyone — `ensureExternalInquiryThreadMirror`, which copied the whole thread directory into `runs/<executionId>/ei/<threadId>` so a run's file listing exposed it — was deleted by ROUND 1 ("Delete the external-inquiry execution mirror: nothing has read it since #11050", merged in #11416-#11422). This is the tail that deletion left behind: the sidecars had a (marginal) audience before round 1 and have had none since.

Same file, same class: `listThreadsByStatus`'s `since` parameter (`:584`, filter at `:587`, `:598`) has ZERO callers anywhere — production or test. All four call sites (`ExternalInquiryTool.ts:392`, `inquiryContinuation.ts:221`, `ExternalInquiryRequestHandler.ts:48` and `:61`) pass only `status`/`scope`/`streamId`, and one passes `limit`.

Kill-condition audit. This IS persisted user data, so I checked it as such. NOT referenced by any doc a user reads: `rg 'ei_threads|question\.txt|answer\.txt' docs prompts packages/extension/resources CHANGELOG.md` returns only `.agents/docs/archived/feature/2026-05-15-prd-external-inquiry-async.md:48` describing the layout as existing infrastructure, plus the round-1/round-2 survey docs. No UI reveals the thread directory (`EXTERNAL_INQUIRY_THREADS_DIR` has exactly one importer, `externalInquiryStorage.ts:6`). No wire format, no settings key, no texra-action field. Backward compatibility is free: the manifest schema is `z.looseObject` (`:107`), so existing manifests carrying the three retired keys still parse, and existing `t*/` directories are simply orphaned — nothing scans for them.

Dedupe: `questionRelativePath`, `answerRelativePath`, `turnDir` all return 0 hits in both survey docs and rounds12-index.md. Round 1's mirror item is a different surface (the run-directory copy, not the canonical sidecars). `git log -8 -- src/tools/inquiry/` shows no touch since #11441.

**Proposal**

Owner that absorbs the responsibility: the thread manifest, which already holds the authoritative full text of every question, context and answer and is the sole thing any consumer reads.

Migration:

1. `externalInquiryStorage.ts`: delete `questionRelativePath` (`:52`), `contextRelativePath` (`:53`) from `InquiryTurnBaseShape` and `answerRelativePath` (`:72`) from `AnsweredInquiryTurnSchema`.
2. In `recordOpenQuestion`, delete the `turnPath`/`td` computation, the `ensureDir(turnPath)`, the `writeOps` array and its `Promise.all`, and the two `normalizeFilePath` path assignments (`:326-350`), keeping only the inline `turn` object.
3. In `recordAnswerForOpenTurn`, delete `turnPath`, `td`, `answerRelativePath`, and the `writeAtomic(answer.txt)` (`:392-402`).
4. Delete the now-unused `turnDir` (`:135-137`) and `threadTurnDir` (`:147-149`) helpers and the `normalizeFilePath` import (`:28`); `path` stays for `threadDir`/`threadManifestPath`.
5. Same PR, same file: drop `since` (`:584`) and its `cutoff` filter (`:587`, `:598`).
6. Tests: drop the `t{N}` file-existence assertions at `InquiryStorage.vitest.ts:145-155` and the three path fields from the fixtures at `:410-411`, `InquiryContinuationSession.vitest.ts:74-75`, `ExternalInquiryResultFormatter.vitest.ts:23-25`.

Side benefit worth naming: this removes one `ensureDir` plus one-to-two `writeAtomic` calls per dispatch and one per answer, on the human-latency path where the panel is waiting to render.

Do not touch: `parentExecutionId` (live, read at `inquiryContinuation.ts:208-215`), `resumeOutcome`/`InquiryThreadUpdatedEvent` (policy-protected, reverted in round 2), or the `attachFiles` array (workspace paths the user uploads to the external model — a different concept from these sidecars).

**What we give up**

A human-readable on-disk copy of each turn. Someone spelunking `~/.texra/ei_threads/<id>/` would have to read `manifest.json` instead of `t1/question.txt` — the JSON has the same text, escaped. Nothing in the product, docs or tooling offers that directory to a user today. Restoring is trivial (re-add three writes) but any thread written in the interim would have no sidecars, so a future reader would need the manifest fallback anyway — which is an argument for deleting the sidecars rather than keeping a format only half the corpus honors.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. BACKWARD-COMPAT MECHANISM IS MISSTATED. The claim says "the manifest schema is z.looseObject (:107), so existing manifests carrying the three retired keys still parse." `z.looseObject` is only on the MANIFEST wrapper; the turn schemas (`OpenInquiryTurnSchema` :57, `AnsweredInquiryTurnSchema` :66) are plain `z.object`, i.e. strip mode. Old manifests do still parse — removing a required field is never a parse hazard for data that has it — but the retired keys are STRIPPED on read and vanish from the file on the next `writeThreadManifest`. Right conclusion, wrong reason.

2. MISSED HAZARD — FORWARD compat, not backward. `EXTERNAL_INQUIRY_THREADS_DIR` lives under the shared root: src/common/storage/storageLayout.ts:19 comments "Global (non-workspace-scoped) storage directory names, **shared across hosts**", and GlobalStorageFS defaults to `~/.texra/`. `schemaVersion` stays `z.literal(1)`, so the version gate at :180-191 cannot flag the change. An OLDER extension/desktop/CLI build reading a manifest written — or merely rewritten, e.g. by a draft autosave — by the new build will fail `questionRelativePath: z.string().min(1)`, and `readThreadManifest` warns and returns `null`, which every consumer treats as "no such thread". That thread then disappears from the older host's Background Tasks panel and from `inquiry read`. No data loss (the file is intact, the failure is a loud `logger.warn`, so it clears the §15 silent-degradation bar), but it is an observable mixed-version regression on shared user state and must be stated in the PR body — the same disclosure round 2 demanded for the NDJSON payload change. This is why I rate the item medium, not low.

3. TEST COST UNDERCOUNTED BY ONE LINE. `atomicWriteSpy` (InquiryStorage.vitest.ts:121) exists only to feed the 147-155 assertion (both were added by #11084); it must be deleted in the same edit or lint flags an unused local. The migration list stops at "drop the assertions at :145-155".

4. LINE NUMBERS DRIFT — re-read before editing. `recordOpenQuestion`'s deletable span is 323-350, not ":326-350": `turnPath` is at 323, `ensureDir` at 326, `td`/`questionRelativePath`/`contextRelativePath` at 328-334, `writeOps`+`Promise.all` at 336-350; the two inline path assignments in the turn literal are at 357-358 (the claim's ":352-361" points at the whole literal). In `recordAnswerForOpenTurn` the deletions are 394-396 plus the write at 399-402, and `answerRelativePath` on the answered turn is at 411. `manifestToTranscript` is at 504-517, not ":496-511". The `cutoff` filter line is ~595, not ":598". `manifestToSummary` :534, `listThreadsByStatus` :579-584 and `turnDir`/`threadTurnDir` at :135/:147 are correct.

5. MIGRATION LIST OMITS A LIVE DOC-COMMENT REFERENCE TO `since`. externalInquiryStorage.ts:483 reads "updatedAt drives listing sort order, the `since` freshness filter, and the 'Updated: ...' text shown to the model" — that clause must be amended in the same PR or it becomes a dangling reference to a deleted parameter.

6. `since` WAS DESIGNED, NOT ACCIDENTAL. .agents/docs/archived/feature/2026-05-15-prd-external-inquiry-async.md:125-137 specifies `since?: string // ISO timestamp lower bound` on both `listThreadsByStatus` and `listRecentClosedThreads`. That is an API sketch in a shipped PRD, not a ruling, and I confirmed zero callers ever passed it (four production sites: ExternalInquiryTool.ts:392-396 passes status/scope/streamId; inquiryContinuation.ts:221; ExternalInquiryRequestHandler.ts:48-52 passes status/scope/limit and :61-64 status/scope). It is textbook speculative generality — but the PR body should name the PRD line so a reviewer does not read the deletion as a spec violation.

7. UNDER-SOLD PRECEDENT WORTH CITING. #11050 already deleted the mirror-side path metadata (`ExternalInquiryExecutionMirrorPaths`, `executionMirrorPaths`) from this exact file for the identical reason. Naming it makes this the third and final tranche of the same removal, not a fresh design change.

8. The "do not touch" list is correct and load-bearing: `resumeOutcome` really was reverted as policy-protected (it is still present at src/shared/schemas/inquiry.ts:48-61 on HEAD), and this finding stays clear of it.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The central factual claim — zero production readers on either side of the pointer — I re-verified independently rather than trusting the census. `rg 'questionRelativePath|answerRelativePath|contextRelativePath'` across the whole repo returns exactly eleven hits: three schema declarations (:52, :53, :72), six assignment/local sites inside the two writers, and four test-fixture lines. `rg 'question\.txt|answer\.txt|context\.txt'` returns only the writers and one test assertion. Neither of the two shapes that leave this module carries a path: `InquiryThreadSummary` (six scalar fields) is what rides the SessionFact and the CLI NDJSON forwarder, and `InquiryTranscriptTurn` (seven fields, all inline text) is what the panel renders. So the fields are write-only in the strict sense, and the .txt files are write-only in the same sense now that round 1 deleted the mirror that once surfaced them in a run's file listing.

This is round 3, so I weighted the "beat the recorded rationale" bar heavily and went looking for the ruling that usually kills these. The two candidates both fail on inspection. #11050 explicitly scoped itself away from the sidecars rather than defending them. #11084 is seven days old and touches these exact three writes, which looks damning until you read #11035's rationale: it is a durability-consistency fix (payload written non-atomically while the sibling manifest was atomic), triggered by a scheduled-refactoring note in #11017, with no claim that anything reads the payloads. Hardening a write is orthogonal to establishing a reader. The PRD is descriptive about the directory layout and, tellingly, its own §6.1 turn-record spec never lists the path fields.

The one thing the claim genuinely missed is the direction of the compatibility risk. It checked "will old manifests still load in the new build" (yes, trivially) and stopped. The harder question is "will new manifests still load in an old build", and here `~/.texra/ei_threads` being explicitly shared across hosts plus a frozen `schemaVersion: 1` means the answer is no: three required fields disappear under an unchanged version number, and the older reader's Zod parse fails into the "treat as missing" arm. I chose not to refute on this because the arm is loud (logger.warn), no user data is destroyed, the file remains readable by the current build, and the affected population is mixed-version installs of an agent-driven feature — but it is a real observable change, it is exactly the class of thing that got two round-2 items reverted, and it moves the risk rating up a notch and adds a mandatory PR-body disclosure.

On cost, I reconstructed the diff rather than accepting -74. Production: 3 schema lines, 26 lines in `recordOpenQuestion` (turnPath, ensureDir, the td/path block, the writeOps array and its `Promise.all`), 2 inline assignments in the turn literal, 9 in `recordAnswerForOpenTurn`, 8 for the two path helpers, 1 import, 3 for `since` — 52. Tests: 10 in InquiryStorage.vitest (the nine-line assertion block plus the now-orphaned spy), 2 more fixture lines there, 2 in InquiryContinuationSession, 3 in ExternalInquiryResultFormatter — 17. Total -69. There is no migration tail at all, which is what makes this cheap: no call site to update because the fields have no call sites, no test to rewrite because every affected test line is a deletion, and no doc to amend beyond one in-file comment clause. Elements: three schema fields, two path helpers, one parameter — the claimed -6 is right.

</details>

#### Collapse `ExternalInquiryPermission`'s two-arm discriminated union: both arms are structurally identical and `mode` drives one badge string derivable from `transcript`

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -42 LoC, -4 elements (proposer claimed -40; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/shared/schemas/prompts.ts`, `src/tools/inquiry/ExternalInquiryTool.ts`, `src/controllers/progressView/backend/ExternalInquiryRequestHandler.ts`, `packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/components/ExternalInquiryPanel.ts`

**Evidence**

A discriminated union that discriminates nothing. `src/shared/schemas/prompts.ts:141-151` builds `ExternalInquiryPermissionSchema` from `NewExternalInquiryPermissionSchema` and `FollowUpExternalInquiryPermissionSchema`, each of which is `ExternalInquiryPermissionBaseSchema.extend({ mode: z.literal(...) })` — the SAME base, no arm-specific field. The file's own comment at `:130-132` admits it: "Both inquiry modes share the same fields and differ only in the `mode` discriminator. Hydration fields live on both so the discriminated union exposes a common surface for renderers." So the union produces zero narrowing at zero call sites.

This must beat a dated ruling, and it does. Issue #6704 (CLOSED 2026-06-29) asked to "Refactor ExternalInquiryPermissionSchema to use z.discriminatedUnion() … This should simplify validation and improve type narrowing at call sites." It shipped as 56013594cb. I read that diff: the hydration fields (`sessionLinks`, `draft`, `transcript`) were put on BOTH arms in the very first commit, with the inline comment "Included so the discriminated union produces a common surface for renderers that access these fields." The stated goal was therefore never achieved and is structurally unachievable — narrowing requires arms that differ, and these cannot differ because the renderer reads the hydration fields regardless of mode. The issue's premise ("per the schema composition guidelines in CLAUDE.md") is also inapplicable: CLAUDE.md prefers `z.discriminatedUnion()` over `z.custom<T>()` for genuinely variant shapes, not for tagging one invariant shape.

One production reader, and two divergent producers. `rg "\\.mode" over the inquiry surface returns exactly one production READ: `packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/components/ExternalInquiryPanel.ts:262`, `${data.mode === 'followUp' ? 'follow-up' : 'new question'}`inside`renderHeader`. The two production WRITERS derive it by DIFFERENT rules: `src/tools/inquiry/ExternalInquiryTool.ts:310`uses`const isFollowUp = !!input.thread_id`, while `src/controllers/progressView/backend/ExternalInquiryRequestHandler.ts:94`uses`manifest.turns.length > 1`. They also disagree on payload content for the same badge: the tool's `mode: 'new'` arm (`:329-334`) hard-nulls `sessionLinks`/`draft`/`transcript`, whereas the replay handler (`:80-96`) always populates all three even when it labels the permission `'new'`. That is a live inconsistency — a first-turn thread renders with no draft at dispatch time but with a restored draft after a webview reload — and it exists only because two places independently answer one question.

Kill-condition audit. `ExternalInquiryPermissionSchema` reaches the webview via `src/shared/schemas/progressView/outbound.ts:31` — the extension/desktop progress-view IPC, which is bundled and shipped with the backend in the same artifact, not a versioned public wire. It is NOT in the CLI NDJSON vocabulary (`projectCliSessionFact` in `packages/cli/src/runtime/sessionProgressSubscription.ts` handles session facts, not permissions). It is NOT persisted: `ExternalInquiryRequestHandler.replayOpenPermissions` rebuilds permissions from the thread manifest on every replay (`:60-104`), so no stored payload carries `mode`. Not a settings key, not a texra-action field.

Dedupe: `ExternalInquiryPermissionSchema` and `NewExternalInquiryPermissionSchema` return 0 hits in both survey docs and rounds12-index.md. Round 2's shipped inquiry items were `setActiveStream.ensureVisible` and `inquiryThreadUpdated.resumeOutcome` (the latter REVERTED) — different fields; this proposal touches neither.

**Proposal**

Owner that absorbs the responsibility: the `transcript` array, which is already on the permission and already tells the panel exactly how many turns the thread has.

Target shape — one object schema, no `mode`:

1. `src/shared/schemas/prompts.ts`: delete `NewExternalInquiryPermissionSchema` (`:141-142`), `FollowUpExternalInquiryPermissionSchema` (`:145-146`) and the `z.discriminatedUnion` (`:148-151`); export `ExternalInquiryPermissionSchema = PermissionBaseSchema.extend(CommonExternalInquiryFieldsSchema.shape).extend(ExternalInquiryHydrationFieldsSchema.shape)` — i.e. what `ExternalInquiryPermissionBaseSchema` (`:133-135`) already is, renamed.
2. `packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/components/ExternalInquiryPanel.ts:262`: `${(data.transcript?.length ?? 0) > 1 ? 'follow-up' : 'new question'}`. Verify against the four cases: tool follow-up (`manifestToTranscript` includes the new open turn, so length ≥ 2) → 'follow-up'; tool first turn (`transcript: null`) → 'new question'; replay with `turns.length > 1` → 'follow-up'; replay first turn → 'new question'. All four match today's labels.
3. `src/tools/inquiry/ExternalInquiryTool.ts:310-335`: delete `isFollowUp` and the ternary; build ONE `permission` object that always carries `sessionLinks: collectKnownSessionLinks(manifest)`, `draft: getOpenTurnDraft(manifest)`, `transcript: manifestToTranscript(manifest)`. This is the behavior change that closes the divergence — a first-turn dispatch now carries its (single-entry) transcript and any saved draft, matching what a reload already shows. `renderTranscript` should be checked to hide a single-turn transcript, which it must already do since replay populates it for first turns today.
4. `src/controllers/progressView/backend/ExternalInquiryRequestHandler.ts:93-97`: `this.stagePresentationForReplay(basePermission)`, ternary gone.
5. Drop the `mode:` line from the ~12 test fixtures listed by `rg "mode: 'new'|mode: 'followUp'" src/test-kernel`.

Honest budget: -12 in `prompts.ts`, -14 in the tool, -3 in the replay handler, ±0 in the panel, -12 across fixtures; +1 for the derivation. Net ≈ -40 LoC, and the concept `mode` stops existing.

**What we give up**

An explicit tag on the wire saying which kind of inquiry this is. If a future panel needs to branch on more than a badge — say, a different layout for follow-ups — it re-derives from `transcript.length` (one expression) or re-adds a field with its reader in the same PR, per build-implies-delete. What is NOT given up is validation strength: the collapsed schema validates the identical field set, because both arms already did.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

C1. The "live inconsistency" is overstated and is not a user-visible bug. For a first-turn dispatch the thread cannot have prior session links — `collectKnownSessionLinks` (src/tools/inquiry/externalInquiryResultFormatter.ts) filters `turn.kind !== 'open'` and returns `undefined` when empty — and cannot have a saved draft, because `recordOpenQuestion` creates the thread fresh. So the tool's hard-nulls and the replay handler's computed values agree in CONTENT at dispatch time; the only difference arises after the user types and persists a draft, where showing it on reload is correct. The change is representational (null -> single-entry transcript), not a defect fix. The PR should say "removes a redundant second answer to one question", not "closes a live divergence".

C2. Proposal steps 2 and 3 list two checks as open; both are already confirmed. `renderTranscript` (ExternalInquiryPanel.ts:273-277) filters to `turn.answer` and returns `nothing` when zero answered turns remain, so an always-populated single open turn renders nothing. `renderSessionLinks` (:413+) guards on `sessionLinks.length`. No render change results from step 3.

C3. Badge equivalence is stronger than the write-up argues. `recordOpenQuestion` throws `External inquiry thread not found` when `params.threadId` is passed and no manifest exists (externalInquiryStorage.ts:291-293), and refuses re-dispatch on an already-open thread (:296-301). So `!!input.thread_id === true` implies the manifest already had >= 1 turn, hence `manifestToTranscript(manifest).length >= 2`. There is no reachable input where today's `mode` and the derived label differ — this is an exact equivalence, not a four-case coincidence.

C4. `NewExternalInquiryPermissionSchema` and `FollowUpExternalInquiryPermissionSchema` are NOT exported at HEAD (they were exported in 56013594cb and unexported since), and nothing does `.options`, `.shape`, or `Extract<ExternalInquiryPermission, ...>`. Blast radius is smaller than the write-up implies; no export-surface or knip-baseline churn.

C5. Fixture count: 13 sites, not "~12 in src/test-kernel" — 12 under src/test-kernel (PermissionActionEventHandlers:84, ExternalInquiryPanelTextarea:46, ExtensionHostInteractions:787 and :797, ApprovalAdapter:317, TuiApprovalRetry:399/:427/:633, ApprovalQueue:50, cli/ExternalInquiry:52, InquiryStorage:236/:243, AppEscapeRouting:1217/:1235 — that is 14 lines) plus `packages/cli/scripts/tui-harness.tsx:2038`, which the proposal omits entirely. One of them (InquiryStorage.vitest.ts:243) sits inside a `toMatchObject` assertion, not a construction fixture.

C6. Element count -4 is generous. Honest: -2 schema consts (three named schemas collapse to one exported name), -1 field (`mode`), -1 local (`isFollowUp`) — call it -4 only if the field and local count as elements; -2 if only exported/named schema elements count.

C7. Unstated but load-bearing for the PR: `PermissionBaseSchema` is `z.strictObject` (prompts.ts:24), so once `mode` is dropped any payload still carrying it fails validation. That is safe here (no persisted, trace, or NDJSON payload carries it — verified above), but schema, both producers, the panel, the fixtures, and tui-harness.tsx must land in one commit; a split lands a hard parse failure at the webview boundary.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

Verified against HEAD 91b9b49444 (post rounds 1-2). Every cited line number is exact: prompts.ts:130-132 comment, :133-135 base, :141-142 / :145-146 arms, :148-151 union; ExternalInquiryTool.ts:310 `isFollowUp`, :324/:331 the two `mode` writes, :329-334 the hard-nulled arm; ExternalInquiryRequestHandler.ts:93-96 the `turns.length > 1` ternary over an always-hydrated base; ExternalInquiryPanel.ts:262 the sole production read. A repo-wide search for `mode === 'followUp'` / `mode: 'new'` / `mode: 'followUp'` confirms exactly one production reader, two production writers, and the rest fixtures (the other `'followUp'` hits are `mediaAttachmentPolicy`'s unrelated media-round vocabulary). The union produces zero narrowing anywhere. The dated ruling (#6704) is genuine but its stated benefit was structurally unreachable from the first commit, which the finding proves with the right artifact. External-consumer analysis holds under my own re-walk: the permission never enters StreamSnapshot, StreamLogEntry, TraceDocument, or the CLI NDJSON path (headless drops it in approvalAdapter). Derivation from `transcript.length` is exactly equivalent given recordOpenQuestion's not-found and already-open guards. Net is pure deletion: prompts.ts about -14, tool about -13, handler -4, panel 0, fixtures -14, harness -1, plus a line or two of comment back = about -42 LoC. Risk is low: one badge string, no wire, no persistence, no ratcheted edge; the only care needed is landing all producers with the strict schema in one commit.

</details>

#### One owner for "can this stream accept input": delete the `appendFollowUp` bypass that writes past the follow-up queue's own admission boundary

- **Kind**: collapse · **Risk**: medium
- **Net**: -30 LoC, -4 elements (proposer claimed -40; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/agent/followUp/ToolUseFollowUp.ts`, `src/agent/followUp/ToolUseFollowUpQueueManager.ts`, `src/agent/core/flows/IToolUseSession.ts`, `src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/ToolUseSessionLifecycle.ts`, `src/agent/runtime/ExecutionHandle.ts`

**Evidence**

Two mechanisms answer one question, and the weaker one wins. `ToolUseFollowUpQueue` documents itself (`src/agent/followUp/ToolUseFollowUpQueueManager.ts:62-74`) as "the session-owned continuation boundary", and `terminalize` (`:235-247`) exists so that "a producer that is not an owner … cannot recreate the queue and trigger a resume of a run that is gone; only an explicit claim reopens the stream." But `admitFollowUp` (`src/agent/followUp/ToolUseFollowUp.ts:129-151`) routes around it: when `submit(streamId, item, 'live_owner')` returns `not_owned` — which means the manager has NO entry for this stream, i.e. the entry was terminalized or terminally released — the code does `target.context.session.appendFollowUp(item)` at `:148`, then `notifyFollowUpSent` and reports `{ status: 'sent' }`. The registry's flow-context pointer thus overrides the manager's refusal. The two states that produce this are real, not hypothetical: `releaseStreamResources` calls `session.followUps.terminalize(streamId)` (`src/tools/approval/index.ts:101`) when a host deletes a stream; `handle.suspend` does the same (`src/agent/runtime/AgentRunLifecycle.ts:715`); and a native child loop does `release(queueLease, 'terminal')` (`src/agent/runtime/childRunLoop.ts:832`, `:1093`), all of which delete the entry while the flow context can still be attached during teardown.

What the bypass actually delivers into. `terminalize` calls `entry.queue.dispose()` first (`:242`), and `FollowUpQueue.dispose` (`FollowUpQueue.ts:155-158`) resolves the parked consumer with `null`. `ToolUseWaitNode` reads that as a cancellation (`src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/nodes/ToolUseWaitNode.ts:144-152` → `noteParkedWaitCancelled` → `{ kind: 'stop' }`). So the flow the bypass hands the message to has already been told to stop. The user gets `status: 'sent'` and a `followUpSent` fact for a message that will not be read.

This surface exists ONLY for that line. `appendFollowUp` on `IToolUseSession` (`src/agent/core/flows/IToolUseSession.ts:8`) has exactly one production caller repo-wide — `ToolUseFollowUp.ts:148`. Its one implementation is `ToolUseSessionLifecycle.appendFollowUp` (`src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/ToolUseSessionLifecycle.ts:39-41`). And `LiveToolUseFlowContext` carries a whole member for it: `readonly session: Pick<ToolUseFlowContext['session'], 'appendFollowUp'>` (`src/agent/runtime/ExecutionHandle.ts:99`), whose only read anywhere is `target.context.session.appendFollowUp` — `rg '\.session\.appendFollowUp' src packages` returns one production hit. `childRunLoop.ts:374` even documents its own context as deliberately NOT implementing this duck-type. Every other producer already goes through the manager: `submitFollowUp` for hosts and inquiry, `deliverChildRunFollowUp` for child results, `enqueueSynthetic` for maintenance turns, `restore`/`drainItems` for resume.

Counter-evidence I must state, because it is three days old. Commit 3fb940c75a (2026-08-23, part of #11329, "five-kind FollowUpSubmission, no QueueEntry.lifecycle") says verbatim: "not_owned stays a distinct kind: the live-target arm's direct appendFollowUp delivery depends on distinguishing 'no queue entry' from 'boundary refuses input', so the cut list's four-kind target is not reachable without conflating them." That commit took the delivery path as GIVEN while collapsing the kinds around it; it did not argue that the delivery is correct. This proposal is the missing half — delete the delivery, and the cut list's own four-kind target (`duplicate | delivered_live | queued | refused`) becomes reachable. The design doc for that campaign, `.agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-08-23-single-owner-sessions.md`, is where the terminalize invariant this bypass violates was written down.

Kill-condition audit. `SubmitFollowUpResult`'s three statuses are unchanged. No wire field, no persisted field, no settings key, no texra-action field is touched — `FollowUpSubmission` and `IToolUseSession` are in-process types with no serialization. Dedupe: `IToolUseSession`, `LiveToolUseFlowContext` return 0 hits in both survey docs; round 2's only `appendFollowUp` mention (round2 doc line 827) is the unrelated `<C>` type-parameter erasure in `followUpMessages.ts`.

**Proposal**

Owner that absorbs the responsibility: `ToolUseFollowUpQueue`. It becomes the ONLY thing that can put an item into a stream's queue, which is what its own docstring already claims.

Migration:

1. `src/agent/followUp/ToolUseFollowUp.ts:144-150`: replace the `refused`-only check plus the direct-delivery tail with a single arm — `if (submission.kind === 'refused' || submission.kind === 'not_owned') return { status: 'failed', reason: 'not_resumable' };`.
