The `no_session` arm below already words a better refusal via `classifyRefusal`, so consider routing there instead: returning `{ status: 'no_session' }` makes `submitFollowUp` (`:254-260`) classify the run and tell the user "This run has finished" or "live in another TeXRA window" rather than the generic "cannot accept messages right now". That is a strictly better message for the case that actually occurs (a stream the user just deleted).
2. `src/agent/followUp/ToolUseFollowUpQueueManager.ts:55-60`: fold `not_owned` into `refused`, reaching the four-kind union #11329's cut list wanted; `submit`'s `if (!entry) return { kind: 'not_owned' }` at `:161` becomes `refused`.
3. `src/agent/core/flows/IToolUseSession.ts:8`: delete `appendFollowUp`. `appendSyntheticFollowUp`, `hasQueuedFollowUp`, `waitForFollowUp` and `noteParkedWaitCancelled` all keep their callers.
4. `src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/ToolUseSessionLifecycle.ts:39-41`: delete the method.
5. `src/agent/runtime/ExecutionHandle.ts:99`: delete the `session` member from `LiveToolUseFlowContext`. `ownerSession`, `modelHandler`, `requestImmediateCompaction`, `switchModel`, `interrupt` and `modelSwitchDisabledReason` all keep their readers (`executionRegistry.ts:561-573`, `childRunLoop.ts:406`).
6. Update the `LiveToolUseFlowContext` stubs in the execution-registry and follow-up tests that currently supply `session: { appendFollowUp }`.

Budget: ~-25 production LoC across five files plus ~-15 in test stubs; -1 interface method, -1 implementation, -1 type member, -1 submission kind.

Before landing, run the one experiment that settles reachability: instrument `ToolUseFollowUp.ts:148` with a `logger.warn` on a dogfood build, delete a stream mid-run and send a follow-up in the same tick. If it never fires, this is pure dead-path removal; if it fires, the deletion is still right but the user-visible message changes from a false "sent" to a true refusal, and that is the change to describe in the PR body.

**What we give up**

A last-chance delivery into a flow whose queue the session has already torn down. Concretely: a message submitted in the window between stream deletion (or child-loop terminal release) and flow detach currently reports `sent` and lands in a disposed queue; afterwards it reports a worded failure and the user can re-send. Given the single-owner campaign's own warning that "the recurring defect in deletion work is … the invariant that stopped being enforced beside it — a refusal path dropped a seeded follow-up batch", the reviewer should confirm no resume/seed path reaches `admitFollowUp`'s `active` arm with a terminalized entry: `resumeRun`'s seeded batch goes through `queue(queueLease).restore(...)` (`src/agent/runtime/resumeRun.ts:356`), not through `submitFollowUp`, so it is not affected — but that is the check to redo at implementation time, not to take from this report.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. WRONG: "the flow the bypass hands the message to has already been told to stop … a message that will not be read." Only conditionally true. `FollowUpQueue` has no disposed flag — `dispose()` (`FollowUpQueue.ts:155-158`) just calls `cancelWait()` and clears `queued`; a subsequent `enqueue` still stores the item and a later `waitForNext` would return it. The "never read" outcome holds only when the flow was _parked_ at dispose time (the parked waiter resolves `null` → `noteParkedWaitCancelled` → `{kind:'stop'}`, `ToolUseWaitNode.ts:144-152`, verified). If the stream is deleted while the flow is mid-round, the bypassed item can still be consumed at the next wait — so the change converts a (weird but working) delivery into a refusal in that sub-case. The stream is deleted either way, so no user-visible surface remains, but the PR body must say this rather than "will not be read".

2. WRONG: "`handle.suspend` does the same (`AgentRunLifecycle.ts:715`) … while the flow context can still be attached during teardown." The suspend callback's own comment two lines above says "the live tool-use session and its interrupt handler are already gone by the time this returns (runToolUseFlow's finally)". Not a window.

3. WRONG: "a native child loop does `release(queueLease, 'terminal')` (`childRunLoop.ts:832`, `:1093`) … while the flow context can still be attached." `:832` is inside `unwindSetup`, which runs only when setup threw before `run` — no flow context ever attached. `:1093` is the loop's `finally`, which runs after every turn settled and after each inner `runToolUseFlow` already detached its own context. Neither is a window.

4. MISSING (strengthens the claim): `runToolUseFlow`'s teardown is detach-then-release — `attemptTeardown('detaching the live flow', …)` at `:596` precedes `attemptTeardown('releasing the follow-up queue', …)` at `:652` — so ordinary run teardown provably cannot produce active+not_owned. The single genuine window is host stream deletion via `releaseStreamResources` → `session.followUps.terminalize(streamId)` (`src/tools/approval/index.ts:101`), whose two callers are `createSessionStores.ts:28` and `ProgressViewMessageHandler.ts:220` — both "the user deleted this stream", i.e. the UI that would send a follow-up is already gone. This is effectively a dead path.

5. MISSING from the migration list: `src/test-kernel/agent/followUp/FollowUpQueue.vitest.ts:159,171,203` assert `{ kind: 'not_owned' }` directly, and two of those test names encode the distinction ("forgets a terminal stream so a late live-owner submission finds no owner", "deletion invalidates a live generation"). Three assertions flip to `'refused'` plus name edits.

6. UNDERSTATED: step 6 calls the test work "stubs that supply `session: { appendFollowUp }`". Three of the sites are not stubs. `SessionResumeRetrieval.vitest.ts:423, 1123, 1497` call `context.session.appendFollowUp(...)` on the REAL flow context inside `attach` as a precise injection point for "input that races the resume startup window"; deleting the method from `ToolUseSessionLifecycle` forces them onto `session.followUps.submit(streamId, item, 'live_owner')` (equivalent — the entry exists at attach time, since the lifecycle claims at `runToolUseFlow.ts:175` before `liveAttachment.attach()` at `:428`). And `ToolUseFollowUpProgressEvents.vitest.ts:93-110` currently proves `status:'sent'` _through the bypass_; it must claim a real `'flow'` lease so `delivered_live` produces the same assertion, or the test loses its subject.

7. OPTIMISTIC LoC: "-25 production LoC across five files" is not there. The honest production delta is ~-14: ToolUseFollowUp.ts ~-5, queue manager -1 union member (the `if (!entry)` line changes rather than disappears) plus a docstring edit, IToolUseSession.ts -1, ToolUseSessionLifecycle.ts -3, ExecutionHandle.ts -1 plus a doc-comment amend, and two comments that become false and must be rewritten rather than deleted (`childRunLoop.ts:374` "Does NOT implement the session duck-type (no `session.appendFollowUp`)" and `resumeRun.ts:365`). Test delta ~-16. Total ≈ -30, not -40. Element count -4 is correct.

8. MINOR: step 1's preferred route (returning `{status:'no_session'}` so `classifyRefusal` words it) is sound — `submitFollowUp:246-252` handles that Admission arm — but note the existing `no_session` arm also emits a `logger.warn`, so that route adds a log line on a path that fires when a user deletes a stream. Either arm is acceptable; pick deliberately.

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

The core structural claim is verified at every load-bearing hop. `ToolUseFollowUpQueue` documents itself as the session-owned continuation boundary and `terminalize` exists precisely so a non-owner cannot put work back into a stream that is gone; `admitFollowUp:148` is a second, weaker answer to the same question that wins whenever the boundary refuses. The surface exists only for that one line: one production caller of `IToolUseSession.appendFollowUp`, one implementation, one read of `LiveToolUseFlowContext.session`, one caller of `submit` at all. Folding `not_owned` into `refused` also reaches the four-kind union #11329's own cut list wanted, so the concept count genuinely drops.

Reachability, which is what an architectural collapse claim lives or dies on, came out better than the report argued: the ordinary teardown order (detach live flow, then release queue) closes the window the report attributed to suspend and to the child-run loop, leaving only host stream deletion — a path where the UI that could submit a follow-up has already been destroyed. So the deletion is closer to dead-path removal than the report's own hedging suggests, and the residual behavior change (a deleted-stream follow-up reporting a worded refusal instead of a false `sent`) is strictly more honest, which is exactly what CLAUDE.md's "silent degradation is a defect" rule asks for.

Risk stays medium rather than low because the argument is a reachability argument over a concurrency window, and the one seam I could not exercise statically is a session-mismatch case (target resolved from `ownerSession.executions` while the lease lives on `ownerSession.followUps` — same handle in practice, but a host passing a different `options.session` is the kind of thing that produced round 2's two reverts). The instrumentation experiment the report proposes is the right gate and should actually be run, not skipped.

</details>

### guard-redundancy — The guard layer: ratchets, tests, lint rules, scripts

#### Delete two name-tombstone suites and the two dependency-direction describes the edge ratchet already owns

- **Kind**: delete · **Risk**: low
- **Net**: -101 LoC, -16 elements (proposer claimed -139; verifier figure governs)
- **Files**: `src/test-kernel/architecture/progressEventHandlerRetirement.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/architecture/sessionFactAmbientHelperRetirement.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/architecture/dependencyDirection.vitest.ts`, `config/ratchets/architecture-edges-baseline.json`, `src/test-kernel/architecture/subsystemEdgeRatchet.vitest.ts`
- **Note**: Also found independently by the repo-shape audit as "Two architecture ratchets are name tombstones"; that finding is a strict subset of this one (same two suites, without the two dependency-direction describes). Merged.

**Evidence**

Two separate duplications, batched because both are 'a guard whose invariant a live neighbour already owns'.

(A) The tombstones. `progressEventHandlerRetirement.vitest.ts` (66 lines) and `sessionFactAmbientHelperRetirement.vitest.ts` (35 lines) walk all four host production roots and assert that the identifiers `ProgressEventHandler` and `emitRuntimeEvent` appear nowhere. `git log --diff-filter=A` dates them to `38fe5a2cc7` (2026-07-08, `refactor(progress): split backend interaction handler`) and `f1fb2b338a` (2026-07-09, `refactor: delete ambient runtime event helper`) — seven weeks at HEAD (2026-08-26).

They enforce a SPELLING, not a shape. `RETIRED_SYMBOL = /\bProgressEventHandler\b/` and `RETIRED_AMBIENT_HELPER_SYMBOL = /\bemitRuntimeEvent\b/` both pass silently if the same architecture returns under any other name — which is the realistic failure mode, since nobody reintroduces a class by its retired name. `RETIRED_DESKTOP_PROGRESS_BRIDGE_TERMS` goes further and matches the prose phrase `'progress-event bridge'` against source read WITHOUT `stripComments` (progressEventHandlerRetirement.vitest.ts:33-38), so it is literally a comment-text guard.

The invariants they gesture at have live owners with documented rules: CLAUDE.md 'Event channels' (`AgentEvent` run-scoped vs `SessionFact` session-scoped, ruled in `.agents/docs/implemented/architecture/2026-06-10-error-pipeline-and-ownership.md`) states the emission rule, and `SessionHandle.events` / `SessionEventHub` own it in code. AGENTS.md 'Testing discipline' says a test must protect 'a consequential current contract, a difficult invariant, or a reproduced defect'; 'nobody re-types a deleted identifier' is none of the three.

(B) The two dependency-direction describes. `dependencyDirection.vitest.ts:167-188` asserts no `src/shared` file and no `src/latex` file imports `@agent/*`, via `AGENT_IMPORT_PATTERNS` regexes (lines 56-66) and an allowlist that is a declared-empty array plus a Set built from it (lines 67-71).

`config/ratchets/architecture-edges-baseline.json` contains no `shared -> agent` edge and no `latex -> agent` edge (its `latex` rows are common/logger/platform/replacement/shared/transcript/utils; its `shared` rows are common/logger/platform/utils). So `subsystemEdgeRatchet.vitest.ts` already fails a new import in either direction as `reason: 'new-edge'`. It is strictly STRONGER: `resolveImportedSubsystem` (subsystemEdgeRatchet.vitest.ts:88-113) resolves relative specifiers as well as aliases, so `import … from '../../agent/x'` inside `src/shared` fails the edge ratchet and passes the regex check. Both run in the same `test` CI job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:261`), so there is no gating difference. The `SHARED_AGENT_IMPORT_ALLOWLIST` is declared `readonly string[] = []` with a comment saying 'Keep this allowlist empty' — an inert construct plus its Set.

Cross-boundary check: nothing here crosses the NDJSON vocabulary, a persisted file, a settings key, or the texra-action contract. These are test files and a CI baseline.

Explicitly NOT proposed here: the `VS Code-free zones` describe (lines 148-165) and the `Production core never imports host layers` / `Agent core dependency direction` describes (190-213). See the 'keep' finding — dated incident evidence (#7722, #7730) proves those earn their double enforcement.

**Proposal**

1. Delete `src/test-kernel/architecture/progressEventHandlerRetirement.vitest.ts` and `src/test-kernel/architecture/sessionFactAmbientHelperRetirement.vitest.ts` outright (two files, 101 lines).
2. In `src/test-kernel/architecture/dependencyDirection.vitest.ts`, delete the `Shared layer dependency direction` and `Latex layer dependency direction` describes (167-188), plus the now-unused `AGENT_IMPORT_PATTERNS` (56-66), `SHARED_AGENT_IMPORT_ALLOWLIST` and `SHARED_AGENT_IMPORT_ALLOWLIST_SET` (67-71) — 39 lines.
3. Owner that absorbs (B): `subsystemEdgeRatchet.vitest.ts` against `config/ratchets/architecture-edges-baseline.json`, unchanged — the baseline already encodes the absence of both edges, so no baseline edit is needed. Add a one-line comment on the `shared` and `latex` baseline rows noting that the absence of an `agent` row is the load-bearing fact, so a future regeneration does not casually add one.
4. Owner that absorbs (A): CLAUDE.md's 'Event channels' rule and the progress-view transcript plane. No code change.

Both halves are independently landable; batch them in one `refactor(test-kernel):` PR with the R6 net-element block.

**What we give up**

(A) If a future refactor literally re-creates a class named `ProgressEventHandler` or a module-level `emitRuntimeEvent` helper, no automated gate objects — only review and the CLAUDE.md event-channel rule. That is the same protection every other retired name in this repo has. (B) The failure message on a new `shared -> agent` import becomes the edge ratchet's generic `shared->agent: new value edge` instead of the friendlier `does not grow shared-to-agent imports`. Restoring either is a `git revert`.

**Verifier corrections — these override the evidence and proposal above**

1. STRIP HALF (B) ENTIRELY. Do not touch `dependencyDirection.vitest.ts`, `architecture-edges-baseline.json`, or `subsystemEdgeRatchet.vitest.ts`. The `Shared layer dependency direction` and `Latex layer dependency direction` describes are a deliberate second gate ruled on by `.agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-08-15-latex-agent-port-design.md:279-288,370-373` and shipped by #10558 eleven days ago. The retitled finding is "Delete two name-tombstone architecture suites", -101 LoC, two files.

The finding understates its own (B) case in one place and overstates it in another (moot now, but for the record): `AGENT_IMPORT_PATTERNS` misses a bare `from '@agent'` with no trailing slash, which the edge ratchet does catch; and both scans cover the identical file set (`sourceFilesUnder` on `src/shared`/`src/latex` vs `sourceFilesUnder(SRC_ROOT)` filtered by `subsystemFromRepoRelative`), so there is no coverage difference in either direction.

Deleting both files drops `expectRealCoverage` (repoScan.ts:74-80) from 3 call sites to 1 (approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet.vitest.ts:104) and `ALL_HOST_PRODUCTION_ROOTS` from 5 scanners to 3 (approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet:65, agentRuntimeProgressEventsBoundary:46, transcriptResidencyLeaseSites:34). A reviewer may invoke CLAUDE.md's single-caller rule on the now-one-caller helper. Do not let this expand into a repoScan sweep.

OBJECTION-IN-WAITING the claim does not pre-empt. `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-prd-approval-policy-unification.md:357-360` names `sessionFactAmbientHelperRetirement.vitest.ts` as the style template `approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet.vitest.ts` was "cloned from the retirement-gate style ... hardcoded allowlists, no baseline JSON", and CLAUDE.md endorses that clone by name. The PR body must say explicitly that the clone is untouched and is a live single-authority gate over current call sites, whereas these two are name tombstones over deleted identifiers — otherwise the deletion reads as an attack on a family CLAUDE.md blesses.

Its scope is CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, five READMEs, `.claude/skills`, `docs/dev` (with `docs/dev/audits` carved out as archival), `docs/architecture`, `docs/guide`. Do not amend it in this PR (dated proposals are historical by design, per the script's own comment and #9730).

<details><summary>Verifier reasoning</summary>

I re-derived every hop rather than trusting the claim. (B): read `subsystemEdgeRatchet.vitest.ts` in full — `collectSubsystemEdges` walks all of `src`, `resolveImportedSubsystem` handles relative paths and longest-alias matching, `findRatchetViolations` flags any pair absent from the baseline as `new-edge` regardless of kind — and confirmed via a Python parse of `architecture-edges-baseline.json` (94 edges) that `shared` has rows only to common/logger/platform/utils and `latex` only to common/logger/platform/replacement/shared/transcript/utils. So the redundancy is real. It is also already on the record: the 2026-08-15 latex-port design named it, called the second guard "belt-and-braces", and shipped it anyway in #10558. That is the single most common round-3 kill and it applies verbatim, so half (B) dies.

(A) is a different animal. I read both files: they regex production source for two identifiers that no longer exist anywhere (`git grep` for `ProgressEventHandler` returns only historical PRDs; `emitRuntimeEvent` returns nothing), assert two deleted module paths still don't exist, and — in the desktop case — match three prose strings against un-comment-stripped source. That is a spelling gate, not a shape gate, and AGENTS.md's testing-discipline section explicitly instructs deleting tests that exist only for retired behavior. I checked all seven kill criteria: no rounds-1/2 duplicate (both files still intact at HEAD, and round 2's only overlap was a self-check trim that did not land), no dated ruling (the two introducing commits carry one-line bodies; the only doc that names one, the approval-policy PRD, cites it as prior art for a different live gate), no migration cost at all (zero importers, zero guidance-refs exposure, no baseline row, no wire/settings/persisted surface), no settled surface (not one of the six `config/ratchets/` baselines, not the frozen SDK, not the vscode-free zones), and no churn (pure file deletion). The only uncounted cost is that `expectRealCoverage` falls to a single caller, which is a reviewer conversation rather than a defect. Honest numbers for the surviving scope: -101 LoC, two suites, six test cases and eight module-level declarations gone, low risk, restorable by `git revert`.

</details>

## 3. Keep rulings

32 apparent redundancies were examined and found to be the right shape. These are dated answers: do not re-open one without new evidence that beats the reasoning recorded here.

### run-lifecycle — The run lifecycle, end to end

#### Two terminal-run choreographies (lifecycle vs registry) — asked, ruled on 2026-07-30, and the extraction it asked for has already landed

Question: the run lifecycle's `finalizeRunTerminal` (AgentRunLifecycle.ts:185-290) is documented as "the single owner of terminal run choreography", yet `ExecutionRegistry.finishWaitingTermination` (executionRegistry.ts:1039-1107) independently emits the terminal `result`, settles `handle.result`, untracks, cancels the stream phase, calls `finalizeRun`, and releases the root lease. Its own docstring admits it "bypasses `runFlowWithLifecycle`'s own terminal handling". Two injected ports on `ExecutionRegistryInit` — `publishResult` (:144, :181-183) and `releaseRootExecutionLease` (:150-152) — exist for this path and nothing else (verified: the only uses are :1068 and :1015/:1093). Lines 970-1107 are 105 code lines. `finalizeRunTerminal` itself has exactly four production callers (AgentRunLifecycle.ts:531, childRunLoop.ts:1158, childStream.ts:346 via `finalizeChildStream`), so this is genuinely the one exception.

The collapse is already ruled out by dated record. gh issue #9418, "Tech debt: extract the duplicated terminal-persistence tail (ExecutionRegistry / AgentRunLifecycle)", closed COMPLETED 2026-07-30, states: "the original finding's bigger framing was refuted: the two-arm split is a designed, guarded peer arrangement (documented at AgentRunLifecycle.ts:124-135, enforced by `claimTerminalFinalize`), and its ordering differences are deliberate and tested… **Do not reroute the waiting arm through `finalizeRunTerminal` and do not delete the injection seams.**"

And the -30 LoC / 1 element that issue did sanction has since landed: commit ebd7c7ebab "refactor: one finalize tail via finalizeRun" moved the persist try/catch, the failure logging, and the `outcomePersisted` return into `finalizeRun` (src/agent/storage/executionLifecycle.ts:264), which both arms now call (AgentRunLifecycle.ts:207, executionRegistry.ts:1074). Commit 4b9536e4ec then collapsed the waiting-termination recovery ladder. What is left is exactly the residue the issue said to keep.

I re-tested whether the two orderings could be unified and they cannot without behaviour change. The lifecycle persists BEFORE publishing ("a completed tool-use flow must not remain resumable while an onboarding write is pending", AgentRunLifecycle.ts:744-748). The registry publishes BEFORE persisting because the parked run's transcript group must be closed first so "every host observes one coherent cancellation boundary" (executionRegistry.ts:1062-1066). The registry also needs `untrackIfCurrent` rather than untrack-by-id, because a resumed successor may already own the execution (:1053-1058) — a case the lifecycle arm cannot reach. And `beginSuspendedTermination` claims the exactly-once gate synchronously with the parked→terminating flip (ExecutionHandle.ts:325-345), so a reroute would need a pre-claimed entry point into `finalizeRunTerminal`, weakening the single gate that makes first-terminal-outcome arbitration safe.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep both arms. The current shape is: ONE arbiter (the stream phase, resolved once at AgentRunLifecycle.ts:196-200), ONE projector for live runs (`finalizeRunTerminal`, four callers), and ONE deliberate peer for the parked-run case the projector structurally cannot reach. The five downstream sinks — persisted `meta.outcome`, transcript stage end, the `result` event, the terminal stream phase, and registry untrack — are projections of the arbiter, not co-owners, and every one has a different consumer and lifetime.

If a future round wants to shrink this, the only remaining honest target is the `publishResult` port: `finalizeRunTerminal` publishes via `params.trace?.emit`, while the parked path must use `SessionHandle.publishRunEvent` because the turn's trace was disposed by `runFlowWithLifecycle`'s finally. Unifying on a `publish` callback would save under 10 lines and #9418 explicitly forbids deleting the seam.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification — routing `terminateWaitingHandle` through `finalizeRunTerminal` with a pre-claimed flag and a publish port — would cost about 60 lines of registry code but would (a) invert publish/persist ordering for stopped WAITING runs, which the existing vitest ordering tests pin, (b) replace `untrackIfCurrent` with untrack-by-id, letting a stop of generation N untrack generation N+1's handle, and (c) split `claimTerminalFinalize` into two entry points, which is the gate that prevents a stop and a concurrent finalize from publishing contradictory outcomes.

#### One run, two `AgentExecutionHandle` objects — the launch handle cannot merge into the lifecycle handle, because the run's streamId is not decided until launch assembly

Question a structural reader will ask: why does one run construct two handles? `runAgent` builds a `launchHandle` (runAgent.ts:113-127), attaches an interrupt handler that aborts `launchAbortController`, and tracks it synchronously so a stop can reach launch preparation. Then, inside the lane step, `runFlowWithLifecycle` constructs a second `AgentExecutionHandle` (AgentRunLifecycle.ts:474-484) and `track()`s it, which replaces the first in the registry map (executionRegistry.ts:406-433). `runAgent`'s finally untracks the launch handle only if it is still the registered one (:246-254).

The merge is blocked by identity. The lifecycle handle's `run` is its immutable birth facts (ExecutionHandle.ts:29-37, `readonly run: ExecutionRun`), and its `streamId` is `ctx.runScope.streamId`, which `buildAgentLaunchContext` resolves as `input.streamTabIdOverride ?? getStreamTabId(config.agent, {executionId})` (AgentLaunchContext.ts:636-637). For a resumed run the override comes from `clearTerminalExecutionState(executionId).streamId` (runAgent.ts:171-173) — a value `runAgent` does not have until it is already inside the lane, after `registerExecution`/`acquireResumedExecutionLease`. So the launch handle's stream id is provably wrong for resumes and cannot be the run's handle.

The window it covers is real work, not a formality: `applyHelperModelPreference`, `registerExecution` or `acquireResumedExecutionLease`, `clearTerminalExecutionState`, and the whole of `buildAgentLaunchContext` (agent YAML load, model validation, tool resolution) all run before the lifecycle handle exists, and all are interruptible only through `launchSignal`.

The alternative — a `Map<executionId, AbortController>` of in-flight launches on the registry — replaces a reused concept with a new parallel one and adds a second thing `kill()` has to consult. That is more elements, not fewer.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep the shape. Record two facts so the next reader does not re-litigate it:

1. The launch handle exists because the stop gesture must reach launch preparation, and the run's identity is not knowable until preparation finishes. It is created only when the execution has no handle yet (`runAgent.ts:112`), so a resume of a parked run correctly leaves the suspended predecessor in place for `terminateWaitingHandle`.
2. `launchHandle.result` (the `pDefer` at ExecutionHandle.ts:139-141) is never settled — the launch handle is replaced, not finalized. Anything that resolves a handle through `executions.getHandle(id)` during the launch window and awaits `.result` hangs forever. Today no production consumer does: the F-2 handle is delivered through `onRun`, which fires only from `runFlowWithLifecycle` (:485, AgentRunLifecycle.ts:508-520) with the real handle. Do not "fix" this by exposing `launchHandle.result`; if a consumer ever needs a launch-window promise, the right move is for `runAgent` to settle the launch handle with a CANCELLED `ResultEvent` when it untracks it at :246-254.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

Nothing. The tempting simplification (reuse the launch handle as the run handle when the ids happen to match) would buy about 15 lines and introduce a branch where a run's handle identity depends on whether it was fresh or resumed — exactly the kind of conditional identity that `ExecutionRun`'s readonly birth-facts contract exists to prevent.

### identity-model — Run, session, execution and stream identity

#### streamId and executionId stay two ids: they are 1:1, and unifying them costs a full history migration to undo a ruling that was made deliberately

The question: `getStreamTabId` (`src/agent/runtime/streamTab.ts:20-25`) mints `${getCleanAgentName(name)}#${executionId}`, so a stream id is a pure function of an execution id plus a display prefix. Every mint site passes a freshly generated execution id (`runAgent.ts:112`, `childStream.ts:94,258`, `bash.ts:504`, `agentCliShared.ts:214`, `detachedChildRun.ts:55`, `WorkflowScriptTool.ts:350`), and resume reuses both (`runAgent.ts:101` reuses `request.executionId`; `resumedStreamId` from `clearTerminalExecutionState` is passed as `streamTabIdOverride` at `runAgent.ts:183` / `executeAgent.ts:556`). So the mapping is 1:1 for the whole lifetime of a run. That makes it fair to ask whether stream identity should stop existing, taking with it `ExecutionMeta.streamId` (`stream.ts:102`), `StreamTabMeta.executionId`, `readExecutionStreamIndex` (`executionListing.ts:209-227`), `lookupStreamExecutionId` (`ToolUseFollowUp.ts:185-193`), `getStreamTabDisplayName`, `streamTabIdOverride`, and the three-source ownership ladder at `SessionHandle.ts:570-620`.

The answer is no, on three independent grounds.

1. The stream id is a persisted directory key. `streams/{streamId}/` holds every sidecar (`meta.json`, `usageStats.json`, `workPlan.json`) and the transcript. Re-keying means renaming every history directory in `~/.texra` for every user — a PERSISTED USER FILE migration with no upside.
2. The stream id is a public wire _value_. `streamId` appears on nearly every frozen NDJSON progress payload (`packages/cli/src/runtime/cliNdjsonProgressEvents.ts:57-90`), and `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-15-shared-contracts-and-retirement.md:114-121` lists the CLI NDJSON progress vocabulary among the "frozen surfaces (fence, never consolidate)", with the external wire described at :614 as a "permanent floor".
3. The opacity is a deliberate, dated ruling, not an accident. `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-run-classification-consolidation.md` records that `legacyExecutionIdentity.ts` (a 374-line resolver that paid an O(all-streams) scan to parse ids back into identity) was "deleted whole with no replacement walker — `meta.streamId` is the one execution→stream mapping, suffix resemblance is never ownership". The comment at `streamTab.ts:27-41` fences the one surviving parse to display only. Unifying the ids would reinstate exactly the id-parsing coupling that deletion removed.

The bidirectional foreign key that looks like duplication is also already adjudicated. `executions/{id}/meta.json.streamId` and `streams/{sid}/meta.json.executionId` are the forward and reverse edges of one relation on a filesystem with no index; `hydrateRunStateFromMeta` (`StreamSnapshotStore.ts:1936-1975`) needs the reverse edge to reach identity without a directory scan, and `readExecutionStreamIndex` needs the forward edge to attribute orphans. The arbitration between them (`applyStreamData`, :2029-2065) is listed under "Adversarially REFUTED — permanently off the deletion lists" in the 2026-08-17 freeze list (`gh issue view 10809`), because it arbitrates against foreign-writable `executions/` registers outside any log's total order.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep the current shape: two ids, one derived from the other at mint time, never parsed back; a bidirectional persisted FK as the filesystem's index; `readExecutionStreamIndex` as the last-resort scan when neither resident source holds the edge. The invariants worth pinning are already written down — `getStreamTabId` is the one minter, `getStreamTabDisplayName` is display-only, `ExecutionMeta.streamId` is the one authored execution→stream edge. The only thing owed here is documentation, not code: this survey is the record that the question was asked and answered, so the next sweep does not re-open it.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

Nothing is given up by keeping the current shape. The tempting simplification — one id, one FK, no index scan — would have cost a rename migration over every user's transcript history, a value change on a permanently frozen wire field, and the reintroduction of id-parsing as an addressing mechanism, in exchange for deleting roughly 150 lines of index and override plumbing. That is a net loss on both LoC and risk. Next round: do not re-ask this.

#### The fabricated AgentConfig on the live path for process and workflow-script runs stays: the honest RunRecord already owns the durable side, and the wire projection is the blessed pattern

The question: `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-run-classification-consolidation.md` §Problem indicted `CreateChildStreamOptions.streamCategory` as "Nothing, for 2 of 3 kinds — `z.strictObject` demands both beside `kind`, so process and script runs fabricate them". Step 8 of that plan shipped the durable half: `src/agent/core/definition/RunRecord.ts` is an honest union whose non-agent arm has no `agentCategory`, no fabricated model, and no tool-permission object; `readRunRecord()` sits at the store seam and `TraceDocumentSchema.config` embeds the union. But the live half did not ship. `src/tools/bash.ts:508-512` still builds `AgentConfigSchema.parse({agent:'bash', instruction: command, agentCategory: ToolUse})` for a `kind:'process'` run, with the comment "The synthetic AgentConfig above feeds the ephemeral live wire only". `src/tools/delegation/WorkflowScriptTool.ts:442-445` carries the same comment for the workflow container. `CreateChildStreamOptions.config` is still `AgentConfig` (`childStream.ts:38`), `ExecutionRun.category` is still a required `AgentCategory` for all three kinds (`ExecutionHandle.ts:33-39`), and `RunConfigEvent.config` is still `AgentConfig` (`src/agent/trace/events.ts:100-105`).

So the redundancy is real. It should nevertheless stay, for two reasons.

1. The fabrication is publicly visible on a permanently frozen wire. `agentConfigToTaskState` (`src/agent/core/state/agentConfigToTaskState.ts:12`) has exactly one caller — `packages/cli/src/runtime/sessionProgressSubscription.ts:123` — and its own docstring says "This is the projection's only remaining purpose". `setTaskState.taskState.agentConfig` is therefore the frozen public shape of a bash or workflow stream's config, and `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-15-shared-contracts-and-retirement.md:114-121` fences both the CLI NDJSON progress vocabulary and `TaskState` as "frozen surfaces (fence, never consolidate)", calling the `CliNdjsonActiveChildRow` boundary re-projection "the projection pattern done exactly right". Making the live record honest changes that payload for two run kinds.
2. The lie is already neutralized at every display site by identity, so it causes no drift. `src/controllers/session/streamTabInfo.ts:76-78` suppresses the fabricated model ("Process streams carry a synthetic AgentConfig whose `model` is the schema's prefault, not a real inference model — only agent runs show one"); `:54-55` reads `config.instruction` as `command` only when `identity.kind === 'process'`; `src/tools/executionFormatters.ts:52,70,254` narrow through `isAgentRunRecord`. The durable record — the one that outlives the process and that `ExecutionsTool` reads back — is already honest.

The honest migration cost confirms the verdict: deleting the two fabrication blocks removes about 20 lines, but retyping `run.config`, `CreateChildStreamOptions.config`, `ExecutionRun.category` and `setRunConfig` onto the union adds `isAgentRunRecord` narrowing at every consumer, plus a `TaskState` boundary re-projection to keep the frozen payload byte-identical. That nets positive — exactly the pattern review-checklist §13 warns about (22 prior "reduction" PRs netted +5,046 LoC).

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep the split: the durable record is the honest `RunRecord` union, the live path keeps an `AgentConfig`-shaped record, and identity-keyed suppression at the three display sites is the mechanism that keeps the fabricated fields off screen. The one thing worth doing is cheap and additive-free: the two fabrication sites already carry the explanatory comment, so also cross-reference `RunRecord.ts`'s docstring from them so a future reader sees both halves of the decision in one hop.

Record the residual on the contracts ledger rather than as separate candidates: if the NDJSON wire is ever cut, `setTaskState.taskState.agentConfig`, `CliNdjsonActiveChildRow.kind`/`toolName`, `setActiveStream.isRemote`/`agentCategory` and `updateStreamUsage.storageKey` are one bundle of pre-consolidation encodings that retire in a single change. Filing them one at a time is wasted work, and each one individually looks deletable to an in-repo grep.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

For keep: nothing. The tempting simplification — one honest `RunRecord` on the live path too — would have cost a change to a permanently frozen public payload plus a net LoC add, to remove a fabrication that three independent display sites already suppress by `identity.kind`.

### persistence — The persistence story, end to end

#### streamLogSummaries/ is a real cache, not duplication: 507 MB of transcripts summarized into 3.3 MB

QUESTION ASKED: streamLogSummaries/<id>.json restates timestamps, three running-flags, and a meta block that is itself a projection of executions/<id>/meta.json plus config.json. Two full-directory scans (StreamLogStore's summary load and executionListing.listExecutions) produce overlapping per-run display metadata. Should the summary tier be retired and rebuilt from its sources?

ANSWER: NO, and the measurement is decisive. On this machine's coauthor bucket, streamLogs/ = 507 MB across 837 files while streamLogSummaries/ = 3.3 MB across 836 files — a 150x reduction. StreamLogStore.readPersistentSummaries :1371-1391 loads every stream's summary at open; without the cache, loadStreamSummary :1414-1433 falls back to reading and parsing the whole authoritative log (summarizeEntries :1478-1491). Retiring the tier would mean parsing half a gigabyte of JSON on every store open on one workspace alone. The mirror is also deliberately bounded — StreamLogStore.ts:75-93 states 'Bounded scalars only: command carries a process run's command line, never an agent run's full instruction text' — and the alternative source it avoids, executions/*/config.json, measures 3.2 MB across 845 files, 13x the size of meta.json.

SAFETY IS ALREADY CORRECT, NOT MASKED. The cache is guarded three ways and every arm is loud: parseSummaryShape :127-143 warns and returns undefined on a stale shape with an explicit no-.catch() rationale citing #9434's derived-tier discard-and-rebuild contract; readSummary :1438-1476 rejects a timestamp-less entry as non-evidence of registration and compares summariesKv.modifiedAt against logsKv.modifiedAt, treating a missing log mtime as orphaned rather than merely stale; corrupt reads warn and rebuild. This is a checklist section 15 L5 decide-once owner, not an M3 silent Zod default.

WHY IT WILL LOOK DEAD AGAIN NEXT ROUND: gh issue view 10809 records that PR #10820 ('refactor: retire transcript summary sidecars') was opened to delete exactly this tier. It never landed — the owner closed #10809 on 2026-08-17 because 'its named JSONL swap and sidecar campaign were retired as divergent from the single Stream-tab authority.' The retirement died with its parent migration, NOT on merit, so an unwary reader finds a closed retirement PR and concludes the tier is doomed. It is not.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep streamLogSummaries/ exactly as it is, including its mtime staleness guard and its loud discard-and-rebuild path. The one change worth making is the narrow one in the FK finding above: drop executionId from the meta block, because that single field duplicates an authored edge rather than summarizing an expensive read. Everything else in the block (identity, agentCategory, description, model, workingDirectory, command, parentStreamId, userFollowUpSupport) exists to keep the progress rail off config.json, and it earns that.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification — 'the summary is derivable, so derive it' — would have traded 3.3 MB of reads for 507 MB of reads at every store open, on one workspace bucket out of five (the largest here is 3.9 GB). It would also have deleted the only always-resident source for getSummaryMeta, which SessionHandle.computeStartupSeedSet :499 uses to walk parent chains without touching sidecars, and which SessionStores.sweepEphemeralStreams :718 uses to recognize a background-shell stream by identity.kind === 'process' when no in-memory phase exists.

#### Two file-backed key-value stores is the right number: KVStore cannot serve config, JsonStore cannot serve runs

QUESTION ASKED (the brief's own): count the storage abstractions and ask how many are needed. The count is smaller than it looks. Filesystem layer: BaseFS -> RelativeFS -> {StorageFS, GlobalStorageFS, WorkspaceFS}, plus AbsoluteFS — six classes, but only two behaviors are added below BaseFS (a base path, and readJson/cleanupOldFiles), and the four leaves differ solely in getBasePath()/validateResolvedPath(), 4-15 lines each. Store layer: exactly two generic stores. KVStore (113 lines, 7 methods) has 7 construction sites, all in src/transcript/, plus its one subclass StorageFSKVStore at src/agent/storage/ExecutionKVStore.ts:178. JsonStore (221 lines) backs config and state. Wrapper layer: PersistedState (207 lines) has 3 production instantiations (src/controllers/progressView/backend/ProgressPresentationState.ts:35, packages/extension/src/webview/frontend/persistence.ts:71, packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/components/LogList.ts:216); MemoryStateStore (20 lines) and createWebviewStorage (PersistedState.ts:18-38) are the other two StateStore implementations; ToggleStateStore is 26 lines of in-memory Map.

WHY THE TWO STORES CANNOT MERGE — A HARD CONSTRAINT, NOT A PREFERENCE. src/platform/defaults/nodeStores.ts:1-9 documents it: these are 'the JSON stores a Node-family host (CLI, desktop, extension) opens before initPlatform.' KVStore reaches disk exclusively through StorageFS -> platform().fs (KVStore.ts:60,72,79,85,92), which throws until initPlatform() has run. JsonStore uses raw node:fs/promises plus write-file-atomic for exactly that reason. A merged store would have to be split again along the same seam on the first bootstrap read.

THEIR CONCURRENCY DISCIPLINES ARE ALSO NON-INTERCHANGEABLE. JsonStore.flush (:171-196) is a read-modify-write under a proper-lockfile cross-process lock plus a module-wide per-path PQueue, because .texra/config.json is edited by the user's editor and by three hosts simultaneously, and because a whole-file dump would clobber a concurrent writer's key (the failure recorded in checklist section 15's cliSecrets.ts precedent). KVStore needs none of that: one key is one file, so writeAtomic per key IS the isolation, and a run's KV is written by exactly one leaseholder (StorageFSKVStore.write wraps every write in runWithExecutionLeaseWriteFence, ExecutionKVStore.ts:186-190). Giving KVStore a cross-process lock would put a lockfile round-trip on the flow-record hot path, which rewrites full shared state on every node transition.

WHAT I CHECKED FOR AND DID NOT FIND: no third generic store, no duplicated JSON-file helper, no hand-rolled promise chain (both stores use p-queue), and no .catch(default) on a persisted read in either — KVStore's header states 'a missing file reads as undefined, every other error throws to the caller', and withMissingFallback :26-35 gates strictly on isFileNotFoundError.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep both stores and the FS class chain. If anything here is ever revisited, the target should be RelativeFS's two extra methods rather than the store split: cleanupOldFiles has 2 callers (src/tools/media/audio.ts:177, src/utils/files/pastedImageUtils.ts:54) and readJson a handful, but folding them into BaseFS would be a pure relocation with zero element delta, so it is not worth a PR on its own.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification was 'two file-backed KV stores is one too many, unify on KVStore.' It would have cost a bootstrap-time crash on every host (config is read before initPlatform), lost the cross-process read-modify-write that keeps a shared .texra/config.json from being clobbered by a concurrent host, and added a lockfile round-trip to the flow-record write path. A PersistedState-over-KVStore variant has the same bootstrap problem one layer up.

#### Do not merge the twin stream stores, and do not fold streamData/ into executions/ — the coupling worth removing is the deletion transaction

QUESTION ASKED: StreamLogStore (1,800 lines) and StreamSnapshotStore (2,182 lines) are both per-stream, both keyed by StreamTabId, both hold KVStore handles, both maintain a resident registry, both seed at startup, both serialize writes. Are they one system that was split? And since streamId is minted as `${agent}#${executionId}` and on-disk counts are near 1:1 (816 streamData dirs vs 845 executions with meta), should the sidecars simply move into executions/<id>/ so a run has ONE directory?

ANSWER TO PART ONE: NO, AND IT IS A DATED RULING. gh issue view 11283 ('Consolidate the twin stream-stores... eager-overlay and seed-replay machinery') was closed by the owner on 2026-08-22: 'Closing the proposed consolidation as superseded by the existing bounded shared extractions, rather than adding a generic persistence/hydration base. ResidentStreamRegistry, KVStoreCache, and StagedDeletionCoordinator already own the genuinely common pieces. The residual similarity is conceptual, not algorithmic... A base class would turn safety barriers (generations/tombstones, corruption boundaries, commit points) into callbacks without eliminating demonstrated duplicate logic.' I verified the shared extraction is real: ResidentStreamRegistry (92 lines) is imported by both (StreamLogStore.ts:28, StreamSnapshotStore.ts:68). I also verified the algorithmic difference: StreamLogStore writes one whole array per stream through a 300 ms max-wait throttle and a single concurrency-1 writeQueue (:396-432), while StreamSnapshotStore writes six independently atomic field files through per-(stream, category) mutexes and an overlay/replay protocol (:1529-1616). Merging a debounced whole-file writer with a per-field overlay writer produces a superset, not a simplification.

ANSWER TO PART TWO: NO, ON COST. Folding streamData/<id>/* into executions/<id>/ would delete streamDataPaths.ts, both FKs, and the cross-domain deletion transaction in one stroke — but it moves 816 live directories of AUTHORED data. usageStats.json is cost data whose per-run verbatim round-trip is a hard invariant (#7464; ParsedUsageData.unparsedRuns, src/shared/schemas/streamData.ts:76-88); workPlan.json is the only durable home for todos/plan (src/transcript/streamDataPaths.ts:31-33); outputFiles.json names user artifacts. That is authored tier under #9434 — it migrates, it is not discarded — so the collapse costs a real migration reader plus a retirement window, on top of contradicting the standing rule that nothing derives a run's identity from the stream-id format (src/agent/runtime/streamTab.ts:27-40). The deletion-transaction win it buys is ~780 lines, and the decide-first reordering in the first finding buys the SAME win without moving a byte of user data.

WHY THIS KEEP MATTERS: rounds keep arriving at these two files because they are the largest in the subsystem and superficially parallel. Both routes to 'merge them' have now been costed — the base-class route was ruled out on 2026-08-22, and the storage-domain route is dominated by a cheaper alternative.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep the two stores and the two on-disk domains. Take the deletion-protocol win instead (finding 1), which removes the only genuine coupling between them — the two-phase rename transaction — and leaves each store owning exactly one format. If a future change does touch both stores, #11283's closing comment sets the bar: a design/invariant matrix plus disposable fresh-process crash simulations at the rename, transcript-delete, sidecar-write, summary-write, and late-reclaim barriers. After finding 1 lands, two of those five barriers no longer exist.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification was 'one stream, one store, one directory.' Its base-class form would have converted commit points and generation guards into overridable callbacks — the exact shape the owner rejected on 2026-08-22 — and its storage-domain form would have required migrating 816 directories of authored cost and work-plan data to buy a deletion win that a step reordering already delivers for free.

### event-planes — The event and fact planes

#### Two subscriptions to the same hub over an overlapping run-fact set are one system correctly split, not a duplicate fold

Question asked: `StreamSnapshotStore.attachSessionEvents` (StreamSnapshotStore.ts:605-700) and `SessionFactApplier` both subscribe to the same `SessionEventHub` and both switch on the same run facts — `updateTodos`, `updatePlan`, `addOutputFiles`, `updateMissingOutputs`, `updateCompileFailures`, `usage`, `run.start`, `run.config`. `SNAPSHOT_RUN_FACT_TYPES` (8 entries, :147-156) is a strict subset of `RUN_FACT_EVENT_TYPES` (13 entries, src/agent/trace/events.ts:405-419), and the store is reachable through the applier's own state as `SessionState.snapshots`. Two switch statements, two subscriptions, one hub, one overlapping vocabulary — it reads like one fold that was split.

It is not. The store is attached in the `SessionHandle` constructor (SessionHandle.ts:222), before any host exists; the applier is constructed per host presentation (ProgressBackend.ts:884-901 on webview attach, runProgressRenderer.ts:162-179 per headless run, sessionSignalsAdapter.ts:217-280 per TUI). Durable sidecar persistence must not depend on a renderer being attached — a quiet headless run or a session with the progress view closed still has to land todos, plan, output files and usage on disk. Merging the two folds into the applier would make persistence conditional on presentation.

The split also carries a real, currently unasserted ordering invariant that a reviewer should know about: because the applier notifies renderers that then re-read the store (`invalidate('files')` at LitSessionRenderer.ts:148-157 reads `state.snapshots.getOutputFiles` immediately after the applier's `addOutputFiles` handler at SessionFactApplier.ts:197-198 fires), the store's subscriber must run first. It does, only because `SessionEventHub.emit` iterates insertion order (SessionEventHub.ts:88-108) and SessionHandle.ts:222 always registers before any host. Nothing asserts it.

One genuine residue inside this shape: the store subscribes with the generic `events.subscribe(..., { scope: 'run', types: SNAPSHOT_RUN_FACT_TYPES })` and then re-narrows with a hand-rolled `isSnapshotRunFact` / `SNAPSHOT_RUN_FACT_TYPE_SET` / `SnapshotRunFact` / `SnapshotRunFactType` (:158-169), reproducing exactly what `SessionEventHub.subscribeRunFacts` (:154-181) already does for every other consumer. That is about -12 lines and four type/const elements, and it is the only thing worth touching here.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep both subscriptions. Do not fold the snapshot store's run-fact switch into `SessionFactApplier` or vice versa — the store is session-lifetime and host-independent, the applier is presentation-lifetime, and one hub with two filtered subscribers is the cheapest expression of that.

Two cheap hardenings, neither a redesign: (1) move the store onto `subscribeRunFacts` and delete `isSnapshotRunFact`, `SNAPSHOT_RUN_FACT_TYPE_SET` and the two type aliases; (2) state the store-before-renderer ordering invariant in the doc comment on `SessionHandle.ts:222` or in `SessionEventHub.emit`, since the correctness of every `invalidate` slice depends on it and today it is only implied by construction order.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification — one subscriber that writes the sidecar and then notifies the renderer, making the ordering explicit instead of positional — would cost durable persistence in every host-less configuration (`texra run` with the live line suppressed, a VS Code session with the progress view never opened) and would move disk I/O into the presentation lifetime. It buys an explicit ordering guarantee that a one-line comment buys for free.

#### `child.activity` sits on the run plane despite being session-owned and never trace-emitted — and that placement is a dated decision worth about zero LoC to reverse

Question asked: is any fact on the wrong plane? `child.activity` looks exactly like `status` did before `status` was moved off the union. It is declared as an `AgentEvent` arm (events.ts:192-197, 'Session-owned subagent activity for a parent run stream') and listed in `RUN_FACT_EVENT_TYPES` (:410), yet no `trace.emit` anywhere produces it: the only two production emitters push it straight onto the hub as a run-scoped envelope — `ExecutionRegistry.emitChildActivity` (executionRegistry.ts:839-849, session-owned) and one desktop presentation re-seed (desktopAgentExecution.ts:453-464). Its payload even re-states the envelope's identity (`parentStreamId` alongside `SessionEvent.streamId`, both the parent stream). `TexraTranscriptRecorder`, which subscribes to the trace and not the hub, carries a `case 'child.activity': return;` arm (TexraTranscriptRecorder.ts:813-816) that can never fire, kept alive only by the union's exhaustiveness check. The contrast is stated in the same file: `StatusEvent` (events.ts:170-186) is explicitly documented as 'Not an AgentEvent arm: status travels only as a canonical session fact', and `RUN_FACT_EVENT_TYPES`'s own comment (:400-404) repeats it.

Why it stays: .agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-07-03-session-scoped-runtime-architecture.md:153-162 put `status` and `child.activity` on `AgentEvent` in the same sentence, and gave the reason explicitly — 'emitted by the session's RunTable which, being session-owned, emits into its own session's hub; no parent-trace lookup gymnastics'. Line 680 records that adding those arms was 'deliberately breaking for exhaustive-switch subscribers — that is the union's documented design'. The design knew it was session-owned and chose run placement anyway.

And the move buys nothing measurable. Every consumer already subscribes to both rails — ProgressBackend.ts:884-901, runProgressRenderer.ts:168-179, sessionSignalsAdapter.ts:217-280, sessionProgressSubscription.ts:204-214 — so no subscription is added or removed. The public NDJSON `updateActiveSubagents` record would be unchanged (its projection just moves from `projectCliRunFact` at sessionProgressSubscription.ts:174-183 to `projectCliSessionFact`), and trace.json already retired the child-activity keys (traceViewerSchema.vitest.ts:215 parses them only as legacy). Deletions: one recorder arm, one `RUN_FACT_EVENT_TYPES` entry, the `parentStreamId`/envelope duplication, two envelope literals. Additions: a `SessionFact` arm and a named payload type in shared/schemas/progressEvents.ts. Net roughly -15 at best, and it edits the frozen `@agent/*` SDK union for a cosmetic gain.

Dated checks: `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search 'child.activity roster session fact'` returns nothing; `git log` shows #11109 (fba8f69c8d) already trimmed this fact's reducer and badge payload; .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-run-classification-consolidation.md:616 filed the desktop re-emission as a separate, still-open concern (one author for the fact).

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep `child.activity` on `AgentEvent`. The apparent misplacement is a recorded 2026-07-03 decision with a stated rationale, the fact's only two emitters are already the session-owned registry and one host seed, and relocating it nets about -15 LoC while touching the frozen SDK union. Round 4 should not re-raise it.

The one thing genuinely worth pursuing in this area is already filed elsewhere: the desktop's hand-built `child.activity` re-emission (desktopAgentExecution.ts:453-464) is a second author for a fact the registry owns, called out at .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-run-classification-consolidation.md:616 as wanting a registry re-seed method instead. That is a single-owner fix, not a plane move.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

By keeping it: the `AgentEvent` union continues to advertise one arm no trace ever emits, and `TexraTranscriptRecorder` keeps a two-line unreachable exhaustiveness arm. Both are documented in place, so the cost is a reader's confusion, not a defect.

#### The four planes are not stacked pass-throughs: exactly one boundary re-encodes, and the remaining reduction is the hard-gated projection-zero plan

Question asked: how much of each plane is pure pass-through to the next, how many times is one fact re-encoded on the way to a screen, and is any plane's schema a strict subset of another's? Traced `updateTodos` end to end: producer -> `emitRunFact` (runFactEvents.ts:28-34) -> `TraceEmitter.emit` stage-stamp and fan-out (TraceEmitter.ts:70-91) -> `SessionHandle.attachRunTrace`/`publishRunEvent` (SessionHandle.ts:875-905) -> hub -> two filtered subscribers -> `StreamSnapshotStore.setTodos` (durable) and `SessionFactApplier` -> `SessionRendererPort` -> one of three renderers.

Findings against the four hypotheses:

1. No plane is a pass-through to the next. `TraceEmitter` has three real per-run subscribers, not just the hub bridge: `TexraTranscriptRecorder` (:874), `channelTrace` (:70), and the hub bridge (SessionHandle.ts:878). Deleting its listener set to emit straight into the hub would break the two run-scoped consumers.

2. Only one boundary genuinely re-encodes. `attachCliSessionProgressProjection` (sessionProgressSubscription.ts) is the single place the frozen NDJSON vocabulary is produced, and it is the one surface where nine arms are near-identity re-wraps. That is deliberate and already ruled: .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-25-cli-controller-seam-audit.md:369 rejects 'collapsing sessionProgressSubscription.ts into the applier' with 'frozen public NDJSON wire; the re-projection is cited approvingly as an exemplar'. Everything else in the chain is a transport crossing (webview postMessage) or a store write, not a re-encoding.

3. No plane's schema is a subset of another's. The payload vocabulary is already SSOT: `CliNdjsonProgressEventPayloads` (cliNdjsonProgressEvents.ts:57-95) imports every payload type from `@shared/schemas` rather than restating it, and `progressView/outbound.ts` derives every targeted arm's value schema through `pickProjection` from one leaf registry (projectionShape.ts:26-41, :72-95). The residual duplication the maintainers know about is the 12 targeted stream-state arms coexisting with the full `SYNC_STREAM_CONTENT` snapshot — and that is the projection-zero target, hard-gated on two named supersessions plus an unperformed bandwidth measurement (.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-15-shared-contracts-and-retirement.md:590-604, :616-640, restated as still-gated at :35 and rejected for re-raising at 2026-08-25-cli-controller-seam-audit.md:370).

4. Checked negative on the most promising apparent duplicate fact: `followUpSent` and `updateQueuedFollowUps` land on the identical renderer action (SessionFactApplier.ts:330-338, both `invalidate(streamId, 'queuedFollowUps')`) and `followUpSent` has no NDJSON projection at all (sessionProgressSubscription.ts:74). They are still distinct: `waitCoordination.ts:55-70` subscribes specifically to `followUpSent` to abort a blocking wait. Not a merge candidate.

5. `AppSignals` (140 lines, 7 signals, 8 emit sites, 11 subscribe sites) is process-scoped app lifecycle, not run or session progress, and CLAUDE.md scopes it explicitly. Two of the seven (`languageModelsChanged`, `approvalPolicyChanged`) are extension-emitted and extension-consumed, but giving them a host-local emitter adds a mechanism rather than removing one.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep all four planes and the current layer count. The next round should not re-ask 'can the trace plane emit directly into the hub' (three per-run subscribers say no), 'can the NDJSON projection be folded into the applier' (rejected 2026-08-25, frozen wire), 'can the targeted progress-view arms be deleted' (that IS projection-zero, gated), or 'are followUpSent and updateQueuedFollowUps the same fact' (no, waitCoordination distinguishes them).

The smallest concept set this subsystem could honestly express itself with, given the gates: one fact vocabulary (already SSOT via `@shared/schemas` payload types plus `pickProjection`), one hub with two scopes, one shared applier, one port, and one frozen public projection. That is what exists. The only unlocked reductions inside it are the two port-shape items in this report.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

By not pushing further: the twelve targeted progress-view arms keep existing beside the full snapshot that already carries the same fields, and `SessionRendererPort` keeps a dozen payload-carrying methods whose payloads are synchronously re-readable from `SessionState`. That is roughly -400 to -600 LoC left on the table by the shared-contracts doc's own accounting — recoverable only by granting the two named supersessions and taking the bandwidth measurement, neither of which a survey can do.

### settings-pipeline — The settings pipeline, end to end

#### The CLI's `cliConfig.ts` reader is not a redundant second settings pipeline — it runs before the platform exists

Question asked: `packages/cli/src/runtime/cliConfig.ts` is 446 lines that read the same `.texra/config.json` the catalog reads, with its own Zod schemas (`TOP_LEVEL_FIELD_SCHEMAS`, `COMMAND_FIELD_SCHEMAS`), its own key list (`CLI_SETTING_PATHS`), its own unknown-key warner, and its own file reader. Should those five keys become catalog rows so `readSetting` is the only reader in the CLI?

Answer: no. Three independent reasons, each verified in code.

(1) Ordering. `buildCliContext` (packages/cli/src/runtime/cliContext.ts:352-362) calls `loadWorkspaceCliConfig(cwd)` and `loadUserApprovalPolicy(init.storageRoot)` in a `Promise.all`, and every command calls `initLocalCliPlatform(context)` only afterwards inside its own `run` (e.g. packages/cli/src/commands/config.ts:58, 92). There is no `platform()` to read through at that point. cliConfig.ts:365-378 documents exactly this: "The CLI resolves its approval policy in `buildCliContext`, before `initCliPlatform` creates that provider, so it reads the user layer here rather than standing up a second config provider." Routing these through the catalog would mean either initializing the platform earlier (a much larger change to CLI startup) or building a throwaway second provider — the thing the comment says it is avoiding.

(2) Shape. `texra.chat` and `texra.run` are nested JSON objects with `{agent, model}` inside (`pickCommandSection`, cliConfig.ts:222-246). Every catalog row is a scalar addressed by one flat dotted key (`StateSettingEntry.key`, `readSetting`'s `stores[slot].get(entry.key)`), and `settingIsBoolean`/`settingIsString`/`settingIsNumber` (stateSettings.ts:1662-1675) are the whole edit-affordance vocabulary. A nested command section has no representation in the row shape.

(3) Validation. `ModelSchema` refines against `isCliSupportedModelId`, which reads `MODEL_CONFIGS` from `llm-zoo` and excludes the Copilot provider (cliConfig.ts:53-56, 101-104); the agent field is validated against the live roster elsewhere. These are runtime-catalog-dependent, not static schemas, and the warnings they produce name the _file path_ ("Ignoring invalid /path/.texra/config.json key ...") — a diagnostic `readSetting`'s one-line `log.warn` does not produce.

The two systems already meet where they should: `KNOWN_TEXRA_KEYS` (packages/cli/src/schemas/knownKeys.ts) is `CLI_CONFIG_SLOT_KEYS` (derived from the catalog) union `CLI_SETTING_PATHS`, so the CLI's unknown-key detector honors both halves from one set, and `texra config show` (packages/cli/src/commands/config.ts:60-67) reads the catalog half through `readSetting(entry, stores, 'cli')`. The overlap is exactly one key, `texra.approvalPolicy`, which is a catalog row and also read pre-platform by `loadUserApprovalPolicy` — and that duplication is addressed narrowly in finding 3 rather than by merging the pipelines.

Dated checks: `git log --oneline -20 packages/cli/src/runtime/cliConfig.ts` shows repeated consolidation inside the file (the `pickFields` single-walk rewrite, the `readJsonConfigFile` extraction) and no attempt to merge it outward; .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-06-26-config-catalog-unification.md's "correction 2" already ruled that the CLI's three stores are distinct and the accessor must be host-aware rather than assuming slot identity.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep both readers. The seam between them is `KNOWN_TEXRA_KEYS`, which is one set built from two derivations — that is the right size for the seam, and it is smaller than the pre-platform provider or the nested-row support a merge would require. If a future change makes the CLI initialize its platform before `buildCliContext`, revisit reason (1); reasons (2) and (3) would still stand for `chat`/`run`/`model`.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification — making `agent`, `model`, `outputFormat`, `approvalPolicy`, `chat.*`, `run.*` catalog rows so `/config` and the launch path share one reader — nets strongly positive. It would add a second pre-platform config provider or an earlier platform init, a nested-section concept to `StateSettingEntry` (row shape, `settingIs*` vocabulary, both settings UIs), a per-file warning channel on `readSetting`, and roster/`MODEL_CONFIGS`-aware refinements inside a host-neutral schema module — against deleting roughly 120 lines of `pickFields`/`TOP_LEVEL_FIELD_SCHEMAS` machinery. Estimated net +150 LoC and one more concept, not fewer.

#### `honoredBy.reader` and `reachability.through` look like documentation stored as data, but both are path-verified and one drives the CLI unknown-key whitelist

Question asked: `StateSettingEntry.honoredBy` carries a `reader` file path per host and an optional `reachability: { command, through }` whose `through` is described in its own doc comment as "a review checklist item, not executable wiring" (stateSettings.ts:127-140). Twenty `through:` lines and twelve `reader:` declarations, plus ~90 lines of shared reachability constants (stateSettings.ts:755-850). That reads like prose that drifted into a type. Delete it?

No, on two counts.

(1) `honoredBy` is load-bearing at runtime, not just in review. `CLI_CONFIG_SLOT_KEYS` (stateSettings.ts:1598-1603) is `ALL_SETTINGS.filter(e => e.slots.cli === 'config' && (e.honoredBy.cli !== undefined || e.writtenBy?.cli !== undefined))`, and that list is half of `KNOWN_TEXRA_KEYS` (packages/cli/src/schemas/knownKeys.ts:23-26), which `cliConfig.ts:147` uses to decide whether a key in a user's `.texra/config.json` warns as unknown. Dropping `honoredBy.cli` from a row makes the CLI start warning about a key it honors. The header comment at stateSettings.ts:88-93 records that this field replaced three separately hand-kept lists (`CLI_CORE_SETTING_PATHS`, `EXTENSION_ONLY_CORE_SETTING_PATHS`, and a reader registry that lived in the test suite) — deleting it re-creates them.

(2) The `reader`/`through` strings are machine-checked, not inert. src/test-kernel/shared/stateSettings.vitest.ts:164-186 asserts every `honoredBy[host].reader` resolves to a file that `existsSync` on disk and that a reader path under `packages/extension/`, `packages/cli/`, or `packages/desktop/` is only claimed by that host. Lines 214-245 assert that every row with `surfaces.cliConfig` has a `honoredBy.cli`, that it declares a `reachability`, that `reachability.command` is a recognized runtime command, that `through` contains the declared reader as one of its segments, and that _every_ segment of the `through` trace is an existing file. So a `through` trace cannot rot silently past a file rename — the suite fails. That is the same standard AGENTS.md applies elsewhere: evidence a machine can check beats a comment.

What is genuinely editorial is `reachability.command` (a representative CLI invocation), which is only checked for its first token. That is one string per editable CLI row, and it is what makes the guardrail's "an editable `/config` row is never a silent no-op" claim meaningful.

Dated checks: `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "settings catalog"` surfaced #10945 "Name the real readers for git.numberOfCommitsToShow instead of the write allowlist" (CLOSED 2026-08-20) — the repo has already ruled once that these reader declarations must name the _real_ reader, i.e. that they are treated as verified facts rather than notes. `git log` on stateSettings.ts shows 0644fa376f "refactor(settings): separate reader and writer evidence" and 3538f283e5 "refactor(settings): inline exceptional CLI writer" deliberately refining this field rather than shrinking it. Neither round-1 nor round-2 proposed touching it.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep the fields as they are. The one shrink worth considering later, and only if it earns its keep on its own: the ~90 lines of shared `*_RUNTIME_REACHABILITY` constants (stateSettings.ts:755-850) are already deduplicated across rows, so there is nothing left to fold. If a future round wants to reduce this surface, the honest target is the number of _rows that need CLI reachability_ (i.e. shrinking `surfaces.cliConfig`), not the evidence field itself.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification — delete `reader` and `through` as prose and keep `honoredBy` as a bare host set — would cost the two guardrails that make the catalog's host claims falsifiable (a row could claim the CLI honors a key by naming nothing, or an editable `/config` row could become a no-op after a refactor with no test failing), for about -110 lines. That trade converts two machine-checked invariants into review discipline, which is the direction checklist §15 warns against.

### approval-system — Approval, permission and policy

#### How many registries does one approval decision pass through, and is that too many? Two for six kinds, three for tool edit — and the third owns filesystem preview state, not a duplicate settlement

Full trace of one extension tool-edit approval: the tool calls `requestToolEditApproval` (src/tools/approval/toolEditApproval.ts:190) → `session.approvals.toolEdit.enqueue` (per-stream PQueue with a dispatch-time bypass re-check, src/agent/runtime/streamApprovalQueue.ts:157-186) → `session.interactions.requestToolEditApproval` (pending registry #1, `SessionHostInteractions.pending`, src/agent/runtime/HostInteractions.ts:404-420,:680-700) → `progressHostInteractions.requestToolEditApproval` → `ToolEditApprovalController.requestApproval` (registry #2, `requests` map with `initializing`/`pending` phases, src/controllers/approval/ToolEditApprovalController.ts:136-215) → `showToolEditPermission` → `ApprovalRequestHandler` in presentation-only mode (registry #3, src/controllers/progressView/backend/ApprovalRequestHandler.ts:31-33). Bash, plan, proposal, retry, user-question and external-inquiry stop at two: `SessionHostInteractions.pending` plus a single `ApprovalRequestHandler` entry in `mode: 'interaction'`, which holds the `complete` callback AND the replay payload together (:10-16,:52-66).

That two-registry state is the resolution of a previously-flagged duplication. .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-07-09-tech-debt-audit-runtime-ui.md:550-576 (A16) recorded "two registries per webview-host kind" — a settle map in `extensionHostInteractions.ts`/`desktopHostInteractions.ts` plus `ApprovalRequestHandler` for replay — and formally closed the merge as won't-do so it would "stop being re-raised", noting the merge target was attempted and reverted (`git 6e4cef543`). At HEAD both of those host files are gone: `ls packages/extension/src/**/extensionHostInteractions.ts` and the desktop equivalent return nothing, and src/controllers/progressView/backend/progressHostInteractions.ts is the one shared adapter for both webview hosts. The settle callback and the replay payload now live in the same `PendingInteraction` entry, so A16's duplication is already gone for six of seven kinds.

The surviving third registry is tool-edit-specific and holds something no other registry can: staged preview files, an open host diff view, `workspaceTempCleanup` closures, and the LaTeX preview/latexdiff entry state (ToolEditApprovalController.ts:120-215,:298-318). Its `initializing` phase exists because `stagePreview` is async and a cancel can land mid-staging (:157-175) — a race `ApprovalRequestHandler`'s synchronous `register` cannot represent without re-adding a phase.

Also re-checked and re-confirmed settled: .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-ssot-consolidation-plan.md:87 ("Per-host interaction registries are the deliberate resting state (HOST-2)"), and .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-25-cli-controller-seam-audit.md:366-369, which lists "Merging `ApprovalRequestHandler`'s registry with the host settle maps" as A16-fenced, "attempted and reverted twice, NET_LOSS-adjudicated". The CLI's `approvalQueue.ts` FIFO is not a third CLI registry either: its header (:1-15) records that it is deliberately the _only_ owner of CLI request liveness, with `reserveApproval` covering pre-modal work so there is "no second registry to sweep".

**Why the current shape is right**

Current shape is right. The correct count is: one session-level owner (`SessionHostInteractions.pending`, which owns attach/detach dispatch and the cancel-selector sweep), one host-level owner per host family (`ApprovalRequestHandler` for the webview hosts, `approvalQueue.ts` for the CLI TUI, inline decision for CLI headless), and — for tool edit only — one resource owner (`ToolEditApprovalController`) because a tool-edit approval has host filesystem state that outlives neither of the other two. Six kinds are already at the two-registry floor.

The next round should record this as answered rather than re-deriving it: the three-registry count for tool edit is not the A16 duplication (that one is fixed), and merging `ToolEditApprovalController` into `ApprovalRequestHandler` would move the preview lifecycle and the staging race into a generic per-kind handler that six other kinds do not need — an extraction across divergent sites, which is precisely the R4 trap A16 named.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

Nothing. The tempting simplification — "one PendingInteractionTable consumed by all hosts" — was costed twice and landed net-positive both times; A16's R4 note adds that the CLI cannot consume it at all, having no settle-map shape.

#### Three mechanisms decide whether a Bash command prompts (session policy, two global settings, per-stream per-kind bypass) — and the product spec requires all three to be independent

`decideTexraApproval` (src/shared/approvalPolicy.ts:118-129) takes four inputs, and three of them are independently owned facts: `policy` from `SessionHandle.approvalPolicy` (src/agent/runtime/SessionHandle.ts:271-278, seeded by five host sites listed in the ratchet's `SEED_CALL_ALLOWLIST`), `promptRequired` from a global config key read per request (`BASH_APPROVAL_CONFIG_KEY = 'texra.toolUse.requireBashApproval'`, src/shared/schemas/agentCliSettings.ts:119, read at src/tools/approval/bashApproval.ts:97; `TOOL_EDIT_APPROVAL_CONFIG_KEY`, src/shared/schemas/coreSettings.ts:36, read at toolEditApproval.ts:194), and `scopedBypass` from the per-stream per-kind bypass in src/agent/runtime/streamApprovalQueue.ts. It is tempting to read that as three owners of one decision.

It is not, and the reason is on the record. gh issue 9597 (CLOSED, tracking): "TeXRA needs one host-neutral approval-policy authority … but it must preserve two independent user choices: 1. Yolo is real yolo … 2. A user may allow Bash automatically while still requiring approval for TeX and other edits", and, on the settings interface, "This must not be encoded as yolo, because yolo permits both." So `ask` + `requireBashApproval=false` is a _required_ product state that no three-value policy enum can express. The third mechanism is a different axis again: the bypass is run-scoped and stream-scoped (`AUTO-BASH`/`AUTO-EDIT` in the CLI status bar, the AUTO-EDIT/AUTO-BASH toolbar toggles split in #11383), and #9597 additionally requires "Run as Goal continues to grant Bash-only scoped permission. It does not grant edit permission" — implemented at src/tools/goal/goalAutoApproval.ts:24-32 by setting exactly one kind's stream bypass.

The evaluator is already the single arbiter: `never` short-circuits before `promptRequired` and before `scopedBypass` (src/shared/approvalPolicy.ts:124), which is #9597's "`never` wins over prompt-disable settings and scoped bypasses" invariant, and src/test-kernel/architecture/approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet.vitest.ts pins the vocabulary to one module and the evaluator to a hardcoded allowlist. .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-ssot-consolidation-plan.md:89-90 separately rules that the per-request `getConfig` reads in `src/tools/approval/*` are "**correct** (in-memory map lookup; live mid-run toggles). Do not cache them" — so even the apparent re-read is settled.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep three inputs and one evaluator. The minimum concept set here is genuinely three, because the three facts have different lifetimes and different owners: policy is session-lived and host-seeded, the prompt-required settings are workspace/global-persisted and user-owned, and the bypass is stream-lived and granted from an approval prompt or a toolbar toggle. Collapsing any pair deletes a product state #9597 names as required. The refactor that _would_ be a win — folding the two boolean settings into a per-kind policy enum — trades two booleans for a nine-cell matrix and a settings migration on a persisted key, which is a net add plus a persisted-format change.

Record this so a fourth round does not re-open "why does `decideTexraApproval` take four arguments".

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

For the tempting simplification: encoding "Bash automatic, edits gated" as a policy value would have collapsed the two settings into the enum and saved roughly 20 lines of config plumbing, at the cost of contradicting the owner's 2026-08-02 supersession recorded in #9597 and requiring a migration of two persisted settings keys.
