### tool-layer — The tool layer as an architecture

#### "Define a tool" is not four collapsible concepts — ITool/BaseTool/defineTool/ToolDefinition each carry a different half of the contract

Question asked: could tools be plain objects (`const readFile = tool({name, schema, execute})`) so that `BaseTool`, the `DefinedToolClass` type gymnastics, and the `new X()` at 53 registry sites all stop existing? Traced it: 54 classes use `extends defineTool({...})` (grep `extends defineTool`), there are zero other `extends BaseTool` / `implements ITool` implementors in production besides the generated class inside define.ts:78 and `MapToolRegistry` (ToolTypes.ts:51), and 14 of those 54 files use instance members inside `execute` — MemoryTool.ts and ExecutionsTool.ts have 24 `this.` references each, PlanTool.ts 11, InlineCommentTool.ts 7, LspTools.ts 6, then DiagnosticsTool/LoogleTool/ExternalInquiryTool/ReadTool/bash/AcceptRunFilesTool/WebFetchTool/ApplyTeamTool/DelegationTools. A function-object form would have to re-express those as closures or module-level helpers in 14 files while rewriting 54, and the four concepts are genuinely four different things, not a layered pass-through: `ITool` is the consumption contract the dispatcher and the frozen SDK use (`packages/agent/src/index.ts:45-48,80` exports `ITool`, `MapToolRegistry`, `defineTool`, `DefinedToolClass` — note `BaseTool` is deliberately NOT exported), `BaseTool.call` is the shared validation-and-error envelope (base.ts:60-92, including the load-bearing sync-validation comment at :62-65 that keeps a host interaction in the caller's synchronous turn), `defineTool` is the flag/host/JSON-schema constructor, and `ToolDefinition` is the wire shape sent to providers. The one shrink actually available is folding base.ts (95 lines, single importer: define.ts:12) into define.ts for about -10 LoC and one fewer module — worth doing only if someone is already editing both, since `DefinedToolClass`'s TS4094 workaround (define.ts:39-54) means the two files are read together anyway. The 5 execution flags all have live readers (ToolUseDispatchNode.ts:228,347,351,372 and agentToolResolution.ts:122), and `.nullish()`-with-fallback (inputSchema.ts:20-25) is a documented provider requirement, off limits.

**Why the current shape is right**

Current shape is right. Keep `ITool` + `BaseTool` + `defineTool` + `ToolDefinition` as four distinct roles and keep tools as classes. If someone wants the cosmetic win, fold src/tools/core/base.ts into src/tools/core/define.ts (one module, ~-10 LoC) — but do not convert tools to plain objects: the migration touches 54 files, restructures 14, and nets positive LoC for no capability gained.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification (tool = plain object) would have cost a 54-file rewrite, the loss of private per-tool helpers in 14 tools, and either widening `BaseTool.execute` to public or re-solving the TS4094 anonymous-class-declaration problem that define.ts:39-54 already solved.

#### BUILTIN_TOOLS is a curated dashboard table, not a drifting mirror of the registry

Question asked: `BUILTIN_TOOLS` (src/controllers/settingsView/ToolDashboardData.ts:68-144) and `EXTERNAL_TOOL_DEFS` (src/tools/externalToolDefs.ts, 10 groups) both list registered tool names by hand — is that a third listing of the registry that silently drifts, and should the group be declared on the tool the way `availabilityCategory` is? src/shared/schemas/toolDefinition.ts:27-32 even states the principle: "Declared by the tool itself — never a side table mapping tool names to categories." I computed the actual gap: of the 53 registry entries, exactly 14 appear in neither table — the 10-tool setup family (probe_environment, verify_setup, unset_api_key, list_api_keys, invoke_command, install_vscode_extension, read_config, update_config, send_to_terminal, apply_team) plus ask_user_question, inline_comment, open_pdf, report_review_issue. Every one of them is host/agent infrastructure with no external dependency to probe and no user-facing toggle, which is what these tables exist to render (`toggleable`, `installCommand`, `authCommand`, `comingSoon`, `hideFromCli` — externalToolDefs.ts:70-107). The omission is curation, not drift. The two tables also answer different questions and are joined, not duplicated: `unavailableHosts` on the tool answers "can this host run it" (registry.ts:194-199, consumed by ToolDashboardData.ts:169 and packages/cli/src/runtime/tools.ts:33-43), while `EXTERNAL_TOOL_DEFS.tools` answers "which tools does this external dependency gate" (toolAvailability.ts:53-61, 192-203). Renames are already fenced: both tables are typed `readonly RegisteredToolName[]`, so a rename is a compile error; only an ADDITION is silent, and a new infrastructure tool being absent from a user-facing dashboard is the correct default.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep both tables where they are. Declaring a dashboard group on each of the 53 tools would add ~24 per-tool lines while still needing a per-group table for id/name/category/description — net positive LoC — and would force a user-facing group decision on 14 internal tools that should not appear. If exhaustiveness ever matters, the cheap move is an `AssertNever`-style guard like the two already in registry.ts:166-174, not moving the data.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification (group declared on the tool, table derived) would have cost roughly +20 LoC net, a group assignment for every infrastructure tool, and a dashboard that grows a row every time an internal tool is registered.

### model-layer — The model layer as an architecture

#### Availability (computeModelOptions) and dispatch (ModelFactory) restate the same route precedence — and should keep doing so

QUESTION: the repo walks the model-routing precedence twice. `resolveModelAvailability` (src/model/computeModelOptions.ts:296-372) orders retired → Copilot → OpenRouter-unsupported → ChatGPT subscription → Grok subscription → OpenRouter → direct key. `resolveModelHandlerCompatibilityKey` (src/agent/runtime/ModelFactory.ts:234-275) plus `createModelHandlerForResolvedCompatibilityKey` (:577-691) orders validation → Copilot → Responses → OpenRouter → provider table, with the Codex and Kimi Code overrides applied after. Two call sites even carry "mirror ModelFactory" comments (computeModelOptions.ts:147-155 and :277-280). Should these become one `RouteDecision` owner?

ANSWER: no, and the current shape is already the right one — the PREDICATES are single-owner; only the ORDERING is restated. Counted owners for every fact both paths need: `shouldRouteModelThroughOpenRouter` — 1 definition (src/model/openRouterRouting.ts:94), 6 consumers; `prefersCopilotRoute` — 1 definition (src/model/copilotRouting.ts), 2 consumers; `isOpenRouterRoutingUnsupported` — 1 definition (openRouterRouting.ts:55), 2 consumers; `resolveDirectModelApiKeyProvider` — 1 definition (:77), 4 consumers; `kimiCodeEffectiveConfig` — 1 definition, 2 consumers; `resolveCodexSubscriptionCapabilities` — 1 definition (src/model/providerCapabilities.ts:139), 3 consumers. There is no second implementation of any routing fact anywhere.

What the two walks differ on is irreducible: the availability walk is async over pre-resolved secret facts and produces a `ModelAvailabilityKind` + user-facing label + a `ProviderCapabilityProfile` for the picker row; the dispatch walk is a pure sync key predicate that must never throw for `modelSwitchDisabledReason` (src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/runToolUseFlow.ts:265-284) plus a throwing async instantiation path. Issue #6644 (CLOSED) already ruled on exactly this axis inside ModelFactory and its rationale generalises: "the two are deliberately not identical, which is what makes a naive 'share one decision function' risky."

COST OF COLLAPSING ANYWAY: a shared `RouteDecision` discriminated union needs ~6 variants plus two mappers (DU → availability kind + label + capability profile; DU → compatibility key + handler class), and the availability side must keep its own async fact-gathering because the dispatch side must stay sync. Estimated delete ≈ 60 branch lines, add ≈ 90. Net ≈ +30 LoC for no element reduction — a §13-style "reduction" that isn't one.

WHAT WOULD ACTUALLY DRIFT: nothing observed. I diffed the two orderings: retired is availability-only (dispatch has no retired gate, deliberately — a persisted run on a retired model must still resume), validation is dispatch-only, Responses-vs-Chat is invisible to availability (both are "provider key"), and the Copilot-first and OpenRouter-unsupported gates match on both sides. The only structural gap is that dispatch's Codex gate is async while availability's is not, which finding 2 addresses separately.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep both walks. The correct maintenance rule — worth writing into src/model/computeModelOptions.ts's header rather than the two per-site "mirror ModelFactory" comments — is: any NEW routing fact must be added as a predicate in src/model/ with a single definition, and both walks must consume it; adding a branch that computes a routing fact inline in either walk is the violation, not the duplication of ordering. Next round should not re-ask this.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification (one `RouteDecision` DU consumed by both) would cost ~+30 LoC, force the availability path's async secret reads into a sync-compatible shape or force the sync key predicate to become async (breaking `modelSwitchDisabledReason`'s never-throw contract), and buy no element reduction — the DU variants would map 1:1 onto branches that already exist.

#### ModelCell is not the only owner of the provider client, and making it one would cost more than it saves

QUESTION: there are two client caches. `ModelCell` boxes one (`#current`/`#building`/`#buildingFor`, src/agent/runtime/ModelCell.ts:43-97) and `ModelHandlerGoogleInteractions` keeps its own (`this.googleClient`, :601-635). `ModelHandler.refreshClient` (src/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandler.ts:1054-1058) exists ONLY as an override seam so `ModelCell.rebind` can clear the Google one (its doc comment says so). Is the cell's cache redundant — or is Google's?

ANSWER: neither. The handler self-calls `getClient()` from places the cell cannot reach, because handlers hold no reference to the cell: `ModelHandlerGoogleInteractions.uploadMediaEntries` passes `getClient: () => this.getClient()` into `uploadGoogleMediaEntries` (:747), which awaits it once per media round (src/agent/modelHandlers/google/googleHandlerShared.ts:51). Delete Google's cache and every media round re-resolves the credential and constructs a new `GoogleGenAI`. Delete the cell's cache and the flow's per-attempt `getClient()` (src/agent/core/flows/ModelInvocationNode.ts:339,683; src/agent/implementations/flows/tooluse/toolUseRound/ToolUseDispatchNode.ts:578) rebuilds per attempt.

Moving the cache down into `ModelHandler` (the shape that would let ModelCell shed ~45 lines) is not free: the cell's `rebind` refuses to publish a replacement that lost an abort race (ModelCell.ts:109-121) and `#buildingFor` refuses to publish a build retired by a mid-flight `swap` (:80-95). A handler-owned memo cannot express the first without threading an `AbortSignal` through `refreshClient` into every provider override, and its variant of the second is only accidentally equivalent (a retired handler's cache dies with it, but the in-flight promise still resolves into the retired handler). That is ~-45 LoC in the cell, ~+18 in the base, ~-14 in Google, and a genuine semantic regression on the cancel-during-rebind path that three suites currently pin (src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/ModelCell.vitest.ts, ModelClientRouteSnapshot.vitest.ts, GoogleClientRefresh.vitest.ts). ModelCell's ownership was ruled on in .agents/docs/rejected/architecture/2026-06-29-prd-runtime-gold-standard.md and the rulings ledger; this proposal would reopen it for ~-40 LoC.

TWO REAL RESIDUES FOUND WHILE CHECKING (both small, both in-repo verifiable, neither crossing a wire/persisted/settings surface — `estimateTokenCount` is NOT on `IModelHandler` (src/agent/types/IModelHandler.ts:51-52 lists only getClient/refreshClient) and `packages/agent/src/*.ts` never mentions ModelHandler): (1) `TokenCountOptions.client` is optional (src/agent/types/ModelHandlerContracts.ts:50-52) but every production caller supplies it — modelHandlerOpenAI.ts:619, modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts:861 and :1905, modelHandlerAnthropic.ts:967, modelHandlerGoogleInteractions.ts:1491 — so the `options?.client ?? (await this.getClient())` fallback in five overrides is a second, unreachable client-acquisition path. (2) `ModelHandlerVscodeLm.estimateTokenCount` (src/agent/modelHandlers/vscodelm/modelHandlerVscodeLm.ts:504-538) has no production caller at all: the vscodelm handler never calls `applyTokenCountLimit`, and nothing outside the handler subtree calls `estimateTokenCount`.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep the two caches and `refreshClient`. Record the answer so the next round stops re-asking: the cell owns the client the FLOW uses and the handler owns the client its OWN internals use, and `refreshClient` is the one-line reconciliation seam between them. The cheap residue is worth a separate low-risk PR: make `TokenCountOptions.client` required and delete the five `?? (await this.getClient())` fallbacks (≈ -10 LoC, and the removal of an unreachable second credential path from the handler subtree), and separately verify-then-delete `ModelHandlerVscodeLm.estimateTokenCount` (≈ -35 LoC). Neither belongs in a ModelCell refactor.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

For keep: the tempting simplification (one client owner = the handler; ModelCell becomes a pass-through) would have cost the cancel-during-rebind guarantee and the mid-flight-swap retirement guard, both documented and tested, in exchange for roughly -40 LoC — and it would reopen a ruled ownership decision (runtime gold-standard PRD) for that price.

### flow-families — The two flow families

#### The two flow families should stay two: the shared skeleton is ~35 lines, the divergence is semantic

Both families have the identical two-level shape: an outer PersistedFlow whose one node builds an in-memory inner Flow. Reflection: runReflectionFlow.ts:236 builds RoundPersistedFlow(prep→texcount→media→ResponseCycleNode→OutputNode); ResponseCycleNode.ts:93 builds createResponseCycleFlow() (prep→ModelInvocationNode→process→continuation, COMPLETE→finalize). Tool-use: runToolUseFlow.ts:485 builds ToolUsePersistedFlow(prepare→cycle→wait); ToolUseCycleNode.ts:99 builds createToolUseRoundFlow() (prep→ModelInvocationNode→process→dispatch). The literally-shared code is: a 4-variant outcome DU, an execFallback→buildFailedRetryInfo pair (ResponseCycleNode.ts:141-148 vs ToolUseCycleNode.ts:181-190, 8 lines each), and an AgentWorkspaceState.fromSnapshot/toSnapshot round-trip. Everything else diverges on meaning, not accident: reflection's round accumulates responseTimeMs across continuations inside one ConversationRoundStateSnapshot and finalizes once (ResponseCycleFlow.ts:355-361, :440-456), tool-use finalizes per model call (ToolUseProcessNode.ts:201-207); reflection's loop is bounded by a configured count with a one-shot compile-repair grant (#7077, RoundPersistedFlow.ts:198-210), tool-use's is unbounded and suspends at WAITING for a follow-up; reflection's inner flow writes and appends an output file, tool-use's dispatches tools; the outer shared schemas persist disjoint field sets (ReflectionFlowState.ts vs tooluse/nodes/types.ts:45). The kernel already holds every genuinely common piece: ModelInvocationNode (865 lines, both), CommonCycleTypes, BaseFlowServices, FlowTransitions. Dated ruling: .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-14-delegation-flow-substrate-consolidation.md item 8 (lines 339-378) inventories exactly this list of twins and rules 'Leave the cycle-node skeletons alone unless a later change touches both … a forced merge is the super-cycle trap'; its one attempted extraction (the persisted-record hydration helper) was measured at +35 production LoC and closed as fails-net-gain (doc lines 24-31). git log on these paths since then (#11434, #11419, #11413) shows only in-family collapses, none re-opening the merge.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep two families, two outer loops, two inner cycle flows. A generic 'cycle node' would need six type parameters (outer shared, outer services, inner shared, inner services, prep input, outcome payload) to express ~35 lines of overlap, and would re-couple the compile-repair round bound to the WAITING suspension. The correct reductions are inside each family (findings 2, 3, 5) and in the kernel's membership (finding 4), not across the seam.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification — one parameterized CycleNode plus one shared round-loop class — would net positive LoC by the same measurement that killed item 8's hydration helper, and would put reflection's bounded-round policy and tool-use's suspension policy behind one set of callbacks that no single caller uses fully.

### progress-rendering — Progress rendering across four hosts

#### Do not generalize #11447: fold only the ALREADY payload-free port methods into invalidate(), never the payload-carrying ones

Question asked: `SessionRendererPort` has 18 methods and three implementations, but two of the three barely use the vocabulary — `TuiSessionRenderer` (packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/sessionSignalsAdapter.ts:54-201) answers 7 of them with a bare `invalidateChildStreams()`, and `HeadlessPort` (packages/cli/src/runtime/runProgressRenderer.ts:394-467) answers 12 of 18 with `{}`. Only `LitSessionRenderer` needs the granularity, because each method maps to one `PROGRESS_VIEW_COMMANDS.UPDATE_*` wire arm. So: should the rest of the family collapse into `invalidate(streamId, slice)` the way #11447 proposes for `onMissingOutputsChanged`?

I checked six candidates and they split cleanly into two classes.

Class A — already payload-free, safe: `onBadgesChanged(streamId)` (port :88) and `onMissingOutputsChanged(streamId)` (port :90). Both take only a stream id; every renderer already re-reads shared state. Folding them changes nothing but the dispatch shape. #11447 (OPEN, `status:ready`, filed 2026-08-26) covers `onMissingOutputsChanged`; `onBadgesChanged` is its twin.

Class B — payload-carrying, DO NOT fold: `onStreamDescriptionChanged` (:73), `onTodosChanged` (:98), `onPlanChanged` (:100), `onGoalActiveChanged` (:104), `onConversationProgressChanged` (:75), `onStageChanged` (:84). Folding these means relocating a READ, not just a dispatch. Each payload is re-derivable — description from `SessionState.setStreamDescription` written one line earlier (SessionFactApplier.ts:629-630); todos/plan from `state.snapshots.getWorkPlan` because `StreamSnapshotStore` subscribes to the same run facts and is constructed with the session, so its write lands before the applier's notify (src/transcript/StreamSnapshotStore.ts:620-672, :1233-1274); goal from `getStreamControls(streamId).goal*`, which reads the same `GoalStore.getForStream` the applier reads and where `isGoalInFlight(goal)` is literally `goal != null` (src/shared/schemas/goal.ts:22-26, src/controllers/progressView/progressStreamControls.ts:35-48). But two of them cannot be folded safely at all: `SessionState.updateStreamState` is a NO-OP when no `StreamExecutionState` row exists (SessionState.ts:340-347), so `handleUpdateConversationProgress` (:706-718) and `handleUpdateStage` (:720-723) can notify with a value the shared state silently refused to store. Reading from state instead of the payload would drop those updates.

And the decisive reason not to do the rest: a DATED, still-live plan deletes the entire band rather than converting it. .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-15-shared-contracts-and-retirement.md §7.2, table 'Projection-zero-only', rows: '`LitSessionRenderer.ts` targeted-send band + debounce apparatus (`:162-306,:434-480,:35,:44-52,:94-96`) −170 net' and '`SessionRendererPort.ts` 113 → ~35, −78 — open point: `onStreamDescriptionChanged`/`onInquiryThreadUpdated` (kept wire) unruled' and '`SessionFactApplier.ts` notify half −40'. Under projection-zero, `onTodosChanged`/`onPlanChanged`/`onGoalActiveChanged` are DELETED, not converted to slices; converting them now is work that gets undone. This is exactly the ground on which round 2's verifier refuted the GoalState candidate ('rework on code with a dated deletion plan and a named freeze on the ipc literals', .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-26-simplification-survey-round2.md §rederivation-helpers).

The gate is real and unmoved: §4b's header says 'STILL GATED (2026-08-19) … the two supersessions item 1 asks for — §0.1 item 8's dual-path clause and item 6's progress-view literal freeze — have not been granted, and both clauses stand unchanged in `2026-08-03-ssot-consolidation-plan.md:62-90`.'

**Why the current shape is right**

The current shape is right, with one principled line drawn through it: a port method may fold into `invalidate(streamId, slice)` if and only if it already carries no payload beyond the stream id. That is #11447's class and `onBadgesChanged` is the only other member. Everything that carries a payload stays a method until the projection-zero gate opens, at which point it is deleted outright rather than renamed — so the correct unit of work is the gate (§4b's two named supersessions plus the bandwidth measurement on the `runUsage` maps), not another slice.

The honest arithmetic for the tempting version, so it does not get re-derived: folding all six Class-B methods is ≈ −55 LoC and −6 interface methods, offset by +6 union members and +18 switch arms across three implementations, with medium behaviour risk on two of the six. That is below the bar on its own and negative once you count it as rework against a −248 LoC gated plan that deletes the same lines.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

Nothing, while the gate holds. What the current shape costs is a port whose method count is set by Lit's wire vocabulary rather than by what the three renderers actually need — visible as 12 empty methods in `HeadlessPort` and 7 identical `invalidateChildStreams()` bodies in `TuiSessionRenderer`. That cost is bounded and is paid off in one move when projection-zero lands, not in six.

#### StreamTabInfo and StreamMetadata are not one per-stream record wrongly split — the tab-info domain is strictly wider

Question asked: the Lit path reshapes one stream's record twice on the way out — `buildStreamInfo`/`buildStreamTabInfo` produces `StreamTabInfo` (src/controllers/session/streamTabInfo.ts:35-90) and `metadataFor`/`buildStreamMetadata` produces `StreamMetadata` (LitSessionRenderer.ts:434-460, src/shared/streams/streamMetadata.ts:22-43) — and the two ALWAYS travel together. `updateStreamMetadata` sends both in one `UPDATE_STREAM_METADATA` (LitSessionRenderer.ts:379-393) and `metadataFor` even takes the already-built `streamInfo` as its first argument; `sendStreamMetadata` sends `streams[]` plus a `streamStates` record keyed by `streamInfo.name` (:400-431). The frontend then keeps them in two parallel maps: `streamById: Map<StreamTabId, StreamTabInfo>` and `streamStates: Map<StreamTabId, StreamState>` (streamLifecycleSlice.ts:48-104). One fact, two shapes, two maps, plus a `mergeBackendOwnedState` reconciler (streamStateMerge.ts) and an `assertKnownActiveStreamId` console.warn for when the two maps disagree (streamLifecycleSlice.ts:106-119). It looks exactly like one record that was split.

It is not. The domains differ: `StreamTabInfo` exists for streams that have NO `StreamState` at all. `mergeBackendOwnedState` returns `undefined` when `!metadata.category` (streamStateMerge.ts:31-40, with the comment 'No state and no resolved category: the stream is still pending — there is no arm to create, and fabricating one (the old `?? Workflow`) is exactly the misclassification this shape exists to prevent'), and `updateStreamInfo` only calls `ensureStreamState` when `stream.agentCategory` is set (streamLifecycleSlice.ts:94-99) — while `draft.streamById = newStreamById` is unconditional (:102). So a stream whose run identity has not resolved yet lands in `streamById` and NOT in `streamStates`, and the tab rail renders it. `buildStreamTabInfo`'s own doc records why that matters: 'A stream whose identity has not resolved yet (no `run.start` seen, no durable record hydrated) renders pending — never a fabricated default kind or category', and its `identityName` fallback (:47-52) cites #9861, where a generic placeholder was tried and 'hid the only real information available'.

The other half of the split is equally load-bearing in the opposite direction: `StreamState` is a category-DISCRIMINATED union carrying frontend-owned data (`taskGroups`, `ui`, log state), and `mergeBackendOwnedState`'s category-change arm rebuilds it from `createStreamState` defaults (streamStateMerge.ts:42-49). Merging `StreamTabInfo` into it would put identity/label/worktree fields inside a record that is deliberately thrown away and recreated whenever the category changes.

Dedupe: `StreamTabInfo` appears in the round-2 doc only in the WorktreePRInfo candidate (deleting a dead sub-shape of it), never as a merge target. `buildStreamMetadata` appears in neither survey doc. Not in rounds12-index.md. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "progress view renderer"` returns no row proposing the merge.

Settled surfaces: `StreamTabInfo` and `StreamMetadata` are progress-view IPC message members. .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-ssot-consolidation-plan.md §0.1 item 6 freezes the progress-view literals; .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-15-shared-contracts-and-retirement.md §2.6 ('"A stream" — 11 schemas, mostly disciplined, four defects') already audited the stream-schema family and prescribed only `StreamIdentityFieldsSchema` declare-once (~−40), not a tab-info/metadata merge.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep the two shapes and the two frontend maps. The right mental model is not 'one record split in two' but 'a tab rail that can show a stream before the stream has a state arm'. `StreamTabInfo` is the wider, always-available projection; `StreamMetadata` patches the narrower category-discriminated `StreamState` that only exists once a category resolves. `metadataFor` taking `streamInfo` as its input is the correct dependency direction, not evidence of redundancy.

The one thing worth doing here has already been prescribed elsewhere: §2.6's `StreamIdentityFieldsSchema` declare-once, which shrinks the overlap between the two builders without merging them. That belongs to the §2.6 PR, not to a new candidate.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification — one `StreamState` per stream carrying tab info — would cost the pending-stream tab (a run that has emitted `setActiveStream` but not yet `run.start` would vanish from the rail until its category resolves) and would put identity/worktree fields inside a record that `mergeBackendOwnedState` deliberately discards on a category change. It would delete roughly one map and ~40 lines of merge bookkeeping and buy back a regression #9861 already litigated.

#### contextState is derived twice on purpose — the CLI reads the session record, the webview folds it out of the transcript rail, and only the second survives a reopen from disk

Question asked: `SessionRenderSlice` includes `'contextState'` (SessionRendererPort.ts:33) and the applier fires it on every `context.state` run fact (SessionFactApplier.ts:176-188) — but `LitSessionRenderer.invalidate` answers that slice with a `return;` and a comment (LitSessionRenderer.ts:181-187). A notification one of the three renderers deliberately ignores is exactly the shape a pattern-matching sweep flags as dead. Is it?

No. The same fact reaches the two hosts by two different routes, and each route is the only one that works for its host. CLI: the applier writes `StreamExecutionState.contextState` (SessionState.ts:87, written at SessionFactApplier.ts:176-187 with `roundedUtilizationPercent`), and the TUI status bar reads it directly (packages/cli/src/chat/tui/panes/StatusBar.tsx:286 `contextState: displayStreamState?.contextState`; the gauge at packages/cli/src/chat/tui/panes/statusBarDisplay.ts:239-259 carries the comment 'The gauge renders `StreamExecutionState.contextState` — the model handler's own number'). Webview: the same emit also lands as a `contextState` transcript log entry (src/shared/schemas/log.ts:50, src/shared/schemas/streamLogEntry.ts:124), and the frontend folds it out of the log rail (packages/extension/src/progressView/frontend/slices/logSlice.ts:151 `streamState.contextState = entry.data`) into `UsagePanel` (components/UsagePanel.ts:161, :285-287 via BaseStreamContent.ts:86-90).

Why neither route can absorb the other: `StreamExecutionState` is EPHEMERAL session state — `clearEphemeralStreamState` drops it (SessionState.ts:571-576) and nothing rehydrates it from disk — while the transcript entry is persisted and replays when a stream is reopened. The Lit no-op comment states exactly this: 'Lit's usage footer restores the same snapshot from the transcript rail's `contextState` log entry (`logSlice`), which — unlike this session-scoped record — also repopulates a stream reopened from disk. Nothing to push here.' Making the webview read the session record instead would require a wire push PLUS a disk-hydration path for a field that currently gets it free — a net add. Making the CLI read the log rail instead would undo the fix that put the number there: .agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-08-15-single-substrate-hosts-as-renderers.md §3 records the CLI gauge as 'a correctness fix wearing a refactor' because it used to re-derive the window from `MODEL_CONFIGS`, and §4's promotion row names `StreamExecutionState` as the target.

Dated-status correction worth recording: that same document's status line still reads 'contextState — OPEN. Not on `StreamExecutionState`; the webview still folds it out of the log rail and `statusBarDisplay.ts:221` still reads `MODEL_CONFIGS[model]?.contextWindow`' (status stamped at `e00b9317f7`, 2026-08-19). Half of that is now STALE: the field IS on `StreamExecutionState` (SessionState.ts:87) and the CLI gauge reads it (statusBarDisplay.ts:239-259; `rg MODEL_CONFIGS packages/cli/src/chat/tui/panes/statusBarDisplay.ts` finds no context-window read). The only live residual is the webview's log-rail fold — and that residual is the correct resting state, not open work.

Dedupe: not in rounds12-index.md; `grep contextState` over both survey docs finds no candidate. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "SessionRendererPort"` returns #11447 (missing outputs) and #10670 (the promotion-list tracker, CLOSED), neither about this.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep both derivations and the `'contextState'` slice, including the Lit arm that deliberately does nothing. The slice is not dead: it is the CLI's repaint signal (sessionSignalsAdapter.ts:95-99 routes it to `invalidateChildStreams()`), and Lit's silence is a positive statement that the transcript rail already owns the webview's copy.

What should change is only the record: the `contextState` row in .agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-08-15-single-substrate-hosts-as-renderers.md §4's promotion-status list should be marked LANDED-for-the-CLI with the webview's log-rail fold declared the intended end state, so the next sweep does not re-open it as an unfinished promotion.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification — one owner for the context gauge — would either add a disk-hydration path to `StreamExecutionState` so the webview can drop its log fold (net add, and it duplicates persistence the transcript already provides), or send the CLI back through a transcript fold and reintroduce the wrong-window bug the promotion fixed. Keeping two readers of one emit costs a slice arm that looks dead in one renderer; that is the cheaper side.

### webview-architecture — The three webview trees as an architecture

#### Two registered sidebar views with `when` clauses (the placement PRD's own preferred design) would not pay for itself; the HTML swap stays

Question asked: the main and progress trees share one VS Code view slot by assigning `webview.html` back and forth, and the two providers hold references to each other to do it (`MainViewProvider._progressViewProvider` + `setProgressViewProvider`, `ProgressViewProvider._mainViewProvider` + `setMainViewProvider`; `switchMode` at `MainViewProvider.ts:413-443` is called from three sites inside ProgressViewProvider at `:391,:417,:458`, and `ProgressViewProvider.resetSidebarReady` is called back from `MainViewProvider.ts:353,434`). `.agents/docs/archived/feature/2026-02-21-progress-view-placement.md:298-346` shows the implemented design is NOT the one the PRD preferred: it lists "`when` clause toggling (preferred)" — two contributed views, each gated on the `texra.activeView` context key — and the key is still set today (`packages/extension/src/common/webview/viewState.ts:24-38`), but `packages/extension/package.json` contributes exactly one view (`texra.mainView`) and uses the key only for six view/title menu `when` clauses (`:657-682`). So the alternative is real, sanctioned, and half-wired. I costed it: deleted would be `switchMode` (~37 lines), the two cross-provider fields and setters (~10), `getMainModeView`'s mode branch (~6), `handleSidebarMessage` + `getContentProvider` + `resetSidebarReady` (~13), and the sidebar arms of `getActiveWebview`/`isViewVisible` (~11); added would be a real `resolveWebviewView` on ProgressViewProvider (~25), a second view declaration plus `when` clauses in package.json and its invariants snapshot (~14 JSON lines), a `view` field on the sidebar arm of `ProgressTarget`, and a rewrite of `src/test-kernel/webview/SidebarSurfaceOwnership.vitest.ts` (171 lines) and parts of `ProgressTargetOwnership.vitest.ts`. Net is roughly -60 production lines. Against that: round 2's REFUTED item "One webview-storage singleton per page" was refuted precisely on this design (.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-26-simplification-survey-round2.md:3387) — main and progress share one `acquireVsCodeApi` state slot sequentially, and the launcher's persisted form state (selected files, instruction text) survives a progress round trip because the progress document snapshots the full state and writes it back untouched. Splitting into two views splits that slot in two, so the migration has to prove the launcher's persisted state still survives a toggle. `mainWebviewReady` does not go away either: `resolveWebviewView` still reloads the document, so the STATE_RESTORE gate is still needed.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep one contributed view and the HTML swap. The recorded rationale for co-location (PRD §Problem: one sidebar, sequential workflow, no split attention) is unchanged, and both designs already destroy the document on every toggle, so the swap costs no state that the alternative preserves. -60 lines is not worth putting the user's persisted launcher form state at risk on the most-used surface in the product. What the swap genuinely does cost is the stale-reference hazard documented in the "one owner for the launcher webview" finding — that is fixable with one `clearActiveView()` call and does not require this restructuring. If this is ever revisited, the gate is: demonstrate that a main→progress→main toggle preserves the launcher's file selections and instruction text under two separate webview state slots.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

Keeping the current shape keeps a genuine mutual reference cycle between two providers and keeps `texra.activeView` as a context key whose meaning ("which content is swapped into the one view") is subtler than the view-visibility meaning the PRD intended. The next round should not re-derive this; it should either accept it or start from the persisted-state question above.

#### Three trees is the right count, and the shared layer is small but real: 388 lines of base classes under 40k lines of view code, with the frontends already merged into one document on desktop

Measured at HEAD: backend LoC per tree is 1,779 (webview) / 1,512 (progressView) / 2,051 (settingsView); frontend LoC is 6,832 / 18,751 / 9,271. The shared extension-host base is 388 lines total across six files in `packages/extension/src/common/webview/`, i.e. 0.85% of the subsystem — whatever the verdict on the base classes, there is no leverage there. The three trees are not three copies of one shape: `packages/desktop/src/renderer/main.ts:22,55,56` imports `@progressView/frontend`, `@settingsView/frontend` and `@webview/frontend` into ONE document, so the three frontends already coexist as three custom elements in a single bundle wherever that is useful; the three-bundle split exists only because VS Code needs three separately-loaded documents (`vite.config.ts` builds one entry per view folder). Merging them for VS Code would make the sidebar parse the settings and progress code it never mounts. Per-base-class verdicts: `BaseViewMessageHandler` (155 lines) carries real shared policy — active-view tracking, the ZodError-vs-UnsupportedCommandError-vs-bug error taxonomy, and the unhandled-command warn — used by all three handlers; keep. `BaseWebviewApp` (185 lines) is the only place the common-message protocol is decoded, used by all three apps plus desktop; keep (its theme and debugMode halves are addressed in separate findings). `BaseWebviewProvider` (45 lines) is a 2-of-3 fit: ProgressViewProvider never assigns `_view` (it owns a richer `ProgressTarget` discriminated union instead) and inherits `_view`/`_viewDisposables`/`cleanupView` purely as decoration; dropping the `extends` there would ADD about six lines of DisposableStore boilerplate, so it stays. The deepest hierarchy in the subsystem, the request panels (`BaseRequestPanel` 48 → `BaseFeedbackPanel` 129 → `BaseApprovalPanel` 132 → `BaseBypassApprovalPanel` 54), has at least two direct consumers at every level (Retry; ExternalInquiry+UserQuestion; PlanApproval+Proposal; Bash+ToolEdit) and real behaviour at each — no pass-through layer. The shared component layer is genuinely shared where it matters: `commonViewStyles` (58 consumers across all three trees), `webAwesomeIcons` (52), `iconNames` (27), `actionButtons` (29), `tabs` (17), `viewHeaderLayoutStyles` (main + progress). Five modules there do have exactly one consuming tree — `settingsSection` (13, settings only), `requestPanelSharedStyles` (12, progress only), `bannerStyles`/`bannerFrame` (main only), `splitButton` (progress only) — but relocating them is a pure move: zero net lines and zero elements.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep three trees, three bundles and the current base classes. The remaining leverage in this subsystem is not structural duplication between the trees — it is single-fact ownership inside them (theme, the active webview reference, debug mode), which is where the other findings point. The one cheap tidy available, `BundledViewContentProvider` (96 lines, three instances) taking BOTH a `viewName` used only to name a log channel and emit one debug line per render AND a `viewFolder` that already identifies the view, could collapse to a function of `(context, viewFolder, webview)` for roughly -35 lines; it is listed here rather than as its own finding because round 2 already stripped this class (ViewBundle, viewPath fallback, readyCommand) and what is left is small.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

Nothing is given up; this closes three questions so round 4 stops re-asking them: (a) merge the trees — no, desktop already merges them where merging helps; (b) delete the base classes — no, they are 0.85% of the subsystem and all three carry real shared policy; (c) the aligned directory structure is convention, not duplication, and enforcing it declaratively (one view-descriptor table feeding the content provider, resource roots, handler and vite config) costs about as many lines as it saves.

### cli-architecture — The CLI as an architecture

#### Does the CLI need its own 1,447-line transcript fold and its own fact→NDJSON projection, or should it read the shared substrate like the webview does?

This is the largest tempting target in the subsystem — `transcriptFold.ts` (923 LoC) + `subscribeStreamLog.ts` (524) = 1,447 lines of CLI-only fold against the webview's `logSlice.ts` (298) doing what looks like the same job. It is not a duplicate, and the count is misleading.

The shared half is ALREADY shared. Both hosts call `projectTranscriptRow` (src/shared/transcript/projectTranscriptRow.ts, 594 LoC), `applyCompactionActivityEntries`/`settleCompactionActivities` (@shared/streams/compactionActivityProjection), `upsertTaskGroupFromStreamLog` (@shared/streams/taskGroupProjection), and the `StreamLogDeltaBuffer` cursor (@transcript). Compare the import blocks: logSlice.ts:14-22 and transcriptFold.ts:19-42 pull the same four owners.

The CLI's extra ~1,100 lines are all consequences of one thing the webview does not have: Ink `<Static>` append-only scrollback. Specifically the settled-prefix promotion frontier (`finalizedFrontier`, advanced at transcriptFold.ts:519,810,866 and mirrored onto the slice at subscribeStreamLog.ts:262-270,432-473), the three-source ordering merge that places CLI-synthetic local rows against log rows and compaction rows (`TranscriptFoldItem.sortSeq/tieBreak/rank`, cliState.ts:66-79), the workflow-operational projection modes whose flip forces a rebuild (cliState.ts:132-134), and transcript residency release (`releaseInactiveStreamTranscript`). The webview re-renders freely and needs none of it. There is no seam to extract because the extractable part is already extracted.

The NDJSON projection is the same answer from the other side. `sessionProgressSubscription.ts` (220 LoC) looks like a fourth fold, but it is deliberately NOT backed by `SessionState`: it projects `SessionFact`/`AgentEvent` straight onto the frozen public wire, with `projectCliActiveChildRow` (:26-40) explicitly re-encoding the internal `identity` struct back into the pre-consolidation `kind`/`toolName`/`childStreamId` shape ("proposal gate G"), and with three facts deliberately unprojected (`run.start`, `context.state`, phase-level `stage.start`). Routing it through `SessionState` would either widen the frozen vocabulary or lose the compatibility re-encoding. `.agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-08-15-single-substrate-hosts-as-renderers.md:130-133` already ruled on exactly this: "B2 NDJSON stdout: frozen public vocabulary; sessionProgressSubscription + cliPresentationHost keep as-is, protected by the import fence."

And the fact rail itself is already single-owner: both SessionState-backed CLI sinks — `TuiSessionRenderer` (sessionSignalsAdapter.ts:52) and `HeadlessPort` (runProgressRenderer.ts:394) — run the same `SessionFactApplier` + `SessionState` reducer, and `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-25-cli-controller-seam-audit.md` §0 verified `SessionRendererPort` has three production implementors and is "the shared reducer, not speculative generality." `childExecutions.ts:1-11` records that the CLI's parallel child-stream machine was already collapsed ("Collapsed from the former 692-line CHILD_STREAMS state machine").

**Why the current shape is right**

Current shape is right. The CLI is ONE fact pipeline (`SessionEventHub`) with four sinks, and the split is principled rather than accidental: two sinks (TUI signals, headless stderr live line) share the `SessionFactApplier`/`SessionState` reducer; one (NDJSON) bypasses it because the public wire is frozen at a pre-consolidation encoding; one (workflow plain stderr text) bypasses it because `workflow.call` and `log` are not in `RUN_FACT_EVENT_TYPES` (src/agent/trace/events.ts:405-419) — and THAT one is the single sink that should move, not onto `SessionState` but onto the StreamLog rows, which is finding 1 of this audit.

What the next round should stop re-asking: (a) "extract a shared transcript fold across CLI and webview" — the shared part is already four shared modules, the rest is `<Static>`-specific; (b) "route the NDJSON projection through SessionState" — ruled 2026-08-15 and gated by the frozen wire; (c) "the CLI has a parallel child-stream state machine" — deleted already, `childExecutions.ts` holds no state of its own.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification — folding the CLI transcript fold into the webview's `logSlice` shape, or deriving NDJSON from `SessionState` — would cost the append-only scrollback invariants (double-printed or dropped finalized history, the exact failure the texra-cli skill's known-origin repaint rules exist to prevent) and would break the frozen NDJSON `kind`/`toolName` encoding for out-of-repo consumers. It would also be a net LoC ADD: the shared surface is smaller than the seam, the trap recorded in .agents/docs/archived/process/2026-08-01-directory-organization.md.

### platform-ports — The platform ports layer

#### The four host-invariant ports (fs, workspace, fileLocks, processes) should stay on Platform even though each has one production implementation

Question asked: `createNodePlatform` (src/platform/defaults/nodeHost.ts:112-135) HARDCODES four of the fifteen Platform members — `fs: nodeFilesystem`, `workspace: createNodeWorkspace(...)`, `fileLocks: nodeFileLocks`, `processes: nodeProcesses`. `NodePlatformServices` (:53-79) has no `fs`, `fileLocks` or `processes` field at all, so no host can supply one. Do they still need to be ports? Production implementation counts: FileSystemProvider = 1 (the VS Code-backed one, packages/extension/src/frontend/vscode/vscodeFileSystem.ts, 159 L, was deleted by #8770 f363f8d892 'use Node providers in extension'); WorkspaceProvider = 1 (vscodeWorkspace.ts died in the same commit); FileLockProvider = 1 family (`createNodeFileLocks`, fileLocks.ts:31); ProcessesPort = 1 (nodeProcesses.ts:105). Production READ counts through `platform()` are also tiny: `platform().fileLocks` has exactly ONE caller (src/agent/index/platformAgentDirectories.ts:160) and `platform().processes` exactly TWO, both in one file (src/agent/storage/leaseOwnerLiveness.ts:37,:82); `workspace.asRelativePath` has one (src/utils/files/workspaceFS.ts:38). By the round-1/2 heuristics these look like ports for one caller.

Answer: keep, because the second implementation is the test fake and the port is the only injection point, and the fakes are load-bearing, not decorative. `FakeFileSystemProvider` (src/test-kernel/support/FakePlatform.ts:260) is reached by `createFakePlatform`/`setupFakePlatform` from 22 suites and 71 `files:` fixture sites — deleting the port would push every one of them onto the real filesystem or onto `vi.mock('node:fs/promises')`, which is strictly worse and would be a large net ADD. `FakeProcesses` (FakePlatform.ts:540-565) scripts per-pid start identities and falls through to the real port for unscripted pids — that is precisely what makes execution-lease liveness testable without spawning processes (src/test-kernel/agent/storage/ExecutionLease.vitest.ts:62), and nodeProcesses itself shells out to `ps`/PowerShell/`/proc`. The fake fileLocks lets ~every suite avoid `proper-lockfile` on disk, and PlatformAgentDirectories.vitest.ts:118,:147 spies on `platform().fileLocks.runExclusive` to test contention. WorkspaceProvider is additionally host-parameterised in a way that is NOT identity: the extension deliberately passes two different path sources — `getWorkspacePath: rawWorkspacePath` to the port and `() => workspace.getWorkspacePath()` (the canonicalised root) to the storage provider (packages/extension/src/extension.ts:268-284) — so the port carries a real host decision.

Two real residues found while answering, worth fixing when the files are next touched but not worth a PR of their own: (a) src/utils/files/baseFS.ts:26-27 still documents 'In VS Code, the provider is replaced with one backed by vscode.workspace.fs at activation via initPlatform()', which has been false since #8770 — a stale comment that would mislead the next auditor into thinking the port has two production implementations; (b) the fake `fileLocks` in FakePlatform.ts:606-625 hand-rolls a 20-line per-path promise chain that is exactly `KeyedMutex.runExclusive` from @utils/core/keyedMutex, the owner the real provider already uses (fileLocks.ts:11).

**Why the current shape is right**

Current shape is right. The tempting simplification — delete `fs`/`workspace`/`fileLocks`/`processes` from the Platform interface and have the three or four production callers import `nodeFilesystem` / `nodeFileLocks` / `nodeProcesses` directly — would save roughly 40 lines of interface and wiring and cost far more than that in test rewrites across 22 suites, while removing the only seam that keeps `src/agent/` from importing `node:fs`, `proper-lockfile` and a `ps` shell-out at module scope. Note also that the codebase already demonstrates the correct escape hatch when the port's uniformity is not wanted: `JsonStore` (src/platform/defaults/jsonStore.ts:20-35) bypasses `platform().fileLocks` and constructs its own `createNodeFileLocks({staleMs: 10_000, retries: 120})` because its flushes are short and frequent, while the port's default is tuned to the 120 s execution-liveness horizon. Port for injection, direct import for tuning — that division is already right and does not need a rule. Record this so round 4 stops re-asking; the only follow-ups are the two residues above.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

Nothing is given up by keeping. What the tempting simplification would have cost: 22 suites moved onto real disk I/O or `vi.mock` of Node builtins, loss of the deterministic pid-identity scripting that makes execution-lease liveness testable at all, and `src/agent/` gaining direct `proper-lockfile` and child-process imports — a clear net add for a ~40-line interface saving.

#### ConfigProvider and StateStore are two ports over three stores, and that is the right shape — the split is semantic, not accidental

Question asked: `Platform` exposes THREE key-value stores through TWO interfaces — `config: ConfigProvider` (get/update/inspect/isExplicitlySet, with a `ConfigTarget`), plus `globalState` and `workspaceState`, both bare `StateStore` (get/update) (src/platform/platform.ts:39-41, interfaces.ts:34-52). All three are backed by the same class in every Node host: `JsonStore` over JSON files resolved by the same `StorageProvider` (src/platform/defaults/nodeStores.ts:71-120). Two schema catalogs describe their contents (src/shared/schemas/coreSettings.ts, 554 L, and stateSettings.ts, 1674 L), and CLAUDE.md tells contributors to pick one when adding a setting. That is a textbook dual-system smell: one fact ('a user preference') owned by two mechanisms.

Answer: keep, on three pieces of evidence. (1) The two ports are semantically different, not two spellings of one thing. `JsonConfigProvider.get` (jsonConfigProvider.ts:41-49) is LAYERED — workspace value shadows global value, then the Zod schema default — and it exposes `inspect()` and `isExplicitlySet()` so callers can tell 'unset' from 'set to the default'. `StateStore` is a flat per-scope map with no layering and no such distinction. Merging them means either giving 30 persisted state rows layered resolution (changing what a user's existing values resolve to) or taking layering away from `.texra/config.json`, which is a documented, hand-editable, cross-host public file. Both are persisted-user-file changes, which is exactly the class of claim an in-repo grep cannot settle. (2) The per-host divergence that would have justified a merge is already essentially closed. Counting `sameSlot(...)` in stateSettings.ts: 32 rows, of which 16 `workspaceState`, 14 `globalState`, 2 `config` — and only ONE row in the whole catalog still differs by host (GIT_AUTHOR_SLOTS, :846-856). So the catalog row, not the port, is already the single owner of 'which store'. (3) A dated ruling already looked at this and deliberately stopped where it is: .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-15-shared-contracts-and-retirement.md §2.1, executed as #10925, collapsed SIX settings catalogs into one `SettingEntry{slots, honoredBy, surfaces, onWrite}` row shape and kept `slots` as a per-row declaration rather than forcing every row into one store; the doc's own risk note (:705) warns that a partially-absorbed catalog becomes 'a seventh catalog — the exact hack'. Merging the two ports now would re-open that.

**Why the current shape is right**

Current shape is right: two ports because there are two resolution semantics, one catalog row shape because there is one settings vocabulary. The remaining work in this area is not a port merge — it is finishing the per-row slot rulings the 2026-08-15 doc started (see the WORKTREE_SHARED_KEYS finding above), which shrinks the divergence without touching the port surface. If a future round wants to revisit, the bar it must clear is stated: show how a merged port preserves (a) workspace-shadows-global resolution for the 2 `config` rows and the whole `.texra/config.json` file, and (b) the 'explicitly set vs defaulted' distinction that `isExplicitlySet` gives to callers, without migrating persisted values in ~30 state rows. Recorded here so round 4 does not re-derive it from the surface similarity of the two `get(key, default)` signatures.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

Nothing. What the tempting simplification would have cost: a migration across three persisted stores and two hand-editable public config files, a resolution-semantics change for roughly 30 catalog rows, and re-opening a maintainer ruling that was executed three weeks ago — for a saving of one four-method interface.

### follow-up-inquiry — Follow-up, queueing and external inquiry

#### Follow-up and inquiry are not one mechanism wearing two names, and the lease manager is not a redundant layer over the flow's own queue

Question 1 — how many queues exist, and who owns each? The census is exactly one queue data structure per stream, plus one deliberate host buffer. `FollowUpQueue` (`src/agent/followUp/FollowUpQueue.ts`, 164 lines) is the only queue type; `ToolUseFollowUpQueue` holds one instance per `StreamTabId` in `entries` (`ToolUseFollowUpQueueManager.ts:77`); `ToolUseSessionLifecycle` does NOT create a second one — its constructor either claims the lease and takes `queue.queue(lease)` or borrows the outer child loop's via `externallyOwnedQueue` (`ToolUseSessionLifecycle.ts:22-36`), and throws otherwise. Every producer funnels through `ToolUseFollowUpQueue.submit`: hosts and inquiry via `submitFollowUp`, child results via `deliverChildRunFollowUp`, resume via `restore`/`drainItems`. The one documented exception is the CLI's interrupted-follow-up shadow buffer (`packages/cli/src/chat/chatSessionController.ts:779`, `.restore(recovery?.followUps ?? [])`), which the brief already flags as deliberate — the dying flow owns the durable queue lease, so the host holds the draft until it can hand it back. The only genuine second write path is the `appendFollowUp` bypass, which is my separate collapse finding above.

Question 2 — should the lease manager collapse into the flow? No. The two objects have different lifetimes by construction, and that is the whole point. A `FollowUpQueue` outlives its consumer: `release(lease, 'recoverable')` (`ToolUseFollowUpQueueManager.ts:220-233`) clears the owner but KEEPS the queued items, so a stream parked at WAITING retains user input across the flow's teardown until `claimRecovery` (`:122-134`) hands it to the resumed run. Moving the queue onto `ToolUseSessionLifecycle` would destroy queued input at every WAITING boundary — the exact class of bug `.agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-08-23-single-owner-sessions.md` was written to fix (its trigger, per the campaign record, was "long prompt lost on No active session"). The lease is what makes single-ownership checkable: `claim` refuses a competing owner (`:304-313`) and `entryForLease` (`:326-329`) makes a stale lease from a superseded flow a no-op rather than letting it terminalize its successor's queue. That machinery was rebuilt three days ago across #11303/#11304/#11311/#11316/#11318/#11329 (2026-08-23), and #11318 already deleted the layers that WERE redundant — the follow-up mutex, ALS lease scoping, the quiescence coordinator, `existing_recoverable`, and the tombstone LRU. What survives is the residue after that cut, not accumulation before it.

Question 3 — are follow-up and inquiry one system? No; they are layered, and the seam is one function call. Inquiry owns durable external-thread state (manifests under `ei_threads/`, a `KeyedMutex` per thread, status transitions open→answered/dropped) and owns nothing about continuation. Its ENTIRE coupling to follow-up is `submitFollowUp(parentStreamId, text, { session })` at `src/tools/inquiry/inquiryContinuation.ts:157`, one call site. There is no inquiry queue, no inquiry wake path, no inquiry resume logic — `deliverContinuation` maps the returned `SubmitFollowUpResult` to a three-value UI badge and stops. Merging them would push manifest knowledge into `src/agent/followUp/`, which is host-agnostic runtime, and would give the follow-up layer a second reason to change. The correct reading is the opposite of "two names for one thing": inquiry is one of four peer producers on the follow-up door, alongside hosts, child-run delivery, and goal continuations.

Question 4 — are `followUpSent` and `updateQueuedFollowUps` one fact? They have identical payloads (`{ streamId }`, `src/agent/runtime/SessionEventHub.ts:34-40`) and identical renderer handling (`src/controllers/session/SessionFactApplier.ts:335-340` invalidates `queuedFollowUps` for both), which looks like a duplicate. It is not. Each has exactly one differentiating consumer: `followUpSent` aborts a blocking `executions wait` (`src/tools/executions/waitCoordination.ts:68`) — `updateQueuedFollowUps` must NOT, or a resume-time drain notification (`resumeRun.ts:346-352`) would spuriously break the wait; and `updateQueuedFollowUps` is the one of the pair projected onto the FROZEN CLI NDJSON vocabulary (`packages/cli/src/runtime/sessionProgressSubscription.ts:72-75`, where `followUpSent` deliberately returns `undefined` with the comment "intentionally session-local"). Merging them into one fact plus a discriminator field is net-neutral in LoC and would either widen the public NDJSON payload or require filtering it back out.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep all four shapes as they are. Concretely, the next round should not re-open: (a) moving `FollowUpQueue` ownership onto `ToolUseSessionLifecycle` or deleting the lease/`claim`/`release` triad — it is what preserves queued input across a WAITING boundary, rebuilt 2026-08-23; (b) merging `src/tools/inquiry/` into `src/agent/followUp/` or extracting a shared "pending message" abstraction over both — the coupling is a single call at `inquiryContinuation.ts:157`, far smaller than any seam that would replace it; (c) collapsing `followUpSent` into `updateQueuedFollowUps` — each has one distinguishing consumer and one is on the frozen wire; (d) the CLI's interrupted-follow-up shadow buffer, which is deliberate. The smallest concept set that expresses this subsystem today is: one queue per stream, one lease per queue, one admission function, one durable thread store that calls it — and the code is already at that count, except for the second admission path I flagged separately.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

What the tempting simplification would have cost. Folding the queue into the flow object looks like deleting a 330-line manager, but it would silently reintroduce input loss at every WAITING/resume boundary and re-open the class of defect that produced a ten-PR campaign three days ago; the honest LoC "win" would be immediately spent re-adding a durable cursor under another name. Fusing inquiry and follow-up would move persisted-manifest concerns into host-agnostic runtime and net-add a seam wider than the one call it removes. Collapsing the two session facts would either widen a frozen NDJSON payload or add filtering that costs more than the duplicated `{ streamId }` shape.

### repo-shape — Repo layering and folder organization

#### src/hosts/ and src/platform/ are two homes for host capability ports, and that is one deliberate axis rather than one system that was split

THE QUESTION. `src/hosts/` is a single 90-line file holding four host-capability interfaces (`DiffViewHost`, `ExternalOpener`, `PromptHost`, `TerminalRunner`) and gets its own top-level alias `@hosts/*`, its own row in `architecture-edges-baseline.json` (`controllers -> hosts`, type-only), and a place in the CLAUDE.md VS Code-free zone list. `src/platform/` is 22 files / 2,579 LoC defining `Platform`, a frozen singleton of 14 host-capability ports. A one-file top-level subsystem describing host ports, next to a subsystem whose stated job is host ports, is the shape that usually means 'one system that was split'.

THE ANSWER: they are separated by DELIVERY MECHANISM, and that separation is load-bearing.

(1) AMBIENT vs INJECTED. `Platform` is reached through the global `platform()` accessor — 222 call sites across 106 production files. `@hosts/uiHosts` types are never global; every one of the 13 production import sites receives the port as a constructor or function parameter: `src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsProfileKeyController.ts:4`, `src/controllers/settingsView/SettingsMemoryController.ts:1`, `src/tools/setup/platform.ts:15`, and 10 host-side implementers (`packages/extension/src/frontend/hosts/VscodePromptHost.ts:11`, `.../VscodeExternalOpener.ts:5`, `.../approval/VscodeDiffViewHost.ts:9`, `.../approval/VscodeToolEditApprovalHost.ts:25`, `packages/extension/src/frontend/setupTerminalRunner.ts:20`, `packages/extension/src/progressView/ProgressViewMessageHandler.ts:38`, `packages/desktop/src/main/desktopDiffHost.ts:11`, `desktopPreviewHost.ts:6`, `desktopAgentExecutionHost.ts:3`, `desktopCredentialSettingsController.ts:14`).

(2) THE SPLIT IS EXACTLY THE UI/INFRA LINE AGENTS.md DRAWS. AGENTS.md 'Platform decoupling rules' rule 3 is 'Push UI side-effects to the caller. Business logic functions should return error information ... instead of calling `vscode.window.show*Message()` directly.' Rule 4 is 'Use `Platform` ports for platform capabilities.' The four `uiHosts` interfaces are all user-facing dialogs and panes — a modal prompt, a diff pane, an external browser, a terminal. Folding them into `Platform` would make UI reachable from anywhere via an ambient global, which is rule 3 inverted.

(3) THE CLI PROVIDES NONE OF THEM. `grep -rl '@hosts/' packages/cli/src` returns ZERO files. `Platform`'s 14 ports are all `readonly` and non-optional except `toolMissingHandler`, so every host composition root must supply them. Moving `DiffViewHost` onto `Platform` would force the CLI to invent a diff-pane implementation it has no surface for, or make four more ports optional — which pushes an `if (port == null)` degraded branch into every caller, the exact shape round 1 shipped a deletion for ('Make ToolCallContext.workPlanState required and delete the plan/todo no-session degraded branches').

(4) TEST COST RUNS THE OTHER WAY TOO. `src/test-kernel/support/FakeHosts.ts` is 126 lines implementing the four interfaces. Under a merged `Platform`, a controller test that needs only a `PromptHost` would have to stand up all 18 ports.

THE COLLAPSE IN THE OTHER DIRECTION IS WORSE. Making `Platform`'s 14 infra ports explicitly injected means threading them through 222 call sites in 106 files. `.agents/docs/archived/process/2026-08-01-directory-organization.md` §7 'Left alone deliberately' and the settled-surface note on the four hosts + platform-ports composition root both point the same way.

ONE REAL LEAK, REPORTED NOT PROPOSED. `Platform.toolMissingHandler` (`src/platform/platform.ts:58`, type at `src/platform/interfaces.ts:276`) is a UI notification port sitting on the ambient side of the line. It has one caller (`src/utils/system/toolUtils.ts:51`, `await platform().toolMissingHandler?.(message, openDocsCommand)`) and one implementer (`packages/extension/src/extension.ts:294`), and its own docstring concedes the shape: 'Single-implementer (VS Code) — hosts without a UI for this omit it; callers treat an absent port as a no-op.' By the axis above it belongs in `uiHosts` and should be passed to `checkToolInstalled`. I am NOT proposing the move: it is a single call site, the port is genuinely optional today, and relocating it is +import churn for 0 net elements — checklist §14 R5/R6 relocation, not removal. It is worth one sentence in AGENTS.md 'Platform decoupling rules' so the axis reads as a rule with one grandfathered exception rather than as a rule with a silent counterexample.

DEDUPE/RULINGS. `gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search 'uiHosts'` and `--search 'DiffViewHost'` return nothing proposing a merge. Round 1 REFUTED 'Delete the FakeDiffViewHost test double and the suite that only tests it' — adjacent, already settled. `grep -rn 'uiHosts' docs/proposals docs/architecture` returns no ruling either way, which is why this keep is worth recording.

**Why the current shape is right**

KEEP the current shape. The axis is delivery, not domain: `src/platform/` owns ambient infrastructure the process always has (config, state, fs, storage, secrets, locks, processes, lifecycle), reached through the frozen `platform()` singleton and supplied identically by all four composition roots; `src/hosts/uiHosts.ts` owns user-facing capabilities that only some hosts have, passed explicitly to the one function that needs them so a host without a UI simply never provides one.

The one thing worth doing is a sentence, not a move. Add to AGENTS.md 'Platform decoupling rules' after rule 4: 'A host capability that draws UI (a prompt, a diff pane, an external open, a terminal) is a `@hosts/uiHosts` interface passed to its caller, not a `Platform` port — `Platform` is for infrastructure every host has. `toolMissingHandler` predates the rule and is the one exception.' That is the cheapest way to stop a future contributor adding port 15 to the frozen singleton because the ambient accessor was easier to reach.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

The tempting simplification — fold `src/hosts/uiHosts.ts` into `src/platform/interfaces.ts`, delete a top-level directory, an alias, and a baseline row — would have cost: four optional ports on the frozen `Platform` (or four fake implementations forced on the CLI composition root), an `if (port == null)` degraded branch at each of the 13 explicit call sites, ambient reachability for modal dialogs from all 222 `platform()` sites, and a `FakeHosts` replacement that must satisfy 18 ports instead of 4. Net LoC would be positive and net elements roughly flat: a directory disappears and a degraded-path concept appears in its place.

#### The two unexecuted directory moves in the 2026-08-01 organization proposal are now permanently closed by that document's own gating rule — the repo is public

THE RECURRING QUESTION. Two moves from `.agents/docs/archived/process/2026-08-01-directory-organization.md` were ruled desirable but never executed, and both are exactly the kind of thing an architectural round re-proposes: §5.C `git mv src/test-kernel test-kernel` (the `src/` prefix strip) and §5.B renaming `@common/state` / `@common/webview` to `@extCommon/*`. §5.B is the sharper irritant — `import { x } from '@common/webview'` and `import { y } from '@common/errors'` in the same file resolve into DIFFERENT packages, one VS Code-coupled (`packages/extension/src/common/`) and one in an enforced VS Code-free zone (`src/common/`), which the proposal itself calls 'the one item in the survey that genuinely misleads a stranger'. CLAUDE.md pays for the collision with a defensive line ('`src/common/webview/` does not exist'), and `subsystemEdgeRatchet.vitest.ts:71-79` pays again by inventing two pseudo-subsystems (`common-state-extension`, `common-webview-extension`) so a `src/`-side import cannot hide inside the whitelisted `common` edge.

THE DEFECT §5.C TARGETS IS REAL AND UNCHANGED. Measured at HEAD: `git ls-files 'src/test-kernel/**'` = 896 of 1,838 tracked files under `src/`; 243,193 of 418,825 TS/TSX LoC = 58.1%. The proposal §1 measured 875 files / 57.4% on 2026-08-01, so the tree grew slightly in the same direction. `ls src/` still shows a non-source directory as the largest thing in the production tree, and every casual `wc -l src/**` still reports roughly double the real production size.

WHY BOTH ARE NEVERTHELESS CLOSED. §6 states the gating condition explicitly: 'The distinguishing property: **moves that break `git blame` and invalidate open branches are cheapest while the repo is private and has no forks.** Anything on the pre-flip list should be paid for now or deferred indefinitely.' It then places both items, and only these two, in a table titled 'Wave 3 remainder — the blame-breaking moves not yet attempted. **Pre-flip or never.**' `gh repo view --json isPrivate,visibility` at HEAD returns `{"isPrivate":false,"visibility":"PUBLIC"}`. The flip has happened. By the document's own rule, both items are now 'never'.

AND THE BILL WAS ALREADY ITEMIZED, SO NOBODY NEEDS TO RE-DERIVE IT. §6 prices §5.C at: 896 renames + 9 config files (`vitest.config.mjs` ×4 lines, `tsconfig.json:58` alias and `:77` exclude, `tsconfig.build.json:45` — where it warns that deleting the exclude speculatively makes `@texra-ai/agent`'s declaration build emit for all 896 test files — `tsconfig.test-kernel.json`, `knip.json:7`, `eslint.config.mjs:50` and `:540`, and a regenerated `packages/desktop/tsconfig.paths.json:50`) + 3 ratchet baseline rewrites (`host-agent-mock-baseline.json` 40 entries, `knip-baseline.json` 2, `shared-schemas-deep-import-baseline.json` 1) + 22 files that resolve paths at runtime with `../../..`, including the shared `src/test-kernel/support/repoScan.ts:10-13` `REPO_ROOT` used by 5 architecture ratchets, which under a root-level `test-kernel/support/` would resolve ABOVE the repo root. It documents two silent-green traps: `hostAgentMockRatchet.vitest.ts:48-51` hardcodes `src/test-kernel/cli` and `src/test-kernel/desktop` and asserts only current-count <= baseline, so after the move it finds zero files and stays green while guarding nothing; and `agentRuntimeProgressEventsBoundary.vitest.ts:36-62` deliberately scans test-kernel for its NDJSON-vocabulary allowlist and would lose coverage the same way. Plus 135 doc mentions across 48 `.md` files and 6 production source files carrying `src/test-kernel/...` doc comments no gate sees. §5.B is priced at 52 specifiers + 4 tsconfigs + 4 eslint sites + 2 packaging scripts + docstrings, with the warning that the `SUBSYSTEM_ALIASES` carve-out must be renamed and not deleted or the guard silently disappears.

BOTH ARE PURE RELOCATION. Neither deletes an element. §5.C moves 896 files and edits 12 config/baseline files for zero behaviour change; §5.B rewrites 52 import specifiers for zero behaviour change. Checklist §13/§14 R5-R6 is unambiguous on this shape, and the 22-prior-reduction-PRs-net-+5,046-LoC number is the reason the rule exists. The honest net is 0 LoC at best and positive once the doc rot and two ratchet rewrites are counted, against a real cost in `git blame` continuity on 896 files at the exact moment strangers start reading history — which is precisely the cost §6 said the public flip makes unaffordable.

DEDUPE. Neither move appears in rounds12-index.md (151 titles). `grep -n 'test-kernel|extCommon|@common/state' .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-26-simplification-survey-round2.md` surfaces only incidental ratchet-scope boilerplate. `gh issue list --state all --search 'test-kernel move'` and `--search 'extCommon'` return nothing open.

DRIFT AUDIT — the rest of the proposal's rules DO hold at HEAD, which is why this is the only outstanding item. Verified: CLAUDE.md:82-97 lists all 17 VS Code-free zones and matches `eslint.config.mjs:60-78` and `dependencyDirection.vitest.ts:28-46` entry for entry, including `src/logger` and `packages/desktop/src` (round 2's zone-list PR closed that gap). No file under `src/shared` imports a `node:` builtin. `@utils/core/index.ts` re-exports neither `idHash.ts` nor `pathCore.ts`, the only two `node:`-importing modules in the browser-safe closure. `packages/desktop/src/renderer` and `packages/trace-viewer/src` import zero `@common/*` or `@tools/*` values and reach only 3 of the 6 browser-safe `@utils` modules. `packages/trace-viewer` IS in the root typecheck (`package.json:31`, `typecheck:trace-viewer`), retiring a stale note in my own memory. Every subdirectory of `src/controllers/` has consumers in at least two hosts (mainView 7 ext / 6 desktop, progressView 4/3, settingsView 6/5, approval 2/2, session 1/2/7 cli, modelAccess 5/3/10 cli, onboarding 2/2/1), so the hypothesised 'host-specific module hiding in the host-neutral controllers tree' does not exist. `src/types/` holds one tracked `ambient.d.ts` and the `@types/*` alias is gone per §5.G.

**Why the current shape is right**

KEEP the current layout, and record in `.agents/docs/archived/process/2026-08-01-directory-organization.md` that Wave 3 is closed. Concretely: change the §6 'Wave 3 remainder — Pre-flip or never' heading to 'Wave 3 remainder — CLOSED, the flip happened' and add one line under it: 'The repository went public before either item was attempted. Under this document's own gating rule these are now `never`; both are pure relocation with 0 net elements, and the blame cost they were priced against is now unavoidable. Do not re-open them.' Move both rows into §'Deliberately never' or §7 'Left alone deliberately'.

That one edit is the whole deliverable, and it is worth making: this is the only outstanding item in a proposal whose remaining 15 items all landed, so every future architectural round will find it, re-measure the 58%, and re-derive the same 896-file bill. Writing the closure down where the next surveyor reads it is cheaper than the fourth re-derivation.

Second, smaller: the two silent-green traps §6 documents (`hostAgentMockRatchet.vitest.ts:48-51` hardcoded `HOST_DIRS`, `agentRuntimeProgressEventsBoundary.vitest.ts:36-62` deliberate test-kernel scan) were discovered only because someone priced the move. They are latent regardless — any future restructure of `src/test-kernel/{cli,desktop}` leaves those suites green while guarding nothing, because both assert current <= baseline rather than exact coverage. Add an `expectRealCoverage`-style file-count floor to `hostAgentMockRatchet`'s `HOST_DIRS` resolution (`src/test-kernel/support/repoScan.ts:74` already exports the helper, and `dependencyDirection.vitest.ts:157-162` already uses the pattern). That is +4 LoC and closes a real silent-degradation hole, which CLAUDE.md's 'Design guardrails' names as a defect class in its own right.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

Two readability improvements stay unclaimed. `ls src/` keeps showing `test-kernel/` as its largest entry and naive `src/` LoC counts keep reporting ~2x the real production size — a genuine first-impression cost on a now-public repo. And `@common/state` keeps reading as core code while resolving into the VS Code-coupled extension package, a collision the repo pays for in a defensive CLAUDE.md line and two pseudo-subsystems in `subsystemEdgeRatchet.vitest.ts:71-79`. Both are permanent under this ruling. If the maintainer ever decides the `@extCommon` rename is worth breaking blame on 52 specifiers — a far smaller blast radius than the 896-file move, and the only one of the two whose defect is a correctness-adjacent misreading rather than cosmetics — the §5.B recipe is complete and executable as written, with the `SUBSYSTEM_ALIASES` rename-don't-delete warning as its one landmine.

### guard-redundancy — The guard layer: ratchets, tests, lint rules, scripts

#### ESLint and the vitest ratchets must both enforce the vscode and host-layer fences — each has caught the other going silently inert

The question this closes: `eslint.config.mjs` and `dependencyDirection.vitest.ts` enforce the same three fences with hand-copied lists — the 17-entry `VSCODE_FREE_ZONE_DIRS` (eslint.config.mjs:59-76) vs `VSCODE_FREE_ZONES` (dependencyDirection.vitest.ts:28-48), and the 10-entry `HOST_LAYER_RESTRICTED_IMPORT_PATTERNS` group (eslint.config.mjs:84-103) vs `HOST_LAYER_IMPORT_SPECIFIERS`/`HOST_LAYER_IMPORT_PREFIXES` (dependencyDirection.vitest.ts:72-88). Should one side go, or should the lists be extracted to a shared owner?

Answer: keep both mechanisms, and keep the lists where they are.

The duplication is documented in-file (dependencyDirection.vitest.ts:17-26) as deliberate — 'a stray `// eslint-disable` line can silently neuter the lint rule, but it cannot bypass a test in the `npm test` gate'. More importantly, the repo has TWO dated incidents where the ESLint half went silently inert and the vitest half was the only thing still catching violations:

- Issue #7722 (CLOSED, `ESLint host-import fence patterns don't match nested alias imports`, 2026-07-09): the `no-restricted-imports` group used single-star globs (`'@webview/*'`), which under gitignore-style matching never cross a `/`. Since essentially every real specifier is multi-segment (`@webview/frontend/MainApp`), the rule was 'effectively inert for the realistic case'. The issue states explicitly: 'The mirrored Vitest check (`dependencyDirection.vitest.ts`, `specifier.startsWith(prefix)`) does not have this gap and still catches violations in CI.'
- Issue #7730 (CLOSED, `Agent-core ESLint override silently drops the host-layer import fence`, 2026-07-09): a later flat-config block scoped to `src/agent/core/**` re-specified `no-restricted-imports`, and per ESLint's cascading rules the later options REPLACE rather than merge — silently dropping the host-layer patterns for the most execution-critical VS Code-free zone. The issue again records that CI stayed green-for-the-right-reason only because 'dependencyDirection.vitest.ts independently re-checks the same host-layer prefixes across all of src/'.

That is two proven inertness events in one week for one half of the pair, both caught by the other half. This defeats any 'one of these is redundant' argument on evidence, not on principle.

On extracting the lists to a single owner: the honest accounting says no. The shared fact is 17 short strings plus 10 patterns. `eslint.config.mjs` is a `.mjs` config that imports `typescript-eslint`, `unicorn`, and `@stylistic`; importing it from a vitest suite drags that whole graph into the test run, so a shared owner means a NEW third module (`config/architecture/zones.mjs` or similar) plus two import sites — +1 element and roughly net-zero LoC to remove ~17 duplicated lines.
