CLAUDE.md is explicit that the open work here is the Tier-1 public manifest and shrinking the frozen lists, 'NOT adding another lint rule' — and by the same logic, not adding another config module either. The current mitigation (a 'Keep in sync with `VSCODE_FREE_ZONE_DIRS` in eslint.config.mjs' comment on both sides, plus the same list restated in CLAUDE.md) is in sync at HEAD: all three copies carry `src/logger` and `packages/desktop/src`, added by `ea6b20ce18` (2026-08-21, #11279, `fix(arch): close VS Code-free-zone gap for src/common and src/utils`).

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep the current shape unchanged: ESLint keeps its four custom rules and four `no-restricted-imports` blocks; `dependencyDirection.vitest.ts` keeps its `VS Code-free zones never import vscode`, `Production core never imports host layers`, and `Agent core dependency direction` describes with their own copies of the lists and their 'Keep in sync' comments. Do NOT extract the zone lists into a shared module, and do NOT delete either half of the pair.

One optional, cheap hardening that does not violate the above: `HOST_LAYER_RESTRICTED_IMPORT_PATHS` (eslint.config.mjs:78-84) lists only `@common/webview`, not `@common/state`, and the pattern `@common/state/**` does not match the bare specifier `@common/state`. The vitest half's `HOST_LAYER_IMPORT_SPECIFIERS` does list it, and `subsystemEdgeRatchet` catches it too (it maps the alias to a `common-state-extension` pseudo-subsystem with no baseline edge, and pins that behaviour in its third `it`), so there is no coverage hole — but adding `'@common/state'` to the ESLint `paths` array restores the IDE-time signal for that one specifier at a cost of one line.

Record this ruling in `.agents/docs/implemented/architecture/2026-08-01-architecture-rulings-ledger.md` so round 4 does not re-ask it.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

By keeping both, we keep ~35 lines of duplicated enforcement and two hand-synced list copies. The tempting simplification — 'delete the vitest half, ESLint already covers it' — would have re-opened exactly the gap #7722 and #7730 document, in the zone CLAUDE.md calls the highest-signal rule in the repo. The other tempting simplification — extract the lists to a shared module — trades 17 duplicated lines for a new module and two new import edges, i.e. a net element add for no net LoC win.

#### The standalone check-*.mjs guards stay outside the vitest architecture suite: their CI job is deliberately ungated

The question this closes: `scripts/check-browser-safe-utils.mjs` (651 lines) and `scripts/check-guidance-refs.mjs` (403 lines) do whole-tree TypeScript-AST import scanning — the same activity as `src/test-kernel/architecture/*.vitest.ts`, using the same `typescript` devDependency, over the same corpus. Should they fold into the vitest architecture suite so there is one guard runner instead of three (vitest ratchets, `check-*.mjs`, ESLint)?

Answer: no. The split is load-bearing on CI gating, and the reason is recorded in the workflow itself.

`.github/workflows/ci.yml:225-236` defines the `guidance-refs` job with an explicit comment: 'Deliberately ungated: this is the one check that must run on BOTH sides of the docs/code split. A guidance-only PR (code=false) skips `checks`, and that is exactly the PR most likely to introduce a stale path; a code-only PR can break the prose by renaming what it cites.' The `test` job (ci.yml:261-268) and the `checks` job (which runs lint and the dead-code ratchet) are both gated on `needs.changes.outputs.code == 'true'`. So a docs-only PR — the highest-risk PR for both of these guards, since both verify PROSE against the tree (the enumerated six-module reachable set and the module count in CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md; every backticked repo path in the guidance corpus) — would run neither if they were vitest suites. Folding them in would silently disarm them for the exact change class they exist for.

Their subject matter is also outside what a vitest architecture ratchet can express: `check-guidance-refs.mjs` validates Markdown link destinations and backticked paths across `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, seven module READMEs, `.claude/skills`, `docs/dev`, `docs/architecture` and `docs/guide`, with an archival carve-out for `docs/dev/audits` and a `<!-- guidance-refs-ignore -->` block marker. `check-browser-safe-utils.mjs` cross-checks a computed import closure against enumerated prose in two guidance files. Neither has a code-side assertion a `.vitest.ts` would own.

The third runner, `scripts/check-dead-code-ratchet.mjs` against `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json`, is separately settled and must not be merged either: `.agents/docs/archived/process/2026-08-19-dead-code-gate-blind-spots.md` ('The governing constraint: one owner of "dead"') rules that this script is THE single authority over deadness, that knip is 'an input to it, never a peer', and explicitly forbids the shape a merge would create — 'the fix must not be "add another check". A second knip pass ratcheted against a second baseline would answer the same question in a second place… That is the shape this repo has repeatedly paid to remove.' Executed and closed as issue #11170; `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` remains the only dead-code baseline (a stated acceptance criterion).

Also settled by round 1 and not to be re-opened: 'Delete the TypeScript-grammar self-test cases from check-browser-safe-utils.mjs' is on the round-1 REFUTED list.

On merging the six baselines in `config/ratchets/` into one file or one mechanism: also no, and cheaply shown. Their key shapes are mutually incompatible — directed edges with a value/type-only kind (`architecture-edges-baseline.json`), per-package specifier sets (`host-agent-import-baseline.json`), `(file, form, specifier)` mock-site tuples (`host-agent-mock-baseline.json`), per-class import statements with a published-surface map (`shared-schemas-deep-import-baseline.json`), per-class public method lists plus aggregate field contracts (`store-public-surface-baseline.json`), and knip `(file, category, kind, name)` findings (`knip-baseline.json`) — and each has a different shrink policy stated in its own `semantics` string. A merged file would need a discriminated union plus one collector per kind, i.e. every line of today's collectors plus a dispatcher. That is a net add with no invariant gained.

**Why the current shape is right**

Keep the current shape: three guard runners with three different CI gating rules. Specifically — leave `scripts/check-guidance-refs.mjs` and `scripts/check-browser-safe-utils.mjs` as dependency-light Node scripts in the ungated `guidance-refs` job (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:225-260`); leave `scripts/check-dead-code-ratchet.mjs` as the single authority over deadness in the gated `checks` job, with `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` as the only dead-code baseline; leave the six `config/ratchets/*.json` baselines as six files with six collectors.

What a future round may still legitimately propose in this area: shrinking any individual baseline (the standing invariant is that baselines shrink and never widen), and reducing the six browser-reachable `@utils/*` modules. What it must not propose without first defeating the cited documents: folding the prose guards into vitest, adding a second dead-code baseline or a second knip gate, or merging the ratchet baselines into one file.

Record this ruling alongside the previous one in `.agents/docs/implemented/architecture/2026-08-01-architecture-rulings-ledger.md`.

**What the tempting simplification would have cost**

By keeping three runners we keep three ways to be told a rule was violated, and a contributor must know that `npm run lint`, `npm test`, and `npm run check:guidance-refs`/`check:browser-safe-utils`/`check:dead-code-ratchet` are all gates. The tempting simplification — 'one guard runner' — would have disarmed both prose guards on docs-only PRs (where `changes.outputs.code == 'false'` skips both `checks` and `test`) and would have re-created the multi-authority dead-code shape that #11170 was opened to remove.

## 4. Refuted — do not re-file

21 findings were killed on independent verification.

### run-lifecycle

- **Retire the stream reservation: a launch lock for a stream-id collision the `name#executionId` format made impossible**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on three independent grounds.

  **(1) A dated ruling already decided it, in a section that names it verbatim.** `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-16-define-out-of-existence.md` §4 is headed "The irreducibility register (survived attack — do not re-flag)" and its first paragraph reads: "Race guards with verified genuine boundaries: ... catalogue/key invalidation (`coalesceAsync` idiom), **stream reservations (`tryAcquire` fused check-and-claim)**, interaction attachment machinery ...". That doc is an adjudicated audit pinned to origin/main `9d7cfa1899`, executing two maintainer rulings, with 18 agents run over exactly this class of race guard. This finding re-flags an item the register explicitly forbids re-flagging. The finding's one piece of genuinely newer evidence — #11318 (30dc3e9f10, 2026-08-23), which postdates the ruling — cuts the other way: that commit's message enumerates by name every guard it retired (`captureOwnedExecutionLease`, `ExecutionLeaseCoordination`, `onOwnedExecutionLeaseLost`, `waitForOwnedExecutionLeaseRelease`, `canAcquireResumeLease`, …) and did not touch `tryAcquire`, `StreamStatusService.ts`, or `AgentLaunchContext.ts`'s reservation. The serial lane was authored with the reservation in view and kept it.

  **(2) WRONG TRACE — the claimed equivalence is false, and a named test pins the difference.** The load-bearing sentence is: "`canTransitionStreamPhase(from, RUNNING, LIFECYCLE)` is true only for `from === undefined` ..., so a plain LIFECYCLE claim on a stream that is RUNNING/WAITING/terminal is refused exactly where `tryAcquire` refuses today." The first half is right; the conclusion does not follow. `canAcquireStreamReservation(phase)` is `!isInFlightPhase(phase)` (streamStatus.ts:111-115) — it **admits** COMPLETED/CANCELLED/FAILED. `canTransitionStreamPhase` **refuses** them: `if (isTerminalOutcomePhase(from)) return cause === RESUME && to === RUNNING` (:129-133). So the two acceptance sets differ precisely on the stale-terminal case, and that case is deliberate, documented, and covered: `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/StreamStatusService.vitest.ts` has a test literally named **"overlays reservations on stale terminal phases and restores them on rollback"** (seeds COMPLETED, asserts `tryAcquire` → true, then asserts `releaseIfReserved` restores COMPLETED, not CANCELLED). Proposal step (1) would turn that launch into an `AgentError`; proposal step (2) (`transition(streamId, CANCELLED, LIFECYCLE)`) would be refused from a terminal `from` anyway, and in the non-terminal case would rewrite a finished run's displayed outcome. Corroborating evidence that the sets differ: the COMPLETED/CANCELLED/FAILED arms of `STATUS_MESSAGES` (AgentLaunchContext.ts:122-130) are **currently unreachable** — `tryAcquire` never refuses on a terminal phase — and the change would make them live, i.e. it manufactures new user-facing launch refusals rather than preserving behaviour.

  **(3) The reachability argument is wrong: reservation is not "fresh launches only".** Two counterexamples. (a) `runAgent` takes `request.executionId ?? generateExecutionId()` and `RunAgentRequest.kind === 'fresh'` carries an optional caller-supplied `executionId` (runAgent.ts:64-68, :100) — a fresh launch's id is not necessarily freshly minted. (b) More importantly, the resume path can reach `tryAcquire`: `streamTabIdOverride: resumedStreamId` comes from `clearTerminalExecutionState(...).streamId`, which is `meta?.streamId` (executionLifecycle.ts:200-203) over `ExecutionMetaCoreSchema.streamId: StreamTabIdSchema.optional()` — whose own comment says "A row without one has no persisted stream" (stream.ts:98-103). A row without one yields `undefined`, so `reservedStreamId = getStreamTabId(agent, {executionId})` for an **already-existing** execution — the same id `repairRestartedStreams` may have already settled to a terminal phase via `transitionToTerminal(..., RESTART_REPAIR)` (restartRepair.ts:283-287). That is exactly the stale-terminal overlay above, on a real user path (resume a legacy-metadata run after a restart), and the proposal breaks it. So "requires two different executionIds to map to one streamId. That is impossible by construction" is not the guard's only job.

  **(4) EXTERNAL CONSUMER / wire — "byte-identical" and "nothing behavioural" are both false.** Two `cause` values on the frozen CLI NDJSON status vocabulary change:
  - Run start: today `transitionRunStart` (AgentRunLifecycle.ts:324-337) calls `transition(RUNNING, LIFECYCLE)`; with a reservation `tableFrom` is forced to `undefined` so LIFECYCLE succeeds and publishes `cause: 'lifecycle'`. Post-change the entry is already RUNNING, `canTransitionStreamPhase(RUNNING, RUNNING, 'lifecycle')` is false, and the call falls through to the RESUME arm — publishing a fresh run's start as `cause: 'resume'`.
  - Rollback: `reservation-rollback` → `lifecycle`. `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/sessionSignalsAdapter.ts:245-246` branches on exactly this: a terminal fact with cause LIFECYCLE or USER_STOP returns TUI focus to the owner stream. Today a rollback never triggers that; after the change a failed pre-activation launch would move the user's focus.
    The finding anticipates only the enum-member deprecation, not the fact that a live consumer reads the value it swaps in.

  </details>

- **Stop reconstructing a synthetic Error from a carried flow failure: RetryErrorInfo -> Error -> ProviderError -> RetryErrorInfo is an identity round trip, and a lossy one**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on cost + wrong trace (kill criteria 3 and 7). The observation is real (a `RetryErrorInfo` is rebuilt into a synthetic `Error` at AgentRunLifecycle.ts:314-322 and immediately re-flattened at :569-571), and the disk-full classification gap is real. But the proposed collapse is a net ADD, not -12 LoC, and it rests on a trace that stops one hop too early.

  (a) WRONG TRACE — the synthetic Error has three more consumers inside `finalizeFailedRun`, not just `classifyAgentError` + `normalizeProviderError`:
  - `logSdkError(ctx.logger, errorMsg, err, ...)` (:578) -> `buildErrorLogData(err)` -> `normalizeProviderError(err)`: the transcript/run-log error row recovers statusCode/provider/requestId/exhaustionReason off the attached metadata. Hand it a bare `RetryErrorInfo` object and that row degrades.
  - `options.onError?.(err, ...)` (:626) is the subagent delivery hook. `nativeSubagentStrategy.ts:306,401` stores it as `lastErr`, and `formatSubagentError` (`subagentResults.ts:239`) calls `normalizeProviderError(lastErr)` AGAIN, using `formatted.userRetryable` and `formatted.message` to build the text the orchestrator model reads. A plain object has no `providerErrorMetadata` symbol, so it falls through to `formatProviderHttpError`, which re-derives from `err.status`/raw body and would lose `statusCode`, `provider`, `exhaustionReason` and flip the `retryable` attribute. That is a user-visible regression in every carried subagent failure.
  - `new AgentError(errorMsg, { cause: err })` (:649).
    So the synthetic Error is not an identity round trip that only feeds the classifier — it is the carrier that makes a carried failure indistinguishable from a thrown one for the log row and the subagent report, which is exactly the rationale the code comment at :571-579 and the `finalizeFailedRun` doc comment record ("so a carried failure is exactly as loud as a thrown one"). Keeping those working after the change means either keeping `toFlowFailureError` anyway (no deletion) or changing `logSdkError`/`formatSubagentError`/`AgentError` call shapes — a much larger diff than the one proposed.

  (b) THE OWNER IS NOT ONE SITE — `shared.lastError` has four production producers, not one. Besides `ModelInvocationNode.getFallbackResult` (:632-670), `buildFailedRetryInfo` produces it at `ToolUseCycleNode.ts:185`, `ResponseCycleNode.ts:129` (catch) and `:145` (execFallback), and `buildFailedRetryInfo` (`providerErrorFormat.ts:635-643`) stamps neither boolean. With `errorKind` written only at `getFallbackResult`, `result.errorKind ?? 'unexpected'` REGRESSES those three arms: today the synthetic Error preserves `message`, so `isContextWindowError`'s third-party prose patterns (`errorPatterns.ts:58-86`) still classify a carried Anthropic/Google overflow as `context-window`. After the change it becomes `unexpected` unless all four producers call `classifyAgentError`.

  (c) MISSING ELEMENT — there is no `AgentErrorKindSchema`. `AgentErrorKind` is a hand-written TS union (`agentErrorClassification.ts:7-8`) with a `Record<AgentErrorKind, RunOutcome>` table beside it. The persisted flow-state schemas are Zod, so the migration must first create that schema (and make the type `z.infer` from it, or accept a dual system the repo's schema-SSOT rule forbids). The proposal counts this as free.

  (d) HONEST COST — new schema + type rewire (~10), `errorKind` on 4 persisted schemas + `AgentFlowMetaSchema` (~10 with doc comments), threading through `ToolUseCycleNode` and `ResponseCycleNode` result types, `post()` assignments and both flow-result builders (~12), `classifyAgentError` at 4 producers (~8), a lifecycle carried-failure entry that still has to serve `logSdkError`/`onError`/`AgentError` (~15), against ~9 lines of `toFlowFailureError` + 3 imports deleted. Plus rewriting the pinned regression test `AgentRunLifecycle.vitest.ts:1218-1252` ("classifies a carried missing-api-key failure through the flattened flag"), which asserts the exact mechanism being removed. Net roughly +30 src LoC and +1 element, not -12/-1.

  (e) The genuine defect the finding surfaced — a carried ENOSPC failure losing `disk-full` because `toFlowFailureError` rebuilds an Error without `code` — is worth fixing on its own, but the fix is ~3 lines inside `toFlowFailureError` (carry the errno the way the two boolean markers are carried), not this restructure. Note the blast radius is small: `AGENT_ERROR_OUTCOME` maps both `disk-full` and `unexpected` to FAILED, so the loss is toast wording and the narrowed `ResultEvent.error` shape, not the run outcome.

  Adjacent dated ruling, non-dispositive but pointing the same way: `.agents/docs/implemented/architecture/2026-06-10-error-pipeline-and-ownership.md:190` rejected-traps table — "Add `kind` to `ProviderError` | No consumer needs it today; status code is the de-facto cross-layer fact. Net-add." A consumer does exist now (T3-2 landed `ResultEvent.error.kind`), so this is not an exact prior kill, but the same shape of change was already measured as net-add there.

  </details>

### identity-model

- **One owner for a stream's agentCategory: delete the ephemeral copy in SessionState metadata and its hand-written drift guard**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on criterion 7 (wrong trace) plus criterion 3 (cost is wrong). Not a duplicate (nothing in rounds12-index.md or either survey doc covers it) and no dated doc ruling lands squarely on it — but the finding misreads the authority direction and omits two thirds of its own call sites.

  1. WRONG TRACE — the proposed accessor inverts a load-bearing precedence and makes a known placeholder sticky. `SessionFactApplier.updateChildRoster` (:746-750) writes a FABRICATED category into the ephemeral tier when nothing knows the real one: "Roster facts can arrive before RUNNING/config creates the parent state (TUI child-event-order `roster-first`). Provision a ToolUse bucket so retention still runs; a later run.config refreshes the real category." That refresh happens **through the very copy the finding deletes**: run.config publishes `StreamSummaryMeta.agentCategory`, `getStreamMetadata` overlays it (SessionState.ts:265-296 → applySummaryMetadata:207), `getStreamCategory` returns the durable value, and the next `getOrCreateStreamState(streamId, category)` (SessionFactApplier:887, LitSessionRenderer:328, desktop:451) heals the execution-state row in place (SessionState.ts:328-335). The proposed `getStreamState(stream)?.category ?? streamLogs.getSummaryMeta(stream)?.agentCategory` reverses that arrow: once the ToolUse placeholder exists, every caller of the tiered accessor returns ToolUse, every `getOrCreateStreamState` is then fed ToolUse, and the durable Workflow value is never consulted again. No production path re-derives the category from the resolved config independently of `getStreamCategory`, so a workflow parent that saw a roster fact before run.config renders permanently as tool-use — wrong sections from `buildStreamContentRender`, wrong tab decorator, wrong CLI `workflowPhase`. `LitSessionRenderer:322-323` reads `metadata.agentCategory ?? existingState?.category` in that order for exactly this reason; the finding reads the fallback ladder as redundancy when it is the healing path.

  2. PREMISE ERROR — there are two storage sites, not three. `SessionStreamMetadata.agentCategory` is not "a third ephemeral copy": `getStreamMetadata` merges the live patch with the durable mirror at read time on every call, and the docblock at SessionState.ts:195-206 names that mirror "the durable metadata authority ... Applied at read time, so there is no stored copy of authority data to go stale and no refresh plumbing" (#9947, re-cited at SessionFactApplier:692 and desktop/desktopAgentExecution.ts:441). The stored slice is a live-only patch covering the window before the mirror resolves; the write-back at :242 repairs exactly that window after `resetStreamMetadataForRun`. The finding's ownership rule ("live execution state for a resident stream, durable for a rehydrated one") contradicts the documented rule that the durable mirror is authoritative for resident streams too.

  3. COST IS WRONG — the Paths list omits six production readers in two hosts. `SessionStreamMetadata.agentCategory` is read at packages/desktop/src/main/desktopAgentExecution.ts:449 and at five CLI sites: chat/tui/state/focusedChildFollowUp.ts:43, state/subscribeStreamLog.ts:285, panes/ConversationPane.tsx:222, panes/StaticConversationTranscript.tsx:148, panes/StatusBar.tsx:314 (plus the doc contract in state/workflowPhase.ts:11). Several are documented pure selectors that receive `Readonly<SessionStreamMetadata>` as an input (focusedChildFollowUp.ts:24) — dropping the field forces a new category input threaded through props at each, which ADDS lines.

  4. THE FIELD CANNOT LEAVE THE WIRE, so the element saving is ~0. `StreamTabInfoSchema = StreamIdentityFieldsSchema.extend({...})` (stream.ts:278), and the extension webview reads `info.agentCategory` at progressState.ts:313 and :476, slices/streamLifecycleSlice.ts:98-99, components/StreamTabs.ts:156 and :339, components/StreamHeader.ts:432-441 (whose comment states "agentCategory rides the live wire"). The finding's own plan keeps it on `StreamTabInfo` via an explicit `buildStreamTabInfo` input, so the field moves from the base object into the extend block: −1 field on `SessionStreamMetadata`, +1 on `StreamTabInfo`, +1 new accessor method. Net element count ≈ 0; only the drift-guard concept is genuinely retired.

  5. TEST COST — ~14 vitest sites seed the category through `updateStreamMetadata({agentCategory})` and would need rewriting to seed execution state or summary meta (src/test-kernel/progressView/StreamContentSync.vitest.ts:238, ProgressBackendRetainedChildren, ProgressBackendStreamLifecycle, ProgressBackendFactProjection, cli/TuiStateAndFocus, SubagentListDisplay, ConversationTranscript). StreamContentSync.vitest.ts:235 ("preserves a provisional tool-use execution across metadata reset") pins the two-tier ladder as intended behavior, not as accident.

  The residual true observation — that `SessionFactApplier.ts:235-244`'s write-back is a hand-maintained sync — is real but small (~10 lines), and it is the cheap half of a design whose expensive half (a category that can be pending, so the placeholder never has to be fabricated) the finding does not propose.

  </details>

### persistence

- **Retire StagedDeletionCoordinator: reorder stream deletion decide-first so the reversible rename transaction is unrepresentable**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on three independent grounds: a dated KEEP ruling in this campaign's own strategy doc, a live PRD that already owns decide-first deletion, and a wrong trace on the one contract item the whole design rests on.

  === KILL 1 — DATED RULING, in this campaign's own charter ===

  .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-10-simplifier-fleet-round1-strategy.md:481, Theme K "Adjudicated-KEEP / rulings to record (so sweeps stop re-proposing)":

  "**StagedDeletionCoordinator: load-bearing port, do NOT refactor opportunistically**; if ever revisited -> typed transition table + p-queue, own PR, existing vitest as gate (area-10)"

  and again at :554 under "Explicit defer / do-not-do (checked against ledgers + taxonomy)":

  "Auth state-machine merge, **StagedDeletionCoordinator refactor**, ... — all recorded as KEEP/wait in Theme K or banned by the taxonomy."

  This is the governing strategy document for the very simplifier campaign whose rounds 1-2 shipped as #11416-#11422 / #11433-#11441. The finding is the named banned item, filed into the campaign that banned it, and the ruling's stated purpose is verbatim "so sweeps stop re-proposing" it. Even the escape hatch is not met: the ruling permits revisiting only as "typed transition table + p-queue, own PR, existing vitest as gate" — the proposal instead deletes the vitest that the ruling designates as the gate.

  === KILL 2 — #11283's closing ruling, four days old, and the finding truncates it ===

  #11283 closed 2026-08-22 by the owner:

  "`ResidentStreamRegistry`, `KVStoreCache`, and `StagedDeletionCoordinator` already own the genuinely common pieces. ... Before any future cross-store change, require a design/invariant matrix and disposable fresh-process crash simulations at rename, transcript-delete, sidecar-write, summary-write, and **late-reclaim** barriers."

  Two problems. First, the ruling names StagedDeletionCoordinator as a correct owner, not as doomed machinery. Second, the finding cites #11283 for "the standing requirement" but reduces five barriers to two — "before and after the transcript unlink" — silently dropping the late-reclaim barrier, which is the exact barrier its design cannot pass (see Kill 4). It also skips the "design/invariant matrix ... rather than a direct refactor PR" precondition entirely.

  === KILL 3 — decide-first is already owned by a live PRD, and #10843's closer asked for exactly that ===

  .agents/docs/rejected/architecture/2026-08-18-session-event-journal.md §3.6 (dated the day AFTER the #10773 abandonment the finding relies on):

  "**Deletion is decide-first** on the #10805 foundations: `SessionState` commits the tombstone + incarnation fence, then the journal is unlinked and derived state dropped; **the id stays unavailable until cleanup completes**; a startup sweep removes derived/artifact residue for ids absent from the registry. The staged rename transaction, rollback recovery machine, and buffered-write diversion have nothing to guard."

  §6 sequences it: stage 3 "Decide-first deletion for journal streams; coordinator bypassed", stage 5 "Legacy retirement at the horizon: delete the frozen store, the coordinator, and their suites in one PR". §5 books the same 730 LoC the finding claims.

  So the finding's core argument — that decide-first "died with the abandoned #10773 family, not on merit" — is wrong about where it went. It was re-homed the next day into a PRD that treats "the id stays unavailable until cleanup completes" as a property the journal design BUILDS, not one it inherits. That is precisely the "fresh, coherent specification" #10843's closer demanded, and it exists. Extracting stage 3 as a standalone round-3 collapse against the legacy store is the opportunistic refactor Kill 1 bans.

  === KILL 4 — WRONG TRACE: contract item (2) does not exist, and its absence is a data-loss regression ===

  The finding asserts item (2) "registry holds the deleted id unavailable until cleanup completes — ALREADY EXISTS as the incarnation-keyed tombstone barrier (SessionState.ts:429-458 beginStreamRemoval, :493 isStreamRemoved, :146 _removedStreams)". Three verifications say the opposite.

  (a) The `_removedStreams` docstring (SessionState.ts:136-139) disclaims exactly this: "Durable sidecar finality is owned by {@link SessionStores} + the snapshot store's staged-deletion/eviction write guards, **not by this tombstone**; its 'removal is final' semantics are scoped to this in-memory projection."

  (b) `claimStreamIdentity` (SessionState.ts:472-490) does `this._removedStreams.delete(stream)` — the tombstone is RETIRED BY the re-claim, synchronously, the instant a live-execution attachment lands. Its call site (SessionFactApplier.ts:284-296) is a synchronous branch with zero coordination against the in-flight durable deletion. The barrier is not held until cleanup completes; it is dropped by the event the barrier was supposed to block.

  (c) SessionFactApplier.ts:360-364 states the current product behavior the proposal removes: "a workflow relaunch that claims the identity while the delete is queued makes the delete report `superseded` instead of erasing the fresh run."

  Consequence. `streamDataDir(streamId)` (streamDataPaths.ts:74-76) keys the sidecar directory by stream id with NO incarnation component, and a deterministic relaunch reuses the same id. Under the proposed order (unlink transcript -> child-detach projection -> `rm -rf streamData/<id>`), a re-claim landing across those awaits writes the fresh incarnation's sidecars into the very directory the pending rm then destroys — irreversibly, and now with the transcript already unlinked, so there is no rollback target. Today's rename-first ordering is what makes that window survivable: writes in it are buffered and `commit(shouldDelete)` re-checks and replays them rather than dropping them.

  The proposal's step 2 ("queueWrite refuses a write for a stream the registry holds tombstoned", ~10 LoC) cannot close this, for two independent reasons: (i) by the time the fresh incarnation writes, `claimStreamIdentity` has already deleted the tombstone, so there is nothing to refuse against; (ii) `StreamSnapshotStore` holds no reference to `SessionState` — the dependency runs SessionState -> snapshots (SessionState.ts:107), so reading the tombstone from the store requires a NEW inverted port. That is an added seam, not a deletion.

  === KILL 5 — the barrier must be cross-process; an in-memory tombstone provably cannot be it ===

  SessionStores.ts:861-864: "A stream can be registered by another host between this process's scan and deletion. Only the durable transcript index may admit the irreversible delete; this instance's cached summary is a prefilter." Hosts share one storage root (WORKSPACE_STORAGE_LAYOUT.streamData under ~/.texra). Host A's in-memory SessionState tombstone is invisible to host B.

  The repo paid for this exact substitution three days ago. Commit f531898b80 (2026-08-23), "fix(runtime): keep admission and disk guards the per-execution lane does not replace": "The lane gives mutual exclusion within one process. It does not refuse a second generation (admission) and **it cannot see a stream deleted by another process (a disk fact)**. Review of #11318 found guards for both that the lane removal had taken with it." It restored `hasAuthoritativeStream` — a disk read — as the resume fence. The proposal repeats the same swap: replace a durable, cross-process transaction with an in-process, in-memory flag.

  === KILL 6 — the staleness argument is factually false ===

  "git log --oneline -15 src/transcript/ shows no work in this area since 95d2b4984f ... the four transcript commits since the #10773 abandonment are local dedup, none touching the deletion protocol."

  Wrong twice. Two commits after 95d2b4984f touched src/transcript/ (5abad05f7f #11441, 21f7ee24fd #11437). More importantly, the deletion protocol itself was rewritten on 2026-08-23 by f531898b80, which changed 124 lines of src/agent/storage/SessionStores.ts — one of the finding's own listed paths — under the bullet "Stream delete: SessionStores.deleteStream runs the deletion as the lane step itself ... waitForExecutionQuiescence and deleteStreamAfterExecutionQuiescence are gone." The `deleteStream` lane docstring the finding quotes as settled background is text that commit introduced three days ago. This is an actively-worked area, not an abandoned one.

  </details>

- **Finish the stream<->execution FK collapse: stop storing executionId in the stream sidecar and the summary mirror**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on rule 5 (persisted user data) and rule 3 (cost accounting), with a wrong-trace component (rule 7).

  THE LOAD-BEARING PREMISE IS FALSE ON REAL DATA. The whole proposal rests on step 7: "both deleted fields are derived duplicates of an authored field, so under #9434 they are discarded, not migrated." I measured the user's actual store (~/.texra/workspace-storage, 6 workspaces) and the sidecar FK is NOT a duplicate for the majority of streams:

  executions/_/meta.json present: 6,755
  ...of which carry the authored `streamId`: 2,762 (41%)
  streamData/_/meta.json present: 6,013
  ...of which carry `executionId`: 5,972
  ...resolvable through readExecutionStreamIndex(): 2,721
  ...NOT resolvable (index has no entry): 3,251 (54%)
  ...mismatched (index disagrees): 0

  Of those 3,251 unresolvable sidecars, only 18 are dangling. The other 3,233 point at an execution row that EXISTS and is readable — the row simply has no `meta.streamId`, because `registerExecution` (src/agent/storage/executionLifecycle.ts:122-135) is the only writer of that field and there is no backfill stamper (the "idempotent entrance stamper" in the ExecutionMetaCoreSchema comment brings `identity` forward, not `streamId`; grep confirms no other `streamId` write to an existing row). For those 3,233 rows the sidecar FK is the ONLY surviving edge, and it is load-bearing today:

  execution rows reachable only via the sidecar FK: 3,233
  ...carrying `identity`: 1,695
  ...carrying `description`: 2,272
  ...with a config.json: 3,233

  Re-walking the path the proposal changes: applyStreamData (:2029) -> hydrateRunStateFromMeta(stream, meta) (:1936-1982) -> `meta.executionId` -> getExecutionStore(id).readMeta()/readConfig() -> identity, userFollowUpSupport, description, config. Replace `meta.executionId` with the proposal's `resolveExecutionId(stream)` backed by readExecutionStreamIndex() (src/agent/storage/executionListing.ts:185-224, which returns `null` for any row without `meta.streamId`) and 3,233 cold-history streams in this one store hydrate with no identity, no config, no description, no userFollowUpSupport. That is precisely the "bare Sessions labels / no category / no model chip" regression, re-introduced at 54% scale. On the very workspace the finding measured (coauthor-5892af56: its own "845 executions, 816 streamData" numbers reproduce exactly) 213 of 802 FK-bearing sidecars — 27% — go dark.

  The finding's "what we give up" section names only the narrow case where executions/<id>/meta.json is UNREADABLE. It never considers the dominant case: the row is perfectly readable and just predates the authored edge. Measured, unreadable-row loss is 0 rows; pre-stamp loss is 3,233.

  WHY THE CITED RULING DOESN'T RESCUE IT. .agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-08-23-single-owner-sessions.md:454 does list the target ("meta.streamId authored; reverse index derived at load") and :472 does say "Migration verdict: none needed" — but that verdict is justified by StreamLogStore.ts:97-105, which is the discard-and-rebuild contract for the SUMMARY cache, not for the stream sidecar. The doc simply assumed what I measured to be false. Commit 9b424bb309 then reached the same wall empirically and recorded it verbatim: "The sidecar executionId write and the summary-mirror executionId stay ... the cut list's stop-writing precondition does not hold." The finding reads that sentence as naming a plumbing gap (thread an index into seedStreams); the actual gap is a data gap that no amount of threading closes.

  WHAT WOULD BE REQUIRED, AND WHAT IT COSTS. To do this without regression you need a one-time authored-tier backfill: scan streamData sidecars, and for each FK whose execution row lacks `streamId`, write it onto the row. That is a NEW migration concept on authored data (the #9434 rule the finding invokes to avoid migration now demands one), needs idempotence, needs enqueueMetaUpdate serialization against live writers, needs an unreadable/absent-row policy, and cannot run at all on a store whose streamData was pruned or whose sidecar predates the FK (41 sidecars here have no executionId either). Call it +80-120 LoC and one durable migration element — which erases the claimed win and then some. Without the backfill, the sidecar FK read must survive forever as the legacy edge, and then almost nothing on the cut list is removable: hydrateRunStateFromMeta keeps its `meta.executionId` read, parseStreamMeta keeps its FK-tolerance arm (streamSnapshotRead.ts:112-133), StreamTabMetaSchema keeps the field, applyStreamData keeps handoff detection keyed on it, and StreamSnapshotStore.writeMeta keeps rewriting both FKs — so the #10809 seed-chain gating stays too.

  The only residue that survives scrutiny is tiny and not what was proposed: `StreamSummaryMeta.executionId` (StreamLogStore.ts:87) is one field of a broader display cache that the file itself documents as "lazily backfills legacy summaries" and is explicitly discard-and-rebuild tier — dropping it plus SessionState.applySummaryMetadata:222's `?? stored.executionId` is roughly 5 lines and 1 element, and even that should wait, because for a legacy stream the mirror's identity/description block is itself populated from the sidecar-FK hydration this finding would delete.

  </details>

### approval-system

- **`onApprovalPolicyDenial` is a session fact wearing a run-scoped callback: one stderr line threaded through 10 runtime files and 3 delegation forwarding hops**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on four independent grounds; any one is fatal.

  **1. A DATED RULING ALREADY DECIDED THIS, names the exact symbol, and carries an explicit do-not-refile (criterion 2).**
  `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-16-services-injection-audit.md` §6 Resolution addendum, line 229: "**C13 — approval trio is resolved; do-not-refile.** … `onApprovalPolicyDenial` **stays explicit on the projection boundary because it is a caller-injected callback, not a session-derived fact** (`:142`, `:154`) — **correct and intentional, not residue**." The same doc carries a permanent rejected row at :163: "**R-2: approval trio → session-owned fields as a mechanical move.** Refuted as described in C13 — the value is a launch-pinned run fact derived from mutable session state, not a session invariant," and C13 at :136 names the rejected shapes: "**session-owned mutable trio** (above), and implicit child inheritance of parent ALS values (dual-ownership) … If no shape clears §15's single-owner bar, the answer is to keep the explicit threading — it is ugly but honest." The finding's proposal ("`SessionHandle` absorbs it … add a listener slot") is verbatim the shape that row rejects, and its own kill-condition check never searched `docs/proposals/`.
  Two more records say the same thing. `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-03-prd-approval-policy-unification.md:157` records the shipped design: "Denial classification propagates as a **run-owned fact** through fresh, resumed, delegated, and workflow-script execution (`RunContext.onApprovalPolicyDenial`, #9692)" — the four forwarding paths the finding calls accidental plumbing are the enumerated deliverable of #9692. And the code itself carries the rationale at `src/agent/runtime/AgentLaunchContext.ts:138-146`: "the tool-policy fields … are projected straight from `ctx.toolPolicy` so callers can't drift a hand-maintained copy; **only `onApprovalPolicyDenial` (a callback that is not part of ToolPolicy) is still supplied explicitly**." Round 2 of this very campaign already refuted a sibling candidate (`tool-layer-dup`, `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-26-simplification-survey-round2.md:3607-3615`) on this same C13 ruling, and that refutation explicitly lists `onApprovalPolicyDenial` as one of the adjacent fields that survives.

  **2. WRONG TRACE — the central premise is false, and the "behavior is unchanged" claim is a regression (criterion 7).**
  The finding asserts the dedupe "is per CLI process rather than per run" and that this is what makes the fact session-scoped. It is not. `warnedApprovalContexts` is a `WeakSet<CliContext>`, and in the chat TUI a **fresh `CliContext` object is minted per run**: `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/runChatTui.tsx:249-253` — `const currentSessionContext = (helperModel) => ({ ...context, helperModel, quietLogs: true })` returns a **new object literal on every call**; it is wired as `getSessionContext` (`:392`), which `beginRunContext` calls on every run start or resume (`packages/cli/src/chat/chatSessionController.ts:220`, called at `:427`, `:555`, `:743`). So today the warning fires **once per run**, exactly as `warnApprovalDenied`'s own docstring states ("Tell the operator, **once per run**", approvalPrompts.ts:73). The proposed registration — once, "next to the `setApprovalPolicy` seed in runChatTui", with body `warnApprovalDenied(context, gate)` — can only capture the single outer process-level `context`, collapsing the affordance to **once per TUI process**: after the first denied gate, every later chat round goes silent. That is a degradation of precisely the operator line the PRD preserved when exit code 4 was retired, i.e. the finding's own stated must-not-break constraint, and it is the opposite of the claimed "small correctness improvement". The removed forwarding hops are what make a delegated/workflow-script child share the parent **run's** dedupe key rather than a process-wide one.

  **3. COST IS WRONG (criterion 3).** The finding lists 13 paths and zero tests, but the symbol has 11 references across **5 test files** that all have to be rewritten: `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/AgentLaunchContext.vitest.ts:364,380,389,393,429` (an entire case whose premise is the assertion `expect(context.onApprovalPolicyDenial).toBe(onApprovalPolicyDenial)` and whose comment reads "the only explicit option left is `onApprovalPolicyDenial`"), `src/test-kernel/tools/BashApprovalPrompt.vitest.ts:93`, `src/test-kernel/tools/ToolEditApprovalGate.vitest.ts:208`, `src/test-kernel/cli/ApprovalAdapter.vitest.ts:226`, `src/test-kernel/cli/RunExecution.vitest.ts:784`. Deleting the field also empties `withExecutionRunContext`'s `options` parameter entirely (`AgentLaunchContext.ts:132-136`), forcing a signature change at both production callers (`executeAgent.ts:427`, `:583`) and at the hand-written mock in `src/test-kernel/agent/runtime/ResumeToolUseCancellation.vitest.ts:35`. Against ~24-28 deleted lines the additions are a listener set field + a register-with-unsubscribe method + a notify method on `SessionHandle` (~12-16 lines with the doc comment the file's style requires), plus registrations in two CLI hosts (~6-10), plus the test rewrites. Honest net is roughly -3 LoC, not -14.

  **4. It adds a subscribe surface and mutable session state to a VS Code-free runtime type (supporting).** CLAUDE.md, Event channels: "don't add a new `bus.emit` from a VS Code-free zone and **don't add a new subscribe surface**." A listener-registration slot on `SessionHandle` (`src/agent/runtime/SessionHandle.ts:271-278`, beside a `texraApprovalPolicy` field that the TUI mutates mid-session via `/approval`) is a new subscribe surface plus a second piece of mutable session state, which is the dual-ownership C13 forbade. The finding correctly rules out `RuntimePresentationEvent` and `SessionFact`; the third option it lands on is the one the audit ruled out by name.

  </details>

### tool-layer

- **Fold the tool layer's three host-capability service locators into the Platform composition root**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on criterion 3 (cost is wrong) and criterion 7 (one misread hop), with the claimed element win collapsing to ~zero.

  **1. The load-bearing test-cost premise is false.** The claim says the migration means "churn in 7 test files that call `setSetupPlatform` and the Lean/inline-comment suites (they already install a fake platform, so this is adding a field to an existing double)." I checked every one. Eight test files call the three setters. Exactly ONE installs a platform: `src/test-kernel/tools/setup/ApplyTeamTool.vitest.ts:23,75-77` (`createFakePlatform` from `@test/support/FakePlatform`). The other seven — `InvokeCommandTool.vitest.ts`, `SendToTerminalTool.vitest.ts`, `DefaultSetupPlatform.vitest.ts`, `CredentialReporting.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/model/ApiProviders.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/tools/LeanInspectTool.vitest.ts`, `src/test-kernel/tools/InlineCommentTool.vitest.ts` — contain no `initPlatform`, no `createFakePlatform`, no platform import at all. They exist in that shape _because_ the locator lets a tool unit test run with no platform installed. Under the proposal each gains a `createFakePlatform` import plus an `initPlatform(...)` call, and two of them re-register mid-suite (`ApplyTeamTool.vitest.ts:194` swaps `signIn`; `DefaultSetupPlatform.vitest.ts:34-35` resets between cases), which becomes a full platform re-init per case. That is roughly +30-40 LoC of test scaffolding the accounting omits entirely, and it is the sentence the whole "cheap migration" argument rests on.

  **2. The Lean hop is misread.** The claim states each host "still constructs its own value" and cites `nodeAgentRuntime.ts:34`. No host constructs a Lean value. `registerDirectLeanLanguageServices(lifecycle)` at `src/tools/lean/direct/directLspAdapter.ts:78-84` builds the adapter _inside_ `src/tools`, calls `setLeanLanguageServices`, AND registers `lifecycle.onShutdown(SHUTDOWN_PHASE.ON, () => leanAdapter.dispose())` — all three in one place. It runs from `initNodeAgentRuntime` (`src/platform/defaults/nodeAgentRuntime.ts:32-35`), which every Node path calls AFTER `initPlatform`: CLI `packages/cli/src/runtime/initPlatform.ts:326` (init at :271), desktop `packages/desktop/src/main/platform/index.ts:143` (init at :93), and the SDK `packages/agent/src/index.ts:247` inside `runAgent`, on an embedder-supplied Platform. Making `lean` a required Platform field inverts that order: construction must move before `initPlatform` and split from the dispose registration, in three separate composition roots. The claim quotes `nodeAgentRuntime.ts:22-26` ("the CLI previously skipped `registerAgentFeatures`, silently losing the memory and goal tool injections") as evidence FOR the change, while the restructure it proposes re-opens exactly the multi-root drift that docstring says the module was extracted to close ("Centralized so the hosts cannot drift").

  **3. The composition root is `createNodePlatform`, not `initPlatform`, and that erases the compile-time win.** All four hosts build their Platform through `src/platform/defaults/nodeHost.ts:113-136` (`extension.ts:280`, `desktop/main/platform/index.ts:93`, `cli/runtime/initPlatform.ts:271`, `packages/agent/src/node.ts:45`). Adding three fields to `Platform` alone does not make a forgotten wiring a type error — `createNodePlatform` would just leave them `undefined`, producing the same runtime failure with a different message. To get the claimed enforcement the three fields must also be required on `NodePlatformServices`, which forces `lean: undefined` / `setup: …` into `packages/agent/src/node.ts` (the SDK's public `nodePlatform()` helper), into `FakePlatform.ts`, into `AgentPackage.vitest.ts`'s `PLATFORM` constant, and into `CliInitPlatform.vitest.ts` whose `createNodePlatform: vi.fn(() => ({}))` mock plus `expect(getSetupPlatform().host).toBe('cli')` assertion (:71, :528) both stop working. None of that is in the accounting.

  **4. The element win is ~0, not -10.** Because the fields must be `T | undefined`, the throwing getters do not die — they become require-helpers reading `platform().setup` / `.lean` / `.inlineComments`, one per capability, or eight inline null checks. Real deletions: `setSetupPlatform`, `__resetSetupPlatformForTests`, `setLeanLanguageServices`, `setInlineCommentProvider`, three module `let`s, one barrel re-export = 8. Real additions: 3 `Platform` fields, 3 `NodePlatformServices` fields, an exported desktop setup-host factory (today `desktopSetupAuth.ts` closes over a module-level `activeSignIn` and installs itself), and a `createDirectLeanLanguageServices` returning-factory to replace the registering one = 8. Net zero.

  **5. The strongest supporting ruling actually argues against these three specifically.** `.agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-06-07-dependency-injection-cleanup.md` (Status: "plan steps 1-5 not yet implemented") does classify `setSetupPlatform` `setup/platform.ts:104` and `setLeanLanguageServices` `lean/leanLanguageServices.ts:53` as P-class "fold into `Platform` ports" (plan step 1) — the claim never cites it, which is the one citation that would have helped. But that same inventory marks both with "Silent no-op? **no** (getter throws)", and the section's stated payoff is "7 setters default to a no-op … Folding into typed `Platform` ports turns each missing wiring into a compile error instead of a silent runtime gap. Do the 5 ext-only no-op setters first (clearest user-facing bug surface in CLI/desktop)." The dated plan aims the benefit at the silent class and explicitly sequences the loud ones last. These three are the loud ones; the argued win is the one they already have.

  Not killed by criteria 1, 2, 4, 5, or 6: no rounds-1/2 overlap (round 1's shipped inline-comment item removed only the `available()`/`UNAVAILABLE` sentinel; round 2's refutation at survey-round2:3610-3614 is a different candidate and the claim correctly disclaims it); #8747/#7810/#8777 scoped shrinking, not folding, and none forbids this; `platform → tools` is already a baselined value edge in `config/ratchets/architecture-edges-baseline.json:58`, so the type-only import is legal and the extension-bundle exclusion survives; and none of the three crosses the NDJSON wire, a settings key, or the texra-action contract.

  </details>

### model-layer

- **One subscription-route catalog: fold the two OAuth rows into the coding-plan descriptor/runtime tables and delete src/model/quotaFallbackRoutes.ts**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on three independent grounds: a dated ruling the finding cites but misreads, an accounting that omits the whole test surface and the relocation half of every "deletion", and a net element count that does not actually drop.

  **1. A dated ruling already decided the load-bearing half (kill criterion 2).**

  `2366c7851e` (2026-08-19), the very commit the finding offers as evidence that "the direction is endorsed and the last mile is unfinished", closes with the opposite conclusion — it states where the union lives and why:

  > "The union lives in `codingPlanSubscriptions` so the OAuth-only module stays off the coding-plan runtime's key-reading import path, **which the onboarding setup paths pull in**."

  That is not an unfinished last mile; it is a stated boundary, seven days old. The finding's step 4 reverses it directly. It routes `src/tools/setup/platform.ts:157`, `src/model/setupCredentialAccess.ts:27`, `src/controllers/onboarding/setupLaunch.ts:37`, and `packages/cli/src/runtime/credentialStatus.ts:41` (verified: the CLI first-run credential gate, `hasCliRunCredential`) off `@model/providerCapabilities` and onto a unified runtime table — i.e. onto the module that carries `hasUsableApiKey(platform().secrets, 'glm')`, `resolveKimiCodeRoutingFacts`, and `writePlatformSetting`. That is exactly the key-reading import path the commit kept the onboarding gate off.

  The same commit made a second decision the proposal undoes: "the shared signed-in probe now returns the profile's `usageRoute` instead of a boolean", and the surviving comment at `src/model/providerCapabilities.ts:153` says it returns the profile's own usageRoute "so callers never restate which route a provider serves". Making `activeSubscriptionUsageRoute` "a find over the one table" requires putting `usageRoute` back on a hand-written OAuth descriptor row — a second source of truth for the OAuth route, which is the exact drift class (`/status` vs status bar disagreeing) that commit fixed.

  A third recorded rationale sits at `src/model/providerCapabilities.ts:150-154`: "The coding plans cannot share this (they have no sign-in probe and add key-set facts), so they answer from their catalog descriptor." The split of activation logic is documented as deliberate, not residual.

  **2. Cost is wrong (kill criterion 3).**

  The accounting counts raw deleted line ranges as deletions and books zero test cost. Both are wrong.

  Relocation, not deletion: of `src/model/quotaFallbackRoutes.ts`'s 78 lines (verified `wc -l`), only `runtimeFor` (21 lines) plus its imports are merge glue. `QuotaFallbackRuntime` (imported by `src/controllers/progressView/ProgressApiKeyRetryController.ts:11`), `setCodexEnabled`/`setXaiEnabled`, the two OAuth runtime rows, and the exported `quotaFallbackRuntimes` — roughly 35 lines — move, they do not vanish. Same for the providerCapabilities block: ~67 raw lines out, but the two OAuth `isActiveForModel` bodies plus `resolveCapabilities`/sign-in wiring on the rows put ~24 back, and the five `isCodexSubscriptionActive(CHATGPT_SETUP_MODEL)` / `isXaiSubscriptionActive` call sites each get _longer_ (a one-line call becomes a `subscriptionRouteById(...)` lookup returning `| undefined`).

  Test cost, entirely unbooked: eight suites change. Four of them (`SetupLaunch.vitest.ts` 167 lines, `SetupCredentialAccess.vitest.ts` 77, `DefaultSetupPlatform.vitest.ts` 126, `CredentialStatus.vitest.ts` 66) today `vi.mock('@model/providerCapabilities')` with a single `vi.fn()` per probe. After the merge they must fabricate a frozen route table instead of stubbing one function — a strictly worse mock shape, +10-20 lines each. `ProgressApiKeyRetryController.vitest.ts` (751 lines) builds fake `QuotaFallbackRuntime` objects at :112 that gain a required `isActiveForModel`. `ProviderCapabilities.vitest.ts` asserts `isCodexSubscriptionActive` at five sites. `CodingPlanSubscriptions.vitest.ts` and `CodingPlanSubscriptionsRuntime.vitest.ts` pin the table shapes.

  Honest total: production ≈ -94, tests ≈ +50, net ≈ **-45 LoC**, not -130.

  **3. The element count does not drop (kill criterion 6).**

  The two descriptor shapes share 5 of 23 fields. Merging them yields: two interfaces (still), plus a `SubscriptionRoute` union, plus a `kind` discriminant on four rows, plus a type-predicate filter, plus `subscriptionRouteById`, plus an optional `resolveCapabilities` field that only two rows can carry. Meanwhile `isCodingPlanQuotaRoute`, `activeSubscriptionUsageRoute`, `QUOTA_FALLBACK_ROUTES`, `CODING_PLAN_SUBSCRIPTIONS`, and `quotaFallbackRuntimes` all survive. Genuinely deleted exports: three (`isCodexSubscriptionActive`, `isXaiSubscriptionActive`, `oauthSubscriptionUsageRoute`) plus one module. Net elements ≈ **0 to -1**, not -6. Nineteen fields present on two rows and absent on the other two is a union of disjoint shapes with a discriminant, not "one catalog".

  **4. The migration plan is internally contradictory (kill criterion 7).**

  Step 1 says "make the coding-plan-only fields optional" and, in the same breath, that `CODING_PLAN_SUBSCRIPTIONS` as a filter "keeps its 8 existing importers unchanged". These are mutually exclusive. With optional fields the literal types are lost and at least five importers break at compile time: `src/shared/schemas/subscriptionUsage.ts:6` (feeds `z.enum(SUBSCRIPTION_USAGE_PROVIDERS)`, a **persisted** snapshot schema), `src/telemetry/UsageLogService.ts:82` (`PLAN_ACCOUNTING_ROUTES`, the set that decides which usage records are sent _regardless of the telemetry opt-out_ — billing accounting), `src/controllers/modelAccess/subscriptionUsage/SubscriptionUsageService.ts:32`, `packages/cli/src/runtime/modelAccessRoute.ts:27`, and `packages/extension/src/settingsView/frontend/tabs/SubscriptionsTab.ts:150`. Preserving them requires a discriminated union with an `Extract<>`-typed predicate filter over an `as const` array — workable, but then the "optional fields" simplification is gone and so is most of the claimed win. The finding never mentions any of these five derivations.

  Separately, "`isCodingPlanQuotaRoute(id)` becomes `routeById(id)?.kind === 'coding-plan'` — the distinction stops being a table-membership scan and becomes a field read" is not achievable. It is a type predicate (`id is CodingPlanSubscriptionId`) and its narrowing is load-bearing at `packages/cli/src/chat/tui/state/subscribeApprovals.ts:781-790`, where the narrowed `codingPlanId` is passed to `setCliCodingPlanSubscription(codingPlanId, false)`, which requires the narrowed type. A `?.kind ===` expression narrows the route, not the id. The function survives; only its one-line body changes. Zero symbols removed there.

  Net: a ~-45 LoC, ~0-element change that reverses a seven-day-old boundary decision, drags the first-run credential gate onto the secrets-reading import path, restates the OAuth usage route the same commit de-duplicated, and touches five undeclared derivations including a persisted Zod enum and the billing-accounting route set. Not worth it.

  </details>

- **Let ModelHandlerCodex own "is the ChatGPT subscription serving this request", the way ModelHandlerXAI already owns the Grok one — delete tryCodexSubscriptionRoute and the last @auth/codex import in ModelFactory**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on three independent grounds.

  **KILL 2 — a dated ruling already decided the placement, in the opposite direction.** The finding cites #6644 as support ("already ruled ... the Codex branch is the deliberate async exception; this proposal is the follow-through that removes the exception"). #6644's actual resolution prescribes the branch rather than tolerating it: "Make the ordered precedence the single source of truth — e.g. have `createModelHandler()` compute the base key via `modelHandlerCompatibilityKey(...)` and `switch` on it for instantiation, **keeping the async Codex special-case as an explicit override (key unchanged) before the switch**." That is the shape on main today, and the JSDoc at ModelFactory.ts:380-389 restates it verbatim ("The only routing decision made here is the async Codex-subscription override, which the pure key predicate deliberately omits because it is key-neutral"). #9328 (CLOSED 2026-08-09) adds the second half: "Per the maintainer's 2026-07-27 comment, the Codex OAuth half was deliberately deferred and should _not_ be met by folding it into the relay seam — **it needs its own design decision**. ... Both survivors are live machinery, not vestigial." A high-risk restructure of exactly that seam, swept in through a mechanical simplification round, is the thing that note defers.

  **KILL 7 — wrong trace: the ModelHandlerXAI symmetry is a misread, and the finding's own remedy is not implementable.** ModelHandlerXAI has NO pre-route optimism: `standardPricingConfig` (:88-95) and `getBaseUrl` (:129-134) both key strictly on a _captured_ route (`activeCredentialRoute === 'xai-subscription'` / `getLastCredentialUsageRoute()`), so before any client is built the handler reports plain API-key semantics. ModelHandlerCodex is the opposite by design: `capabilitiesForRoute(undefined) → configuredSubscriptionCapabilities()` (:198-205) and `getBaseUrl`'s second branch (:258-263) both report the **subscription** profile whenever the preference is on and no route has been captured yet — introduced deliberately by #9030 ("snapshot model credential routes"). That pre-route optimism is sound only because the factory guarantees a `ModelHandlerCodex` is constructed only when `isCodexSessionRoutable()` was true. Remove the factory probe and a prefer-on/signed-out user gets a handler that, before its first client build, reports the 272k Codex input limit, zero pricing, `backgroundMode: 'disabled'`, `streaming: 'forced'`, `storesResponsesServerSide: false`, `supportsResponseChaining: false`, and `CODEX_BACKEND_BASE_URL` — for a plain OpenAI API-key request. Those reads are genuinely reachable pre-client (`CommonCycleTypes.ts:165` reads `getEffectiveContextWindow()` for the cycle budget; `ModelHandler.ts:1323/1355/1450` for compaction thresholds; `modelHandlerOpenAIResponse.ts:635` for the input-limit percentage; and `ModelFactoryRouting.vitest.ts:670` asserts `handler.getBaseUrl()` on a freshly created handler with no client built).

  The finding sees a sliver of this and says the PR "must fix it by making `capabilitiesForRoute(undefined)` also require routability, or by capturing the route before the first capability read." **Neither is available.** `isCodexSessionRoutable()` is `Promise<boolean>` (`codexAuthAccess.ts:50`, it performs a token refresh), and the only other probe, `isCodexSignedIn()` (`codexSignedIn.ts:19`), is also `Promise<boolean>` — both are async because they go through `platform().secrets`. There is no synchronous sign-in/routability probe anywhere in the repo. The consumers are all sync overrides: `getActiveProviderCapabilities()`, `getUsageProviderCapabilities()`, `getBaseUrl()`, `getEffectiveContextWindow()`. So option one is impossible, and option two ("capture the route before the first capability read") is a launch-time async probe — the second owner the finding itself concedes flips the verdict to keep. Shipping without the fix means a handler quietly mis-reporting context limits and cost on the API-key path, which CLAUDE.md's "silent degradation is a defect" rules out. The finding's framing that this is a "latent inconsistency [that] exists today but is currently unreachable" understates it: the factory probe is not an accident that happens to mask a bug, it is the invariant the handler's route snapshot was built on.

  **KILL 3 — cost accounting omits the tests entirely.** The claimed -35 counts only production lines. `src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/ModelFactoryRouting.vitest.ts` breaks in five places: (1) "falls back when subscription re-authentication is required" asserts `handler.constructor.name === 'ModelHandlerOpenAIResponse'` — now ModelHandlerCodex, FAILS; (2) "treats Codex and signed-out Responses handlers with the shared key as compatible" asserts `codex.constructor).not.toBe(signedOut.constructor)` — now the same class, FAILS, and the invariant it encodes evaporates; (3-5) three `await expect(createModelHandler(codexEligibleConfig)).rejects.toMatchObject({name:'AgentError', ...})` cases (superseded re-auth, session-verification read failure, raw token failure) — no probe at creation, all FAIL and must be rebuilt around `handler.getClient()`. A sixth, "falls back to the API-key Responses handler when the subscription switch is on but signed out," still passes only because it asserts the compatibility tag, which the Codex handler shares — it silently becomes a false test. Two design comments also go stale (ModelFactory.ts:380-389 and the ModelFactoryRouting.vitest.ts:980-990 note that the override "is key-neutral: a signed-in run still tags ModelHandlerCodex..."). Net after the rewrite is roughly -20, not -35 — and negative-to-positive once the capability fix is real.

  Two things in the finding DO check out and should be preserved if this is ever revisited: the dual-mode fallback in `ModelHandlerCodex.getClient` (:267-283) really is load-bearing for quota fallback (`quotaFallbackRoutes.ts` flips `setPreferCodexSubscription(false)`, `ModelCell.rebind` → `rebindFor.refreshClient(selection)` on the SAME instance → `getClient` re-decides — verified at ModelCell.ts:107-120), and neither `tryCodexSubscriptionRoute` nor `allowCodexSubscriptionOverride` appears in the rounds-1/2 index or in either survey doc as a candidate (the 49-candidate survey names `tryCodexSubscriptionRoute` only as the downstream consumer in the unrelated `isSubscriptionSessionRoutable` inline candidate at :1563/:1594). So it is not a duplicate — it is simply wrong on the mechanism.

  </details>

- **Stamp the resolved OpenRouter route on the config at dispatch on BOTH paths, so "am I on OpenRouter" stops being re-derived from live settings inside the handler**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on criteria 4 (settled surface, not beaten) and 3 (cost is wrong). I implemented the proposal exactly as written and measured it.

  **Kill 1 — a named regression test pins the OPPOSITE invariant, and step 2 inverts it.** `src/test-kernel/agent/modelHandlers/ModelHandlerOpenAIResponse.vitest.ts:436` is titled "uses the client route instead of mutable OpenRouter configuration during an attempt". It builds a handler whose config has `openRouterOnly: true`, tags the client `'api-key'`, and asserts `usesOpenRouter() === false` inside `responses.create`. My implementation fails it (`true !== false`). The finding reads the three ternaries as "config/live-toggle vs. captured route, and the code already knows the captured route is the truth" — correct — and then proposes to make the CONFIG the truth, which is the inverse. The captured route is not a two-valued openrouter/direct fact: it can be `chatgpt-subscription`, `xai-subscription`, or a Kimi Code reroute, and `isOpenRouterRoutingEnabled` shapes the request wire format from it. `activeCredentialRoute !== undefined ? … : <fallback>` is "captured client route wins; fall back only before a client exists", which is exactly #9030 (cb6ea79b95, "refactor(runtime): snapshot model credential routes", 2026-07-21) — the commit that introduced `resolveClientCredential`, `rememberClientCredentialRoute`, and this very test file. That is the recorded design and the claim does not beat it; it misreads it.

  **Kill 2 — step 1, verbatim, silently disables a live guard.** `createModelHandlerForResolvedCompatibilityKey` runs `isOpenRouterRoutingUnsupported(config, useOpenRouter)` at ModelFactory.ts:588-595 on the config it is handed. Stamping `forceDirectProvider: true` before that call makes `isOpenRouterAccessSelected` false, so the guard never fires. Measured: `ModelFactoryRouting.vitest.ts:504` "rejects GPT-5.6 Pro when OpenRouter routing is enabled" fails — the model stops reporting "requires reasoning mode pro, which OpenRouter does not support" and is silently built as a direct Responses handler instead. That is the CLAUDE.md "silent degradation is a defect" shape, introduced by the proposal's own one-line step 1. The resume path can stamp before the guard only because it also forces `useOpenRouter = compatibilityKey === 'ModelHandlerOpenRouterNative'`; the live path cannot. So step 1 is NOT "the same one-line call the resume path already makes".

  **Kill 3 — the cost is inverted.** All of the −26 comes from the part that is wrong (collapsing the three captured-route ternaries). Strip that and keep only the defensible half — stamp the route on the live path AFTER the guard, and make `resolveClientCredential` prefer the stamp — and the change is a net ADD of ~4 lines, keeps `shouldUseOpenRouter`, and is a bug fix, not a collapse. Filed as `kind: collapse, −25 LoC, −1 element`, the accounting only works if you also ship the regression.

  **Kill 4 — `withCompatibilityRoutingMode` is not a pure stamp; it overwrites a registry field.** The non-OpenRouter arm writes `openRouterOnly: false`, clobbering llm-zoo's own registry value. On the resume path that is safe (the persisted key is authoritative). On the live path it rewrites a fact read off `ModelConfig` by `resolveCodexSubscriptionProfile` (providerCapabilities.ts:221), `resolveXaiSubscriptionCapabilities` (:219), `isUsableSetupModel` (setupModelDefaults.ts:80), and `UsageMonitorModelInfo`'s Pick (UsageMonitor.ts:69), which reads `modelCell.handler` — the stamped config. The claim never mentions that the stamp is destructive.

  The underlying defect the finding identified is real (a mid-run toggle flip re-resolves the other route's credential under a handler class and wire model id chosen for the first — `ModelHandlerOpenRouterNative` always sends `config.openrouterFullName` at :202/:363, so both directions produce a malformed request). That deserves an issue as a _bug fix_, scoped to `resolveClientCredential` only, with the stamp applied after the guard. It is not a simplification and it does not remove `shouldUseOpenRouter`.

  </details>

### flow-families

- **Finish the D6 fence: three of core/flows' eight modules serve exactly one family**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on kill criterion 2 (a dated ruling already decided it, by name), reinforced by criterion 3 (cost wrong) and one misread hop (criterion 7).

  1. THE DATED RULING NAMES ALL THREE MODULES AND DECIDED THE OPPOSITE, 11 DAYS AGO. .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-14-delegation-flow-substrate-consolidation.md:3-9 (Status, PR #10475, implemented 2026-08-15): "item 9's relocations follow the single-consumer test (`CycleServices` and `IToolUseSession` stayed; `toolCallParsing` moved up to `core/flows/`)." That is the executed application of the exact test the finding invokes, recorded against the exact three files the finding wants moved — and it moved toolCallParsing UP into core/flows deliberately, so demoting three of its symbols back into tooluse partially reverses a decision made in the same pass. The finding quotes this sentence and then argues the executor applied the test per-file rather than per-symbol; that is an assertion that the ruling was wrong, not evidence beating it.

  2. THE README ROW IS NOT AN UNPATCHED FENCE, IT IS THE RULING'S OWN RECORD. The finding's headline evidence is that src/agent/core/README.md says core/flows holds "Only what both flow families use" while listing modules that fail it. But that same row (README.md:14) explicitly enumerates `CycleServices`, `IToolUseSession`, and `toolCallParsing` as kernel members, and item 9's fix was "Relocations and one README correction" — the row was amended by #10475 to record this membership. The finding reads a decision as an oversight.

  3. THE "FALSE UNIT" ARGUMENT MISAPPLIES THE TEST. `CycleRunServices` (CycleServices.ts:19-24) is extended by ResponseCycleServices (reflection) and ToolUseRoundServices (tooluse). Both families use it. The finding's counter — "has no consumer of its own" — is the wrong test for a base interface: its consumers are its two extenders, one per family, which is precisely the "both families use it" criterion. The same document states the principle directly at :373-375: "the shared shape is the D6-fenced kernel's job, and a forced merge is the super-cycle trap." Replacing one shared four-field declaration with the same four fields written twice is +1 duplication of a shared contract, against CLAUDE.md's "no dual systems / fewer elements" guardrail, in the one place D6 fenced.

  4. NINE TEST FILES ARE ABSENT FROM THE WRITE-UP. `@agent/core/flows/CycleServices` is imported by src/test-kernel/agent/ResponseCycleTools.vitest.ts, ResponseCycleContinuation.vitest.ts, ToolUseDispatchParallel.vitest.ts, core/FinalToolSynthesis.vitest.ts, core/ToolUseProcessNodeResponseFinalized.vitest.ts, followUp/ToolUseRoundFollowUpMedia.vitest.ts, followUp/ToolUseRoundPrepNodeFollowUpTranscriptLog.vitest.ts, followUp/ToolUseDispatchInterruption.vitest.ts, and tools/BashTool.vitest.ts. The evidence section claims ResponseCycleServices "is imported only by reflection/ResponseCycleFlow.ts" and ToolUseRoundServices "only by tooluse's four round files" — true for production, silently false repo-wide. Fourteen import specifiers must be rewritten, not zero.

  5. THE DuplicateCallMap HOP IS WRONG AND COLLIDES WITH A SECOND DATED TRIAGE. The finding says DuplicateCallMap "has exactly one consumer, tooluse/toolUseRound/ToolUseDispatchNode.ts." It has zero: ToolUseDispatchNode.ts:19-22 imports only normalizeToolCallError, parseToolInput and partitionDuplicateCalls, and declares `new Map<string, number>()` inline at :130. The alias is used only at toolCallParsing.ts:42-43. .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-07-prod-structural-leads-triage.md:56-58 already triaged this twice, with a recorded fix shape that is not the finding's: "Inline Map<string, number> as partitionDuplicateCalls' return annotation (ToolUseDispatchNode needs no change), relocate the…", plus the TS4023 note about the packages/agent d.ts build. A round-1 verifier already refused to re-file that class piecemeal (2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md:2361).

  6. THE PROPOSAL GROWS THE TWO FILES THAT ARE ALREADY OVER THE SMELL THRESHOLD. ToolUseDispatchNode.ts is 622 lines and would take ~90 more (partitionDuplicateCalls + normalizeToolCallError + their docs); ResponseCycleFlow.ts is 650 and takes ~20. It shrinks a 45-line and a 19-line file to inflate two 600+ line ones, against the repo's >400-500 LoC modularity smell rule.

  Not killed by: 1 (not in rounds12-index; the nearest entry, "Fold three single-implementation seams in the flow kernel", is a different change, and the round-2 candidate touching CycleServices was the `<C>` generic erasure, which already shipped — HEAD has no generic), 4 (no ratchet moves: architecture-edges is top-level-src-subsystem only, host-agent-import-baseline covers packages/{cli,desktop,extension,agent}/src and test-kernel is not a host, knip-baseline names only ModelInvocationNode under core/flows), 5 (no wire, persisted file, settings key, or texra-action contract; packages/agent/src/{index,node,schemas}.ts never mention these symbols, and packages/agent/dist is gitignored build output), 6 (edits are narrow).

  </details>

- **One outcome vocabulary at the cycle boundary: both cycle nodes re-declare RUN_OUTCOME as bare literals, and tool-use derives it twice**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on three independent grounds: a dated ruling that names these two exact types and says "Keep"; a wrong end-to-end trace; and a cost recount that lands near zero.

  **(1) DATED RULING — the strongest kill. `.agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-06-10-lifecycle-status-ownership.md` is the proposal that CREATED `RUN_OUTCOME`/`deriveRunOutcome` (header: "Status: Partially landed (2026-07-04 refresh). Step 1 landed: `RunOutcome` + projections..."). The same document deliberately excluded these two DUs from that vocabulary, three times:**

  - Inventory table, line 39: "**Cycle-internal outcomes** | `InvocationResult` (`RetryState.ts`), `ToolUseCycleOutcome` (`ToolUseCycleNode.ts`), reflection `CycleOutcome` (`ResponseCycleNode.ts`), `WaitExecResult` (`ToolUseWaitNode.ts`) | success/failed/cancelled/skipped variants | **Keep.** Node-scoped discriminated unions; converting them into one shared type is the extract-across-divergent-call-sites shape the LOC lesson rejects. Not the disease."
  - "What is NOT broken (do not 'fix')", line 202: "**Cycle-internal outcome unions.** Node-scoped, divergent semantics, no shared consumer."
  - "Rejected alternatives (traps)", line 250: "Unify the four cycle-outcome unions into one shared type | Node-scoped, divergent semantics — **the measured net-ADD extraction shape**."

  The same table, line 253, also rejects the specific shape this proposal builds: "Make `RunOutcome` carry the error payload (sum type `failed(error)`) | The structured error already has an owner ... duplicating it into the status type creates two sources." The proposed `{ outcome: RunOutcome | 'skipped', lastError? }` is that shape with the payload flattened alongside instead of inside.

  The finding anticipates the wrong ruling — it argues against survey item 8 ("bans merging the nodes, not typing their result") and never engages this one. It does not beat it.

  **(2) THE SEMANTICS ARE NOT THE SAME FACT (why the ruling is right).** `RUN_OUTCOME`'s own docstring (`src/shared/schemas/stream.ts:47-56`): "Canonical terminal outcome of an agent run — the single fact 'how did this run end', decided **exactly once at the run-lifecycle boundary**." A cycle outcome is per-turn and explicitly non-terminal: `ToolUseCycleNode.post():237-239` says "All outcomes continue to WaitNode (FlowTransition.DEFAULT). Tool-use agents are conversational — the user can send a follow-up to retry after a failure"; `ResponseCycleNode.post()` returns `FlowTransition.WAITING` on `cancelled` so a resume "retries the interrupted response". A cycle that ends `failed` or `cancelled` routinely belongs to a run that ends `completed`. Typing the per-turn result as `RunOutcome` asserts an identity the run-lifecycle boundary then contradicts — it invites exactly the "decided in more than one place" smear Step 1 was written to end. The labels coincide; the facts do not.

  **(3) WRONG TRACE — the "third derivation" is a different boundary over a larger fact set.** The finding calls this "derive → discard → re-derive → flatten → re-derive for one fact, **inside one turn**." `runToolUseFlow.ts:557` is not inside the turn: it sits after the `do { ... } while (resumedFollowUps.length > 0)` follow-up loop, i.e. once per RUN across arbitrarily many cycles, and it derives from a strictly larger fact set — `cancelled = signal.aborted || Boolean(shared.userCancelledRetry) || sessionLifecycle.parkedWaitCancelled` (:553-556). `parkedWaitCancelled` and `signal.aborted` are not cycle-local and cannot be produced by the cycle node. Its result also drives `prepareForFollowUp` checkpoint rewind (:569-571), which the cycle-level value has no bearing on. That is the run-lifecycle boundary doing its one job, not a redundant third derivation.

  **(4) THE "DERIVE → DISCARD" CLAIM IS FALSE.** "computes `sessionOutcome`... **uses it only for the stage badge at :172**, and immediately re-splits the same two booleans into DU tags at :141-158" — `sessionOutcome` is read for CONTROL FLOW at :148 (`if (sessionOutcome === RUN_OUTCOME.CANCELLED)`), and the `failed` arm at :141 branches on `lastError` because it must destructure that payload, which is the same predicate fed to `deriveRunOutcome({ failed: lastError !== undefined })`. It is also read at :172 on the **throw** path, where no DU is ever returned — the `let sessionOutcome: RunOutcome = RUN_OUTCOME.FAILED` initializer at :129 exists for exactly that case. Nothing is discarded and nothing is re-derived.

  **(5) COST IS WRONG (rule 3).** Claimed -15 LoC / -2 elements.
