Honest recount below; best realistic case is about -5 LoC and 0 elements, and the optimistic path buys its lines by deleting a compile-time invariant, not by removing a concept. `ToolUseCycleOutcome` and `CycleOutcome` both remain named type aliases after the change, so no element disappears; `RUN_OUTCOME` gains one more importer, so the element count moves the wrong way.

  </details>

### progress-rendering

- **Hand the shared progress-view command registry the two objects both hosts already hold, instead of re-declaring seven of their methods as injected callbacks**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  KILLED on (1) DUPLICATE and (2) DATED RULING. (1) rounds12-index.md line 74 already carries this surface as a REFUTED round-1 candidate: "Fold the per-host approval/follow-up/retry adapter lambdas into the shared progress-view command factories". This finding is a strict subset of it (approval lambdas + externalInquiry.dismiss + two lifecycle members vs approval + follow-up + retry). Its refutation lives at .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-25-simplification-survey-49-candidates.md:2401 and names `submitBashDecision` and `ProgressViewApprovalCommandActions` explicitly — i.e. exactly these members. The finding's dedupe grepped for `handleBashApprovalAction`, a method name that appears nowhere in the index, and so reported "not in rounds12-index.md"; grepping the surface instead of the symbol finds it immediately. (2) The ruling is closed issue #11282 "Consolidate cross-host settings/progress-view orchestration duplication (extension + desktop)" (labels tech-debt, area:progress-view, risk:medium; CLOSED 2026-08-23 by owner LionSR). Closing comment, verbatim: "The remaining progress-view command paths were rechecked during the review: both hosts already route first- and second-tier commands through the shared handler factories, with the residual wiring limited to host ports and policy. No parallel orchestration path remains to consolidate safely." The finding's dedupe (`gh issue list --label tech-debt --state all --search "ProgressViewCommandHandlers approval"` -> only #5451) reproduces the identical miss that killed the round-1 candidate two days after #11282 closed. #11282 further records why this is not a cheap deletion: a safe merge "needs live UI testing in both the VS Code extension host and Electron", and the only existing coverage is the mocked bag at src/test-kernel/controllers/ProgressViewCommandHandlers.vitest.ts:106-162 plus ProgressViewRunCommands.vitest.ts:113-117, which would pass regardless of a routing regression — which directly contradicts the finding's "Behavior is identical by construction". The documented revisit path is a comment reopening #11282 with the byte-identity evidence, not a new round-3 candidate. NOT killed on: settled surfaces (the finding's §8 / HOST-2 / A16 analysis is correct — A16 at .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-07-09-tech-debt-audit-runtime-ui.md:550 is the ApprovalRequestHandler replay-vs-settle split, not this), ratchets (grep -c progressView config/ratchets/architecture-edges-baseline.json = 0), external consumers (no wire, persisted file, settings key, or texra-action contract), or cost (recount lands at about -66, close to the claim).

  </details>

### webview-architecture

- **Delete the debugMode gate on webview schema-error reporting: the extension only ever pushes the flag to the launcher, so the progress and settings views drop every validation failure silently**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  KILL 7 (wrong trace): the finding follows only one of the two paths into `logSchemaError` and the one it misses inverts the conclusion.

  `BaseWebviewApp` passes `onSchemaError: (context, error) => this.logSchemaError(context, error)` into `handleCommonMessage` (src/shared/BaseWebviewApp.ts:84), and that hook is invoked at :33 whenever `CommonViewMessageSchema.safeParse(raw)` fails. That schema (src/shared/schemas/commonViewMessages.ts:52-58) is a five-member discriminated union — THEME_SET, DEBUG_MODE_SET, STATE_RESTORE, WEBVIEW_READY, SWITCH_VIEW — with no catch-all. So EVERY view-specific message fails it: that failure is the routing mechanism, documented inline at BaseWebviewApp.ts:51-53 ("Every other common command falls through to `default`: unknown commands (including SWITCH_VIEW) are passed to the subclass's handleMessage") and by the `return false` at :36 that hands the message to `handleMessage`.

  Consequence of the proposed edit: deleting `if (!this.debugMode) return;` does not "make two dead diagnostics live at the cost of six lines". It makes `console.warn('[CommonMessage] Schema validation failed.', <ZodError>)` fire unconditionally for every inbound message in all three views and both GUI hosts — including the progress view's hot path, where ProgressApp's own `handleMessage` (ProgressApp.ts:273-277) only runs after the common-union parse has already "failed" for each stream update. Those warnings are false positives by construction, so the change replaces a silent-but-correct path with a loud-and-wrong one. That is not the "loud failure" CLAUDE.md's silent-degradation rule asks for; it is devtools noise per message plus a full ZodError allocation per message on a streaming path.

  The premise sentence in the proposal — "a message that fails its own Zod schema is always a bug" — is false for the `handleCommonMessage` call site: a message failing the COMMON schema is the normal case, not a bug. It is true only for the dispatcher path (`logMessageSchemaError`), which is a different caller.

  The underlying observation (ProgressApp/SettingsApp's `logMessageSchemaError` is dead by construction in VS Code, since nothing ever posts DEBUG_MODE_SET to those two webviews) is real and I confirmed it at HEAD. But the fix it proposes regresses behavior, and the correct fix has the opposite shape — remove the `onSchemaError` hook from `handleCommonMessage` (routing artifact, not a bug signal), and only then consider un-gating `logSchemaError` for the dispatcher reporters. That is a different change with different accounting and needs its own verification pass; it is not what this finding asks to merge.

  Other kill conditions checked and NOT triggered: (1) not a duplicate — `debugMode`, `logSchemaError`, `DEBUG_MODE_SET` are absent from rounds12-index.md and from round1-index.md/round2.md/round2-doc.md (only an unrelated `isDebugModeEnabled` export mention); #11433 touched BaseWebviewApp but only added `logMessageSchemaError`, it did not touch the gate. (2) No dated ruling found — `git log -S "if (!this.debugMode)"` traces the gate to b4aab4bf29 (2026-01-25, "fix: align lit history behaviors"), incidental, and .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-01-25-prd-progressview-phase5.md specifies the debugMode wire but rules nothing about schema-error logging. (5) No external consumer: DEBUG_MODE_SET is webview IPC only, `texra.logger.debugMode` (coreSettings.ts:403,517) is untouched. So it dies on the trace, not on the paperwork.

  </details>

### cli-architecture

- **Headless workflow progress re-folds raw AgentEvents that the transcript recorder already folded into rows — project the StreamLog instead**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on three independent grounds: a dated ruling that fenced this exact file, three falsified mechanism claims, and inflated cost.

  **(A) A DATED RULING ALREADY DECIDED IT.** `.agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-08-15-single-substrate-hosts-as-renderers.md` §8 "Honest non-collapses (new fence rows) — Found collapsible-looking and adjudicated **keep**, so the next sweep stops re-flagging them" lists, verbatim:

  > "**`workflowPlainOutput.ts` (235).** Looks like a duplicate of the task-group projection; is actually a write-once line emitter over arrival order whose 27-line buffer exists to handle calls arriving before their `stage.start`. Folding it over `TaskGroup[]` snapshots requires snapshot→line diffing — strictly more code. Nets positive; leave it."

  The finding argues the ruling is escapable because it proposes the `StreamLogDeltaBuffer` + `projectTranscriptRow` route rather than `TaskGroup[]` snapshots. That distinction does not survive: the delta buffer is a coalescing snapshot-diff feed (`src/transcript/StreamLog.ts:64-157`, `drain()` returns `appended`/`dirtied`/`textChunks` and `resyncRequired` forces a whole-log re-read), so the consumer has to do exactly the snapshot→line diffing the ruling priced. The ruling's stated rationale — write-once line emitter over arrival order — is the load-bearing part, and it is untouched.

  **(B) WRONG TRACE — all three "already owned" facts are misread.** I re-walked each hop.

  1. _Per-call dedupe is NOT the recorder's upsert._ `lastCallLines` (`workflowPlainOutput.ts:98,107-109`) dedupes on **rendered line text**, not on entry identity. `TexraTranscriptRecorder.ts:596-612` upserts one _entry_ per `logId`, and every `workflow.call` event still produces a `writer.update`/`writer.settle` → a `dirtied` delta → a `workflowTask` row with a `line`. The existing suite pins the case that breaks: `src/test-kernel/cli/WorkflowPlainOutput.vitest.ts:126-128` emits `readTask('running')` then `readTask('running', otherStreamId)` (a `childStreamId` change on the _same_ `logId`, same target stream — `readTask`'s second arg is the call's `childStreamId`, not the emit target, `:39-56`) and asserts exactly one `Running: Read the argument` line. Under the proposal that prints twice unless the line-text map is kept. `lastCallLines` cannot be deleted.

  2. _`openedPhases` is also a print-once guard, not just a reorder gate._ `stage.end` does `writer.update(event.id, { type: GROUP_END, … })` (`TexraTranscriptRecorder.ts:487-499`) — it mutates the **same entry id** from `GROUP_START` to `GROUP_END`. `projectTranscriptRow` returns a `kind: 'phase'` row for both (`src/shared/transcript/projectTranscriptRow.ts:551-577`, whose `previousRow` contract exists precisely because "a closing phase row carries no counts of its own"). A delta consumer that prints `◆ ${row.heading}` on every phase row emits every phase heading **twice**. `openedPhases` cannot be deleted.

  3. _`groupId` gives grouping, not ordering — "log order is strictly better" is false._ The recorder is a subscriber on the same trace, appending synchronously in event-arrival order (`appendSettled` at `:470-483` for `stage.start`). So StreamLog order **is** arrival order; a `workflow.call` that arrives before its `stage.start` sits before the `GROUP_START` entry in the log too. `projectTranscriptRow` recovers the _grouping_ from `groupId`, but a write-once stderr emitter cannot reorder already-printed lines. The suite pins the regression: `WorkflowPlainOutput.vitest.ts:117-152` emits `readTask('planned')` **before** `stage.start phase-map` and asserts `['◆ Map (1/2)', 'Planned: Read the argument', …]`. Under log order that flips to `Planned:` then `◆ Map (1/2)`. The finding's escape ("that path currently prints a bare `◆ ${call.phase}` fallback") describes `flushPendingPhases` (:129-136), which fires only for phases that **never** open — not this pinned case, where the phase does open and the buffer yields the correct `(1/2)` heading. `PendingCallsByStage`/`flushPendingPhases` cannot be deleted.

  **(C) COST IS WRONG (§13).** After (B), the only genuine duplication left is `workflowCallText.ts` (12 lines) versus `src/model/projectWorkflowCallEntry.ts` — a ~5-10 LoC shared-helper fix, not -175. Against that the proposal adds: a `StreamLogStore` handle threaded into `attachWorkflowPlainOutput` (which still needs `session.events` for the `run.start` identity gate and the `status` completion, so it consumes **two** sources instead of one — more coupling, not less); a per-stream `StreamLogDeltaBuffer` + `onChange` subscription; a `resyncRequired` path (impossible to honor cleanly in a write-once emitter without a printed-id set — i.e. re-adding the maps (B) says survive); and a rewrite of the 215-line `WorkflowPlainOutput.vitest.ts` from a `SessionEventHub` fixture to a store+recorder fixture, plus `RunExecution.vitest.ts:96-97,329-336` mock churn.

  **Two costs the finding does not mention at all:**
  - **Determinism.** `StreamLogDeltaBuffer.push` coalesces (`:112-122`: a later `dirtied` overwrites the buffered value for the same id; `:98-101` drops stale emissions), so which intermediate statuses reach stderr becomes flush-timing dependent. Today one event → at most one line, deterministically. The texra-cli skill treats headless determinism as sacred; the finding's own "before/after diff" mitigation does not cover a nondeterministic emitter.
  - **Ratchet.** `src/test-kernel/architecture/transcriptResidencyLeaseSites.vitest.ts:15-23` allowlists `.ensureLoaded(` at exactly 7 sites; `packages/cli/src/runtime/workflowPlainOutput.ts` is not one ("extend the allowlist only for a new focus/runtime owner"). The resync path pushes toward either widening that allowlist or an async `readEntries()` inside a synchronous line emitter.

  **Supporting evidence is real but does not carry the claim.** #10122 (CLOSED) and #11142 (OPEN) both check out. But #11142's body asks for a one-line vocabulary decision on `completionLine` — and the proposal explicitly keeps `completionLine` as-is, so it resolves nothing. #10122 is the model-label duplication, i.e. the ~10 LoC residual, already the smallest part of the claim.

  Not killed by: #1 (not in the rounds-1/2 index; the only survey hit is `workflowPlainOutput.ts:24 WorkflowPlainOutputOptions` in the round-1 unexport batch, unrelated), #5 (correct that stderr is outside the frozen stdout NDJSON and texra-action contracts), or #6.

  </details>

- **splitTranscriptEntries computes a `finalized` bucket on every live-region frame that no production caller reads**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on cost (criterion 3) and churn (criterion 6). The structural observation is correct — I confirmed `splitTranscriptEntries` has exactly one production caller (`ConversationPane.tsx:227-231`) which takes `.pending`, and that `orderedStaticTranscriptEntries` (:256, consumed by `StaticConversationTranscript.tsx:848`) computes the identical set (same three predicates: `isFinalizedTranscriptRow` && `isRenderableTranscriptEntry` && `!userPromptAwaitsLiveContinuation`, with `canPromoteToStatic` mirroring orderedStatic's break-at-first-unfinalized). It is CLI-local, crosses no NDJSON wire, no persisted file, no settings key, no texra-action contract, and no dated ruling covers it (the only doc hit, docs/dev/audits/2026-05-31-tui-performance-audit.md:93,125, merely cites the function and grades the region LOW).

  What kills it is the honest migration cost. The production diff is -6 LoC, not -15: only the `readonly finalized: TranscriptRow[]` line, the `const finalized: TranscriptRow[] = []` declaration, and the object-type braces go away; the ternary-to-`if` swap, the `return`, and dropping `.pending` at the call site are all net-zero same-line edits.

  Against that, `.finalized` is read at 18 assertion sites across THREE suites, not two, and the finding's cited line numbers are call sites rather than `.finalized` reads. Migrating them costs +30 LoC on the cheapest route (a ~10-line two-call helper per suite, since only ConversationTranscript.vitest.ts:19 already imports `orderedStaticTranscriptEntries`) and +50 to +65 if the calls are inlined at each site, because prettier formats a three-argument call across 3-5 lines. Net is +24 LoC at best.

  Element count moves the wrong way as well: one shared function returning a partition is traded for two production functions plus three test-local re-splitters that reconstruct the exact concept being deleted. The concept count does not shrink, it multiplies into test files — section 14 R5 churn.

  The perf rationale is also wrong on substance. The loop already walks every entry to maintain `canPromoteToStatic`, which the finding agrees must stay, so the marginal cost of `.finalized` is N pushes onto an already-mandatory O(n) walk, not a third traversal. The May 2026 TUI performance audit already graded this exact code path LOW.

  </details>

### platform-ports

- **WORKTREE_SHARED_KEYS is a third, extension-only owner of 'which store holds this state key', competing with the catalog slot the 2026-08-15 ruling made canonical**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on cost (criterion 3) plus one load-bearing misread hop (criterion 7). The diagnosis is partly real, but the -150 LoC never arrives and the mechanism proposed for the only family that blocks it does not do what the finding says.

  1. THE DELETION IS BLOCKED BY THE VERY EXCEPTION THE 2026-08-15 RULING CARVED OUT. The ruling (.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-15-shared-contracts-and-retirement.md:185-190) reads: "Use this as the precedent when the same question recurs for other keys in the `slots` migration: prefer the shared store unless **a worktree-scoping need is documented on the row**." For 4 of the 12 keys (GIT_MARK_COMMITS, GIT_AUTHOR_NAME, GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL, GIT_WORKTREE_SUPPORT — all four use GIT_AUTHOR_SLOTS at stateSettings.ts:990,1010,1021,1032) that need IS documented, in the catalog, at the slot constant: "Git identity rows keep the one documented slot divergence in the catalog: the extension and desktop store them in worktree-shared WorkspaceState while the CLI reads them from `.texra/config.json`" (stateSettings.ts:846-851). So the exception fires, WORKTREE_SHARED_KEYS never reaches empty, and worktreeStateStore.ts (67 L) + its vitest (89 L) + the extension wrap (7 L) all survive. The headline -150 is unreachable under the ruling the finding itself invokes as its warrant.

  2. THE ESCAPE HATCH FOR THE GIT FAMILY IS A WRONG TRACE. The finding says "the CLI already stores it in .texra/config.json at the repo root ... give the extension and desktop the same `config` slot ... which makes the value repo-scoped in all three hosts through the mechanism that already exists." I walked the hop: `config`'s workspace store is `openTexraWorkspaceConfigStore(storage, workspaceRoot, warn)` → `workspaceTexraConfigPath(workspaceRoot)` (src/platform/defaults/nodeStores.ts:70-90). `workspaceRoot` is the CLI's `cliWorkspaceCwd` (packages/cli/src/runtime/initPlatform.ts:250-254) and the extension's `workspace.getWorkspacePath()` from `vscode.workspace.workspaceFolders[0]` (extension.ts:235-238). In a git worktree that path IS the worktree directory. `resolveGitCommonRoot` is called separately at extension.ts:245 and feeds ONLY the WorktreeStateStore namespace. So `config` is per-worktree, not per-repo: the CLI's git identity is already NOT repo-shared, and moving the extension to `config` would DELETE repo sharing rather than preserve it — the exact behavior the store exists to provide (`git log -S WorktreeMemento` bottoms out in commits titled "fix: rebuild worktree state sharing on current main"). The proposal's one non-lossy-sounding resolution is factually the lossy one.

  3. HONEST COST ON THE ACHIEVABLE SCOPE IS A NET ADD. What the ruling actually licenses is 6 rows (CODEX_SANDBOX_MODE / _REASONING_EFFORT / _APPROVAL_POLICY, CLAUDE_AGENT_MODEL / _PERMISSION_MODE / _EFFORT — all `sameSlot('workspaceState')`, no worktree need documented, stateSettings.ts:1096-1180). Their readers do not consult the catalog at all: `createEnumStateGetter` hardcodes `platform().workspaceState.get(...)` (src/tools/support/enumConfig.ts:19), so each move needs a store selection plus a `warnAbandonedSlotValue` call in the pattern of src/agent/runtime/detachSubagentsOnStop.ts:31-35 (+5 L each, or +8 L once if folded into the getter), enum relocation with its explanatory block (stateKeys.ts:70-77 precedent, +8 L), catalog comment updates, and a one-row-per-PR cadence the finding correctly demands. The two uncataloged keys (AGENT_ROSTER_SELECTION, CUSTOM_AGENT_PRESETS — confirmed: zero hits in stateSettings.ts) cannot be given to "the catalog as single owner" without ADDING two catalog rows (~+40 L). Net for the licensed scope: roughly +25..+60 LoC, zero files deleted, zero concepts removed — the three mechanisms all still stand afterward. That is section 13's pattern exactly.

  4. WHAT IS LEFT IS SMALLER THAN THE TITLE. The genuine residue — "6 codex/claude rows sit in WORKTREE_SHARED_KEYS with no documented worktree need, contradicting the 2026-08-15 precedent" — is a correctness/consistency item worth one issue, not a collapse of a third ownership mechanism, and it does not shrink the concept count.

  Not killed by criteria 1, 2 (as a whole — the ruling is on the finding's side for the 6 rows), 4, 5, or 6: no duplicate in rounds12-index.md or either survey doc, no prior issue proposes it (#9043 NOT_PLANNED/consolidated, #9053 COMPLETED via 8456cbb993 documenting lifetimes only), no ratchet/frozen-wire crossing.

  </details>

### repo-shape

- **The @shared/schemas deep-import ratchet is 887 lines of three-class classification machinery guarding a baseline of exactly one entry**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on kill condition 2 (a dated ruling already decided it), reinforced by kill condition 4 (settled surface, not beaten).

  **1. The governing skill for this very campaign forbids exactly this proposal shape.** `.claude/skills/find-simplification/SKILL.md:21`, under the heading "Settled Surfaces — Do Not Propose Collapsing", names this baseline by filename and rules verbatim:

  > "The five checked-in architectural ratchets under `config/ratchets/` (`host-agent-import-baseline.json`, `shared-schemas-deep-import-baseline.json`, `host-agent-mock-baseline.json`, `architecture-edges-baseline.json`, and `store-public-surface-baseline.json`). Baselines freeze remaining edges or public surface — they shrink, never widen. **Proposing to _shrink_ one is a good candidate; proposing to delete the ratchet mechanism is not.**"

  The proposal's migration step (1) is "delete `config/ratchets/shared-schemas-deep-import-baseline.json` — dropping the five settled ratchets to four" and steps (2)-(3) delete 95% of the mechanism (887 -> ~40 lines, 14 functions, 4 interfaces, 3 self-tests). That is the sentence's second clause, not its first. The finding never quotes or engages this line; it argues only against issue #9254's construction rationale.

  **2. Two dated re-verify passes examined the exact state the finding cites as its evidence, and ruled the opposite way — four and five days before this survey.**

  `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-21-agent-sdk-readiness-reverify.md:147`:

  > "**`shared-schemas-deep-import`** remains effectively sealed — one documented floor entry (`@shared/schemas/log`), `forced`/`gratuitous` both empty. **No action.**"

  `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-22-agent-sdk-readiness-reverify.md:224` repeats it verbatim (minus "No action").

  This is the decisive collision. The finding's entire load-bearing premise — "the population is 1, so the 3-class classifier is obsolete" — is the _identical observation_ both dated passes made ("one documented floor entry, `forced`/`gratuitous` both empty"), and both concluded "sealed / no action" rather than "shrink the mechanism". `git log` confirms the sequence: #10609 ("empty the shared-schemas deep-import baseline (B1)") landed _before_ both passes, so the readiness reviewer was looking at post-burn-down HEAD, not at the 394-statement population. The finding's "DEFEATING THE RECORDED RATIONALE" section defeats the wrong rationale: it beats #9254's 2026-07-26 construction argument (already superseded) while missing the two 2026-08-21/-08-22 rulings made against the post-completion state. "The burn-down is complete, so retire the classifier" was the available inference, and the standing ruling took "complete and sealed" to mean keep-as-is.

  **3. Kill conditions 1, 3, 5, 6, 7 do NOT fire.** Dedupe is clean (not in rounds12-index.md; the two survey docs mention `shared-schemas-deep-import` only as untouched-surface boilerplate at 49-candidates:713/1055 and round2:115/430/2281/3359, never as a subject). The end-to-end trace is essentially correct: I re-walked `collectCurrent` :548-640 (`SURFACE_INTERIOR` skip at :564, `text.includes` gate at :568, three-bucket sort, `contractedSurface` :656) and confirmed the baseline at HEAD holds one `floors` key, `forced: {}`, `gratuitous: {}`, one `surface` leaf. Cost is a genuine net LoC reduction, not a hidden add. No wire format, persisted file, settings key, or texra-action field is crossed.

  The finding is technically well-traced and honestly scoped. It dies on the rulings, not on the mechanics.

  </details>

- **Three special-case layering rules are each a strictly weaker instance of the one general subsystem-edge ratchet**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  REFUTED on kill condition 2 (a dated ruling already decided it), reinforced by kill condition 4 (settled surface).

  THE RULING. .agents/docs/archived/architecture/2026-08-15-latex-agent-port-design.md:278-283, section "Architecture-edges ratchet update": "Optionally adds a `src/latex` check to `src/test-kernel/architecture/dependencyDirection.vitest.ts`, mirroring the existing `src/shared` no-`@agent` guard. This is belt-and-braces: the subsystem edge ratchet already rejects a re-added `latex -> agent` edge, but the dependency-direction test gives a second independent gate in the `npm test` path."

  That is the finding's whole argument, stated eleven days ago, and decided the other way. The same proposal is what deleted the `latex->agent` baseline row (dropping the baseline 96 -> 95; it is 94 today after 1111625562 dropped two more). It was implemented the same day: 6579ae99b7 "refactor(latex): narrow the three latex->agent coupling sites (#10558)", 2026-08-15, which is the commit that introduced AGENT_IMPORT_PATTERNS' second consumer and the "Latex layer dependency direction" describe. So the latex special case is not a fossil the general mechanism outlived; it was added AFTER the general ratchet existed, BY an author who wrote down that the general ratchet already covered it, as a deliberate second gate. The finding's sentence "neither defends the shared/latex/auth describes" is the load-bearing claim and it is false.

  WHY THE RATIONALE ISN'T STALE. The finding argues the special cases are "strictly weaker" on coverage. On coverage, yes (I confirmed the regex misses `../agent/...` while subsystemEdgeRatchet resolves relative specifiers at :95). But the axis the ruling cares about is authority, and on that axis the special cases are STRONGER. subsystemEdgeRatchet's authority is an editable JSON list whose own `semantics` field frames rows as grandfathered pairs; making a new `shared->agent` import green costs one sorted row in config/ratchets/architecture-edges-baseline.json. dependencyDirection.vitest.ts hardcodes the invariant with an allowlist commented "Keep this allowlist empty; a new entry would recreate the inversion." CLAUDE.md draws exactly this distinction as a repo-level design fact: "Kernel architecture tests under `src/test-kernel/architecture/` ... also pin single-authority invariants with hardcoded allowlists rather than baseline JSON." The same proposal shows the authors know baseline rows are the soft layer: "The `latex -> agent` row is deleted rather than left as stale headroom ... a frozen row with no live site can silently absorb a future re-introduction." Deleting the hardcoded gate and leaving only the row-editable one inverts that.

  SELF-UNDERCUTTING. The finding's own "DO NOT DO" for the auth->model ESLint rule concedes that a provably redundant layer earns its keep on feedback quality (-19 LoC "for a real feedback-latency regression"). The 2026-08-15 ruling makes structurally the same trade for the vitest layer, and the finding's own "What we give up" admits the surviving error message loses the offending filename. It cannot accept the argument for instance 3 and reject it for instances 1 and 2 on a 36-line prize.

  Kill conditions 1, 3, 5, 6, 7 do NOT fire: the trace is correct end to end (I re-walked it), it is not a rounds-1/2 duplicate, there is no wire/settings/persisted/texra-action exposure, and the LoC number is roughly honest. It dies on the ruling alone.

  </details>

### guard-redundancy

- **One table-driven containment suite instead of four hand-copied corpus scanners**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  KILLED under rule 2 (dated ruling already decided it), with rule 3 (cost is wrong) as an independent secondary kill.

  **Rule 2 — the dated ruling.** `.agents/docs/archived/process/2026-08-01-directory-organization.md` §7 is headed "Left alone deliberately / So nobody 'fixes' these later." Item 7 reads: "**The one-Vitest-root convention.** ... `src/test-kernel/architecture/` (10 ratchets, **each named for what it pins**) and `src/test-kernel/support/` (a composition root registered at `eslint.config.mjs:50` ...) move as units." The proposal replaces three ratchets named for the invariant they pin (`approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet`, `transcriptResidencyLeaseSites`, `sessionPresentationBoundary`) with one file named for its _mechanism_ (`referenceContainment.vitest.ts`) holding three unrelated invariants — approval-policy authority, transcript residency leases, and session/presentation ownership. "Each named for what it pins" is the exact property that section freezes, and the finding does not engage with it at all. The finding cites AGENTS.md and checklist §14 R7 as permission, but neither is the governing ruling here; §7 of the directory-organization proposal is, and it is more specific and points the other way.

  **Reinforcing precedent from round 2 itself.** `.agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-26-simplification-survey-round2.md:2479-2528` proposed item (c), collapsing the triplicated `expectRealCoverage` self-check across these same architecture files. The verifier's correction 12 recorded: "it converts a per-file self-check into an implicit cross-file dependency (a `progressEventHandlerRetirement` vacuity regression now surfaces from `approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet`'s failure). For -10 LoC in test code, that trade is defensible but arguable. **Recommendation: ship (a)+(b) as the PR and either drop (c)**." The shipped PR (`0cf3c630ad` / #11439) did drop it: all three `expectRealCoverage` call sites still stand at HEAD (`approvalPolicyAuthorityRatchet:104`, `progressEventHandlerRetirement:64`, `sessionFactAmbientHelperRetirement:33`). This finding's step 3 is a superset of exactly that declined change, applied across the same directory, and does not acknowledge the prior verdict.

  **Rule 3 — the cost is inflated ~2x.** Per-rule lines do not shrink. The current form:

  ```
    it('restricts evaluator call sites to the shared boundaries and CLI adapters', () => {
      expectNoOffenders(
        offendersMatching(EVALUATOR_CALL, EVALUATOR_CALL_ALLOWLIST),
        'call the shared TeXRA approval evaluator; if intentional, extend EVALUATOR_CALL_ALLOWLIST in this PR',
      );
    });
  ```

  is six lines; the equivalent `{ name, pattern, allowlist, guidance }` row is also six. The allowlist constants (~37 lines in the approval suite alone, ~15 in the residency suite) are irreducible data and survive verbatim. So the only real savings are the duplicated import blocks (14+13+9 -> ~10, about -26), the four helper bodies (about -25), and two `describe` wrappers (-4), against +12-15 in `repoScan.ts`, a new row type, and a new optional `files?` scope-override parameter the two presentation rules need because they are single-file assertions, not corpus scans. Honest net is about **-50 LoC**, not -94. Element math: the claim's own list (-2 files, -4 helper fns, +1 shared helper, +1 table constant) sums to **-4**, not the stated -6, and the required row type makes it **-3**.

  **The performance argument does not carry weight either.** Measured at HEAD: `npx vitest run` over the three suites is 819ms of test time (1.66s wall). The memoization win is under a second on a full suite that runs in minutes. The cited precedent `3930a619a5` (#11273) is misapplied — `git show --stat` shows it changed six lines in `subsystemEdgeRatchet.vitest.ts` only, and it addressed TypeScript **parse** cost in a different ratchet, not `readFileSync`+`stripComments` cost in these.

  **What is salvageable.** The finding's own "minimal fallback" — step 1 alone: hoist `filesMatching(pattern, allowlist)` into `repoScan.ts`, delete the longhand copies, keep the three named suite files — does **not** collide with §7's ruling, matches the #11023 precedent (`86bd8bfc8f`, "share TS parse and module-specifier scanning across ratchets") exactly, and is worth roughly **-25 LoC / -3 elements**. That is a clean, low-risk item and should be filed on its own. It is not what this finding claims, and it is not what the -94/-6 accounting describes.

  </details>

- **Retire the shared-schemas ratchet's three-class taxonomy and published-surface tracker — the live population is one import statement**

  <details><summary>Why it was refuted</summary>

  CROSS-VERIFIER CONFLICT, resolved against this item. A second agent (repo-shape) proposed the same retirement and its verifier REFUTED it with evidence this verifier missed: (1) .claude/skills/find-simplification/SKILL.md:21 names this baseline by filename under "Settled Surfaces - Do Not Propose Collapsing" and rules verbatim that "proposing to shrink one is a good candidate; proposing to delete the ratchet mechanism is not" - and the proposal deletes ~95% of the mechanism; (2) two dated re-verify passes, .agents/docs/archived/simplification/2026-08-21-agent-sdk-readiness-reverify.md:147 and 2026-08-22-...:224, made the IDENTICAL observation this finding rests on ("one documented floor entry, forced/gratuitous both empty") four and five days earlier and concluded "sealed - No action". The finding defeats issue #9254 construction rationale, which is already superseded, and never engages either dated pass.

  </details>

## 5. Subsystem summaries

Each auditor described how its subsystem actually works today. These are the most reusable artifact of the round: read the relevant one before changing a subsystem.

## 6. Acceptance criteria

- `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run format`, affected vitest suites, and `npm run check:dead-code-ratchet` all clean per PR.
- Where a deleted export **had** a baseline row, `config/ratchets/knip-baseline.json` shrinks in the same PR. Never add a row: if a deletion orphans another export into dead code, un-export that symbol instead.
- A `collapse` PR must state its **measured** net LoC against `origin/main`, not the estimate, and must name the owner that absorbed the deleted responsibility.
- Anything touching the CLI NDJSON vocabulary, a persisted file, a settings key, or the texra-action result contract needs evidence beyond an in-repo grep.
- Tier C items do not ship without an explicit maintainer decision recorded on the PR.
